Last updated: 2026-09-13 (**The storage check works on a library of any size: a scan in slices, and repairs that look up only the files they were handed** - core only, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no migration, no new permission, no new env var, no new dependency. **THE PROBLEM, on live Deskwell:** the media page's storage check reported "B2 - storage holds more than 50,000 objects, too many to check in one pass". Measured read-only: 64,719 objects / 7.52 GB under `media/`, 63,904 `Media` rows, a whole-bucket listing 21s from the Mac, the usage index ~17s on top (31 MB haystack). `KEYS_PER_PROVIDER_LIMIT` was applied AFTER the full listing had already been paid for, and every repair batch (`delete-orphans` 25 keys, `adopt-claimed` 50, `purge-missing` 200, `correct-sizes`) re-ran the whole `reconcileMediaStorage` - so a cleanup of 1,000 files was 40 full bucket scans, each against the 60s ceiling. **SCAN, `GET /api/admin/media/storage-check?cursor=`:** now returns ONE slice (`StorageReconcileChunk` = `StorageReconcile` + `next: string | null`) from NEW `scanMediaStorageChunk(cursor)` in `lib/media/reconcile.ts`; `reconcileMediaStorage` is gone. Cursor = base64url JSON `{ provider, after }`, Zod-validated by `decodeScanCursor` (a mangled one is a 400, never a guess). Providers holding rows are visited in sorted order. S3-compatible providers (B2, R2, S3, Spaces, Wasabi, MinIO) are sliced by key range with NEW `listStoredMediaKeysAfter(provider, after, { maxObjects, deadline })` in `lib/media/upload.ts` (`ListObjectsV2` + `StartAfter`; `SLICE_LISTING_BUDGET_MS` 15s, `SLICE_MAX_OBJECTS` 50k), and each slice is diffed against rows in `(after, lastKey]` from a raw `$queryRaw` using **`"key" COLLATE "C"`** - byte order, the order S3 lists in; the DB's own collation would drop rows between slices. First slice is open below, last slice open above, so rows outside `media/` still read as missing. The usage index is built IN PARALLEL with the listing. Vercel Blob and Supabase cannot resume from a key and are still listed in one pass (`SINGLE_PASS_LIMIT` 250k, memory guard only). Verified by running the real code read-only against live Deskwell (B2 LIST + DB SELECT only): 2 slices at ~20s each, totals identical to the one-pass count (64,719). B2 honouring `StartAfter` and byte-order alignment with `COLLATE "C"` checked separately: 0 rows lost at a boundary. **REPAIRS, `POST`:** body is now `{ action, items: { provider, key }[] (1-500), force? }`, Zod discriminated union; `keys` is gone and `correct-sizes` takes items too. NEW `deleteOrphanedObjects` (moved out of the route), and `correctRecordedSizes` / `adoptClaimedObjects` / `purgeMissingRows` now take `StorageTarget[]`: each looks up ONLY its own keys (rows by `key IN`, objects via NEW `statStoredMediaObject`, 16 at a time) and applies NEW pure `classifyRowlessObject` - the one verdict function the scan's `diffStorageAgainstRows` also uses, so the pile shown and the check before acting cannot disagree. **`statStoredMediaObject` THROWS when it cannot tell, and for S3 it is a one-key `ListObjectsV2` (`Prefix: key, MaxKeys: 1`), NOT a HEAD:** a HEAD on a wrong bucket/endpoint is the same bare 404 as a missing object, which would have made `purge-missing` drop every row. A lookup error means stale/skipped, never missing. Adoption stays sequential (parallel adoptions in one new folder would each create it). `PurgeMissingResult.skipped[]` gains `provider`. NEW client-safe `lib/media/reconcile-report.ts` (`emptyReconcile`, `mergeReconcile`, `sortReconcile`; type-only import of reconcile). **UI (`MediaStorageCheck.tsx`):** walks the slices (retrying each like a repair batch, `MAX_SLICES` 500), shows "Checking… N files so far", and only shows the report once every slice is in; all four repairs batch through one `inBatches` helper (`correct-sizes` 200). **Gates:** `tsc` clean for these files (3 errors remain in `modules/modular-configurator-for-shop`, a peer's in-progress refactor), `eslint .` clean, `check-client-graph` + `check-import-cycles` clean, `npx vitest run lib/media` 293 passed. New tests: slices cut at every boundary sum to exactly the one-pass report, cursor round trip and refusal, verdict ordering. No schema or `lib/backup` change, so the backup round-trip gate does not apply. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-13 (**Public pages stop carrying megabytes nobody reads: packed payloads, lazier pictures, a CDN that answers stale instead of re-rendering, and two import edges cut** - core + `shop`, `shop-variations`, `product-3d-views-for-shop`, `filters-for-shop`, `product-discovery-tool`, `space-planner-for-shop`, `quote-for-shop`, `gazette`; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no schema, no permission key, no env var, no dependency. Two new Puck field keys (`imageLoading` on Shop: Product Grid and on the discovery block) and two new gitignored generated files. **CORE.** `lib/cache/page-cache.ts`: both `cdnCacheControl()` and `cdnCacheControlForWindow()` now return `public, max-age=<w>, stale-while-revalidate=<w>` through the private `sharedCacheDirectives()`; was `public, s-maxage=<w>`. `s-maxage` implies `proxy-revalidate`, so no shared cache could serve stale and every visitor after a window closed waited for a full render. Module API responses (`lib/cache/module-api-cache.ts`) carry the same value. The old "Cloudflare BYPASSes any other directive" note was Cloudflare's Authorization-header rule; if a live page ever shows `cf-cache-status: BYPASS` after this, put the line back. Settings > General > Speed window hint reworded. `app/layout.tsx`: `theme-init` and `consent-init` are plain inline `<script>` tags first in `<head>`; `next/script beforeInteractive` had only queued them on `self.__next_s` until Next's main chunk loaded. `scripts/generate-module-router.mjs` now writes FOUR files: `router.ts`, `router.public.ts`, **`lib/modules/public-head.ts`** (`collectModulePublicHead`, imported by `app/(public)/layout.tsx`, which may no longer import `router.public.ts`) and **`lib/modules/router.public-slug.ts`** (`PUBLIC_INDEX_PAGE_LOADERS`, `PUBLIC_ROOT_SLUG_CLAIMS`, `resolveModuleIndexPage(base)`, `resolveModuleRootSlugPage(slug)`, imported by `app/(public)/[slug]/page.tsx` only). `router.public.ts` re-exports both helpers. Reason for both: Next follows server-side lazy `() => import()` loaders when collecting a route's client components, so the layout carried every module public page (space planner, three.js, cart, checkout, purchase-order portal) to every page, and the single-segment route carried every deep module page to every product and filter landing page. Guards: `lib/modules/router-split.test.ts` (single-segment router holds only `app/public/<base>/page`, `app/root/[slug]/page` and `lib/root-slug` loaders; `[slug]/page.tsx` never imports `router.public`; no layout imports it), `lib/modules/public-graph-weight.test.ts` (follows `import()` in server files, walks `layout`, `page`, `[slug]/page`, `[slug]/[...path]/page`, and fails on a static import of `crypto`/`stream`/`buffer` (`POLYFILLED_NODE_BUILTINS`) from client code). `scripts/analyse-public-bundle.mjs` also walks from the layout. **NEW `lib/design/font-face-css.ts`**: the `@font-face` sheet kept in module memory and inlined by `app/(public)/layout.tsx` as `<style>`; when memory is empty the layout emits the old `<link>` and refreshes via `after()`; at most one Google fetch per instance per day, 10-minute back-off after a failure, Zod-checked (no `<`, no `@import`, only `/api/fonts/file/` urls). `app/api/fonts/css/route.ts` uses the same fetch and fills the same cache. **NEW `lib/puck/imgDimensions.ts` + `lib/puck/mediaDimensions.ts`**: `width`/`height` on `ImageChipPanel` and `ImageBlock` from the `Media` row, passed through Puck metadata by `renderMetadata.ts`, `renderInfoPage.tsx`, `CactusRender.tsx` and the public layout; one `Media` read by url (`Media_url_idx`) per render that contains those blocks; the editor gets no metadata and renders as before. `lib/puck/siteLogoAlign.ts` `siteLogoFetchPlan()`: a light/dark logo pair renders both `<img>` lazy and preloads each under its `prefers-color-scheme` media query; a single logo is unchanged. **SHOP.** `lib/strip-css-comments.ts` (new); `shopCardCss()` returns the comment-free sheet, memoised per breakpoint pair (also served to filters, discovery and attributes); the product detail sheet is stripped too. `ShopGridPager.tsx` and the editor halves of `ShopProductGrid.tsx` / `ShopRelatedProducts.tsx` emit CSS through core `SharedStyle`. Shop: Product Grid gains `imageLoading` ("Pictures in the first row": `auto` = load as the shopper scrolls, default and what old blocks get; `eager` = first row eager + `fetchPriority="high"` on page one). Dedicated category/collection/tag/supplier pages mark 3 cards eager (was 4). `ProductDetailIslands.tsx` gallery image `fetchPriority="high"`. `lib/card-media-pack.ts`: alt table entries after the first may be `[sharedPrefixLength, rest]`; `sourceId` uuids travel as 22-char url-safe base64 via new `lib/compact-id.ts` (anything else behind `~`); `unpackCardImages` still reads the old shape. **SHOP-VARIATIONS.** NEW `lib/variation-bootstrap-pack.ts`: the product page `initial` prop is packed on the server (`lib/variation-bootstrap.ts`, request-cached) and unpacked once in `lib/use-variation-selection.ts`; islands in `DetailSlotParts.tsx`, `variant-parts.rsc.tsx`, `ShopVariantPurchase.rsc.tsx` pass the packed form; the public `by-slug/[slug]/variations` route is unchanged. NEW `lib/card-options-pack.ts` for card option summaries; `FitOptionRow.tsx` exports `PackedFitOptionRow`. `CardOptionPreview.tsx`: values are buttons from first paint and the preview fetch starts on card pointer-enter, tap or focus - since v0.1.185 the options block was `pointer-events:none` plain text until data arrived, so the preview could never start. Stage `<img>` in `DetailSlotPartsClient.tsx` has `fetchPriority="high"`. **PRODUCT-3D-VIEWS.** `lib/db/heal.ts` `withFreshStorageForAll()` (one `media.findMany` for the tree); `getModelsForProductTree` uses it and reads child ids only, not `getVariationLabels`. NEW `lib/pack/item-tables.ts`, `id-list.ts`, `gallery-payload.ts`, `card-payload.ts`; `gallery-provider.ts` packs and signs each distinct url once; `card-media-provider.ts` packs the overlay; `Gallery3d.tsx` and `CardModel3dOverlay.tsx` unpack via `readGalleryPayload` / `readCardPayload` (old shape passes through). **FILTERS-FOR-SHOP.** NEW `lib/grid-pack.ts` (`packFilterGrid` / unpack) and `lib/compact-id.ts`; `FilterShell` takes one `packedGrid` prop instead of eight, unpacked into `FltGridData`; `ShopFilterGrid.rsc.tsx` and `lib/search-filter-grid.impl.tsx` pack. `lib/db/matching.ts` `getProductFilterMatches` returns a fresh object - it had returned one shared module-level empty object that callers then mutated, leaking one request's price bands and categories into the next. **PRODUCT-DISCOVERY-TOOL.** `lib/db/notes.ts` `listNotesForFlow(flowId)` + NEW `lib/notes-scope.ts`; `notes` left the RSC props and ride the dataset response (`buildDiscoveryDatasetWire()` in `lib/dataset.ts`, `readDatasetWire()` in `lib/dataset-wire.ts`); `datasetHref` carries `&v=2` so an hour-old CDN copy without notes is never reused. NEW `lib/dataset-demand.ts`: the dataset is fetched when the block comes within one viewport or on first pointer/press/focus, immediately when the url has `pick`, idle fallback without IntersectionObserver. Discovery block gains `imageLoading` (same values as shop's); starter layouts set `auto`. **SPACE-PLANNER-FOR-SHOP.** NEW `components/public/SpacePlannerLazy.tsx` and `RenderFrameLazy.tsx` (`next/dynamic`, no `ssr:false`) used by `app/public/space-planner/page.tsx` and `render/[id]/page.tsx`. **QUOTE-FOR-SHOP.** NEW `lib/code-server.ts` holds `generateQuoteCode` (Node `crypto`); `lib/code.ts` no longer imports `crypto`, which had put a ~121 KB gzip polyfill on every shop page through `RetrieveQuoteButton.tsx`. **GAZETTE.** `PostCard` `eagerImage` (default lazy), `PostCardGrid` `eagerImageCount` (default 0), `PostGrid` passes `LISTING_FIRST_ROW_CARDS = 3` on the dedicated listing pages. Wiki: `Architecture-overview.md` (page cache header).)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**An edit did not remake the thumbnail after all, and a delete left its thumbnails behind** - core only; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key. Two defects, both reported from the live site within the hour, both of which the earlier work in this session got wrong. **ONE - THE EDIT THAT CHANGED NOTHING.** `refreshRenditions` called `generateImageRenditions`, which REUSES a copy already filed under the name it was about to write - the branch that stops a backfill minting a second file per picture. After an in-place edit that copy is the pre-edit picture, filed under exactly that name, so it was handed straight back as though it had just been made: `fresh === old.url`, this session's new `if (old.url === fresh) continue` correctly declined to delete it, and the stale thumbnail stayed on every card. Before that guard the same path DELETED it (the Iris 404). So the guard turned a broken picture into a silently out-of-date one, and the claim made earlier in the session - that optimise, resize, crop and replace regenerate the small copy - **was wrong for any picture whose key survives the edit**, which is every exact-named one: a crop, a resize and an extension-only optimise all keep `bella.webp` on `bella.webp`. Live proof: `bella.webp` (id `cmtyvyze3...`) uploaded 21:17:28, thumbnail minted 21:18:45, optimised 21:20:50, and at 22:40 there was still exactly ONE `bella-thumb.webp` row, created 21:18:45. **FIX:** `generateImageRenditions` gains `remake?: boolean` which skips both reuse lookups, and `refreshRenditions` passes it - so the resizer re-encodes from the current bytes, files it at a fresh address (a new address matters: the old one is cached for a year), repoints every reference and then retires the stale copy. The `old.url === fresh` line stays as a backstop and is now unreachable from the refresh; the test that asserted the old no-op behaviour was WRONG and has been replaced by one asserting the re-encode. **TWO - DELETE LEFT THE COPIES.** Nothing ever cleared an item's own renditions on deletion: `carryRenditionsWithOriginal` handles a move, `refreshRenditions` an edit, `discardRenditions` a replacement with something unshrinkable, and the delete routes took the item's row and blob and stopped. So deleting a photograph left its 300px copy in the library for good - unfindable (its name derives from a key that has gone) and undrawable. **NEW `discardRenditionsOfDeleted(media)`** in `lib/media/renditions.ts`: for each known size it detaches whatever held the copy (through `detachMediaReferences`, so `shp_product_media.thumb_url` is emptied rather than left naming a file about to go) and then deletes the row and the blob. Skips an item that IS itself a copy - `oak-thumb-thumb.webp` is not a thing - and applies the same `isAnotherOriginalsRendition` guard, so deleting one of two originals whose names differ only by extension cannot take the other's thumbnail. Never throws. Wired UNCONDITIONALLY (not gated on references) into `app/api/admin/media/[id]/route.ts`, `app/api/admin/media/route.ts` and `bulk-delete`; deliberately NOT into `deleteFolderCascade`, whose subtree already contains the `thumb` child folder. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `check-client-graph` and `check-import-cycles` clean, full suite **7172 passed / 0 failed**; four new cases in `lib/media/renditions.test.ts` (the re-encode, and three for the delete-time sweep). NOT verified in a browser. **STILL TO DO ON THE LIVE SITE:** `bella-thumb.webp` is still the pre-edit picture and re-saving the product will NOT fix it - the save finds the existing copy and keeps it. Either delete that one copy in the library and re-save, or re-edit the picture once this is released. And every picture deleted before this ships has left its copies behind; sweeping those is a separate job.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Two server-only extension points leave the public client map; the three.js hunt is CLOSED as a bundler issue, not an import one** - `product-3d-views-for-shop`, `shop-variations`, `advanced-shipping-for-shop`, `shop`, plus the generated core maps; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency. **KEPT, and correct on its own merits:** `shop.gallery-media` and `shop.card-media` are SERVER points - every provider has an async prisma `load` - but they also carry client components as fields, and shop read them from `extension-points.public.ts`, which the public layout statically imports. So every provider became a client entry on EVERY route. All contributors are now `serverOnly` (the generator withholds 47 entries, was 44) and shop's two resolvers read the SERVER map, which the public layout does not import and which they already loaded lazily. This also removes shop-variations' card-options provider - the 133.8 KB-unused chunk - from every route's graph. **CLOSED WITHOUT A FIX: the 214 KB of three.js on every page.** It is NOT an import edge. Nothing in the repo statically imports `three`; traced across 1,000+ files from the public layout, the page, config.rsc, module-rsc-components and the server map. **Turbopack has put three in a shared chunk group that ~140 CLIENT MODULES depend on** - `ThemeToggle.tsx`, `ConsentBanner.tsx`, `EmailDeobfuscator.tsx` among them. None of those needs a 3D engine, which is the tell: it is a bundler grouping decision, so severing any single import cannot move it. Three attempts failed; see the project memory note before trying a fourth. **TWO SIGNALS THAT LOOK LIKE PROOF AND ARE NOT, both of which I used to claim success wrongly.** (1) CHUNK CONTENTS - "no chunk mixes three with page-global code" proves the merge is clean and says nothing about whether the chunk is still emitted. (2) THE CLIENT-REFERENCE MANIFEST'S `chunks` ARRAY - it lists a client module's chunk DEPENDENCIES, not the page's script list; counting 24 of them against 25 live `<script>` tags and calling them the same thing is simply wrong. The only reliable verifier is the deployed page's script tags. **WHAT DID WORK, kept from the earlier attempt:** `Gallery3dLazy.tsx` stopped three being MERGED into chunks carrying the header menu and the variation swatches - the live chunks are three-only now. Real, but invisible to a shopper. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` and `check-client-graph` clean; 1,547 tests pass across lib/modules, shop and shop-variations.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**A concurrent re-file left 2,744 swatch columns naming files with no library entry; root cause fixed, live data repaired** - core script only, plus a live data repair on Deskwell; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key. **ROOT CAUSE, in `scripts/move-media-into-folders.mts`:** it shards its work with `i % shards === shard` - by LINE, not by ITEM - and its own header claimed "shards never meet on a row however the list is ordered". That holds only while no media id appears twice in the list. The list is a query's output and the query returned duplicates, so the same row landed on several shards at once and each worker read the row's folder before any of them wrote it. Each then did a full `moveOrRenameMedia`: new nanoid blob, row update, former address, reference rewrite, delete of the OLD blob. With N workers on one row that is N new blobs, N former-address rows with the same source key, and N reference rewrites - **the row ends on the address the last UPDATE wrote while the columns end on the address the last REWRITE wrote, and they need not be the same one.** **MEASURED:** 128 items moved more than once from the same address, 898 duplicate move rows, worst item **24 moves inside 3 seconds**, 2.6s average span, **125 orphaned blobs**, **2,744 columns** naming one (`pat_attribute_values` 145 small + 156 tiny, `svr_option_values` 1,130 small + 1,313 tiny). Full-size `swatch` clean in both tables throughout - only the renditions were re-filed. Nothing was ever broken for a customer: every orphan url returned 200, and both usage providers still reported them so no leftover sweep could reach them. **THE FIX:** `const unique = new Map(pairs)` before the shard split, and a line saying how many duplicates the list held. The guard belongs in the script, not in whoever wrote the list. **THE REPAIR, run on the live catalogue with Chris's go-ahead:** derived each correct url exactly as `findRenditionUrls` does - name from the CLEAN full-size swatch, looked for in that picture's own `thumb` folder first and beside it second, oldest wins a tie - so no file was made, nothing was downloaded and nothing was encoded. Dry run first: 1,482 rows, **145/145, 156/156, 1,130/1,130 and 1,313/1,313 resolved, 0 unresolved, 0 proposed urls without a Media row, 0 no-ops**; five replacements curl-checked 200 before writing. Applied in ONE transaction with the verification SELECTs inside it: `UPDATE 158` (pat) + `UPDATE 1324` (svr), all six dangling counts zero before COMMIT. Post-commit sweep: pat small/tiny, svr small/tiny, gallery thumbs, gallery originals and category images **all 0**; six live urls re-checked 200. **BACKUP AND ROLLBACK** in `/Users/chris/Git Local/Deskwell/backups/swatch-repair-2026-09-12/`: both tables' four columns in full (9,111 + 4,607 rows), the old->new map, and a 1,482-statement `rollback-swatch-repair-2026-09-12.sql`. **LEFT ALONE:** the 125 orphan blobs, now genuinely unreferenced - deleting storage objects was not part of what was asked, and the media library's own storage check is the supported way to sweep them. **NOTE FOR NEXT TIME:** a correlated `NOT EXISTS` with `lower()` over `svr_option_values` x `Media` (4,607 x 63,893) ran past five minutes on the live database and was killed; the same shape with exact `=` on the indexed `originalName`/`url` columns returns in seconds. `Media` has both `Media_url_idx` and `Media_originalName_idx` - do not wrap either in `lower()` in the hot path.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**A small copy could be deleted by the very edit that was supposed to remake it** - core only; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new file, no config key. **THE SYMPTOM:** two thumbnails 404 under the main photo on the live Iris chair page, minutes after a new photograph was uploaded. **WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENED**, from the live data: upload landed 20:19:03 as `.../iris.jpeg`; the product save at 20:19:24 wrote all 21 gallery rows; the optimiser re-keyed the original to `.../ZFy9T0P0dOGwFXiuRDfCc-iris.webp` at 20:20:25 and the small copy `b7_OiqLK0egkcZARjdcuO-iris-thumb.webp` was deleted, row and blob, with NO `MediaFormerAddress` entry and NO reference rewrite - `shp_product_media.thumb_url` still named it on positions 0 AND 2. **TWO COLLISIONS STACKED.** (1) `renditionFileName` strips the extension, so `iris.jpeg` uploaded into a folder already holding `iris.webp` derives the same copy name `iris-thumb.webp` - which is why TWO gallery rows shared one thumb url, the new photograph having been handed the old photograph's small copy by `generateImageRenditions`' reuse branch. (2) `refreshRenditions` then asked for a remake, got back a file that already existed (that same reuse branch), and deleted it: `rewriteMediaReferencesInContent` no-ops on an identical url pair, so the repoint was silent and the delete went through anyway. **THE FIX, `lib/media/renditions.ts`:** `if (old.url === fresh) continue` in the delete loop - never delete the file the remake just adopted; and a new `isAnotherOriginalsRendition(media, name, suffix)` (one sibling query per size) applied in both `refreshRenditions` and `discardRenditions`, so a copy another original in the same folder still answers to is left alone rather than treated as this item's stale one. `discardRenditions` gained `id` in its media param for that query. **NOT FIXED, flagged:** the naming ambiguity itself - two originals in one folder differing only by extension still SHARE a small copy, so one picture can be drawn with another's thumbnail. Fixing it properly means carrying the extension in the derived name (orphans every existing non-WebP original's copy once) or a `renditionOfId` column (core schema change). **TWO NEW CASES** in `lib/media/renditions.test.ts`, both verified to FAIL with the guards neutralised; suite 17 pass. `npm run typecheck` and `eslint .` clean, `lib/media` 276 pass. **NO SELF-HEAL for rows already broken:** the sweep only fills `thumb_url IS NULL`, so a row naming a missing copy is never repaired - re-saving the product rewrites every gallery row from scratch and mints the right copies. NOT verified in a browser.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Deleting a picture now unhooks it, instead of leaving a hole in the product page** - core + `shop` + `shop-variations`; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key. **NEW CORE EXTENSION POINT `core.media-reference-detachers`**, contract `lib/media/reference-detachers.ts` (`MediaReferenceDetach {id,url,key}` -> `Promise<void>`), the counterpart to `core.media-reference-rewriters` for the one media operation with no new address to point at. **WHY:** every other operation MOVES a blob and every reference follows it; a delete has nowhere to repoint TO, so the reference was simply left naming a blob that had stopped existing - a broken picture on a live product page while the library looked healthy. The 409 "still in use" gate is the right first answer and was already there; what it did not cover is the owner who means it and clicks **Delete anyway**. Same shape as the Jackson incident below, different half of it. **NEW `lib/media/detach.ts`** - `detachMediaReferences(media)`: runs every module detacher FIRST, then core's own dangling-id columns (the ten `SiteConfig` logo/favicon/app-icon/manifest ids, `InfoPage.ogImageId`, `Member.avatarMediaId` - all plain `String?` with no FK behind them, so nothing in the database tidies them; `MediaTag` and `MemberDataExportRequest` are real cascades and are left alone). Module-first is deliberate: a detacher may throw and a throw aborts the delete, so core's certain writes wait until nothing else can fail. **WIRED INTO FOUR PATHS:** `app/api/admin/media/[id]/route.ts` DELETE, `app/api/admin/media/route.ts` DELETE (query-param form), `app/api/admin/media/bulk-delete/route.ts`, and `deleteFolderCascade` in `lib/media/organise.ts` - the last one previously deleted a whole subtree's blobs and rows with no reference handling at all. Gated on `refs.length > 0`, so an Unused-tile sweep pays none of the cost. Not wired into `app/api/admin/media/optimise/route.ts` (deletes only when refs are empty), `organise.ts`'s collision victim (`takeOverMediaReferences` already hands references over) or the dedupe/refresh paths (they rewrite). **NEW `modules/shop/lib/media-reference-detacher.ts`** (`shopMediaReferenceDetacher`): DELETEs the `shp_product_media` row, then repacks `"position"` per affected product with `row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY product_id ...)` and promotes a new `is_primary` via `DISTINCT ON` + correlated `NOT EXISTS` for products that lost theirs; nulls `thumb_url` (the small copy is derived, the original is still there, so every card falls back to it); nulls `shp_categories.image_url` and the four id columns `shp_products.og_image_id`, `shp_collections.image_id`/`og_image_id`, `shp_categories.og_image_id`. All in one `$transaction`. **DELIBERATELY LEFT ATTACHED:** `shp_digital_files.url` (somebody paid for it), `shp_order_request_photos` and `shp_shipments.signature_url`/`signature_key` (evidence), `description_puck` (a url inside a builder block - whether the block goes, shrinks or shows a gap is an editor's judgement). **NEW `modules/shop-variations/lib/media-reference-detacher.ts`**: nulls `swatch`, `swatch_small`, `swatch_tiny` on `svr_option_values` and keeps the value, which is still orderable; `svr_uploads` left alone (a customer's supplied artwork). **NEW npm script `test:media-detach`** and **NEW `modules/shop/lib/media-detach.live.test.ts`** (`RUN_MEDIA_DETACH=1`, own throwaway `cactus_rt_mdet_*` database on the OVH VPS, real core init SQL + real shop and shop-variations migrations, dropped in `afterAll`) - seven cases, all PASS: row dropped, positions repacked to 0..n-1, primary promoted, a product that still had its primary not given a second, `thumb_url` nulled not dropped, the url/id columns cleared, the swatch blanked with the label intact, and a second run changing nothing. **This is the only thing that executes that SQL** - `tsc`, `eslint`, `npm test` and the module build gate are all green on SQL Postgres will not parse, and the first two runs of this suite failed on the fixture (`shp_products.price` NOT NULL, `type` CHECK is PHYSICAL|DIGITAL|SERVICE, not SIMPLE). **ALSO NEW `lib/media/detach.test.ts`** - five cases locking module-before-core ordering and the "only the columns that named THIS item" rule. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `check-import-cycles`/`check-client-graph`/`check-frozen-migrations` clean, full suite **7169 passed / 0 failed / 478 skipped**. NOT verified in a browser. **BEFORE RELEASE:** both modules statically import the new core contract file, so core must ship FIRST and `requiresCoreVersion` must be raised on `shop` (currently `0.5.1597`) and `shop-variations` (currently `0.5.1593`) to that core version - an install taking either module against older core fails the build on a missing module. Wiki: `Managing-media.md` gains "Deleting something that is still in use", `Authoring-a-module.md` gains the extension-point row.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Swatch chips ask for a chip-sized picture; the legacy-polyfill and image-sizing items turned out not to be fixable as reported** - `shop-variations`; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key. Continues the PageSpeed sweep. **FIXED - THE SWATCH CHIP, ~21 KB.** `card-option-rows.tsx` put the swatch url straight into a box the stylesheet draws at 1.5em; Lighthouse measured the four largest at 128x128 serving a 16x16 chip. Now `resizedImageSrc(value.swatch, CHIP_PX * 2, resizing)` with **`CHIP_PX = 24` declared beside the style that draws it**, so the drawn size and the requested source cannot drift. `resizing` threaded from the block's `puck.metadata` through OptionRow, FitOptionRow, Value and SwatchValue, and through CardOptionPreview for the interactive path. This is the ONE image case on the page that is determinate - a fixed box, a known size. **NOT FIXED, DELIBERATELY - the hero and the discovery tiles (~73 KB of Lighthouse's 94.6 KB).** The core image blocks declare `sizes` of `700px` while the homepage's author-chosen layout renders them at 446px; a Puck image block cannot know the column width it was dropped into, and declaring less would make every full-width image on every install blurry. The discovery tiles are the same shape: an `auto-fit` grid, so 216px is what one viewport happened to measure and the tile is ~300px on others - the existing 300px rendition is the right fit. Lighthouse computes these savings from one viewport and cannot see that the layout is variable. The clean way to claim them is an explicit width option on the image block, which is a feature rather than a tweak. **NOT FIXABLE - the 13.8 KB of legacy polyfills, and the attempt is worth recording.** Added a browserslist (`chrome/edge/firefox >= 100`, `safari/ios >= 15.4`, Safari 15.4 being the oldest browser carrying all six flagged features), rebuilt, and the polyfill chunk came back BYTE-IDENTICAL: the live chunk Lighthouse flagged and the locally built one are the same module (turbopack id `966849`, opening `"trimStart"in String.prototype||...`). It is Next's own polyfill bundle and browserslist does not govern it. **The change was reverted** rather than left in - it narrows supported browsers across every install for a saving it demonstrably does not buy. `npx browserslist` confirmed the config was being read, so this is not a misconfiguration; there is no supported way to drop that bundle in this Next version. **STILL OPEN - the 133.8 KB-unused chunk.** It carries the shop card and variation islands, and it loads on `/contact` too, which is the same defect shape as the three.js one. The fix is the same lazy-registration trick, but the trade is worse: those islands are wanted on most commercial pages, so deferring them buys quiet pages a saving and costs card pages a round trip. Worth measuring before doing. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` and `check-client-graph` clean; 799 tests pass across lib/modules, lib/media, shop-variations and product-3d-views-for-shop.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**224 KB of three.js was downloading on EVERY page of the site, including the contact page** - `product-3d-views-for-shop` plus one core guard test; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key, ONE NEW MODULE FILE. **HOW IT WAS FOUND.** Chris asked what else could speed the homepage up. Measured directly: 815 KB brotli of JS/CSS across 26 files, ALL of them plain `<script async>` - and the same 25 scripts load on `/contact`, which has no cards and no 3D. Three of those chunks carry three.js: **224 KB brotli** (101 + 79 + 44). His PageSpeed report agreed independently, listing them as ~100% unused (98.6 KB of 98.7, 76.8 of 76.8). **THE CHAIN**, found with a static-import walker over 1,036 files: `app/(public)/layout.tsx` -> `lib/puck/config.rsc.tsx` -> `lib/puck/mobile-bar-items.ts` -> `lib/modules/extension-points.public.ts` -> `lib/gallery-provider.ts` -> `components/public/Gallery3d.tsx` -> `lib/three/load-model.ts`. **THE MODULE WAS DOING EVERYTHING RIGHT** - three itself is only ever `await import('three')`, never a static import, anywhere in the repo. But a lazy import inside a module that sits STATICALLY in the graph still gives the bundler something to merge, and Turbopack merged it into chunks carrying the header menu and the variation swatches (a 149 KB chunk holding three + variations + menu/header, and another with three + page-global code). **THE FIX: `components/public/Gallery3dLazy.tsx`**, a small 'use client' module wrapping Thumbs and Stage in `next/dynamic`, registered in place of the direct import. It cannot live in the provider: that file is server-only (prisma) and shop hands those components across the RSC boundary as PROPS, which only a client reference survives. No `ssr: false` - the markup still renders server-side; only the JavaScript waits. **VERIFIED BY BUILD:** after the fix, three sits in 10 chunks containing three-only code and **no chunk mixes it with page-global code** (one 'aos' match was a single incidental `data-aos` string, not the library - checked). **TWO BUILD HAZARDS HIT, both documented and both walked into anyway.** (1) `npm run build` runs `checkout-modules`, which did `git checkout HEAD -- .` across all 34 module repos and SILENTLY REVERTED this fix mid-verification; the untracked new file survived, the one-line edit in the tracked provider did not. Core files are safe (it only touches `modules/`), and no other session lost work - checked every module. Use `npx next build` instead, and keep a scratchpad copy. (2) `npm run build` also runs `prisma migrate deploy` via prebuild, so exporting Deskwell's DATABASE_URL to get past the 27 'Environment variable not found' errors would have run MIGRATIONS AGAINST THE LIVE CUSTOMER DATABASE. Not done. The build therefore cannot complete locally - it dies prerendering `/logged-out` - but compilation finishes first, so `.next/static/chunks` is written and the chunk check is valid (timestamps confirmed: edit 21:17:34, chunks 21:18:24). **NEW GUARD `lib/modules/public-graph-weight.test.ts`**, and the first version of it was WORTHLESS: it looked for a static `from 'three'`, which does not exist anywhere, so it passed happily with the defect reintroduced. The rule that actually matters is the lazy one - a file reached STATICALLY from the public layout must not CONTAIN `import('three')` (or maplibre, react-three, chart.js). Verified both ways: passes clean, fails with the old import and prints the chain through load-model.ts and thumb-stage.ts. **It also needed comments stripped** - Gallery3dLazy's own header quotes `await import('three')` in prose, and the guard read its own documentation as the offence. That is the THIRD guard today to trip on its own explanatory comment. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` and `check-client-graph` clean; 1,763 tests pass.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The re-filing actually run against the live catalogue, and what it taught** - core + `shop` + `shop-variations`, run from this Mac against Deskwell. **WHAT MOVED:** 25,417 variant pictures into `<product>/variations`, ~28,000 small copies into `thumb` folders, and **12,594 duplicate copies deleted from B2** (Media rows 76,468 -> 63,882). Final state: 0 variant pictures outside `variations`, 0 duplicates, 0 stranded urls, 0 broken thumbnails. **THE BOTTLENECK WAS NEVER THE STORAGE.** A B2 server-side copy is 743ms; the reference-rewrite chain is ~330 tiny statements at 21ms round trip from this Mac = **~7s per file, twice per picture** (original + its carried copy). Sequentially that was 106 hours. In-region it is ~0.3s, so this is a remote-operator problem, not a defect. **SHARD BY THE WORK, NOT BY ITS OWNER.** The backfills shard by what they walk - a product, a folder - which balances until the tail, where the remainder sits inside one or two enormous items: the last 970 pictures were six listings with 393 in one, so nine workers were really two. **NEW `scripts/move-media-into-folders.mts`** takes an explicit `media id -> folder id` list and gives each worker every Nth line, which fixed that in one step. Every move still goes through `moveOrRenameMedia`, so nothing is reimplemented. **THE POOL IS THE CEILING, NOT THE MACHINE.** PgBouncer on the live VPS: `max_client_conn` 1000 but `max_db_connections` **40**, `default_pool_size` 25, transaction mode. At 40 workers `sv_idle` is 0 and clients queue; more workers would have queued the SHOP behind the backfill for no throughput gain. The VPS itself is 2 cores / 3.8 GB at load 3.8 - not a place to install a Node toolchain while it serves a live shop. **NEW `lib/media/rendition-dedupe.ts` + `scripts/dedupe-renditions.mts`** collapse duplicate copies onto one file: oldest-and-plainly-named survives (that is the row every lookup in renditions.ts resolves to - `orderBy createdAt asc`, matched on the key-derived name), references handed over via the now-exported **`takeOverMediaReferences`** BEFORE the row goes, and the blob last, never when another live row shares the key. Sharded on `hashtext(folder || normalised name)` so workers never meet on a group and need no cursor. **THE MISS WORTH REMEMBERING:** the first pass matched `originalName LIKE '%-thumb.webp'` and reported "0 duplicates remaining" while **11,429** copies named `x-thumb (1).webp` / `(2)` sat untouched - every one with a plain sibling in the same folder. Matching a name SHAPE is a guess; enumerate the shapes that actually exist (`GROUP BY` the tail) before trusting a cleanup's own all-clear. **A JS TEMPLATE LITERAL EATS `\(`** - the normalising regexes silently became `(d+)` and the count read 0; write `\\(` in the source. **THE DATABASE IS ON UTC AND THIS MAC IS ON BST** - an hour's difference that made a "0 rows written" reading look like the takeover never ran. **`getInUseMediaIds` FAILS SAFE AND SAVED 37 FILES:** the boards module is not installed on Deskwell, its usage provider threw, the index marked itself `degraded`, and everything read as in use. A full-schema scan of all 2,075 text/json columns then found 217 references to the 37 "orphans" in `svr_option_values.swatch_small/tiny` and `pat_attribute_values.swatch_small/tiny` - columns an earlier hand-written check had not looked at. They are `-small`/`-tiny` swatch copies named from the original's DISPLAY name rather than its storage key, which is why core's sweep cannot see them; they are staying put. **STILL OPEN:** three thumbnails lost their reference during pass 1 despite the takeover; the repair now reconstructs a missing `thumb_url` from the copy on file and runs at every phase end, but the cause is not established. One genuine duplicate ORIGINAL remains (`ariel.jpg` twice in one folder) - out of scope, not touched.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The finder's loading panel is gone; the opening question is simply in the HTML** - `product-discovery-tool` only; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key. **A DELIBERATE PARTIAL REVERSAL, and worth reading as one.** **WHAT THE LAST RELEASE LEFT.** Splitting the block so step one renders from cheap queries worked - the question, 18 tiles and "Step 1 of 2" were all in the server HTML - but the OUTER Suspense boundary was still there, and **any async component suspends however small its queries are**. So a cold render painted "Finding your options…" at byte 123,724 with a pending marker, and the question arrived at byte 166,950 in a streamed patch. Verified on the live homepage: a CACHED visitor never saw it (whole document in 0.13s TTFB / 0.51s total, question and all 31 cards present), but a freshly deployed, uncached site showed the panel first - which is exactly what Chris saw, twice. **THE FIX: the cheap half no longer has a boundary.** `ProductDiscoveryRsc` is now async and simply returns `ProductDiscoveryBody(props)`; `DiscoveryFlowLoading` is deleted. The flow row, its nodes, its questions, its settings and one batched picture lookup now block the page's first flush - about 100ms on a cold render of a page carrying the flow - and buy an opening question that is THERE, in the HTML and in the cached copy, with nothing to swap and nothing to flash. **THE EXPENSIVE HALF IS STILL STREAMED**, which is the part that ever cost seconds: `DiscoveryCards` keeps its own boundary, so the 500-product pass still costs nobody anything. The trade is 100ms of first byte against a visible loading panel, on Chris's explicit call. **THE TEST WAS REWRITTEN, NOT DELETED.** Its first two cases pinned the old shape ("must NOT be async", "must return a Suspense") and were part of what had to change - leaving them would have pinned the panel in place. The file now pins the rule that actually matters: the expensive pass lives behind its own boundary, the half that renders the shell never touches it, and **there is no boundary around the cheap half** - asserted as no `<Suspense>` wrapping `<ProductDiscoveryBody>` and no loading copy in the source. Verified by reintroducing exactly that wrapper plus a panel, which fails it. **A TRAP WORTH KNOWING, hit immediately:** the new assertion read the file's own COMMENT explaining the panel it no longer renders, and failed on a correct file. Comments are now stripped before anything is asserted - the same lesson, and the same fix, as `lib/puck/CactusRender.test.ts`. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` and `check-client-graph` clean; 1,699 tests pass across product-discovery-tool, shop and lib/puck.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The finder's opening question arrives with the page instead of behind "Finding your options…"** - `product-discovery-tool` only; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key. **WHAT CHRIS SAW:** two screenshots of the live homepage - the whole viewport below the hero taken up by a black box reading "Finding your options…", then the same area a moment later showing "Step 1 of 2 / What are you looking for today?" and five tiles. The streaming fix had made the PAGE fast while leaving the finder's own opening step gated behind its Suspense fallback. **THE SPLIT.** Everything the opening step needs - the flow row, its nodes, its questions, its settings - is small table reads. What is expensive is the pass over five hundred products (match matrix, prices, ordering, cards), and the opening step needs NONE of it. They were in one async body behind one boundary, so the cheap half waited for the expensive half. Now `ProductDiscoveryBody` renders the shell and step one straight into the HTML, and a new `DiscoveryCards` component holds the product pass behind a boundary of its own, with core's CardGridSkeleton reserving the grid. **THE TRADE-OFF, ACCEPTED DELIBERATELY:** tile counts ("43 products") come out of that pass, so they cannot be in the first paint. Tiles now appear instantly with picture and label and the numbers land with the fetched dataset - about a tenth of a second, since the route is CDN-cached. Already safe because the earlier deferral made `StepBrowse` treat an unknown count as show-without-a-number rather than hide, with a test pinning it. **A cheaper count was NOT used**: the module's one-pass rule exists because a number that disagrees with the products behind it is worse than a late one. **THREE THINGS MOVED TO THE FETCHED SET**, all for the same reason - they are measured against the match matrix and none is wanted on the opening step: `groups` (the culled question groups, back in the wire), `initialCounts` (gone from the block entirely; the client computes them), and `renderedIds`, which the server cannot state before it has drawn the cards - **the shell now seeds "what is on screen" from the grid's own DOM** when it goes ready, which is the more honest answer either way. `initialMatchCount` becomes `number | null`, where null is a real third state meaning not-counted-yet: it prints "Finding products…" rather than a zero, and the empty-state block stays hidden, because `null === 0` is false. **NEW TEST, 3 cases in `ProductDiscovery.stream.test.tsx`** (6 total now): the function that renders `<DiscoveryShell>` must NOT contain `buildDiscoveryDataset`, must contain `<Suspense>` and `<DiscoveryCards>`, and `DiscoveryCards` must contain the builder. Static on purpose - it is a one-line mistake to undo, an await on the wrong function puts the loading screen straight back, and nothing else notices because it typechecks, lints and renders the same markup in the end. Verified by adding exactly that await, which fails it. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` and `check-client-graph` clean; 1,645 tests pass across the three affected modules.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Cold first byte: six catalogue blocks were holding up the whole page** - core plus `shop`, `filters-for-shop`, `product-attributes-for-shop`; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key, ONE NEW CORE COMPONENT, ONE NEW CORE GUARD TEST. **THE MEASUREMENT THAT FOUND IT.** Cold TTFB with a cache-buster, after the payload deferral shipped: homepage **2.90s**, `/green-office-chairs` **4.33s**, `/24-hour-office-chairs` 3.46s, product page 1.22s, a 404 0.40s - and `/find-your-furniture` **0.70s**. That last figure is the whole diagnosis: the finder's page resolves five hundred products, runs every filter over all of them, prices and orders them - far MORE work than a category page - and answered in a fifth of the time, because its heavy half already sat behind a Suspense boundary. **THE CAUSE: an async server component with no boundary above it blocks the ENTIRE first flush, not just its own corner.** So one block's product query held up the header, the nav, the hero and every other block. Six blocks were in that state: shop's ShopProductGrid, ShopFeaturedCollection, ShopRelatedProducts and ShopProductCard, filters' ShopFilterGrid, and product-attributes' ShopAttributeFilterGrid. The homepage carried FIVE grid blocks and 24 cards; a filter collection page - and there are ~19,700 of those in the sitemap, so this is the bulk of the site - carried ShopFilterGrid. **THE FIX, six times: a plain function returning `<Suspense fallback>` wrapping an async body.** The boundary has to sit OUTSIDE the async work - a `<Suspense>` written inside an async component has already awaited everything by the time React sees it, looks identical in review and streams nothing. Same shape, and the same hard-won reason, as ProductDiscoveryRsc, which is where the pattern was proven. The registered `render` is the sync wrapper; the async half is a private `…Body`. As a bonus these now hand `wrapResponsiveRender` an element rather than a Promise. **NEW `components/CardGridSkeleton.tsx`** reserves the grid: same column count, same tile count, each a square picture over two text lines, tokens only, no client component, no shimmer (a pulse on a block that usually lands in a few hundred ms reads as breakage). HEIGHT IS THE JOB - a fallback shorter than the real grid jumps the page when the cards land, which is worse than waiting. It lives in CORE because four blocks across three modules need it and three near-copies is how three of them end up reserving the wrong height. **Core therefore releases FIRST** - the modules import a new core file. **CHECKED BEFORE COMMITTING TO IT:** streaming commits the HTTP status at the first flush, so a block that wants to 404 afterwards cannot. None of the six calls `notFound()` or `redirect()` - the route settles that before any block renders, which is why a bad URL already answered 404 in 0.40s. LCP is unaffected: the cards arrive at the same absolute moment, the shell simply arrives sooner. **TWO TESTS.** `modules/shop/components/puck/grid-stream.test.tsx` (9 cases) asserts each of shop's three grid blocks is not async, returns a Suspense with a fallback, and that the fallback reserves real columns and an aspect ratio - verified by making one wrapper async again, which fails 3 of them. `lib/puck/grid-streaming.test.ts` in CORE is the module-agnostic half: it sweeps every `modules/*/components/puck/*.rsc.tsx`, and any file that both declares an async component and reaches a catalogue helper (listProducts, listGridProducts, buildGridCardItems, renderCards, getProductFilterMatches, loadScopedProducts, buildDiscoveryDataset) must carry a `<Suspense>` - verified by removing one, which fails and names the file. That one guards blocks nobody has written yet. **EXPECTED, NOT YET VERIFIED LIVE:** cold TTFB on the homepage and on every filter collection page should fall to roughly the 0.70s the finder's page already manages, from 2.9s and 4.3s. Warm TTFB was already 0.13s and is unchanged. Total transfer time is unchanged - the same work, started sooner. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` and `check-client-graph` clean; 1,735 tests pass across the four affected modules.)

(My first reading of 3.9s TTFB was my own cache-buster forcing a cold render; corrected before acting on it.) Payload split, single-assignment: **product finder 802 KB (31%), product card images + overlays 730 KB (28%), page chrome 626 KB (24%), card variation swatches 383 KB (15%)** across 31 cards and 862 swatch chips. Images on that page are already done - 6.4 KB total, everything else being `-tiny`/`-thumb`/SVG. **(1) THE FINDER, ~660 KB.** `ProductDiscovery.rsc.tsx` serialised its whole answer set into the page: a 214 KB match matrix, 241 KB of swaps, 119 KB of interned combinations, 71 KB of sort keys. The module's own notes explain why the SERVER cannot skip that pass - the one-pass bargain says a browse tile's count and the products behind it must come out of the same pass - so the fix is where the answer GOES, not whether it is computed. **NEW `lib/dataset.ts`** holds the build, called by both the block and a **new `app/api/public/dataset` route** (`publicCacheTtl = 3600`, so the dispatcher's module-api cache serves one build per hour to everybody). Chosen over a server action deliberately: an action is a POST no shared cache will hold, so every engaged shopper would have paid for a fresh build of the identical answer. **New `lib/dataset-wire.ts`** carries the type alone, the same client-graph split `lib/cards-binding.ts` documents. The block now passes `datasetHref`, `initialCounts` (one integer per node, ~70 of them, computed for EVERY node so a `?pick=` deep link is counted too) and `initialMatchCount`. `DiscoveryShell` fetches on `requestIdleCallback` after paint and holds a new `ready` gate - `urlRead && dataset !== null` - across the card-window fetch and all three grid layout passes. **THAT GATE IS THE WHOLE SAFETY ARGUMENT:** without it an empty matrix means an empty window, and those passes would hide every server-rendered card and put them back a moment later. **A TRAP FOUND AND FIXED ON THE WAY:** `StepBrowse` HIDES a tile whose count is 0 (a type nothing is filed under is a configuration gap). Reading a not-yet-known count as 0 would have blanked the entire first step until the fetch landed. `count` is now `number | null`, null means shown-without-a-number, and **`StepBrowse.deferred.test.tsx` (5 cases)** pins it - verified by reverting the filter to `count > 0`, which fails it. Also fixed: a deep link to the features step would have flashed "0 products / Nothing matches all of that" (now reads `initialMatchCount` until ready) and would have recorded a false DEAD_END beacon (now gated on `ready`). **(2) THE CARD MATRIX, ~255 KB.** Measured precisely: **4,170 `{s,v}` variant entries = 254.7 KB**, against 112 KB for the `options` rows. The rows are the visible half and stay inline; the matrix only answers "which photo is THIS combination?" and is wanted only once somebody points at a swatch. So `card-options-provider.ts` stops building it - which also drops **two set-wide queries from every grid render on the site** - and `CardOptionPreview` fetches its own product's matrix from a **new `app/api/public/card-preview` route** (`publicCacheTtl = 3600`, per product, so it caches for every grid that product ever appears on) on the first `pointerEnter`/`focusCapture`/`touchStart` of the tile. Pointer-enter fires on the TILE, so the answer has usually landed before a shopper reaches a swatch; a missing matrix already rendered the rows plain rather than interactive, so there was no new rendering state to invent. **THE SUBTLE RISK IS SEAT NUMBERING:** rows carry `vi` seats and the matrix answers in seats, so the two halves must number identically or a shopper hovering Walnut is shown a different chair, silently. Both sides call the same `buildCardOptionsFacts`, and **`card-options.split.test.ts` (5 cases)** pins it - including that seats cover values `cardLimit` TRIMMED OFF, which is the off-by-one shape of the bug. Verified by making seats respect `cardLimit`, which fails it. **EXPECTED, NOT YET VERIFIED LIVE:** ~915 KB off the homepage payload (2,541 -> ~1,626 KB) and ~3,061 -> ~2,146 KB of HTML. Cannot be confirmed until the install takes it. Cost: catalogue changes reach the finder within the hour rather than instantly, matching the `s-maxage=3600` the pages already use. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` and `check-client-graph` clean; 1,633 tests pass across the four affected modules.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The add-on picker's 44px thumbnail stops fetching the 130 KB original** - `product-addons-for-shop` plus a comment on core's guard test; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key. **FOUND BY RE-MEASURING THE LIVE PAGE** after core v0.5.1595 / shop v0.1.426 / shop-variations v0.1.184 / pad v0.1.35 went on: the three images that had been full-size now carry a `srcset` and the page went from **339.1 KB to 29.0 KB across them, 310 KB saved** (stage 188.2 -> 15.0 KB at 900w, showcase cards 130.5 -> 9.3 and 20.4 -> 4.8 at 400w). Transforms verified served, not 404ing: the same original is 192,690 bytes of WebP and 5,730 bytes of AVIF at 360w. **WHAT WAS STILL WRONG:** 2 `pad-thumb` images, plain, pointing at the same full-size originals - `.pad-thumb` is a **44px box** and it was being handed a ~1,700px picture, about 150 KB for the pair. Different component from the showcase (`AddonsBox`, the picker rows) and it accepted no `resizing` prop at all. **A FIXED BOX WANTS ONE SOURCE, NOT A LADDER:** `resizedImageSrc(thumbUrl, PAD_THUMB_PX * 2, resizing)` for a 2x screen, rather than a `srcset` - there is no width for a browser to choose between. `PAD_THUMB_PX = 44` is now the single number behind both the stylesheet rule and the source requested, so shrinking the box cannot leave the picture being fetched at the old size. `ShopProductAddonsProps` gains Puck's metadata channel and `ShopProductAddons.rsc.tsx` passes it. **THE GUARD DID NOT CATCH THIS AND CANNOT:** `lib/media/resize-url.guard.test.ts` checks that every component ACCEPTING `resizing` is passed it, and `AddonsBox` accepted nothing - so it was invisible. Catching that shape means ruling on which `<img>` tags ought to be resized, and many legitimately should not (already-small renditions, SVGs, a document rendered for print), which is a judgement rather than a grep. The limitation is now written into the test's own header so a green run is not read as more than it is. **A GREP TRAP WORTH KNOWING:** `AddonsBox.tsx` contains a byte that makes `grep` treat it as binary, so every plain `grep -n 'pad-thumb' ...` over it returned NOTHING and the component looked absent from the tree. `grep -a` finds it. **REMAINING ON THAT PAGE, all deliberate:** 70 swatch dots at `-tiny`, 4 related-product cards and 2 thumb-strip pictures at `-thumb`, 6 SVG logos. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean; 330 tests pass across `lib/media` and product-addons-for-shop.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**"Send pictures at the size they are shown" now reaches the surfaces shoppers actually see** - `shop`, `shop-variations`, `product-addons-for-shop`, plus one guard test in core; uncommitted working tree, no migration, no new dependency, no config key. **THE MEASUREMENT THAT PROMPTED IT:** a live product page (`/iso-mesh-back-stacking-visitor-meeting-and-conference-chair`) fetched with a cache-buster returned 88 `<img>` tags and **zero** carrying a `srcset`, on an install confirmed at core 0.5.1594 with shop v0.1.425 and the setting on. The 8 `/cdn-cgi/image/` references on the page were all one core block's mascot photograph, sitting in the flight payload. **THE CAUSE: two components are reached through shop's slot and tab CONTRACTS rather than as Puck blocks, so neither could ever see `puck.metadata`.** (1) `ShopDetailGalleryRsc` has two branches - a companion module's replacing gallery (`ctx.slot.Gallery`) and shop's own `ProductGallery`. Only the second was wired, and the first is the branch every product with options takes, which on a real catalogue is most of it. (2) `product-addons-for-shop` has two showcase surfaces: the hand-placed BLOCK reads the setting off Puck's metadata, and the TAB - the one nearly every shop uses - rendered `AddonsShowcase` with no `resizing` at all. **CHANGES.** `shop/lib/detail-slot.ts`: `ShopDetailGallerySlotProps` gains `resizing?: ImageResizing`. `shop/lib/detail-tabs.ts`: `ShopDetailTabPanelProps` gains the same. `shop/components/puck/parts/detail-parts.tsx`: the SlotGallery branch is passed `props.puck?.metadata?.imageResizing`; `buildDetailSections` takes `resizing` in its `opts` and hands it to each contributed `<tab.Panel>`; `TabsProps`, `SectionsProps` and `SectionNavProps` each gain Puck's `metadata` channel so the three call sites can supply it. `shop-variations/components/public/DetailSlotPartsClient.tsx`: the replacing gallery's stage image now uses `responsiveImg(main, '(max-width: 900px) 100vw, 640px', HALF_WIDTH_LADDER, resizing)`, with the ORIGINAL still served while magnified - same reasoning as shop's own stage, since `srcset` cannot know about a CSS transform that has not happened yet. `product-addons-for-shop/components/public/AddonsTabPanel.tsx`: passes `resizing` through to `AddonsShowcase`. **RELEASE ORDER IS FREE:** both provider modules spell `resizing` out in their own props type as an intersection with shop's, so each builds against the shop version core currently pins as well as the new one - no core-first dance, no `requiresModules` bump. **NEW GUARD, in core so it covers every module:** `lib/media/resize-url.guard.test.ts` collects every component whose props include `resizing?: ImageResizing` and asserts every JSX rendering of it passes `resizing`, spreads a props object, or is the Puck editor's own `preview` draw (the builder canvas is a client component with no route to the site config). Verified by reverting the add-on fix - it fails and names the file. Greps are scoped to `*.ts`/`*.tsx` with `node_modules` excluded, which took it from 28s to 2.4s. **WHAT IS STILL FULL-SIZE ON THAT PAGE, and deliberately:** nothing. The remaining images are 70 upholstery swatch dots already served as `-tiny` renditions, 2 thumb-strip pictures and 4 related-product cards already served as `-thumb`, and 6 SVG logos. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean; 1,780 tests pass across `lib/media`, `lib/puck`, shop, shop-variations and product-addons-for-shop.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The feed stops publishing rows Google rejects on sight, and says which** - `google-shopping-for-shop`, uncommitted working tree, **NEW MIGRATION `012_withheld_items.sql`**, no core change, no new dependency. **THE MEASUREMENT THAT PROMPTED IT:** with Merchant API access now working, all 19,689 live items were swept via `product_view`. **19,684 ELIGIBLE, 5 NOT_ELIGIBLE_OR_DISAPPROVED - and all five are the same issue**, `item_missing_required_attribute [image_link]`, disapproved on SHOPPING_ADS + FREE_LISTINGS in GB. Confirmed against the live database: IP000152, AC000096, AC000097, AC000098, BR000333 are ACTIVE, not catalogue-hidden, and have **zero rows in `shp_product_media`** - so they are also live on the site with an empty gallery. Not a feed bug; a content gap. **THE FEED BUG IS THAT IT SENT THEM ANYWAY:** `renderItem` destructures `imageLinks` and writes `g:image_link` from it with no guard (`lib/feed-xml.ts`), so an imageless product goes out as an item missing a required attribute and is refused every time, for ever. **(1) NEW PURE `lib/withholding.ts`** - `partitionPublishable` splits built rows into publishable and withheld. In its own file specifically so it is testable: `collectFeedItems` needs several thousand queries before it has anything to decide about, which is how a rule like this never gets a test. Treats a BLANK url as no picture (media rows whose url never resolved arrive as `''`, and `<g:image_link></g:image_link>` is refused exactly like a missing one), but keeps an item whose first url is blank and has a real one behind it, since `g:image_link` takes the first and the rest become additional images. 6 tests. **Deliberately NOT a general validity check** - every other thing Google can refuse is a judgement we would only be guessing at; this is the one defect knowable here, certain, and cheap. **(2) `FeedData` gains `withheld`**, and the withheld rows' ids are also stripped from `osdCandidates` so a promotions source can never advertise an item that is not in the product feed. **(3) NEW `lib/withheld.ts` + migration 012** (`withheld_items` JSONB, `withheld_at` TIMESTAMP(3), both idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`). **Recorded on fetch rather than computed on demand, and that is the whole design decision**: answering "what is being withheld" means building the entire feed - 51MB and several thousand queries on Deskwell - which no settings page can do per render. The scheduled fetch already pays that cost, so it writes down what it found. Capped at `MAX_STORED = 200` stored titles; the total is counted BEFORE the clip so the number stays true. `checkedAt` null means no fetch since this shipped, which is NOT "nothing withheld" - the tab draws nothing rather than an unearned zero. jsonb read is shape-tolerant (it is a column, not a contract) and returns empty rather than throwing on a settings page. The write is `.catch()`ed in the feed route: Google is there for the feed, and a note for the admin is not worth handing Merchant Center a 500. **(4) `GsfSettingsView.withheld`** + a bordered notice on the Feed address card in `SettingsTab.tsx`, colour tokens only, singular/plural throughout, first 10 titles then "…and N more". **CHECKS:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean, **138 passed / 0 failed** across the three modules touched. **RAW SQL WAS EXECUTED, not just typechecked** - NEW `lib/feed-sql.test.ts` provisions a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database on the OVH VPS, applies core init -> shop -> gsf migrations in install order (gsf's carry FKs onto `shp_products`, shop's onto core's tables - a first attempt loading only gsf died on `relation "shp_products" does not exist`), then runs 012 TWICE for idempotency and both of `withheld.ts`'s statements including the `::jsonb` cast against a title carrying an apostrophe and an inch mark. **3 passed, a real run and not a skip.** First attempt used `pg` directly and died on `Hostname/IP does not match certificate's altnames` - node-postgres now hostname-verifies and the cert is for `db.dwoffice.furniture`; the house pattern (`lib/backup/shop-sql.test.ts`) uses PrismaClient over `connectionUri`, which does not. **SHARED-TREE EVENT WORTH RECORDING:** mid-session another agent COMMITTED this session's shop and shop-variations work as `381c7bd` / `b02c90c`, bundled with its own files, and core pinned them in `bbf53cb4` (shop v0.1.424 - which happens to match the `requiresModules` minVersion set here, by luck rather than coordination). Nothing was lost, but `git status` going clean in a module is NOT proof your work is safe either way. **11 unrelated failures in the full suite** are another agent's in-flight `lib/puck/block-spacing.test.tsx` beside a brand-new untracked `lib/puck/CactusRender.tsx` - not this work, and not to be staged with it. **THE BIGGER FINDING, no code:** disapprovals were never why Deskwell is missing from competitor product pages. 99.97% of the catalogue is eligible. Only **3,447 of 19,476 GTIN-carrying items (17.7%)** are matched into a Google product cluster at all - measured by whether `price_competitiveness_product_view` returns a `benchmark_price`, which only exists for a clustered offer. Standalone products cluster 93.3%, variations 17.3%; but 91% of listings have at least one variation clustered, so the landing page is NOT the blocker. On the Luna listing 14 of 33 cluster with nothing in the data distinguishing them - KC0448 yes, KC0450 no, same day, same brand, same page, same valid unique EAN-13. That is Google's catalogue coverage, not a defect here, and no change on this side is known to move it.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The product page now describes the chair the URL names, and quotes it on the side of tax a shopping channel reads** - `shop` + `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree, **no schema change, no core change, no new dependency, no route change**. **THE STARTING POINT, measured against the live site rather than guessed at:** the feed is clean - 19,689 items, 19,476 GTINs, every one a valid EAN-13, all unique, zero `identifier_exists: no`. The LANDING PAGE was the problem, on every variations listing. For `?arm-option=loop-arms&upholstery-colour=ginseng-chilli` the feed sends `gtin 5055852226318`, `mpn KCUP4394`, `151.20 GBP`; the page's JSON-LD carried **no gtin, no mpn, no sku**, an `AggregateOffer` of `lowPrice 48.00 / highPrice 132.00` (net figures the feed's own price does not even sit inside), and an `@id`/`offers.url` pointing at the bare parent while the canonical tag correctly pointed at the variation. Three addresses in one document, and nothing a shopping channel could match on. **(1) NEW POINT `shop.product-selected-variation`** (`modules/shop/lib/product-selected-variation.ts`, provider `modules/shop-variations/lib/selected-variation-provider.ts`): which single combination this request resolves to - `productId`, `canonicalQuery`, `price`, `compareAtPrice`, `retailPrice`, `inStock`, `imageUrls`. Reads the request's query string from shop's existing request-scoped holder, same as `shop.product-canonical-query`, and runs the SAME derivation (`withStrandedFilled` -> `withAutoSelected` -> `resolveVariant`) so the markup and the canonical tag can never name two pages. Returns money in STORED terms - the caller decides which side of tax it wants - and deliberately returns NO identifiers: brand/GTIN/MPN come back through the existing `shop.product-merchant-facts` keyed on that `productId`, so one rule decides them for page and feed alike. **THE CONTRACT IS LOOSER THAN THE CANONICAL POINT'S AND THAT IS DELIBERATE** - it does NOT decline a half-made or unbuyable selection. `isBuyable = enabled && (inStock || showStockCounts)`, so on a shopper payload an out-of-stock combination is stranded-filled to a buyable one and a half-selection is auto-settled; the page really does open on that chair, and the canonical already says so. Only a listing where NOTHING is buyable declines (option left empty -> `resolveVariant` refuses a partial -> range restored). Four test assumptions were wrong about this before the fixtures were run; the tests now pin the real behaviour, not the intended one. **(2) `makeGrossAdjuster`** in `modules/shop/lib/tax-display.ts`: structured data is now quoted TAX-INCLUSIVE whatever the storefront prints. Reuses `displayAmount` with the mode pinned to `'INCLUSIVE'` rather than a fresh `* (1 + rate)`, so rounding matches every other price to the penny. `resolveTaxDisplay`'s skip changed from `mode === 'AS_ENTERED'` to `mode === 'AS_ENTERED' && storedIncludesTax` - a net catalogue on the default setting needs the rates it used to skip loading. The ordinary gross-storing shop still fires **zero** extra queries and nothing about its markup moves. **WHY IT MATTERED:** Deskwell is `taxMode EXCLUSIVE` + `priceDisplayTax EXCLUSIVE` (suffix "ex. VAT"), so the page said £126.00 and the feed said 151.20 - the classic Merchant Center price mismatch on a feed that was right all along. Verified on a second product: page £32.00/£38.00 vs feed `38.40`/`45.60`, arithmetic exact, availability agreeing, ONLY the VAT side out of step. **(3) `ShopProductDetail.rsc.tsx`:** `resolveSelectedVariation` rides the existing `Promise.all`; the child's merchant facts cost ONE extra query and only on a URL that names a combination. When one is named the block publishes a single `Offer` - its own price, its own strikethrough (own normal price, else its own RRP where the owner switched that on), its own availability, its own images first (deduped, absolutised), and `url`/`@id` off the canonical query. `sku` is published for the LISTING only: a child's SKU is staff-only in the selector payload and never reaches a shopper's render, so a named combination publishes none rather than wearing its range's code - the MPN carries the part number, which is what the channel matches on. `shippingOptions` moved to the gross adjuster too, since `shippingRate` is structured data. **NO FALLING BACK between the two identifier sets** - a listing's barcode on a combination would claim forty colourways are the same part. **`requiresModules` on shop-variations raised 0.1.402 -> 0.1.424**: it imports a NEW file from shop, and nothing local catches that. **IF THE RELEASER BUMPS SHOP TO ANYTHING OTHER THAN 0.1.424, THIS PIN MUST FOLLOW IT**, and shop must be released before shop-variations. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7123 passed / 0 failed / 468 skipped** (17 new: 7 in `modules/shop/lib/tax-display-gross.test.ts`, 10 in `modules/shop-variations/lib/selected-variation-provider.test.ts`). No migration, no SQL, so no backup round-trip and no ledger guards. NOT verified in a browser; the live feed and the live page were read with curl and the live database read-only. **KNOWN GAP, flagged not fixed:** because the derivation moves a shopper off an out-of-stock combination, a feed row for an out-of-stock item links to a page describing a DIFFERENT combination at a different price. Harmless today - Google does not serve out-of-stock items - but it is a real disagreement and nobody has looked at it. **ALSO DIRTY IN `modules/shop` AND NOT MINE:** `components/public/ProductDetailIslands.tsx`, `components/puck/parts/detail-parts.tsx`.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The two components that actually carry the weight: add-on cards and the product-page stage** - `product-addons-for-shop` + `shop`, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no core change, no new dependency. **THE HONEST CONTEXT:** the responsive-images switch shipped in core v0.5.1591 and was turned on for the live site, and on the homepage it was worth about **16 KB** - because only 2 of that page's 317 pictures go through a Puck image block. Everything else on it was already handled by pre-generated 300px copies. The two surfaces where the switch is actually worth something are both on a PRODUCT page, and both were deliberately left out until the path was proven on a real site. It now is: transformed urls answer 200, mostly AVIF, and the hero went 44 KB -> 28 KB on a 1x desktop. **(1) ADD-ON SHOWCASE CARDS** (`pads-img`): a card is about 305px wide and the pictures on it are routinely 1,700px - one measured at **130 KB to fill 305px**. Now `responsiveImg(..., CARD_WIDTH_LADDER)`. `AddonsShowcase` is a client component, so the origin is handed down as a prop from `ShopAddonsShowcase.rsc.tsx`, which reads it off Puck's `metadata` channel; the block's props type gains that channel. **(2) THE PRODUCT GALLERY STAGE** (`spd-stage-img`): 1,920px original in a ~630px box, **81 KB**. **THE MAGNIFIER IS WHY THIS IS NOT A PLAIN SRCSET, and it is the whole subtlety of the change.** Zooming scales that very element by `ZOOM_SCALE` (2.5), so a source chosen for the unzoomed box is visibly soft the instant somebody leans in - and `srcset` cannot know about a CSS transform that has not happened yet. It would have been an easy, invisible regression: lighter page, worse product photography, on the one screen where a shopper is deciding whether to buy. So the ORIGINAL is served while `magnified` is true and the responsive set the rest of the time. The swap costs the shopper who zooms one fetch - which is exactly what they were already paying on arrival - and saves it for everyone who does not. `sizes` is `(max-width: 900px) 100vw, 640px` against HALF_WIDTH_LADDER. The origin reaches it through `GalleryProps.puck.metadata`, passed on by the Gallery part. **TWO SELF-INFLICTED LINT SLIPS WORTH REMEMBERING:** a prose comment inserted BETWEEN an `eslint-disable-next-line` and its `<img>` silently un-disables the rule (caught twice in this session, same shape), and the "insert after the last line starting with import" heuristic lands inside a MULTI-LINE import block - `AddonsShowcase.tsx` had an import spliced into the middle of another one. **NOT MINE, also dirty in the shared tree:** `modules/shop/components/puck/ShopProductDetail.rsc.tsx` and `modules/shop/lib/tax-display.ts` are another session's work and must not be staged with this. **STILL OFF THE LIST:** swatch dots (128px drawn at 16px, ~22 KB) are already the smallest copy the attributes module makes; a smaller tier is possible and not worth it. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7106 passed / 0 failed**. No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser - the effect will be measured on the live product page after release, as everything else in this session was.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Pictures at the size they are actually drawn, and five megabytes of fabric that nobody asked for** - core + `product-3d-views-for-shop`, `product-discovery-tool`, `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree. **Core schema change: one new column**, so both halves of the core DB rule (init migration edited in place, plus new **`prisma/core-reconcile/040_cloudflare_image_resizing.sql`**). **THE FINDING THAT REFRAMED IT:** a live PRODUCT page weighed **14.3 MB**. The head of the payload list was not photographs of furniture - it was ten upholstery swatches at **500 to 627 KB each**, the full-size originals. **(1) THE 3D MODULE PRELOADS EVERY FABRIC TEXTURE A PRODUCT COULD PAINT, ON EVERY VISIT.** `preloadProductAssets` is careful work - idle-scheduled so it trails first paint, abortable, capped at twelve models - but it lives in the THUMBNAIL STRIP, which mounts on every product page carrying a model. So roughly **5 MB of full-size fabric photographs** was fetched by every visitor for a viewer most never open. The textures must be full-size (a shrunk one blurs into mush on a model at true scale), so the fix is WHEN, not what: the preload now waits for the shopper to hover a 3D thumbnail, focus one with the keyboard, or open the view - all of which happen a comfortable moment before a first colour change, which is the only thing the warming is for. **(2) THE COLOUR CHIPS DREW THE 400px COPY AT 28px.** One `picture` served both the chip and the 200px hover preview and was sized for the preview - which only appears on hover, so every shopper paid for it. Twenty-five swatches, 19 to 84 KB each, **about 1.2 MB**. Split: `chipPicture` takes `swatchTiny` (128px), the preview keeps `swatchSmall`, and the original stays out of both. **(3) THE GUIDED FLOW'S BROWSE TILES DREW THE ORIGINALS AT 216px** - 1920x1920 among them, **988 KB across five, 959 KB of it waste**. They now resolve the 300px copy in one batched lookup, and saving a tile in the admin makes that copy, so a picture chosen for a tile and nothing else no longer has nobody to make one for it. 37 of the 41 tile pictures already had a copy from the catalogue sweep; the missing ones were made against the live library. **(4) RESPONSIVE IMAGES, the general fix.** The media worker has had a resize path since it was written and it has never worked: `?w=460` answers **502 Upstream error**, because `cf.image` needs Cloudflare Image Transformations and the worker combines it with a SigV4-signed B2 fetch. Transformations IS enabled on the zone - `media.deskwell.co.uk` is a subdomain of `deskwell.co.uk`, not a zone of its own - and Cloudflare's own url form works today, verified: `/cdn-cgi/image/width=460,quality=80,format=auto/<src>` turned a 45.8 KB WebP hero into **15.1 KB of AVIF**, and the re-encode is the bigger half of that. **NEW `lib/media/resize-url.ts`** (client-safe: no sharp, no prisma, no env, because the `<img>` tags live on both sides of the RSC boundary) builds those urls and a `srcset`; **NEW `SiteConfig.cloudflareImageResizing`** gates it, off by default and reaching blocks through Puck's `metadata` channel beside `lazyImages`. **A SETTING RATHER THAN A DETECTION, deliberately:** Transformations is a paid feature on a zone the platform cannot see, and guessing wrong does not serve a heavy picture - every image on the site 404s. The switch's help text says so in as many words. Applied to four Puck image blocks including the LCP hero; **9 tests** cover what would fail silently - another host, a relative url, a data uri, SVG, GIF, double-wrapping, a nonsense width - since rewriting a url that cannot be transformed is invisible in review and fatal on screen. **STILL TO COME once the switch is on:** the add-on grid (1754px at 305px) and the gallery stage (1920px at 634px) want the same treatment. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7106 passed / 0 failed** (9 new). `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` required by the column and run as a real PASS. NOT verified in a browser.)
