Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The fonts come from your own domain, and a block stops asking twice** - core only, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no new dependency, two new routes. **THE MEASUREMENT (Lighthouse, live homepage):** four render-blocking stylesheets, of which the two third-party ones cost **750 ms each, 1.5 seconds for 2.8 KB**. Preconnects were already in place, so that is not connection setup - it is what asking Google costs from a browser. **TWO SEPARATE DEFECTS.** **(1) THE PAGE ASKED GOOGLE FOR FRAUNCES TWICE.** `family=Fraunces:wght@400;500;600;700` from a BLOCK (the phone-number block, whose font the owner had set to the site's own heading face) alongside `family=Inter:wght@400;600&family=Fraunces:wght@500;600` from the design tokens. React de-duplicates stylesheet links by href and these two urls genuinely differ, so hoisting could never fix it - the block has to decide not to ask. It now can: **`buildFontHref` asks for the full `400;500;600;700` range for every token family** (free at runtime - a browser downloads only the faces a page uses - and it is what makes a block's wider need already covered), **new `fontFamiliesFromTokens`** hands those families to Puck's `metadata` channel alongside `lazyImages` (**new `lib/puck/blockFont.ts`** is the pure reader, because config.tsx is shared by the client editor and the RSC render), and `googleFontHrefForFamily` returns null for a family the page already loads. Wired through the phone block and both menu render paths; `MenuBlockClient` takes `loadedFonts` as a prop because it is a client component. **(2) THE FONTS WERE A THIRD PARTY AT ALL.** **NEW `app/api/fonts/css/route.ts`** proxies Google's css2 response from this site's own origin and rewrites every `fonts.gstatic.com` url inside it, and **NEW `app/api/fonts/file/[...path]/route.ts`** serves those files - so BOTH third-party connections disappear, not just the stylesheet. Behind the site's own CDN that is tens of milliseconds rather than 750. It is also a privacy fix: no visitor request reaches Google, no IP and no Referer, on a platform that ships a consent banner. **THE SECURITY SURFACE IS THE WHOLE POINT and it is tested** (**new `lib/design/font-proxy.test.ts`, 14 cases**): `sanitiseFontQuery` allows only `family` (name + weight axes) and `display`, so the css route cannot be turned into an open proxy for arbitrary Google urls; `isSafeFontFilePath` refuses a leading slash, `..`, a backslash, `://`, `@`, a query string, whitespace and anything over 300 characters, so the file route can only ever read from one hard-coded origin. Both functions typecheck and lint perfectly happily while waving a `..` through, which is exactly why there are tests. **A FIXED MODERN USER-AGENT is sent upstream, deliberately:** Google varies that response, handing older browsers woff and newer ones woff2, so forwarding the visitor's own would mean a separate cached object per browser build. woff2 is universal since 2015. **FALLS BACK TO GOOGLE ON FAILURE** - a 302 to the original url rather than an error, so a proxy having a bad minute costs speed and never the site's typeface. **VERIFIED END-TO-END AGAINST GOOGLE'S REAL RESPONSE, not asserted:** the live css2 answer for this site's families is 40 `@font-face` blocks and 40 gstatic urls; the rewrite leaves **zero** references to Google, and the file-path guard accepts all 40 real paths while rejecting every traversal and host-injection shape tried. **PRECONNECTS MOVED:** the root layout's `fonts.googleapis.com`/`fonts.gstatic.com` pair is gone, since a public page no longer connects to either; the admin and setup layouts, which still ask Google directly, carry their own. CSP needed no change - `style-src` and `font-src` already allow `'self'`, and the Google origins stay listed for the fallback and the admin. Checked that the PDF/print harness is unaffected: it drives a real url through `page.goto`, so a relative font href resolves normally. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7097 passed / 0 failed** (14 new). No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser; the TTFB and blocking-time effect will be measured against the live site after release. **STILL BLOCKING, and not touched:** 11 KB + 2 KB of Next's own CSS chunks. Those are render-blocking by design and most of their reported 470 ms was queueing behind the font requests; genuinely reducing them means inlining critical CSS, which is a large change for a small gain.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The guided flow stops holding the whole page up** - `product-discovery-tool` only, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no new dependency, no core change. **THE MEASUREMENT FIRST, because the obvious diagnosis was wrong.** Cold-render TTFB across five live pages: `/about-us` 0.45s, `/gazette` 1.0s, a product page 1.45s, `/shop` 2.6s, and the **homepage 7-8 seconds** (four samples: 7.0, 7.8, 8.0, 7.2 - so not cold-start variance). **It is not the database.** Timed against the live Postgres: the 500-product scope query **4 ms**, variants for 500 products **17 ms**, variant child pricing **63 ms** - under a tenth of a second of queries behind a seven-second render. `pg_stat_statements` is installed on the VPS but not in `shared_preload_libraries`, and loading it needs a restart of a live customer's database, which was not done. TTFB tracks HTML SIZE across all five pages instead, so the time is render CPU and serialisation: the flow's one pass resolves five hundred products, runs every filter group over them, prices them, orders them and interns the result, and **none of the rest of the page could be sent until it finished**. **THE FIX IS A SUSPENSE BOUNDARY, and its whole correctness is in the SHAPE:** `ProductDiscoveryRsc` is now a plain, synchronous function returning `<Suspense fallback={...}><ProductDiscoveryBody/></Suspense>`, with every await moved into the body. A `<Suspense>` written INSIDE an async component has already awaited everything by the time React sees it - identical in review, identical in markup, and it streams nothing. Verified nothing in the render path stringifies the tree (`Render` is used as JSX in renderInfoPage/renderLayoutWithContent; only tests call renderToStaticMarkup), so the boundary can actually flush. **NEW `ProductDiscovery.stream.test.tsx`, 3 cases**, guarding exactly what nothing else can see: the exported component is not async, what it returns IS a Suspense with a fallback, and the placeholder carries a min-height and `aria-busy`. Typecheck, eslint, markup and every existing test are identical whether the boundary is in the right place or the wrong one; the only symptom is a slow first byte on somebody else's machine. **THE ONE-PASS BARGAIN IS UNTOUCHED** and deliberately so: the counts on a browse card and the products behind it are the same answer only because one pass produced both (see DiscoveryShell's own header). Streaming keeps that promise exactly - the total work and every byte that arrives are unchanged. **THE TRADE, stated:** a streamed response carries the placeholder markup AND the real content in the same body, so a cached page grows by a few hundred bytes and a cached visitor sees the placeholder for the milliseconds before the inline swap. Worth it against seven seconds. **NOT VERIFIED LOCALLY** - proving the TTFB drop needs a production build and a server, neither run unbidden; it will be measured against the live site after release, the way everything else in this session was. **ALSO FOUND, a setting rather than code:** the sitemap carries **19,706 urls of which 18,970 (96%) have a query string**, and "How long to keep the odds and ends" is set to "Same as above" - so all 19,000 expire hourly and every expiry is a fresh render. Raising it to 24 hours is the single cheapest thing available on this whole subject and is the owner's to do. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7083 passed / 0 failed** (3 new). No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Search dropdown cards arrived unstyled on pages with no product cards of their own** - `modules/search` only, no schema change, no new dependency, no core code change beyond the `modules.json` pin. THE DIAGNOSIS: the dropdown's `shopCards` mode fetches `/search/cards` and lifts the `#srch-shop-cards` fragment (`modules/search/components/public/SearchBoxClient.tsx`), but the shop's card stylesheet reaches that page through `components/SharedStyle.tsx`, which React hoists **out of the markup and into `<head>`** as `<style data-href data-precedence>`. The sheet was therefore never inside the fragment - the old comment claiming "style tags included" was stale. A page that already draws product cards (category, product, search results) carries that sheet anyway, so nobody noticed; a page that draws none - **checkout**, contact, a plain content page - showed raw markup: no grid, no card chrome, no swatch row, no price styling. THE FIX: new `modules/search/lib/adopt-hoisted-styles.ts`, called from the cards fetch handler, copies across whichever hoisted sheets the live page has no namesake for. Matching is on the **name** half of `data-href` (`<name>-<contenthash>`), not the whole value: the cards page renders inside full site chrome, so its head also carries header/footer sheets, and a page running a variant of one of those must keep its own rather than have the fetched page's land on top of it. Only a sheet with no namesake at all - the card CSS - is adopted. No `CSS.escape` (jsdom has no `CSS`, and a module name is only as escapable as whatever an owner called it), so names are compared in a `Set` in JS. New test `modules/search/lib/adopt-hoisted-styles.test.ts`, jsdom, four cases: adopt a missing sheet with its `data-precedence`, dedupe an identical one, refuse to clobber a same-named different-hash sheet, ignore styles with no `data-href` and anything outside `<head>`. Checks: `npm run typecheck`, `eslint .`, `npm test` all clean.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**The homepage stops downloading the admin** - core only, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no new dependency, no module change. **THE DIAGNOSIS, from a real production build rather than a guess.** `.next/server/app/(public)/page_client-reference-manifest.js` listed **215 client modules for the homepage, 65 of them admin screens**, and a 189 KB (brotli) / 772 KB chunk carrying `AdminTooltip`, unified-inbox's `followUpMinutes` and `excludeColleagues`, product-attributes' `useForVariations` and discovery's `flowScopeMissing` was in the page's own `<script>` list. Earlier static tracing had said there was NO path from a public entry, which pointed at Turbopack chunk grouping - **and that was wrong, because the tracer treated a dynamic import as a boundary and stopped**. Following those edges too found a genuine, entirely static chain ten hops long: `(public)/page -> renderInfoPage -> config.rsc -> MembersBlocksRsc -> members/admin-link -> members/registration -> email/templates -> email/index -> **email/identity** -> extension-points`. A members block can send a registration email; the email stack resolves its sender through an extension point; and `lib/email/identity.ts` read the COMPLETE registry, which statically imports all 160 entries including every module's admin screen. **THE GAP IN THE EXISTING SPLIT.** Core already had `extension-points.public.ts` for exactly this, and its own header describes this leak - but the four files that needed it could not use it: the points they read (`core.outbound-email-identity`, `core.outbound-email-record`, `core.message-destinations`, `core.agent-content`) are marked `serverOnly` and are therefore withheld from the PUBLIC map. A server file needing a serverOnly point had nowhere to go but the full map. **NEW THIRD MAP, `lib/modules/extension-points.server.ts`** (generated, gitignored beside its siblings): everything except the admin COMPONENTS, with serverOnly entries KEPT - split by what an entry IS rather than where it is read, which is the only rule that cannot regress when a module ships a new point. `lib/email/identity.ts`, `lib/email/record.ts`, `lib/conversations/destinations.ts` and `lib/agent-content/providers.ts` now import it. **SECOND DEFECT, in the generator's own blind spot, which its header admits:** the directory rule only sees an entry's OWN path, so a provider under a module's `lib/` counts as public and is emitted into the public map - and then imports an admin component. `product-3d-views/lib/variant-field-provider.ts` reaches `Product3dVariantColumn`, which reaches `Model3dPreviewModal`, which reaches the **whole 3D viewer**, and a public search block reading the public map dragged the lot onto the homepage. `scripts/generate-module-extension-points.mjs` now WALKS each entry's static imports and treats "can reach components/admin/" as admin - memoised, build-time only, and deliberately not following dynamic imports, since that is the shape a module should use to keep an admin component out of a public graph. Withheld count 40 -> 44. **MEASURED ON TWO REAL BUILDS:** homepage client modules **215 -> 150**, admin screens **65 -> 0**, and the 729 KB chunk is gone (largest is now 504 KB). The byte saving on the wire is to be confirmed against the live site after release - the manifest's chunk set is not the same thing as what the page executes, and claiming a figure from it would be guessing. **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: the three Puck editor field widgets.** `MediaPickerField`, `SiteColourField` and `ClearableNumberField` still reach the public graph, because a module's block file carries its editor `fields` next to its render and the RSC export spreads the editor component. Fifteen files across EIGHT module repos import them. Measured before deciding: all 150 client modules share all 22 chunks, so those three are 3 of 150 in a set the page loads anyway and removing them saves about their own size - roughly 8 KB compressed. The only core-side fix is to split the 475-line media picker behind a `next/dynamic` facade, which risks the page builder's image picker and cannot be verified without a browser. Numbers put to the owner rather than the work done. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7076 passed / 0 failed**, and `npm run build` green twice (asked for explicitly). No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Blog covers draw the small copy, and the rendition lookup stops dragging sharp around** - core + `gazette`, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no new dependency. **NEW `lib/media/rendition-lookup.ts`**, split out of renditions.ts: finding a copy touches the database and nothing else, but it lived in the file that imports sharp - and sharp brings libvips, which the file tracer copies into every function that can reach it (measured on a real build: 2 GB of written function payload across 130 functions). Any module can now resolve a small copy for the price of a query rather than an image library in its bundle. `findRenditionUrls` is re-exported from renditions.ts so every existing caller is untouched. Carries a deliberate narrow copy of `findChildFolders` (four lines, `thumb` only) rather than importing organise.ts, which pulls upload.ts, which is where sharp is - importing it would have defeated the whole point. **GAZETTE POST CARDS** (`lib/post-cards.ts`): `imageUrl` is now the 300px copy where the library has one. Posts on this platform reuse product photographs as covers, and a product photograph is a studio shot - **three cards on the live homepage were pulling 591 KB between them to fill three thumbnails**, every one of which already had an unused small copy sitting in the library. One extra batched query for the whole set, never one per card, and `.catch` back to the originals so a lookup failure costs weight rather than the page. Safe because `imageUrl` is read in exactly ONE place - PostCard.tsx's own `<img>` - so the feed block, the listings, the related-posts strip and the load-more endpoint are all covered by the one change, and the post page's own header reads its picture separately and still gets the full one. **TWO FINDINGS, NOT FIXED, both reported rather than guessed at.** **(1) The 189 KB (brotli) admin-code chunk on the homepage cannot be traced from here.** Re-measured on the current deploy as `05dpsw2e16ds6.js`, 772 KB raw, 41 admin hits: `AdminTooltip` x16, unified-inbox's `followUpMinutes` x13 and `excludeColleagues` x10, product-attributes' `useForVariations` x14, discovery's `flowScopeMissing` x8 - alongside 188 `p3d-` hits, which is the public 3D card code the homepage legitimately needs. So admin code from four modules is sharing a chunk with public code. Static tracing found NO import path from any public entry (`app/(public)/layout.tsx`, `[slug]/page.tsx`, `lib/puck/module-rsc-components.ts`) to any of those admin screens, which points at Turbopack's chunk GROUPING rather than an import leak - and that cannot be read without a real production build (`.next` holds a dev manifest only, and dev chunking is not production chunking). **A build is the next step and it needs asking for** (CLAUDE.md: never run one unbidden). **(2) The 2.8 MB of homepage HTML is mostly not waste.** Flight payload profiled at 2.21 MB: **758 KB** the `find-your-furniture` wizard, **746 KB** 47 shop-card rows, **325 KB** shop-variations' `card-varopts` chips, 372 KB everything else. The wizard's payload is NOT data for something hidden - `pdt-*` classes are in the rendered markup, so it is server-rendered and on screen, and DiscoveryShell's own header defends the single server pass on purpose (counts on a browse card and the products behind it are only the same answer if one pass produced both). Deferring it would be a redesign of that module's core bargain, not an optimisation. The 47 card rows are 47 real cards - 32 distinct products, several legitimately on more than one grid. **What IS compressible is the SHAPE of `card-varopts`:** every value carries a full swatch url whose ~95-character folder prefix repeats for every value on every card, plus `"swatch":null` on every text option and a uuid per option. Interned the way `packCardImages` already interns card photographs, that is roughly **100 KB of the 350 KB** - real, but the least valuable of the three jobs and a payload-format change to another module with its own client decoder and parity risk. Numbers put to the owner rather than the work started. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7076 passed / 0 failed**. No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser. **NOT RELEASED.**)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Google files the shop's listings alongside everyone else's selling the same thing** - `google-shopping-for-shop` **v0.1.18**, uncommitted working tree, **new module migration `011_mpn_and_category_taxonomy.sql`**, **new module route**, no new dependency, no core change. **THE DIAGNOSIS, measured rather than reasoned about.** The live Deskwell feed was fetched whole (45.8MB, **19,689 items**) and taken apart: **19,476 carry a GTIN, 0 with a bad check digit, 0 duplicated**, brand on all 19,689 - and **0 items carried `g:mpn`, 0 carried `g:google_product_category`**, with `gsf_product_data` holding **0 rows** on the live site. Google files an offer against an existing product cluster by GTIN, then by brand + MPN, then by how much the title and picture resemble somebody else's. Three competitors' pages in the cluster Deskwell was missing from were read: **none of them publishes a GTIN at all**, and they key on the manufacturer's part number (`KCUP4452`, `KCUP4460` - DACS publishes it as `mpn`, County Office Supplies as `sku`). So the shop's barcodes were real, unique **and unique to the shop**, matching nobody; MPN was absent; and the title ("... with Sliding Arms - None / Black" against "... Bespoke Colour Seat Bergamot Cherry") was never going to carry it. Three tests, three misses, a product page of one. **THE CAUSE IN THE CODE:** `lib/identifiers.ts` read `const mpn = opts.standalone ? data.mpn : undefined` - a variation was refused a part number outright, and Deskwell's catalogue is variations end to end - and the file's own note said SKUs are deliberately never used because the shop withholds its buying codes. On a catalogue bought in, that column IS the maker's part number: `shp_products.sku` on the Capra children reads `BR000321`, `KCUP4449`, which is exactly what the other retailers publish. **CHANGE 1, `mpn_from_sku` (BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false).** Off by default and deliberately so: the same column on a shop that numbers its own stock is a private buying reference, and publishing it tells the world what the shop calls its stock. On, each row publishes **its own** code - the child's for a variation, the product's for a standalone. **CHANGE 2, a variation may now carry its own part number.** The ban that stays is inheritance: a child still never claims its parent's typed-in MPN, because a part number names one part. A typed-in MPN still outranks the derived code on the listings entitled to one. `identifiersOf` now takes `codes: { barcode, sku }` and `opts: { standalone, mpnFromSku }` rather than a bare barcode; all three callers updated, `lib/merchant-facts-provider.ts` included, so **the page's JSON-LD and the feed publish the same part number** - the whole reason that file shares the function. NEW `lib/identifiers.test.ts` (8 tests), which it had none of. **CHANGE 3, `google_product_category`, held per CATEGORY.** NEW table **`gsf_category_taxonomy`** (`category_id` PK, FK to `shp_categories` ON DELETE CASCADE, `google_product_category` TEXT NOT NULL - nothing to say is no row). Per category and not per product because a shop with twelve thousand products has perhaps forty categories. NEW `lib/category-taxonomy.ts`: `googleCategoryResolver()` walks a category's ancestors to the nearest mapped one (memoising the whole chain, 20-hop cycle guard matching `buildCategoryPaths`), `taxonomyRows()` builds the admin list with each row's inherited fallback, plus the two db helpers. NEW `lib/category-taxonomy.test.ts` (8 tests). `lib/feed-data.ts` gains `leadCategoryOf()` - the one category an item is filed under - and both `product_type` (the shop's own trail) and `google_product_category` (Google's taxonomy) now come off that same answer, so the two can never describe an item as two different things. A product's own typed-in value still wins. Values are NOT validated against Google's published list: Google revises it, and refusing a save because this module's copy was a year old is the worse failure. **NEW ROUTE** `app/api/admin/category-taxonomy/route.ts` (GET/PATCH, `shop.manage`) - its own address because it is a list as long as the category tree and the settings tab has no business re-fetching forty rows every time somebody ticks a box; PATCH checks the category still exists so a deleted one is a 404 rather than a foreign-key 500. **NEW COMPONENT** `components/CategoryTaxonomySection.tsx` (own file, own fetch), plus the MPN switch in the settings tab's Defaults card. `GsfCategoryTaxonomyRow` lives in `lib/types.ts`, not beside the db helpers, so the client component names the shape without importing a file that reaches Prisma. `cactus.module.json` teardown gains `gsf_category_taxonomy`. **`001_initial.sql` WAS edited to match** - it is this module's own first migration, not a frozen released one, and a fresh install must land where an updated install lands. **GATES:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean whole repo, `npm test` **7076 passed / 0 failed**, and - because no standard gate executes a line of SQL - **every new statement was run for real** against a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database on the OVH VPS: migration 011 applied twice over (idempotent), the settings SELECT/UPDATE, the upsert, the re-upsert, the clear, and the FK cascade when a category is deleted; the two altered `shp_products` SELECTs were parse-checked read-only against live Deskwell. `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a **real PASS** (4 tests, 219s), required because a module migration added a table and a column. NOT verified in a browser. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX:** the brand on all 19,689 live items is `Dynamic Office Solutions`, the supplier, where the cluster's other members say `Capra` or `Dynamic`; and the titles still carry the shop's own wording. Both are site data, not platform behaviour. Wiki: `Google-Shopping.md` gains "Getting your listings onto the same page as everyone else's".)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**A product's folder reads as the product's pictures again: small copies in `thumb`, variant pictures in `variations`** - core + `shop` + `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no new dependency, **one new module route**, **three new terminal scripts**. **THE PROBLEM:** everything a product owned was filed flat in one folder - its own photographs, every variant's photographs, and a small copy of each. On the live catalogue that is 28,335 pictures and 40,314 copies over 1,274 folders, with 26,054 of the pictures belonging to a variant; the copies outnumbered the originals and a product folder could not be read by eye. **CORE.** New `lib/media/rendition-naming.ts` holds `RENDITION_FOLDER_NAME`, `renditionFileName`, `KNOWN_RENDITION_SPECS` and `isRenditionFileName` - split out of `renditions.ts` deliberately, because that file pulls in sharp and `organise.ts` now needs to recognise a rendition without dragging libvips behind it. `generateImageRenditions` files each copy in the original's `thumb` child folder, creating it **only when something is actually written** so a folder of pictures that all decline to be shrunk gains nothing. Every lookup - `findRenditionUrls`, `refreshRenditions`, `discardRenditions` - accepts a copy in **either** the `thumb` folder or the original's own folder, with `thumb` winning, so a half-finished tidy-up is untidy and never broken. New `organise.ts` helpers `getOrCreateChildFolder` / `findChildFolder` / `findChildFolders` (batched, one query per page render). **THE HOOK THAT EARNS ITS PLACE:** `moveOrRenameMedia` now carries an item's copies with it (`carryRenditionsWithOriginal`, dynamic import, new `carryRenditions` opt-out for the carry itself and for the superseded-victim parking). Left behind, a copy is stranded twice over - wrong folder, and a name derived from a key that no longer exists - so every lookup comes back empty and the next render mints a duplicate. Not hypothetical: `setProductMedia` makes the copy and `reorganiseProductMedia` files the picture straight afterwards. **SHOP.** `productFolderSegments` and friends take `subfolder`; `relocateProductFolderIfMoved` deliberately resolves its path WITHOUT one, because what moves on a category rename is the product folder and the subfolder rides along as a descendant. **SHOP-VARIATIONS.** `VARIATIONS_FOLDER`, `resolveVariationsFolderId`, `findVariationsFolderId`; **new route `app/api/admin/products/[id]/media-folder`** (GET looks, POST creates - the same split, for the same reason, as shop's own) so the variations grid uploads and browses in `variations` rather than the parent's folder. Swatch pictures are untouched and still go to `colours`. The variant save route and the CSV import pass `subfolder: VARIATIONS_FOLDER`. **BACKFILLS, both terminal jobs and both restartable and idempotent:** `modules/shop-variations/scripts/backfill-variation-folders.mts` (walks parent products, resolves the folder once per parent, moves each variant picture; its copies follow automatically) then `scripts/backfill-rendition-folders.mts` (walks folders, recognises a copy by matching the names that folder's OWN originals would give theirs - not by the `-thumb.webp` tail alone - and moves them into `thumb`). **Run the variations one FIRST**, or the rendition sweep does the same work twice. Neither has been run against the live site. **SHARED PICTURES ARE LEFT ALONE.** A picture that sits on a listing AND on one of its own variants (510 of them live) would otherwise be dragged into `variations` by the variant save and dragged back out by the listing's next save, each tug a real copy and delete at the provider - so `reorganiseProductMedia` skips any url the folder-owner listing also uses when a `subfolder` is asked for, and the backfill excludes the same rows from both its count and its sweep. **CHECKED AGAINST THE LIVE DATA, read-only:** all three new raw queries parse and return, and all **40,314** existing copies match the derived-name rule the sweep recognises them by - nothing orphaned, nothing falsely claimed. **Also new:** `scripts/css-stub-hook.mjs` and `modules/shop-variations/scripts/css-stub-hook.mjs`, copies of shop's - a terminal script that touches the media library reaches the module extension points, which reach Puck's stylesheet. **WATCH OUT:** running `scripts/prebuild.mjs` locally runs `checkout-modules.mjs`, which **reverted every uncommitted edit to a tracked module file** mid-task. Re-applied from a saved patch. **Unrelated flake fixed while here:** `lib/puck/module-layout-site-logo.test.tsx`'s render test timed out on some full runs and passed on others - it is one dynamic import of `config.rsc` (every block of every installed module), 1.9s idle against a 5s cap, several times that with 567 files running in parallel around it. Timeout raised to 20s; nothing about the test or the code it covers was wrong.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**A small copy appears when a picture does, and is remade when the picture is edited** - core + `shop`, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no new dependency, one new module route. **THE TWO GAPS CLOSED.** **(1) UPLOADS.** Already covered, and verified rather than assumed: every writer of `shp_product_media` goes through shop's `setProductMedia` - the product editor route, `import-engine.ts`, shop-variations' variant route and its CSV, so a variation's photographs are a child product's rows and need nothing of their own. `setProductMedia` gains **`opts.makeThumbs`** (default true); `import-engine` passes **false**, because a copy is a download, a decode and an upload each and an import writes hundreds of products in one request. **NEW `modules/shop/app/api/cron/thumb-top-up/route.ts`** (manifest cronJobs, 03:40 daily) is what makes that safe: a bounded trickle - 60 pictures, 40s of a 60s ceiling - that picks up whatever the inline path skipped. Deliberately NOT a backfill; a first-time catalogue is tens of thousands of pictures and several gigabytes and belongs in `scripts/backfill-thumbs.mts`. **(2) EDITS IN THE MEDIA LIBRARY - the real defect.** Crop, resize, replace or optimise a picture and its small copy was a picture of what used to be there, on every card and in every thumbnail strip, indefinitely. Nothing notices: a stale copy is a perfectly valid image file, and `tsc`, `eslint`, the build and every test were green over it. **NEW `refreshRenditions(media, oldKey)`** in `lib/media/renditions.ts`, called from **`repointMediaToBlob`** - the one place every in-place rewrite passes through (optimise, resize, crop, replace, video), so a caller added later cannot forget. `oldKey` is required, not convenient: a copy is named after the original's key and an optimise re-extensions that key, so copies are FOUND by the old name and REMADE under the new one. Dynamically imported there, because renditions.ts pulls in sharp and a static import would drag libvips into every function that can reach upload.ts. **THE ORDER IS THE WHOLE THING:** remake at a fresh address, repoint references through `rewriteMediaReferencesInContent` (so shop's `thumb_url` follows, via the rewriter it already has), and only THEN delete the stale copy. **A REMADE COPY MUST NOT REUSE ITS OWN ADDRESS** - renditions are `immutable` in the media worker now, so overwriting one hands every browser and edge cache bytes they have been told to keep for a year and the new picture is simply never fetched. **SO RENDITION KEYS ARE NOW NANOID-FORM**, not the exact `<original>-thumb.webp` they were: they are looked up by `originalName` (derived from the original's key) and never by key, so nothing needed the key to be readable. Three things fall out - a fresh address on every remake, immutability by the ordinary worker rule rather than the `isRenditionKey` exception, and **no collisions at all**. That last one was costing real money: the exact form is a read ("is this name free?") then a write with nothing holding the gap, and a twelve-way backfill had eleven runners lose the same name repeatedly - 1,532 failures in one stretch, and a per-folder lock did not shift it. The lock and the retry are both gone; the problem is gone with them. **A FAILED REMAKE LEAVES THE STALE COPY ALONE**, and that was a bug the new test found: the first cut deleted it regardless, so a storage hiccup turned an out-of-date picture into a broken one. Also handles the item ceasing to be shrinkable (a photograph replaced with an SVG): references go back to the original, which every renderer already falls back to. **`KNOWN_RENDITION_SPECS`** names all three sizes core mints (thumb 300, small 400, tiny 128) so an edit remakes whatever copies an item happens to have without being told which exist. **BACKFILL FINISHED on the live install: 27,803 of 27,814 pictures carry a small copy, 40,314 copy files, and the 11 remaining are correct** - every one already within 300px and under 30 KB, where a second near-identical file would cost storage and save nothing. **MEASURED LIVE, homepage: 5,537 KB of photographs -> 2,152 KB.** Integrity checked rather than assumed: 25 copies sampled against their originals as 8x8 greyscale fingerprints, all identical, all 300x300 - the collision path never put the wrong picture anywhere. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7052 passed / 0 failed** (4 new in `lib/media/renditions.test.ts` covering the remake, the no-copies case, the no-longer-shrinkable case and the failed remake). No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser. **NOT RELEASED** - core and shop both want a patch bump, and the cron only starts running once the install takes it.)

Last updated: 2026-09-12 (**Two defects found by checking the live site after the update, not by a gate** - `ultimate-seo` (**new module migration `006_document_builder_revision.sql`**) and `advanced-shipping-for-shop`, uncommitted working tree. Both are holes in what shipped yesterday, and neither would have been caught by `tsc`, `eslint`, the suite or a build. **(1) A BUILDER CHANGE COULD NEVER REACH AN EXISTING INSTALL.** ultimate-seo 0.1.18 taught the twin builder to publish FAQs and to stop publishing attributes the product page hides. The install took it, ran it, and every twin stayed byte-identical - `Markup | 6` still in them, still no questions. `rebuildLlmDocuments` decides staleness purely on `source_updated_at` vs the entity's `updatedAt`: has the CONTENT moved. A change to the code doing the writing moves nothing, so a product nobody has edited is skipped for ever, and the nightly cron (`rebuildLlmDocuments()`, no force) would have skipped it every night indefinitely. The only cure was a human finding **Rebuild everything** (`?force=1`). **FIX:** new `builder_revision INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` column, `DOCUMENT_BUILDER_REVISION = 1` exported from `lib/ai/documents.ts` and bumped whenever the output shape changes, written by `upsertDocuments`, read by `listDocumentFingerprints` (now returning a `DocumentFingerprint` rather than a bare timestamp), and a second clause in the staleness filter. Default 0 rather than 1 deliberately - every existing document WAS written by an older builder, so all of them are stale, which is exactly true - and the time budget drains the backlog a night at a time with pruning suppressed while `incomplete`. **`003_ai_seo.sql` was NOT edited to match, on the second attempt:** the first one added the column there too for fresh-install tidiness and `scripts/check-frozen-migrations.test.ts` failed it - 003 is released and has run on live installs, so its bytes are frozen (the same guard that caught a peer session four days ago). A fresh install runs 001-006 in order and lands in the same place. The reason is written into 006's header. **(2) 78% OF PRODUCT PAGES PUBLISHED NO DELIVERY MARKUP.** Yesterday's `shippingDetails` work is correct and works - verified live on `boho-forest-green-soft-seating-accent-chair`, three services with right prices, days and `addressCountry` - but shop asks `shop.product-delivery-timing` about the LISTING id, and Deskwell scopes delivery by a range attribute ("Shipping") that on a variations listing sits on the hidden CHILDREN. `resolveProductDeliveries` resolves a parent from parent-level facts only, so it returned nothing and the listing published no `shippingDetails` at all. Measured on the live catalogue: **95 of 439 listings carry the range at parent level, 344 do not** (235 of those have children that all agree, 109 differ). **FIX, and deliberately NOT in the cart path:** `resolveProductDeliveries` is what prices a checkout and was left alone - a parent is never a cart line. The fallback goes in `lib/delivery-timing-provider.ts`, the published seam: anything that resolved to nothing is asked again through `getVariantChildIds`, and the children are merged by **`mergeVariantDeliveries`** - which already existed, is what the storefront's own service picker uses, and gives every service the variations offer between them at the DEAREST price and LATEST date among the ones carrying it. So the listing never quotes cheaper or sooner than the combination finally chosen, and the seam publishes what the page shows. **`mergeVariantDeliveries` moved out of `lib/estimate-service.ts` into `lib/merge-variant-deliveries.ts`** rather than being exported from it: estimate-service imports `@/modules/shop/lib/tax-display`, and the delivery-timing provider sits in core's generated extension-point registry, so an edge from the provider into it is the cycle shape `check-import-cycles` exists to catch (it caught the equivalent one in google-shopping yesterday). The function is unchanged, and gains its **first tests** - 6 cases, it had none. `timingForChunk` factored into a reusable `timingOf`. **NO FEED CHANGE:** the Google feed asks about variation CHILD ids, which resolved on their own already, so its rows are untouched. **VERIFIED AGAINST LIVE DATA:** the Eclipse listing's two child range values carry 5 and 3 services respectively, so it will now publish all 8 - the same set its picker shows. **CHECKS:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7046 passed / 0 failed**, `check-frozen-migrations` green after the revert. `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` run for the new column. NOT verified in a browser. **STILL OPEN AND STILL CONFIG:** the SEO structured-data profile's return-policy fields are unfilled, so the live Organization record carries no `hasMerchantReturnPolicy` and llms.txt still has no Returns line - checked again today, unchanged. Wiki: `Ultimate-SEO.md` gains "An update rebuilds the copies on its own"; `Advanced-Shipping.md` gains "A listing answers for its variations".)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**The attachment-filing backlog now clears itself, without a button** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no new dependency. Filing collected email attachments into the Media Library shipped with a manual button on the settings screen (`AttachmentFilingSection`) that a site owner had to press, repeatedly, to work through a backlog. Replaced with a nightly sweep: `sweepAttachmentFiling` runs from `app/api/cron/housekeeping/route.ts` alongside the other sweeps, on its own 12s budget (`FILING_BUDGET_MS`), last in the list because it is catching up on history rather than keeping up with the present - a morning it gets none of the budget still gets every other sweep done. `BackfillOutcome` renamed `FilingSweepOutcome`. Removed: the settings button, `AttachmentFilingSection.tsx`, the admin backfill route, and the `CollectingPanel` toggle for it - there is no setting, because "should these move?" is not a question worth asking a site owner. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint modules/unified-inbox` clean, module suite green. No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Unified-Inbox.md` loses the settings-button mention, gains the nightly sweep.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**Proof of delivery becomes a filed library item instead of a loose object nothing owned** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no route change, no new dependency. **WHAT WENT WRONG, on the live site:** DW000172's signature 404ed on its own order screen. `captureSignature` wrote the bytes through core's `uploadMedia` and stopped there - no `Media` row, and no entry in shop's media usage provider. To core's storage check that is an **orphan**: an object in the bucket with no row and nothing pointing at it, listed with a delete button over it. The classification core already has for this shape is `claimed` - an object with no row that the site is nonetheless using - and it is decided purely on whether a usage provider names the address. Shop's did not, so the object read as spare and was binned within the week, taking the one picture whose entire job is to exist on the day there is an argument. **FIX (1):** `lib/tracking/signature-capture.ts` now files properly - `getOrCreateFolderByPath(['Orders', orderNumber, 'Proof of delivery'])`, `buildLibraryUploadKey` for a readable non-colliding name, `uploadMedia` with the folder path and preset key, then `saveMediaRecord` (`isDecorative: true`, no alt text to chase on a courier's scrawl). `captureSignature` gains `options.orderNumber` and returns the **row's** url rather than the upload's, because on a proxied provider only the row knows the canonical address. `Orders / <order number>` is the same tree the damage-report photographs already use (`app/api/member/orders/[id]/photos/route.ts`), so an order's evidence sits in one place. **FIX (2):** `lib/media-usage-provider.ts` returns `shp_shipments.signature_url` AND `signature_key` - url because it is what renders, key because it is what storage is reconciled against. Belt to the braces: an item somebody drags out of the folder still reads as in use. **LIVE DATA REPAIRED (asked for this turn, rows backed up first):** both existing proofs recovered through the module's own fixed code - DW000172 re-fetched from Furdeco's S3 via `parseSignature` on the live Multidrop page (their copy was still there, 65,740 bytes), DW000182's surviving unfiled blob re-read from its own url (47,029 bytes) - both written to `media/orders/dw0001NN/proof-of-delivery/`, `Media` rows minted under Orders / DW0001NN / Proof of delivery, `shp_shipments.signature_url`/`_key` repointed, and the two old unfiled objects removed. Both serve 200 image/jpeg. **THE RISK WINDOW IS REAL:** the rows now exist, so the storage check no longer calls them orphans - but until shop ships with fix (2) and the install takes it, nothing references them and they sit in the library's **Unused** tile. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint` clean on all three changed files, `modules/shop/lib/tracking` suite 110 passed / 0 failed. No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md`'s proof-of-delivery section gains where the picture is filed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**Pictures the size of the box they are drawn in, stylesheets written once, and the 3D viewer only when asked** - core + `shop`, `shop-variations`, `filters-for-shop`, `product-attributes-for-shop`, `product-discovery-tool`, `search`, `gazette`, `product-3d-views-for-shop`, uncommitted working tree. **Core schema change: a new index**, so both halves of the core DB rule (init migration edited in place, plus new **`prisma/core-reconcile/039_media_original_name_index.sql`**). **Module schema change: new `modules/shop/migrations/058_product_media_thumb_url.sql`**, with `001_initial.sql` kept correct for fresh installs. **THE STARTING POINT, measured on the live homepage:** 2.85 MB of HTML, 1.03 MB of compressed JavaScript over 26 chunks, **5.5 MB of photographs across 76 files** - every one of them the full-size original, a 646 KB studio shot inside a 300px tile - and 183 KB of inline `<style>`, 68 KB of it the same bytes over again. **(1) SMALL COPIES.** **NEW `lib/media/thumb-renditions.ts`** (core, constants only, no sharp: `THUMB_RENDITION_MAX_PX = 300`, suffix `thumb`, 40 KB worthwhile floor). **`lib/media/renditions.ts` gains `renditionFileName` and `findRenditionUrls`** - two queries for a whole batch, matched on folder AND name because an exact-form key can share a basename across folders. It also gains a **P2002 retry**: `buildLibraryUploadKey` is a read-then-write with nothing holding the gap, and a twelve-way backfill hit one collision in three thousand (two originals in one folder differing only by extension). Retried rather than adopted - the winner is a copy of the OTHER picture, so taking its url would put the wrong photograph on a product. **NEW `Media.originalName` index**, because the lookup runs on a page render. **SHOP:** `shp_product_media` gains **`thumb_url`**; `ShpProductMedia.thumbUrl`; **NEW `lib/thumb-renditions.ts`** (`resolveThumbUrls` = lookup only, `ensureThumbUrls` = lookup then make, bounded by a 20s budget so a bulk import leaves the remainder to the sweep) and **NEW `lib/thumb-backfill.ts`** (`countProductThumbsPending`, `backfillProductThumbs`, `backfillThumbFiles`). `setProductMedia` resolves and makes copies on every save - that is "add a photo and its small copy appears" - outside the transaction and forgiving, because a thumbnail is not worth losing an owner's edit over. **`PartImage` gains `fullUrl` and `thumbUrl`** and the CARD context now puts the small copy in `url` itself, which is the whole trick: every card renderer in every module - shop's, the discovery tool's, the attribute filter grid's - draws the right size **without being rebuilt**, and none of them can put a full-size photograph in a tile by accident. The DETAIL context keeps the original in `url` (the stage magnifies it) and carries `thumbUrl` for the strip beneath. shop-variations carries it through `baseImages`, a new `imageThumbUrls` on each variant, and both of its own gallery strips. **`media-usage-provider` reports `thumb_url`** - without that, 27,763 files read as spare and the library offers the catalogue's card pictures up for deletion - and **`media-reference-rewriter` repoints it**. **BACKFILL: NEW `modules/shop/scripts/backfill-thumbs.mts`** + `css-stub-hook.mjs`, run with the global tsx from the repo root (the runner pattern, because this repo has no local tsx and shop's graph `require()`s Puck's CSS). `--files-only` exists because the two halves become available at different times: making the copies needs only the media library and takes hours, pointing the rows at them needs the column and takes minutes. **Run against the live install at ~222 pictures/min**, 300x300 WebP, a 646 KB original becoming 14.8 KB. **(2) THOSE COPIES CACHE FOR A YEAR.** The worker gave exact-form keys five minutes because they can be replaced in place - and a rendition's key is exact-form by design, that being how it is found again. **NEW `isRenditionKey`** in `workers/media-worker/index.ts` (`-thumb|-small|-tiny`, optional `-N`): nothing ever writes over a rendition, so these join the immutable branch. Regenerated into `lib/media/worker-source.generated.ts`. **STILL OUTSTANDING:** pre-existing plainly-named ORIGINALS are still on five minutes; version-stamping those needs the marker to reach every render site and was not attempted here. **(3) ONE COPY OF EACH STYLESHEET.** **NEW `components/SharedStyle.tsx`** - React 19 `<style href precedence>`, hoisted and deduplicated, with the href a readable id plus an **FNV-1a hash of the content**, so two grids whose breakpoints differ cannot be served each other's rules. Five tests, including the one that matters: a `>` escaped to `&gt;` inside a `<style>` silently kills every child-combinator rule on the site, and `renderToStaticMarkup` does NOT hoist, so the tests use `renderToString`. Applied to `shopCardCss` (13 KB x5 on the homepage, 15 call sites across four modules, editor path included so the parity rule holds), the detail parts' `Style` helper (12 KB x2 on a product page), `searchCss` (15 KB x2 on EVERY page), `GazetteStyles` (10 KB x4 on the gazette), and core's site-logo and nav-menu snippets. Combined sheets were split rather than concatenated, so a page holding a plain grid and a filter grid carries one card stylesheet, not two. **(4) THE 3D VIEWER WAITS FOR A CLICK.** three.js was already behind dynamic imports, but `Viewer3d` itself was a **static** import in `CardModel3dOverlay` and `Gallery3d` - and a static import is a bundler edge - so **180 KB compressed / 700 KB parsed** came down with every category page and every homepage carrying a grid. Both now `next/dynamic(..., { ssr: false })` with a spinner; new `.p3d-stage-loading` in `viewer-css.ts`. The thumbnail strip's small previews are a separate, lighter path and are untouched. **(5) THE PUBLIC SITE STOPS FETCHING THE ADMIN'S FONTS.** `app/layout.tsx` wraps every page on the site and hardcoded a Google Fonts request for **Instrument Sans + JetBrains Mono** - the admin's typefaces, which the live site draws not one character in (it uses Inter and Fraunces from its own design tokens). A render-blocking third-party round trip on every public page of every install, for nothing. Moved to `app/cactus-admin/layout.tsx` and `app/setup/layout.tsx`; the two `preconnect`s stay in the root for whichever layout does ask. The frontend `--font-heading`/`--font-body` defaults in `globals.css` become a system stack rather than naming a face the page no longer fetches. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7040 passed / 0 failed / 468 skipped** (5 new in `components/SharedStyle.test.tsx`). `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` NOT yet run - it is required, both migrations touch schema. NOT verified in a browser.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**A product page tells search engines and assistants what it is, what it costs to get, and what people thought of it** - `shop`, `google-shopping-for-shop`, `reviews-for-shop`, `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree, **no schema change anywhere**, no route change, no new dependency. **THE STARTING POINT:** the live Product JSON-LD was a name, a description, photographs and an `AggregateOffer` carrying `lowPrice` and a strikethrough. No brand, no GTIN, no MPN, no condition, no `highPrice`, no `offerCount`, no `url`, no `@id`, no shipping, no returns, no rating. **NEW FILE `modules/shop/lib/product-jsonld.ts`** - the whole block pulled out of `ShopProductDetail.rsc.tsx` and made PURE, which is the point: this is the markup that decides whether a shopping assistant puts the shop in front of somebody, and until now the only way to check a change to it was to deploy and paste a URL into a validator. 21 tests. **WHAT IT NOW PUBLISHES:** `@id` and `url` off the product's own canonical (`productUrl(getSiteUrl(), slug, config.productUrlStyle)`); `sku`; `brand` as a `Brand` object (a bare string parses, every reference example uses the object); `gtin`/`mpn`/`itemCondition`; `highPrice` + `offerCount` on a variations listing; `offers.url`; `shippingDetails[]`; `hasMerchantReturnPolicy`; `aggregateRating`. **THREE SEAMS, all optional, all resolved in the page's existing `Promise.all`.** **(a) NEW POINT `shop.product-merchant-facts`**, filled by `google-shopping-for-shop/lib/merchant-facts-provider.ts`: brand, GTIN, MPN, condition and the shipping country. Reusing the feed's own `identifiersOf` rather than re-resolving, deliberately - a shopping assistant reading the page and Merchant Center reading the feed must not be told two different makers. **`scripts/check-import-cycles.mjs` caught the first attempt at that**: importing it from `feed-data.ts` closed `extension-points.public.ts -> merchant-facts-provider -> feed-data -> delivery-timing -> extension-points.public.ts`, the class of cycle Turbopack can fail a production build on while `tsc` and `eslint` stay green. `identifiersOf` moved to a **new dependency-free `lib/identifiers.ts`** (imports only `feed-xml.ts`, which imports nothing at all) and both callers now read it there - one implementation still, and no path from the registry back into it. Standalone terms throughout, since a product page is a listing and not one of its combinations. Google's lower-case conditions are mapped to schema.org's `NewCondition`/`RefurbishedCondition`/`UsedCondition` **here**, because shop has no opinion about Google's spelling and should not learn one. A shop without the module falls back to its own `shp_products.barcode` as the gtin. **CONSEQUENCE WORTH KNOWING:** `brand_from_supplier` now reaches the public product page, not only the feed - on a shop buying from a distributor that puts the distributor's name on every page. Said plainly in both wikis. **(b) EXISTING POINT `shop.product-delivery-timing`** (advanced-shipping) read by shop for the first time, via new `lib/detail-delivery.ts`: every service the product can be bought with becomes an `OfferShippingDetails` - name, description, `shippingRate`, `shippingDestination`, and handling/transit as `QuantitativeValue` with `minValue` AND `maxValue` (Google reads a bare `value` as no answer). The live site offers **eight** - flat-pack free, express, pre-assembled, installation, made-to-order - and published none of them anywhere a machine could read. Prices converted with the PRODUCT's own `displayAdjust`, because the charge rides on the product line and is taxed at the product's rate. **(c) NEW POINT `shop.product-rating-summary`**, filled by `reviews-for-shop/lib/rating-summary-provider.ts` over its existing `getProductSummary`. The existing `shop.product-reviews` point is a site-wide paged LIST - computing one product's average from it would mean reading every review on the shop on every render, which is why this is a second point rather than a reuse. The live site has 440 published reviews averaging 4.42 and was showing all of it to people and none of it to machines. A product with no reviews is ABSENT from the map and no rating is published - a rating with nothing behind it is what manual actions are for. **`ShopCardFromPrice` gains optional `highPrice` and `offerCount`**, filled by shop-variations' `card-price-provider.ts` (`dearest` was already computed; `offerCount` counts enabled variants with a live child row, not `variants.length`). Optional for the same reason `onSale` and `rrp` were: a provider older than the field says nothing and the markup carries the low figure alone, so **no `requiresModules` bump is needed in either direction**. `highPrice` is suppressed when it equals `lowPrice` - claiming a range and then describing one figure is worse than saying nothing. **RETURNS, deliberately half a feature:** only `returnsPolicy(...) === 'NONE'` travels, as `MerchantReturnNotPermitted` plus the owner's own note. A returnable product publishes nothing - the window and who pays the postage are the shop-wide facts on ultimate-seo's Structured data tab, and restating a half-remembered version on twenty thousand offers is how two policies drift apart. `DISCRETIONARY` is not a schema.org category and is NOT guessed at. **HIDING PRICES:** the old code built the block and then `delete`d four keys; the builder is now told `hidePrices` and never writes them. Shipping and the return policy survive it on purpose - neither says what the thing costs, and they are the half a buyer most wants. **CHECKS:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across the whole repo, `eslint` clean on all four modules, **1549 passed / 0 failed / 51 skipped** across them (21 new in `product-jsonld.test.ts`). The one new raw query (`SELECT id, supplier, barcode FROM shp_products WHERE id IN (...)`) was executed against the live Postgres read-only, since no standard gate parses SQL. No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser. **KNOWN GAP:** `shippingDestination` is omitted entirely on a shop with no google-shopping module, because nothing else on the platform knows the country - omitted rather than guessed, since a DefinedRegion naming the wrong country is a delivery promise to people the shop will not ship to. Wiki: `Shop.md` gains "What search engines and AI assistants are told about a product"; `Google-Shopping.md`, `Advanced-Shipping.md` and `Reviews.md` each gain a paragraph on what they now contribute to it.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**The Markdown twins publish the questions, and stop publishing the margin** - `ultimate-seo` only, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no route change, no new dependency. **TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY READING THE LIVE SITE, both AI-side only.** **(1) FAQs were invisible to assistants.** A live product page carries 43 questions in its HTML body AND in `FAQPage` JSON-LD; its `.md` twin carried **none**, and nor did a category twin (12 -> 0) or `llms-full.txt`. The one surface written specifically for a reader that does not parse HTML was dropping the most quotable content on the site. **NEW FILE `lib/ai/faqs.ts`**: `normaliseFaqSet`/`normaliseFaqItems` (forgiving - an unreadable blob reads as no questions rather than failing a catalogue rebuild; an absent `inherit` reads as true, since the flag arrived after the rows), `resolveFaqs({own, ancestors, shopWide})` and `faqSection`. A deliberate COPY of shop's `lib/faq.ts` merge rule, not an import - same rule every loader in `documents.ts` follows, because this module must build on an install with no shop in it - with the two files named in each other's headers. `MAX_FAQS_PUBLISHED = 60`, a backstop and not a budget: the real set on the site this was run against is 43 and a cap of 30 would have withheld 13 answers to save a few hundred words. **VERIFIED AGAINST LIVE DATA, not asserted:** the resolver was run over the live product's own set, its category chain (recursive CTE, `master_category_id` else first by position/name - the same start shop's `getProductFaqCategoryChain` picks) and the 26 shop-wide questions, and reproduces the live page's `FAQPage` **exactly - 43 questions, same order, same text**. Wired into product, category and collection twins; a collection is flat so it gets own-then-shop-wide, a category walks its own chain. Honours shop's `productFaqsEnabled` master switch. Skipped outright on a batch with no shop content, so a batch of blog posts pays for no queries. **(2) THE TWINS WERE PUBLISHING ATTRIBUTES THE PAGE HIDES.** `loadProductExtras` read `pat_product_values` raw and ignored `pat_product_attributes.show_in_spec`, which is what shop's own `getProductSpecView` filters on. Live consequences, present in `.md` and absent from the HTML: `Markup | 6` (the margin multiplier), `Catalogue Code`, `Commodity Code`, `ISPC Code`, and a `Shipping` attribute carrying the **buy-in carriage schedule** ("NxtDy £4.95 - 7-10DInstl £17.95") on a site that advertises free delivery. **146 of 439 published product twins** carry at least one. The query now joins `pat_product_attributes`, filters `show_in_spec = true`, takes `COALESCE(NULLIF(TRIM(name_override),''), a.name)` and orders by `spec_position` - so the twin says what the page says and nothing else. `assignment_id IS NULL OR = ppa.id` because that column is nullable on ticks saved before helpings could repeat; `SELECT DISTINCT` so the null branch cannot double a row. A product with nothing flagged for the panel now has no Specification table in its twin, which is correct - its page has none either. **RAW SQL WAS EXECUTED, not just typechecked** (nothing in `tsc`/`eslint`/`npm test` parses a query): the new attributes query and all four new FAQ queries were run against the live Postgres read-only - the attributes one returns the same 12 rows as before minus `Markup`. **CHECKS:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint modules/ultimate-seo` clean, module suite **255 passed / 0 failed** (11 new in `lib/ai/faqs.test.ts`). No migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser. **NEEDS A REBUILD TO TAKE EFFECT** - twins are materialised by the nightly `cron/ai` job or the admin button, never on the request. **CONFIG GAP RAISED, not code:** the SEO structured-data profile has no return policy filled in, so the Organization record publishes no `hasMerchantReturnPolicy` and `llms.txt` carries no Returns line - the code for both has been there since the AI half shipped. Wiki: `Ultimate-SEO.md` gains "The questions come too" and "The specification is the one on the page".)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**A library item remembers the addresses it used to live at** - core + `shop`, uncommitted working tree, **core schema change: new model `MediaFormerAddress`**, so both halves of the core DB rule (`prisma/migrations/20260626000000_init/migration.sql` edited in place, plus new **`prisma/core-reconcile/038_media_former_addresses.sql`**). **THE INCIDENT:** three live product photographs on the Jackson chair were deleted from the Media Library because it said they were not in use. They were: the product's media tab listed all three. The library was not lying about the `Media` rows - it was lying by omission. The pictures went up as `.png`, were **optimised to `.webp` at 20:03**, and the product's media tab was **saved at 20:06 with the pre-optimise `.png` urls** it had been holding since the screen was opened. Core's `core.media-reference-rewriters` hook did fire at 20:03 and did rewrite `shp_product_media` - there was simply nothing in it to rewrite yet. So `shp_product_media` named `.png`, `Media.url` named `.webp`, nothing in the site named the item's current address, the item read as unreferenced, and `getMediaReferencesBulk` (the delete gate) agreed because it asks the same index. Storefront 404s from 20:03; deletion, bytes and all, at 20:46. **THE SHAPE OF IT:** every rewriter covers references that exist at the moment of the move. Nothing can cover a reference **written after the move against a url captured before it** - and that is the one that reads as "unused". **THE FIX, three parts.** (1) **`MediaFormerAddress`** (`mediaId` cascade, `url`, `key`, `reason` - optimise|resize|crop|replace|rename|move|dedupe): every blob move now writes down the address it left. Recorded in `repointMediaToBlob` (optimise, resize, crop, replace, video) and in `organise.ts` (move, rename, folder-rename knock-on, and the dedupe take-over, where the victim's url AND id are recorded against the survivor). Written **before** the reference rewrite, so an address is on record even if a module rewriter throws and aborts. Never throws - a failed audit line must not fail an optimise. (2) **`MediaUsageIndex` gains `referencedViaFormerAddress`**: `buildMediaUsageIndex` reads every remembered address and asks the token set it has just built, so this is a lookup per address, not a second scan of an 11 MB haystack. `isMediaInContent` consults it first, which means **a stale reference now keeps an item out of the Unused tile**, for every module, with no module change needed. The delete gate names it separately ("at an address this item has moved off") so the two cases read differently to whoever is holding the button. (3) **`getMediaReferencesBulk` now honours `index.degraded`** - a straight contradiction found on the way past: when a module's usage provider throws, the grid marks everything in use (`isMediaInUse` returns true) while the delete gate reported **no references at all** and deleted unwarned. **SHOP:** `setProductMedia` now runs its urls through core's new `repointToCurrentMediaUrls` before storing them, so a screen saved after an optimise stores the live address rather than a dead one - that is the half that stops the 404, where part 2 is the half that stops the deletion. **NEW FILE** `lib/media/former-addresses.ts`: `recordFormerMediaAddress`, `resolveCurrentMediaUrls`, `repointToCurrentMediaUrls`. **LIVE DATA REPAIRED:** the three blobs were recovered from B2 version history (the delete wrote hide markers at 20:46:59; removing them restored the bytes), three `Media` rows recreated, and the product's four `shp_product_media` rows repointed `.png` -> `.webp`. Site-wide orphan count (`shp_product_media.url` with no `Media` row) is back to **0** from 3. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7013 passed / 0 failed**, two new cases in `lib/media/references.test.ts` covering exactly the stale-address shape. `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS, no skip - required by the new model. NOT verified in a browser. **NO BACKFILL IS POSSIBLE** - the moves that already happened left no trace, so this protects from here on only. Wiki: `Media-library.md` gains "When a file moves".)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**Filter collection pages answer questions of their own** - `filters-for-shop` only, uncommitted working tree, **one new module migration** `migrations/005_collection_faqs.sql` (`flt_collections.faqs JSONB`). **WHY IN THIS MODULE:** those pages are `flt_collections` at this module's own `filterCollection` layout type, and shop must not read a table it does not own. **BUT THE DRAWING IS SHOP'S:** the new block imports `FaqAccordion`, `getShopBreakpoints`, `getShopConfigCached`, `normaliseFaqItems` and `resolveCategoryFaqs` from shop, so a site carrying FAQs on a product page, a category page and a filter page publishes ONE accordion shape and ONE FAQPage shape rather than three. That is a module-to-module import of files that first exist in shop 0.1.419, so **`requiresModules` shop minVersion 0.1.333 -> 0.1.419** - nothing local catches that class of miss (see the reference memory), and shop must be released and pinned before this module's own gate can build. **NEW BLOCK** `FilterCollectionFaqs` (`Filter Page: FAQs [Anchor]`, `filterCollection` only) with `title`, `scope` ('own' default / 'inherited' = its own then the shop-wide) and `columns` ('2' default, grid not CSS multi-column, one column below the shop's `tabletBp`) - the same three fields and the same CSS rules as shop's `ShopCategoryFaqs`. `FilterCollectionFaqs` added to `CONTEXT_BLOCKS` in `lib/inject-filter-collection-context.ts` so `filterPageSlug` reaches it. **DATA:** `faqs` added to `COLLECTION_COLUMNS`, to `rowToCollection` (through shop's `normaliseFaqSet`, so a hand-edited blob reads as no questions rather than taking the page down), to `FltCollection`, to `FltCollectionUpdate` and to `updateCollection`'s single long raw UPDATE, with the same store-NULL-only-when-empty-and-still-inheriting rule shop uses. Admin: a questions box plus an inherit tick at the foot of each row's panel on the Filter collections screen, using shop's `FaqListEditor`; the PUT validator takes shop's `FaqSetBodySchema`. **A REAL GUARD CAUGHT A REAL MISTAKE:** I first added the column to `003_filter_collections.sql` as well, the way shop's fresh-install file carries its columns - `scripts/check-frozen-migrations.test.ts` failed, because 003 was released at v0.1.51 and has already run on live installs, so its bytes are frozen (the 2026-09-08 incident). Reverted; 005 alone is correct, since a fresh install runs 001 through 005 in order. The reason is written into 005's header so the next person does not try it. Shop's `001_initial.sql` edits are untouched by this - the guard deliberately exempts the initial file. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7000 passed / 0 failed**, `check-client-graph` and `check-import-cycles` both clean. **`npm run test:filters-sql` a real PASS, 5 tests, no skip** - a new case writes and reads a page's questions against a throwaway OVH Postgres and proves the new jsonb CASE clause did not trample its neighbours in that one statement (a comma or cast wrong there breaks EVERY edit on the screen, not just the questions, and nothing short of Postgres would say so), that an edit saying nothing about questions leaves them alone, and that an emptied inheriting set stores NULL. **`npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS, 4 tests, no skip** - required by the new column. NOT verified in a browser. **RELEASE ORDER MATTERS HERE:** shop first (it carries FaqAccordion/lib/faq and shop's own 055/057), then core's pin, then filters-for-shop - its build gate composes against core's newest release, so it cannot go green until the shop pin it needs is live. Wiki: `Shop-filters.md` gains "Questions on a filter page" and the block in the layout list.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**FAQs reach the shop home page and get a set per collection, and list in two columns off the product page** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree, **one new module migration** `migrations/057_collection_faqs.sql` (`shp_collections.faqs JSONB`, also added to `001_initial.sql`). Note 056 is the peer session's product-questions migration; this took 057. **1. `ShopCategoryFaqs` now registers for `shopIndex` as well** as shopCategory/shopCollection/shopTag - a Shop Home layout has no category, so the block prints the shop-wide list, which is what puts a shop's main FAQs on `/shop`. **2. TWO COLUMNS on the non-product surfaces**: new `columns` field ('2' default, '1' the alternative), rendered as a GRID (`.shop-faqs-2{grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr}`) and not CSS multi-column - a multi-column list reflows items between columns every time one is opened, so the question the shopper just clicked jumps elsewhere on the page as its answer appears. Collapses to one column below the shop's own `tabletBp` (`getShopBreakpoints`, not a literal). Anything that is not '1' reads as two, so a layout saved before the field existed looks the same as one saved after. The JSON-LD `<script>` is `display:none` by UA rule and never takes a cell. **3. PER-COLLECTION QUESTIONS** (migration 057): `shp_collections.faqs`, same `{items, inherit}` shape and the same NULL-only-when-empty-and-inheriting write rule as products and categories. A collection is not a tree, so there is no chain - `getCollectionFaqSetBySlug(slug, {client?})` is one row, and `scope: 'inherited'` means "its own, then the shop's" via `resolveCategoryFaqs([set], shopWide)`. **Deliberately NOT inherited by the products in the collection**: a product sits in as many collections as somebody filed it under, and inheriting from all of them would answer one question three ways with no rule for which wins - categories carry a master, collections deliberately do not. `ShopCategoryFaqs` added to `COLLECTION_CONTEXT_BLOCKS` in `lib/inject-collection-context.ts` so `collectionSlug` reaches it. **ADMIN:** a third tab, **FAQs**, on each row of the Collections screen (its count in the tab label), fed by `collectionFaqs` on the collections GET (`getCollectionFaqSets()`, kept out of `listCollections` for the same reason as the category one) and saved through the existing `saveDetails` PUT - one row, one Save, and a questions tab with a Save of its own would be the only thing on that screen with one. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **7000 passed / 0 failed**. **`npm run test:shop-sql` a real PASS, 18 tests, no skip** - a new case reads a collection's own set off a throwaway OVH Postgres and proves the `inherit:false` flag survives the column (a collection that said "just mine" quietly printing the shop's questions is the failure that would never be noticed). **`npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS, 4 tests, no skip** - required by the new column. NOT verified in a browser. **NOT DONE, and deliberately:** the block has NOT been added to the live `/shop` layout. The live site runs shop v0.1.419, where `ShopCategoryFaqs` is registered for shopCategory/shopCollection/shopTag and NOT shopIndex - a module layout's component set is derived from its declared layout types, and Puck renders nothing for a type its config has never heard of (see the `Split` note in `lib/puck/config.core.tsx`). Writing it into that layout before this ships would put a block on a live customer page that the live code cannot render. It goes in after the release and the owner's update. **ALSO NOT BUILT:** per-filter-collection questions. Those pages are `flt_collections` in `filters-for-shop`, a different module with its own `filterCollection` layout type, so they need that module's own column, admin field and block - shop must not read another module's table. Raised with the owner rather than guessed at. Wiki: `Shop.md` gains the collection level, the Shop Home placement and the two-column paragraph.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**Email attachments move into the Media Library, filed under who the correspondence was with** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. **NEW migration `055_attachment_library_filing.sql`** (two columns on `uin_attachments`). **THE ASK:** attachments should appear on `/hq/media` like everything else, at `Inbox/<their address>/Received` and `.../Sent`. **THE REVERSAL BEING MADE ON PURPOSE:** this module deliberately gave email attachments NO library row so a customer's invoice out of `accounts@` could not appear in the media picker for everybody holding media permission. That is given up knowingly - anyone with media permission can now see them. **THE ADDRESS IS THEIRS IN BOTH DIRECTIONS:** an outbound message's `from_address` is whichever of our own mailboxes sent it, so filing Sent by From would pile every reply the site has ever written into three folders named after the site; Sent is filed by `to_addresses[1]`, so one person's whole paper trail is one folder. **STILL PRIVATE, and both would drown the library:** inline parts (`content_id IS NOT NULL` - one `image001.png` per email from anybody with a fancy footer) and anything with no correspondent (a note, a malformed header). Those keep the old `unified-inbox/` prefix and `lib/media-usage-provider.ts` keeps vouching for them. **THE BUG THIS UNCOVERED AND FIXES:** `uin_attachments.media_key` held two quite different things - bytes this module wrote, and a library item somebody PICKED (a product photograph) or a forwarded original. Every delete path deleted the blob regardless, so emptying the bin removed a product image from storage and left the shop's own library row pointing at nothing. New `owns_object` column settles it and `releaseStoredObject` is the only way any of the four delete paths removes anything. **AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE SAME PROBLEM:** a forward travels with the ORIGINAL's attachment, so one object has two rows pointing at it - the migration gives ownership to the earliest row per `media_key` (`DISTINCT ON`), the delete path refuses while any other row still holds the key, and the backfill re-points the siblings before it deletes the old copy. **NEVER "NOT IN USE":** the usage provider now hands core `media_id` as well as key and url - the id is the value that survives an optimise or a folder rename, so there is no window in which an invoice reads as unreferenced and arms the bulk-delete button. `lib/media-usage-provider.test.ts` asserts all three forms against core's real classifier, with a negative control. **BACKFILL IS THE NIGHTLY TIDY, not a button and not a migration** - `sweepAttachmentFiling` rides the existing housekeeping cron on whatever time the other sweeps leave it (12s, batches of 10), so the backlog drains itself over a few nights with nothing to press and nothing to set. There was a settings section and an admin route for one revision; both are gone. **NEW live SQL suite** `npm run test:inbox-attachment-filing` - none of this SQL is executed by any standard gate.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**The product page's FAQs become a search box, without hiding a word from a crawler** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree, no schema change, no route change, no new dependency. **THE ASK:** a product inheriting thirty questions from its range and the shop opened its FAQs section on thirty collapsed headings - a second page to read rather than an answer. Questions now start hidden behind a search box; the shopper types, picks off auto-suggestions, and that one answer expands. **THE INVARIANT THIS IS BUILT AROUND:** hiding from the SHOPPER must never hide from a crawler. So every question and answer is rendered into the server HTML on every request, always; the filtering is a `hidden` ATTRIBUTE toggled in the browser, never a missing element and never a fetch; the `FAQPage` JSON-LD carries the full set whatever is typed; and a `<noscript>` rule hides the box and unhides every item (`.spd-faq[hidden]{display:block!important}`) so a scripting-off visitor gets the plain list the section used to be. Four tests in the new `ProductFaqSearch.test.tsx` assert exactly that against `renderToStaticMarkup` output - it is the class of regression nobody spots in review OR on the page, surfacing weeks later as answers that stopped being quoted in search results. **NEW ISLAND:** `components/public/ProductFaqSearch.tsx` (`'use client'`, `.sfs-` scoped CSS like the ask form's `.spq-`) - combobox input (`role=combobox`/`aria-autocomplete=list`, arrow keys, Enter, Escape), a popup listbox of up to 8 suggestions, and the **Can't find your answer? Ask a new question** button as the popup's FIRST child, above the near-misses (the shopper who found nothing is the one with a question). Opened questions are tracked by question text - `resolveProductFaqs` has already de-duplicated them, so two levels of the chain cannot collide on one key - and `onToggle` syncs a shopper collapsing one by hand rather than letting React reopen it. **RANKING moved to `lib/faq.ts`** as `matchFaqQuestions(items, query, limit = FAQ_SUGGESTION_LIMIT)` so it is exercisable without a browser: question-prefix beats question-substring beats answer-substring, ties keep resolution order so the nearest level's answer leads, empty query matches NOTHING (opening on all thirty would be the wall this replaced). **`FaqAccordion` gains three optional props** - `visibleQuestions`, `openQuestions`, `onToggleQuestion` - with every item still rendered whatever they say; undefined keeps the category-page block byte-identical. **`AskProductQuestion` gains an optional controlled `open`/`onOpenChange`** (uncontrolled unchanged), so the suggestion link opens the FORM rather than the button that opens the form - one click, not two. **NEW SETTING:** `productFaqSearchPlaceholder` (default *Search our answers, or ask your own question*), carried to the parts on `DetailPartContext.faqSearchPlaceholder` and edited under Settings → General → Product FAQs. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **6982 passed / 0 failed** (11 new in `ProductFaqSearch.test.tsx` - jsdom, `createRoot` + `renderToStaticMarkup`, covering the four crawler assertions plus opens-on-nothing-shown, suggest-by-question, suggest-by-ANSWER, pick-expands-only-that-one, ask-link-is-first-child-and-opens-the-form, ask-link-absent-when-questions-are-off, and the no-match note; 6 new `matchFaqQuestions` cases in `faq.test.ts`). No SQL touched, so no live probe needed; no migration, so no backup round-trip. NOT verified in a browser. **KNOWN TRADE-OFF, stated rather than hidden:** a JS-executing crawler now sees the items with `hidden` set. They stay in the DOM and Google indexes accordion-hidden text, and the JSON-LD is the explicit FAQ signal either way - but it is a weaker position than a visible list, and that is the cost of the ask.
