Wiki: `Shop.md`'s FAQs section gains "On a product page it's a search box, not a list", "Search engines and AI still see every answer" and the ask-link paragraph.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**The Media Library can be told to look again** - core only, uncommitted working tree, no schema or route change. `MediaToolbar.tsx` gains an `onRefresh` prop and a spinning ⟳ button beside the view toggle; `MediaLibrary.tsx`'s new `refreshLibrary()` re-fetches the grid, the folder tree and the tag list together via `Promise.all` and is wired in as `onRefresh`. Worth having because plenty of what lands in the library arrives from elsewhere - an optimiser finishing, another tab's upload, a module filing its own files (the product-FAQ photo flow among them) - and none of that pushes a message to a page already sitting open; until now the only fix was a full reload, which also threw away the folder you were browsing and the search you had typed. **CHECKS:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean. NOT verified in a browser - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**Shoppers can ask a question of their own, and the answer becomes a FAQ** - `shop` only (v0.1.417 -> v0.1.418), uncommitted working tree, **one new module migration** `migrations/056_product_questions.sql` (`shp_product_questions`, FK to `shp_products` ON DELETE CASCADE, two indexes). No new permission key, no env var, no new dependency, no core change. **THE OTHER HALF OF 055:** migration 055 gave a product FAQs somebody at the shop had thought to write; this collects the ones nobody anticipated. A button under the questions in the FAQs section opens a three-field form (name optional, email required, question), the question lands in an admin queue, and answering it **emails the asker AND writes the question and answer into that product's own `shp_products.faqs`** - which is the whole reason it lives with the FAQs rather than in an inbox. Auto-publish was the owner's explicit choice with the downside stated: a one-off question becomes a permanent FAQ, removable on the product's own FAQs tab like any other. **SETTINGS (all new on `ShpConfigSchema`, nested under Product FAQs on Settings -> General):** `productQuestionsEnabled` default **false** - every other setting there changes how something already on the page looks, this one puts a new button on every product and opens a route strangers can post to, so a shop updating must choose it; plus `productQuestionsButtonLabel`, `productQuestionsIntro`, `productQuestionsThanks`, `productQuestionsNotifyEmail` (empty sends nothing). **THE SECTION NOW APPEARS EMPTY:** `buildDetailSections` gates the FAQs section on `ctx.faqs.length > 0 || ctx.askQuestion` rather than on questions alone - the product nobody has written a question for is exactly the one a shopper needs to ask about - but `FaqAccordion` is still skipped when the list is empty, so an empty section carries no stray `FAQPage` markup. `DetailPartContext` gains `askQuestion` (`{buttonLabel,intro,thanks} | null`), resolved in `ShopProductDetail.rsc.tsx` and requiring BOTH `productFaqsEnabled` and `productQuestionsEnabled`. New client island `components/public/AskProductQuestion.tsx`, scoped `.spq-*` CSS, button first and form only once pressed. **PUBLIC ROUTE** `app/api/public/product-questions` (POST): shop closed gate, the feature switch (404, not an explanation), 5/hour/IP through `checkInMemoryRateLimit`, and a hidden `website` honeypot answered with 200 and no write. **ADMIN:** `app/api/admin/product-questions` (GET list) and `[id]` (POST answer, PATCH status, DELETE), all on `shop.products` with `allowAccess`; new screen `ProductQuestionsScreen` at `/m/shop/questions`, reached from a **Questions** tab that `resolveCatalogueNavTabs` adds only when the feature is on - the Suppliers rule, a setting rather than a permission - so that function is now `async`. **THE ORDER IS THE SAFETY STORY:** the answer route SENDS FIRST, then publishes, then records, the way `contact-form`'s `lib/reply.ts` arrived at. A failed send returns 502 with the draft untouched and the row still PENDING; the other way round leaves a question marked answered that the customer never received, which nobody at the shop would ever find out. Answering an ANSWERED row is refused 409 rather than emailing somebody twice. **A CAP THAT WOULD HAVE BRICKED A PRODUCT EDITOR:** `FaqSetBodySchema` caps a question at 300 characters and the ask form takes 2000, so publishing a long one would have left a product whose FAQs read back fine and whose editor refused to SAVE. `lib/faq.ts` now exports `FAQ_QUESTION_MAX`/`FAQ_ANSWER_MAX`, the schema is written in terms of them, the answer route caps on `FAQ_ANSWER_MAX`, and `appendProductFaq` refuses anything over either - the answer has still gone to the person who asked, and the queue badges it **Not on the page**. **`appendProductFaq`** is a read-modify-write of a jsonb column, so it runs in a transaction with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` (two answers on one product would otherwise each read the set without the other), de-duplicates on the same loosened key `resolveProductFaqs` uses, appends rather than prepends, and fires `notifyProductSaved(id, ['faqs'])` AFTER the commit. **GDPR:** `productQuestions` added to `app/api/member/gdpr-export`, matched on `member_id` only - a question asked signed-out carries an address and no account, and guessing two addresses are one person is not that route's call. Two new email templates, `shop.product-question-received` (admin alert, not transactional) and `shop.product-question-answered` (transactional), both escaping the typed text themselves and declaring it `rawTags` so the asker's line breaks survive. Backup needs nothing: `lib/backup/dump.ts` discovers tables rather than listing them. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint modules/shop` clean, full suite **6965 passed / 0 failed**, `check-client-graph`, `check-import-cycles` and `check-frozen-migrations` all clean. **Migration 056 has NOT been executed anywhere** - no throwaway Postgres run, no `test:shop-sql`, no backup round-trip - and NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md`'s **Frequently asked questions** section gains a **Letting shoppers ask one** part, plus a clause on "Nothing written, nothing shown" - the section now appears on every product once asking is on. Added alongside a peer session's category-FAQs edits to the same file, both uncommitted and neither pushed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**Category, collection and tag pages can carry the FAQs too** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree, **no schema change** - the columns went in with migration 055 earlier today and this reads them from a second angle. New Puck block **`ShopCategoryFaqs`** (`Shop: FAQs [Anchor]`), registered for `shopCategory`, `shopCollection` and `shopTag`, with `categorySlug` injected the usual way (`ShopCategoryFaqs` added to `CATEGORY_CONTEXT_BLOCKS` in `lib/inject-category-context.ts`; collections and tags inject nothing and are handled below). **WHY A BLOCK AT ALL:** ultimate-seo's `SeoFaq` declares no `layoutTypes` in its manifest, so it lands in the ordinary-page component map only - `getModuleLayoutPuckConfig` composes a module layout from that module's own declared blocks plus the core content/layout categories, `SiteLogo` and the retired ones, so the FAQ (SEO) block was never in the Category layout's picker and never could be. **FIELDS:** `title` (blank hides the heading) and `scope` - `'own'` (default) prints the category's own questions, `'inherited'` prints the whole chain plus the shop-wide list. `'own'` is the default deliberately: the shop-wide answers are already on every product beneath the category, and the alternative puts identical `FAQPage` markup on the category page and on everything in it. On a Collection or Tag layout there is no category, so the block prints the shop-wide list regardless of `scope` - said in the field hint, because a setting that does nothing on the page you are editing is worse than none. **NEW QUERY:** `getCategoryFaqChainBySlug(slug, {client?})` in `lib/db/catalogue.ts` - the category-rooted twin of `getProductFaqCategoryChain`, a recursive CTE walking a slug up to the root, nearest first. Kept as a second query rather than folded into the first: the product walk has to resolve a product id to a category, and paying for that on a page that already knows its own category is a join for nothing. **NEW RESOLVER:** `resolveCategoryFaqs(categories, shopWide)` in `lib/faq.ts`, which delegates straight to `resolveProductFaqs` with an empty product level - so a category page and the products under it can never disagree about which answer wins or where the walk stops. **MARKUP FACTORED OUT:** new `components/public/FaqAccordion.tsx` holds the one `<details>` list and the one `FAQPage` emission, taking its class names from the caller (`spd-faqs`/`spd-faq` on the product page, dressed inside `.spd-tabs`; `shop-faqs`/`shop-faq` on the block, which brings its own CSS) - `detail-parts.tsx`'s local `FaqList` is gone in favour of it. The block's heading matches `.spd-tabs h3` so the same questions look the same wherever a shopper meets them. **GATING:** the block returns null when `productFaqsEnabled` is off, so the one switch covers every surface; its settings label is now **Show frequently asked questions on your shop pages** rather than "on product pages", and the hint names the new block. A block with nothing to print renders nothing at all - no heading, no empty box, no structured data claiming a page answers questions it does not. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **6961 passed / 0 failed** (2 new `resolveCategoryFaqs` cases). **`npm run test:shop-sql` a real PASS, 17 tests, no skip** - two new cases execute the category-rooted CTE against a throwaway OVH Postgres (walk order from a slug, a top-level category on its own, an unknown slug). Backup round-trip NOT re-run and not required: no migration changed in this pass. NOT verified in a browser. **SHARED TREE:** a peer session is mid-flight in `modules/shop` on a separate "Product questions" feature (`migrations/056_product_questions.sql`, `ProductQuestionsScreen`, `app/api/*/product-questions`) - different numbers, different files, no overlap with this. Wiki: `Shop.md`'s **Frequently asked questions** section gains an "And on a category page" part.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**Product pages answer the questions people keep asking** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree, **one new module migration** `migrations/055_product_faqs.sql` (`shp_products.faqs JSONB` + `shp_categories.faqs JSONB`, both also added to `001_initial.sql` for fresh installs). No new route, no new permission key, no env var, no new dependency, no core change. **THE SHAPE:** one stored object per level - `{items:[{question,answer}], inherit:boolean}` - parsed in exactly one place, the new `modules/shop/lib/faq.ts` (`normaliseFaqSet`, `normaliseFaqItems`, `resolveProductFaqs`, `buildProductFaqJsonLd`, `faqJsonLdScript`, plus `FaqSetBodySchema` for request bodies, which is capped at 100 items / 300-char questions / 4000-char answers while the READ schema is uncapped so a stored set can never vanish). **THREE LEVELS, nearest wins:** the product's own column, then its category chain, then `ShpConfigSchema.productFaqs` (new, `.catch([])` so a malformed list costs the shop its FAQs and not its whole configuration) - gated by `productFaqsEnabled` (new, default **true**; off skips the category query entirely, so a shop that never writes one pays nothing). Duplicate questions are matched on a loosened key (case, whitespace and the trailing `?` ignored) so a nearer answer replaces a further one; `inherit: false` at any level stops the walk dead, and an empty-but-not-inheriting set is deliberately KEPT in the column (both `updateProduct` and `updateCategory` store NULL only for an empty set that still inherits) because "this product shows no questions at all" is a real answer. **THE READ:** `getProductFaqCategoryChain(productId, {client?})` in `lib/db/catalogue.ts` - one recursive CTE walking master category -> ancestors, nearest first, falling back to the lowest-positioned filed category when a product has no master; `getCategoryFaqSets()` for the admin screen, deliberately NOT folded into `listCategories` (that projection feeds public pages, same reasoning as `description_puck`). Resolved once in `ShopProductDetail.rsc.tsx` onto `DetailPartContext.faqs`. **THE SECTION:** `TAB_ORDER` in `detail-parts.tsx` gains `faq: 35`, so the section and its nav link land between Dimensions (30) and Downloads (40) in the Tabs, Sections and Section links blocks alike - all three build from `buildDetailSections`, so the link can never point at a section that isn't there. Markup is a copy of ultimate-seo's FAQ (SEO) block (plain `<details>`, no client JS, `FAQPage` JSON-LD with `<` escaped), copied rather than imported because shop must not reach into another module. **This is the first `FAQPage` markup the platform emits anywhere** - the 2026-09-11 AI audit noted its absence. `.spd-acc summary` selectors tightened to `.spd-acc > summary`: a descendant selector dressed every nested question in the section heading's serif and gave it a second +/- marker. **ADMIN:** new **FAQs** tab on the product editor (`SHOP_TAB_ORDER.faq = 50`, `panels/faq.tsx`, dirty-tracked through `TAB_EXTRAS`); a questions list plus an inherit tick on each row of the Categories screen (rows with questions carry an **N FAQs** badge) fed by `categoryFaqs` on the categories GET; a shop-wide list under Settings → General → Product FAQs. The Categories screen and the settings screen share a new `components/admin/FaqListEditor.tsx` (add/remove/reorder); the product editor uses its own `spe-` field primitives. Both PUT validators gained `faqs` - Zod strips what it does not name. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite **6959 passed / 0 failed** (16 new in `modules/shop/lib/faq.test.ts` covering the merge, the stop rules, the loose question match and the JSON-LD escape). **`npm run test:shop-sql` a real PASS, 15 tests, no skip** - four new cases execute the recursive CTE against a throwaway OVH Postgres, proving order (nearest first, empty middle rung present), the inherit flag coming back off the column, the no-master fallback and a product filed nowhere; nothing standard runs raw SQL. **`npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS, 4 tests, no skip** - required because this adds columns to a module migration. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md` gains a **Frequently asked questions** section, a FAQs bullet in the product tab list, a line in Categories, a line in the Settings summary and a paragraph in the section-strip docs.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**The agent endpoint can narrow a catalogue, twin prices tell the truth about tax, and the business states its returns policy** - `ultimate-seo` only (v0.1.15 -> v0.1.16), uncommitted working tree, **one new module migration** `migrations/005_ai_commerce.sql` (`seo_llm_documents.facets JSONB` + a price expression index and a `jsonb_path_ops` GIN index). No new permission key, no env var, no new dependency, no core change. **AUDITED LIVE FIRST (deskwell.co.uk, read-only):** `/robots.txt` carries the Content-Signal line and points at both `llms.txt` and the MCP endpoint; `/llms.txt` 246KB / 793 lines with the business-facts block at the top; `/llms-full.txt` 1.29MB; `.md` twins answer `text/markdown` and are linked from every page head as `rel=alternate`; the MCP endpoint answers `initialize`, `tools/list` and all four tools; every page carries the `Organization`/`OnlineStore` block and product and post pages carry `BreadcrumbList` plus `Product` (shop's) or `BlogPosting`. **The AI half is working.** Three gaps found, all fixed here, plus three that are the site's own settings and are reported rather than changed. **1. `find_products`, a fifth MCP tool** (`lib/mcp/tools.ts` `findProducts`, dispatched in `lib/mcp/handler.ts`): filters the catalogue by `query`, `category`, `min_price`, `max_price`, `availability` and `sort` (`relevance` | `price-low-to-high` | `price-high-to-low`). `max_price` is tested against the listing's LOW price, `min_price` against its HIGH, so a £200-£400 listing belongs in an answer to "under £250". Backed by the new `facets` column, built in the same pass as the twin from the same rows (`productFacets`/`productAvailability`/`productGroups` in `lib/ai/documents.ts`): `{kind, currency, priceMin, priceMax, priceSuffix, availability, sku, groups}`, where `groups` holds the lowercased NAME and SLUG of every category and collection, so "office chairs" and "office-chairs" both match. `search_site` hits now carry the same price/availability line (`facetLine` in the handler), so no comparison needs a follow-up fetch. A product whose twin predates the column is excluded from `find_products` rather than ranked unpriced alongside the others. `optionalNumberParam` added to `lib/mcp/protocol.ts`: a budget sent as a string used to read as "no budget". **2. Twin prices now respect the shop's price DISPLAY tax mode.** `lib/ai/documents.ts` replaced `moneyFormatter`/`shopCurrency` with `shopPriceConfig()` + `priceView()`, reading `shp_settings.config` for `taxMode`, `priceDisplayTax` and `priceDisplayTaxSuffix` and, when the two disagree, the default zone's rates from `shp_shipping_zones` (catch-all zone, else first by name) and `shp_tax_zone_rates` - the same answer the shop's own `lib/tax-display.ts` gives. Every printed price (product facts, the Variations table, category/collection product tables - `taxonomyProducts` now selects `tax_class_id`) goes through it and carries the shop's own suffix, so an assistant quoting a twin quotes the figure on the page. **Before this, a shop storing net and displaying gross published net prices to every AI reader with nothing saying so.** Deskwell is `AS_ENTERED`, so its figures do not move; the suffix appears if one is set. **3. `hasMerchantReturnPolicy` on the Organization** (`buildReturnPolicyJsonLd` in `lib/structured-data.ts`), from seven new profile fields (`returnPolicyCategory`, `returnDays`, `returnMethod`, `returnFees`, `returnFeeAmount`, `returnFeeCurrency`, `returnPolicyCountry`, `returnPolicyUrl`) with a new **Returns** card on the Structured data screen. Blank category = nothing published; `merchantReturnDays` only on a finite window; method and fees suppressed on `MerchantReturnNotPermitted`; a fee published only with a currency beside it. The same policy is said in English as a **Returns** line in `businessFacts` (`returnsLine`), so it reaches `/llms.txt` and `get_site_info` as well. **Also:** `publishSupplier` AI setting (default **true**, preserving what the twins already did) gates the "Supplier" line on a product twin - a reseller's supplier list is published to every crawler otherwise; `lib/ai/crawlers.ts` gains `anthropic-ai`, `meta-externalfetcher`, `FacebookBot`, `Google-CloudVertexBot`, `AI2Bot` and `FirecrawlAgent`, and the referrer table gains **claude.com** (Anthropic's newer domain - a site counting only claude.ai reads its own referrals as falling away), `edgeservices.bing.com`, Phind and Brave Leo; `/llms.txt` now names `/llms-full.txt`, which a reader had no way of guessing at. New tests: `lib/ai/crawlers.test.ts`, plus `find_products` cases in `lib/mcp/handler.test.ts`, returns cases in `lib/structured-data.test.ts` and `lib/ai/business-facts.test.ts`. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 6943 tests pass, and **`npm run test:backup-roundtrip` run for real against throwaway OVH databases: 4 passed, no skip** - required because this adds a column to a module migration. Migration 005 and every clause `findProducts` can emit were ALSO executed against a real throwaway Postgres before this was called done (nothing standard runs raw SQL), including an `EXPLAIN` proving the price filter reaches `seo_llm_documents_facet_price_idx`; the group filter is written as top-level containment (`"facets" @> '{"groups":[…]}'`) rather than `"facets" -> 'groups' @> …` for the same reason - `jsonb_path_ops` indexes the whole document and a query that digs a key out first cannot use it. Both admin API validators gained the new keys (`publishSupplier` on `app/api/admin/ai/route.ts`, the seven returns fields on `app/api/admin/structured-data/route.ts`): Zod strips what it does not name, so a field missing from the schema saves as a blank rather than as an error. **SITE SETTINGS, NOT CODE, reported not changed:** deskwell.co.uk publishes no `WebSite` block (so no sitelinks search box) because `emitWebSite` is off; no page anywhere carries `FAQPage` markup though the *FAQ (SEO)* block exists; and every AI crawler stance is "unset", so `robots.txt` carries no explicit allow group.)

There was no way to say "yes to Twilio, no to texting customers about orders" short of pulling the number that also carries SMS login codes and 2FA elsewhere on the site. **`ShpConfigSchema`** (`modules/shop/lib/config.ts`) gains `smsUpdatesEnabled` (default `true`, so an existing shop's behaviour is unchanged). **Three gates, all ANDed with the existing Twilio check rather than replacing it:** `modules/shop/app/api/public/orders/status/route.ts` folds it into `smsAvailable`, which is what hides the whole card on the confirmation page; `modules/shop/app/api/public/orders/notifications/route.ts` refuses a `channels.sms: true` save with the same 503 it already gives a Twilio-less shop; `modules/shop/lib/order-notify.ts`'s `notifyOrderCustomer` checks it before `isSmsAvailable()` in the actual send, so an order that chose texts before the switch was flipped stops getting them rather than carrying on until someone re-saves its preference. **Admin:** new checkbox on `ShopSettingsTab.tsx`'s Checkout sub-tab (`hq/config?tab=shop&sub=checkout`), under Checkout rules, alongside "Require a phone number at checkout" - **Offer text message updates on the confirmation page**. Saved through the existing `ShpConfigSchema.partial()` PUT, no route change needed. **CHECKS:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean on the five touched files. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md` "Order updates by text message" gains a paragraph on turning it off.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**A customer who has already reviewed something off an order stops being asked to** - `reviews-for-shop` only, uncommitted working tree, one new module migration. **THE ASK:** with review invitations on, the nightly job used to drop the products already reviewed out of the email and send the rest; it now sends nothing at all for that order. `lib/db/invites.ts` gains `hasReviewedOrder({orderId,email,productIds})` - true if a review carries this `order_id` (one left from the order page) OR matches the order's address against the pages it bought (one left off their own bat from a product page). Status is not filtered: a review sat in the moderation queue still counts. `lib/invites.ts` calls it once per candidate, after the purchased lines have been resolved to pages, and `continue`s on true. **THE SCHEMA CHANGE, and why:** a skipped order left no row, so it came back as a candidate every night for ever and held one of the forty slots a run has - forty of them and the job invites nobody. `migrations/002_invite_skipped_reason.sql` adds nullable `rvw_invites.skipped_reason` (also in 001 for fresh installs), `recordInvite` becomes `recordInviteOutcome(orderId, productId, email, skippedReason)` and a skipped order writes a row per page with `'already reviewed'`. **NULL still means 'we emailed them', so both readers of the table filter on it:** the `invited` flag in `lib/published-reviews-provider.ts` and the member GDPR export in `app/api/member/gdpr-export/route.ts`. `InviteRunResult` gains `skipped`, which the cron route already returns. **THE SEND TIME MOVES:** `cronJobs[0].schedule` in `cactus.module.json` goes `0 8 * * *` -> `0 9 * * *` - mid-morning, not first thing. Cron schedules are UTC and core has no per-site timezone anywhere in `lib/cron/`, so that is 10am through British Summer Time and 9am through the winter; the dispatcher wakes hourly on paid plans, so a half-past would only have run on the following tick anyway. **THE EMAIL ITSELF:** the bulleted product list is replaced in the DEFAULT body by `{{orderItems}}` - shop's own `renderOrderItemsTable` over `orderEmailLines(getOrderItems(orderId), config)`, with `#reviews` appended to each line's url, since shop builds a plain product link and this email wants the tab open. Built off the ORDER's lines, not the deduplicated review pages, so a customer sees the variation they bought. `{{orderUrl}}` + `{{hasOrderUrl}}` add a **Review your order** link - `orderTrackingUrl(orderNumber, config)`, which is the postcode-gated link every other order email uses and which lands on `/shop/account/orders/[id]`, the only page that renders `shop.member-order-panels` and so the only page carrying this module's own review card. **Gated on `orderStatus === 'COMPLETED'`**, because that card refuses a SHIPPED order (`lib/order-panel-provider.ts`) and a button onto a page with nothing on it is worse than no button - hence `InviteOrderRow.status`, new in the candidate query's SELECT and GROUP BY. `requiredTags` moves `productList` -> `orderItems` (the table carries a link per line come what may); `productList` is still filled in, undeclared changes nothing for an owner who rewrote this email before today. `hasOrderUrl` IS declared in `mergeTags`, following shop's replacement templates rather than this module's own older `hasTitle`/`hasLink`. New cross-module imports - `lib/db/orders`, `lib/order-items-email`, `lib/order-tracking`, `lib/config` - all present at the pinned `requiresModules` shop 0.1.404 (added in 0.1.381/0.1.393), so NO minVersion bump. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, schema-coverage + module tests 26 passed, email + reviews suites 182 passed, `check-client-graph` and `check-import-cycles` clean, both raw SQL queries `EXPLAIN`ed read-only on the live database, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS against throwaway OVH databases (4 tests, 286s - it builds every module schema, so migration 002 ran there). NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Reviews.md` "Asking past customers".)

Every replacement message already led with the PARENT order number (`{{parentOrderNumber}}`, `lib/replacement-emails.ts`), but the only link on it was `{{orderUrl}}`, which `notifyOrderCustomer` fills with the REPLACEMENT's own tracking page - one line and a parcel. The invoice, the delivery address and the damage report are all on the original. **`parentOrderVars` now also returns `parentOrderUrl` + `hasParentOrderUrl`**, built with `orderTrackingUrl(parent.orderNumber, config)` off `getShopConfigCached()` (so it is empty, and takes its whole `{{#if}}` line with it, on a shop with guest order tracking off). One function covers all four call sites - `replacement-emails.ts`, `shipment-email.ts`, `order-status.ts`, `tracking-added-email.ts` - and so all four triggers: REPLACEMENT_SENT, REPLACEMENT_DISPATCHED, REPLACEMENT_TRACKING_ADDED, REPLACEMENT_DELIVERED. Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**The email templates tab stops being a wall of buttons** - core only, uncommitted working tree, UI shape change with no schema, route, permission or config key behind it. `app/cactus-admin/config/EmailTemplatesClient.tsx` rewritten: the 280px scrolling list of every template the site can send is gone, replaced by a **Section** `<select>` that shows nothing at all until a section is picked (a single-section install is chosen for it, inside the loader's `.then` rather than in an effect, because `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` is on and it is an error not a warning). A group is keyed `source:groupLabel` and not by label alone - two modules may both call a section "Orders". Templates in the chosen section render as pills (`.emt-pill`, `aria-pressed`) above the editor rather than beside it, carrying the existing Edited/Off badges. The editor and the preview now sit side by side in `.emt-split` (`minmax(0,1fr)` twice, one column and a non-sticky pane under 1180px; the preview pane is `position: sticky`). **THE PREVIEW IS LIVE AND THE BUTTON IS GONE:** a 600ms debounce posts the editor's contents to the existing `/api/admin/emails/templates/preview` and the previous in-flight request is aborted, so a slow early response cannot land on top of a newer one. The "Updating…" flag is **derived, not state** - `previewBuiltFrom` holds the serialised inputs the visible HTML was built from and is compared with the current ones; a `setPreviewPending(true)` in the effect body was the other eslint error. The error path marks it built anyway, or the pane says "Updating…" for ever. The same `JSON.stringify` is both the comparison key and the request body, so the two cannot drift. Each merge tag chip gains a copy button writing `{{tag}}` to the clipboard with a 1.5s tick, matching `MediaLibrary.copyLink`'s `navigator.clipboard.writeText` pattern. **`app/cactus-admin/config/ConfigPageClient.tsx`**: new `hasEmailSubTabs` (`tab === 'email' && canManageConfig && canManageEmailTemplates`) drops the main TabStrip's `marginBottom` from `2rem` to `0.5rem` when the email sub-strip sits directly beneath it - two underlined rows with 2rem of nothing between them read as a mistake - and replaces the duplicated condition on the sub-strip itself. **New CSS at the end of `app/globals.css`** (`.emt-pills`, `.emt-pill`, `.emt-split`, `.emt-preview-pane`, `.emt-tag`, `.emt-copy`), tokens throughout, no hex; the selected pill's badges borrow `--color-on-primary` rather than vanishing into the primary fill. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `lib/email` suite 155 passed / 0 failed. **NOT verified in a browser.** Wiki: `Configuration-reference.md` Templates section rewritten for the section picker, the pills, the copy buttons and the always-on preview. **THE WHITESPACE, once the screenshot arrived:** all three red bands were the same defect, and not local to this page. `globals.css` has no `p` margin reset, and `.field-hint` is almost always a `<p>` - so every hint carries the browser's own `margin: 1em 0`, which inside a `.field` lands on top of the flex `gap` above it and the field's `--form-gap` below it. A one-line hint therefore sat in a band roughly twice its own height, three times over on this page. **FIX:** `.field > .field-hint { margin: 0 }` - scoped to a direct child of a `.field`, where the flex gap already does the spacing, so a hint used loose elsewhere (and the `<span className="field-hint">` variants, which never had a UA margin) is untouched. Platform-wide by design: 292 `field-hint` usages across core and modules all tighten the same way, which is the point - the margin was never intentional.)

Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**One message can be thrown away, or moved out onto a conversation of its own** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree, plus one line in core's `package.json`. **WHAT THIS IS:** the dots beside a message gain two entries, ruled off below the everyday ones - **Move to its own conversation** and **Delete this message** - both behind `unifiedinbox.manage`, both asking first through `ConfirmDialog`. They are the two answers to the same problem from opposite ends: threading is a heuristic, and a dropped References header plus two customers who both wrote "Re: Quote" is all it takes to glue a stray email onto somebody else's conversation. Until now the only answers were to live with it or throw the whole conversation away. **THE PART THAT IS NOT THE OBVIOUS PART:** deleting the row is not enough, and a version that only deletes the row works perfectly for ten minutes. `findMessageByIdentity` - the collection's dedupe - is a lookup in `uin_messages` itself, so a deleted email misses it and is filed again as a discovery the next time it is seen at a location `uin_processed_messages` has not walked (the owner archiving it from their phone is enough, and a UIDVALIDITY reset certainly is); and a channel's message is a COPY of something the owning module still holds and hands back on every listing, deduped on `(thread_id, provider_message_id)`, so deleting it locally brings it back on the next tick, and the one after that, for ever. That is migration 053's bug one size down. **NEW MIGRATION `modules/unified-inbox/migrations/054_message_deletions.sql`:** table `uin_message_deleted` (`scope` TEXT, `identity` TEXT, `deleted_at` TIMESTAMP(3), PK on the pair) - TEXT and TIMESTAMP(3) only, no dollar-quoting, so the backup serialiser and `schema-coverage.test.ts` need no new branch. `scope` is `'email'` with the Message-ID as `identity`, or the manifest CHANNEL key (not the module) with the channel's own message id. **The email scope is the whole site rather than one connection, deliberately:** a Message-ID is meant to be unique in the world, so one line covers every mailbox including one added next year. The narrow case it gets wrong is internal mail between two of the site's own addresses, which is filed twice under one Message-ID - delete one side and the other cannot be RE-collected, though the copy already filed is untouched. Written down in the migration header. Added to `cactus.module.json`'s `teardown` list, which `lib/teardown.test.ts` checks against the migrations on every `npm test` (it caught the omission). **NEW in `lib/db.ts` (section S9):** `messageWasDeleted(scope, identity)`, called at the top of BOTH `insertMessage` and `insertProviderMessage` - the two doors, and the only callers are `lib/sync.ts` and `lib/provider-sync.ts`; `messageForAction(id)` (message + its thread + a real `COUNT(*)` of siblings rather than the thread's own `message_count` column, which is bookkeeping and can be stale); `storedObjectsForMessage(id)`; `deleteMessageHere(message, userId)` - gravestone and delete in ONE transaction, so there is no window where the row is gone and nothing stops the next collection; `splitRefusal(message)` (pure); `splitMessageToNewThread(message, userId)`. **NEW ROUTES**, both POST, both `unifiedinbox.manage` + `canOpenThread`, both taking no body: `app/api/messages/[id]/remove/route.ts` and `app/api/messages/[id]/split/route.ts`. `remove` deletes stored attachment bytes BEFORE the rows, carrying a storage failure rather than failing on it, exactly as `bin/empty` and retention do. **NOT the same route as `app/api/messages/[id]/route.ts`**, which is the far-end DELETE a channel offers (a Twilio voicemail leaving the phone company); the menu entry is deliberately NOT drawn on a message that already carries that button, because two controls a few pixels apart both saying Delete and meaning different things is how somebody deletes the wrong thing. **THE SPLIT'S DECISIONS:** the new conversation keeps the inbox, channel, status and any snooze, and is born with `person_id` NULL so the people pass works out whose it is from its own counterparty message rather than inheriting the name it was wrongly filed under - being filed under the wrong person is usually why it is being split. `merged_from_thread_id` is left exactly as it is, so an earlier merge's undo quietly skips a message that has since moved on rather than dragging it back. **The undo is the merge that already exists** - no bespoke snapshot, because that would be a second, subtly different implementation of moving messages between conversations. Three refusals, in order: a conversation that has itself been merged away, the only message in a conversation, and a message a channel owns (that one would be handed straight back on the next listing, onto the conversation it came from). **UI:** `MessageMenu.tsx` gains `actOn`, `canDeleteHere`, `canSplit`, `base`, `params` and two `ConfirmDialog`s; `ThreadPane.tsx` gains a `canManage` prop and works the two out per message; `InboxPanel.tsx` passes `canManage`. A successful split `router.push`es to the new conversation (`inboxHref` with `page: null`), because refreshing would leave somebody looking at a conversation a message had just vanished from. `EVENT_WORDS` gains `message_deleted`, `message_split`, `split_from` - and `unmerged`, which was missing and had been reading "Chris changed something" in the log since merges shipped. **THE LOG DELIBERATELY KEEPS NOTHING OF THE DELETED MESSAGE** - `detail` is NULL - since an audit row holding the subject and the sender would not be much of a deletion, and the same row has to survive an erasure under D17. **NEW LIVE SUITE** `modules/unified-inbox/lib/message-actions.live.test.ts` + `npm run test:inbox-message-actions` (`RUN_INBOX_MESSAGE_ACTIONS=1`), five tests against a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database on the OVH VPS: the delete leaves the conversation standing and the count agreeing, a deleted email is refused by `insertMessage` at a fresh location AND on a different conversation, a deleted channel message is refused by `insertProviderMessage` onto the very conversation it was deleted from, an undeleted message still files on both paths, the split settles both sides and leaves no person on the new one, merging puts it back, and all three refusals refuse. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` 505 files / 6,876 passed / 0 failed, `npm run test:inbox-message-actions` a real PASS (5/5), and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a **real PASS** - owed, because a module migration added a table. **Wiki:** `wiki/Unified-Inbox.md` gains "Moving one message out, and deleting one" under the merging section, and the `manage` row of the permissions table names both. Module README gains the same under "What is in this version" and a paragraph on `uin_message_deleted` under Tables. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's. The round-trip again left `uk-bookkeeping` out of its module schema build with `cannot ALTER TABLE "bk_accounts" because it has pending trigger events`, which is pre-existing and not mine.)

NEW optional `FeedItem.promotionIds` in `lib/feed-xml.ts`, rendered as repeated `g:promotion_id` and sliced to 10. **THE DERIVATION:** `collectFeedItems()` now returns `FeedData` (`{ items, promotions }`) rather than `FeedItem[]` - one pass, so the two documents cannot drift. Its `ChildRow` SELECT gains `order_size_deduction`; `considerForPromotion()` reproduces `lib/checkout.ts:305-319` exactly - the supplier on the ROW ITSELF with no parent fallback, `deductionAmount()` non-null, the item genuinely on offer, and the amount strictly under the charged unit price (the rule floors a line at zero, so a bigger stamp would advertise more than ever comes off). The one supplier query, `getDeductionRules()`, fires only when something qualifies. **NEW CROSS-MODULE IMPORTS** from shop: `lib/order-size-deduction` (`deductionAmount`) and `lib/db/suppliers` (`getDeductionRules`), so `requiresModules` shop `minVersion` **0.1.243 -> 0.1.401**, the release both landed in. **THE SHAPE MISMATCH, written down rather than papered over:** Google's `minimum_purchase_amount` is a WHOLE-BASKET precondition and "does not filter the products eligible"; shop's threshold is one supplier's goods with delivery excluded. Last updated: 2026-09-11 (**The AI half of the module was audited live, a proven cron failure and an unreachable time budget fixed, and the business's own identity added to what agents are given** - `ultimate-seo` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no migration, no new permission key, no env var, no new dependency. **WHAT WAS WRONG, found by reading the live site rather than the code:** `/llms.txt` on deskwell.co.uk served 664 bytes - a heading, a summary, and the sentence "Every page below is also available as Markdown", above no pages at all. `/llms-full.txt` ended at the words "# Full text". Every `.md` twin answered 404. The MCP endpoint spoke the protocol correctly and answered `list_sections` with "This site publishes nothing for agents to read yet". No page carried a `BreadcrumbList`, a `CollectionPage` or a `BlogPosting`, and no page carried its `rel=alternate` Markdown link. `seo_llm_documents` held **0 rows**, with every AI setting switched on and all seven content types ticked. The site-wide `Organization`/`OnlineStore` block, by contrast, is present on every page fetched and is complete. **WHY IT IS EMPTY, STATED EXACTLY:** `CronRun` has `/api/m/ultimate-seo/cron/ai` at `lastStatus` **`seeded`**, `lastRunAt` 2026-09-10 11:05 - and core's dispatcher writes `seeded` on first sight of a job WITHOUT running it, then never touches that status again. The job is `0 3 * * *`, so at 2026-09-11 01:35 UTC (when this was read) it had not yet reached its first scheduled fire, due 03:00 that morning. **The store is empty because the job has never had a turn, not because it runs and fails.** That is the whole finding, and the fixes below are a guard rail rather than the thing that unblocks Deskwell: at ~780 entities the rebuild is about 12s of work and would very likely have completed on its first run regardless. **THE LATENT DEFECT IS STILL REAL AND STILL FIXED:** `lib/ai/materialise.ts` budgeted `TIME_BUDGET_MS = 240_000` and both the cron and rebuild routes exported `maxDuration = 300`. **A module route file cannot set its own maxDuration** - core's catch-all at `app/api/m/[module]/[...path]/route.ts` sets one ceiling of 60s for every module route and says so in a comment, and core's cron dispatcher then hands a job whatever is left of its own 60s tick (~54s at best). The 300 was inert and the 240s budget unreachable, so any install whose catalogue needs more than ~54s would be cut off mid-flight, recorded as a failure and left silently short - the identical defect fixed for the weekly audit on 2026-09-08 and reintroduced by the AI job that shipped on 2026-09-09. Budget now **40s**; both `maxDuration` exports removed with a comment saying why. Live Deskwell's real inventory is ~780 entities: 439 visible products (the other 20,395 `shp_products` rows are `catalogue_hidden` variants), 48 categories, 39 collections, 168 filter collections, plus the pages and posts. **THE AUDIT CRON, BY CONTRAST, IS PROVABLY FAILING,** `lastStatus` "The operation was aborted due to timeout" on 2026-09-07: the 38s budget gated the START of a fetch, never its end, so a worker could pick a page up at 37.9s and wait the full `FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` on it - 48s of crawl plus `collectUrls` before it. The loop now refuses to start a page it cannot also finish (`Date.now() - started + FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS < TIME_BUDGET_MS`) and the budget is **30s**. **NEW `lib/ai/business-facts.ts`** (pure, 14 tests) and **NEW AI setting `businessFacts`, default ON**: the registered name, the company registration number under the label the owner gave it, VAT, DUNS, founding date, the address on one line, the countries served, price range, opening hours, contact email and telephone, languages and the profile links - read straight off the structured-data profile, so llms.txt, the agent endpoint and the JSON-LD on every page cannot disagree about who the business is. Rendered as `## About this business` **above** the page lists in `/llms.txt`, on the reasoning that a reader with a small context window takes the top of the file and identity is what decides whether anything below it is worth fetching. **NEW MCP tool `get_site_info`** returning the same facts as plain text, and `initialize` now names it first in its instructions. **TWO FILES THAT LIED, both fixed:** `/llms.txt` only claims Markdown copies when there are rows to claim it about, and says "No page index is published yet" when there are none, rather than looking like a site with nothing on it; `list_sections` now says the *copies* have not been built and points the caller at the site, instead of telling an agent the business publishes nothing. **ADMIN:** the AI screen gains the **Say who you are** toggle and a warning banner when nothing has been built - every symptom of an empty store looks like a different bug, so it is now stated once, loudly, where the Rebuild button is. On Structured data, **Offer a search box** is now disabled unless the website record is switched on (the builder drops the search box with the record it hangs off, so the switch did nothing and said nothing about it) and an info banner appears when the organisation is published without the website. `components/admin/StructuredDataClient.tsx`'s local `Toggle` gains `disabled`. **CAUGHT BY A TEST I WROTE FOR SOMETHING ELSE:** reading the site config and the structured-data profile in one `Promise.all` meant a profile read that threw took the site's own name and description with it and published a file headed "This site". Now `Promise.allSettled`, each failure costing only itself. **SETTINGS FOUND OFF ON LIVE DESKWELL, for the owner to decide:** `emitWebSite` and `emitSearchAction` are both false, so the site publishes its Organization/OnlineStore record - which is complete and correct, Companies House number, VAT, address, contact point, `sameAs` and all - with no `WebSite` node naming the site or tying the two together. **NOT A MODULE DEFECT, REPORTED NOT FIXED:** the live `Product` JSON-LD is the shop's, and carries only name, description, image, sku and a bare Offer with price, currency and availability - no `brand`, `gtin`/`mpn`, `offers.url`, `priceValidUntil`, `itemCondition`, `shippingDetails`, `hasMerchantReturnPolicy` or `aggregateRating`, which are the fields an AI shopping agent reads; and `/sitemap.xml` is 19,710 URLs of which 18,974 are variant query-string permutations, drowning 736 real pages. **Gates:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` **6833 pass / 0 fail**, 27 new tests across `business-facts.test.ts`, `llms-txt.test.ts` and `mcp/handler.test.ts`. Backup round-trip **not required** - no schema, migration or `lib/backup` change. Live Deskwell read READ-ONLY throughout (`seo_settings`, `seo_llm_documents`, `seo_audit_runs`, `CronRun`, row counts); nothing written to it. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A conversation ending in our own email gets a chase, not a reply to ourselves** - `google-ai-studio`, `unified-inbox`, `contact-form`, uncommitted. **THE DEFECT, reported off the live inbox:** following up an email nobody answered produced a suggestion that ANSWERED our own email - a reply in the customer's voice, written on our behalf. It reads perfectly well right up until you notice who it is addressed to. The prompt had no notion of who spoke last, and "write a reply to this conversation" is a coherent instruction whichever way round the last message is. **THE FIX, in `lib/reply-prompt.ts`:** NEW `stanceFor(messages)` -> `'answering' | 'following-up'`, walking back from the newest and **skipping notes** - a colleague writing "chase this on Friday" has said nothing to the customer and must not turn a chase into an answer; a conversation of notes alone, or of nothing, answers `answering`. NEW `daysSinceLastOwnMessage(messages, now)` -> whole days or **null**, never a guess: a follow-up naming a length of time it invented is worse than one that does not mention it. `composeReplyPrompt` branches on the stance - "drafting a FOLLOW-UP", the fact stated **BEFORE the fences** (by the time a model has read our own email it is already composing an answer to it), "It went out N days ago", plus rules forbidding an answer to our own message and thanking somebody for a message they have not sent, and asking for a short nudge / a fuller one / one that gives them a way out rather than three wordings of one sentence. The noun follows the stance throughout (`3 different follow-ups`). **THE BUTTON SAYS WHAT IT WILL DO:** `Suggest a follow-up` rather than `Suggest reply`, via core's existing `label` prop - no core change. unified-inbox reads it off `quotedPreviews[last].ownSender` (that list is already note-free and already oldest-first regardless of the newestFirst setting, so it is exactly the right rule); contact-form off `threadMessages[last].direction === 'out'` (an empty list means the customer wrote and nobody answered, which is not a chase). **Both hosts work the fact out independently of the prompt** - the wording and the instruction are decided separately and neither trusts the other. **VERIFIED AGAINST GOOGLE FOR REAL**, because no gate here executes an HTTP contract: three drafts came back, all three chases, none answering our own email, none thanking her for a message she had not sent, and two of them offering to CHECK stock rather than asserting the "two left" from a week-old note - the invent-nothing rule holding under a note that tempted them. **ALSO IN THIS PASS - THE FROZEN-MIGRATION GUARD EARNED ITS KEEP:** `npm test` failed on `scripts/check-frozen-migrations.test.ts` because **google-ai-studio had already been RELEASED as v0.1.3** with `003_reply_suggestions.sql` in it (which is how the broken `gemini-3.1-flash` default reached live Deskwell). 003 restored byte-for-byte from the tag with `git show v0.1.3:migrations/...`; the whole repair now lives in `004_text_model_default.sql`. **Next gas release is v0.1.4, and it carries 004** - an install on v0.1.3 is holding the broken default until it takes it. **Gates:** typecheck clean, eslint clean, `npm test` green, backup round-trip a real PASS. NEW tests in `reply-prompt.test.ts` (20 total, 11 of them new for the stance). **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**The writing model was a name Google has never had - fixed, and the settings screen now asks Google what exists instead of guessing** - `google-ai-studio`, uncommitted, on top of the Suggest reply entry below. **THE DEFECT:** `DEFAULT_TEXT_MODEL` shipped as `gemini-3.1-flash`, invented by pattern-matching the picture default `gemini-3.1-flash-image`. **There is no such model.** Google's 3.1 generation ships flash as `-image` and `-lite` and nothing in between, so every suggestion came back 404. Confirmed by `GET https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models` with the live key: 55 models, 40 answering `generateContent`, no `gemini-3.1-flash` among them. **THE ROW WAS ALREADY WRONG ON LIVE DESKWELL** - `gas_settings.text_model` read `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview` (hand-typed while trying to make it work; a Live model speaks `bidiGenerateContent`, not `generateContent`, so that 404s too) and the column DEFAULT held the invented name. Migration 003 had already been applied there, and `run-module-migrations.mjs` never runs a file twice - so 003 is corrected for fresh installs AND **NEW `migrations/004_text_model_default.sql`** resets the column default and rewrites the row **only where it still holds the exact broken default**. A name somebody typed for themselves is their choice and is left alone even when it is also wrong: a migration quietly overwriting a setting is a worse failure than the one it mends. **THE REAL FIX - NEW `lib/models.ts`:** `listGoogleModels(apiKey)` reads Google's own ListModels (10-minute cache keyed on the API key, so pasting a new key does not answer out of the old one's list) and `splitModels` sorts it into the two menus. **The listing says which METHODS a model supports and never which modalities it EMITS**, so an image model and a writing model are the same shape in the response and only the name tells them apart - `isImageModel` matches `-image` and `nano-banana`, `isWritingModel` needs `generateContent` and excludes tts/transcribe/lyria/robotics/computer-use/deep-research/antigravity by name. A name-based rule is allowed to rot here: the worst case is a model missing from a menu that still has a text box beside it. NEW route `app/api/admin/models/route.ts` (`google-ai-studio.manage`) - **a soft failure throughout**, answering 200 with empty lists and a sentence when there is no key or Google is unreachable, because a settings screen that will not open is worse than two text boxes. NEW `ModelField` in the settings tab: the menu where we have a list, the box where we have not, a link between them either way, and a **red line naming the saved value when Google's list has no such model** - which is the feedback whose absence made this take a day to notice. **TWO MORE THINGS THE REAL CALL TAUGHT, neither catchable by any gate** (verified with a live `generateContent` against the module's exact body shape - the `responseSchema` ARRAY/STRING with min=max=3 works, three usable drafts came back, and the internal note was used without being repeated to the customer): (1) **these are THINKING models** - `thoughtsTokenCount: 1004` on a 279-token prompt - and the API may return reasoning as a part of its own, which `firstText` was joining onto the answer; joined it fails `JSON.parse` and the raw payload gets served to somebody as a draft. NEW exported `answerText(parts)` filters `thought: true`. (2) **`gemini-3.8-flash` answered `503 UNAVAILABLE` ("experiencing high demand") on every attempt**, so the default is **`gemini-3.5-flash`** - proven on this key - rather than the highest number; 3.6/3.7/3.8 are on the menu. `describeHttpFailure` gains a 503 branch saying the model is busy and to pick another, and the 404 now says "pick one from the list" rather than "check the model name". NEW `modules/google-ai-studio/lib/models.test.ts` (13 tests, fixtures taken from the real listing, including one asserting the invented name is never offered). **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS - owed, 004 touches schema. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**An emptied bin stays emptied - conversations from other channels no longer come straight back on the next check** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. NEW migration `053_provider_deletions.sql`. **THE BUG:** delete a live chat, an enquiry, a call or a text, empty the bin, and the next collection copied the lot back in - confirmed on the live Deskwell database, where 24 conversations dating back to 9 August were re-created at 21:12 on 2026-09-10. Email has been safe since 001 because `uin_processed_messages` keeps its `(connection, folder, uid)` row with a NULL `thread_id`; the channels had no equivalent, and the owning module is the source of truth and offers the conversation again on every pass. **THE AMPLIFIER:** `providerWatermarks()` derived `since` from `MAX(last_message_at)` over SURVIVING provider threads, so emptying a bin wound the watermark BACKWARDS and the next pass re-listed weeks of settled ground - which is why one deletion returned a month of chats. **NEW TABLE `uin_provider_deleted`** (`provider_module` TEXT, `external_id` TEXT, `through` TIMESTAMP(3), `deleted_at` TIMESTAMP(3), PK on the first two) - a gravestone holding the channel's own id and the newest message the conversation held, and deliberately no name, address or body, so it outlives a D17 erasure without keeping what was erased. **`deleteThreads()` writes it** (in `lib/db.ts`, so all three destroy paths get it: Empty bin, the retention sweep and person erasure), covering merged-in losers too since the external id stays on the losing side. **NEW `providerDeletionFloors(providerModule, externalIds)`** in `lib/db.ts`; **`providerWatermarks()` now UNIONs the gravestones** so the watermark only moves forwards. **`syncProvider()` reads the floors once per pass** and skips a conversation whose `lastMessageAt <= through` - not opened, not upserted, not counted - and where the party HAS written since, files only messages with `sentAt > through`, so a chat that reopens carries the new words and not the destroyed history. Letting it back at all is deliberate: the far end never closed the conversation, so a customer typing into a chat the site had silently stopped reading is the worse and more invisible failure. **TESTS:** three new cases in `lib/provider-sync.test.ts` (25 pass) and claim 8 in `lib/bin.live.test.ts`, which is real SQL on a throwaway VPS database - `npm run test:inbox-bin`, 7 pass. No permission key, route, env var or Puck block changed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Suggest reply - a generic reply-drafting seam in core, google-ai-studio publishing it, unified-inbox and contact-form consuming it** - core plus `google-ai-studio`, `unified-inbox`, `contact-form` and one line of `contact-form-reply-catcher`, all uncommitted. **WHAT IT IS:** a **Suggest reply** button under the writing box wherever this site has one. It sends the conversation off, gets three drafts back, and clicking one puts it in the box to read; a tick keeps it and a cross puts back whatever was written before. **THE SEAM IS THE POINT.** NEW extension point **`core.reply-suggestions`** (`serverOnly`): the module holding the conversation never learns which model answered, and the module that answered never learns where the words came from. Core names neither end. **NEW `lib/conversations/types.ts` additions** - `REPLY_SUGGESTER_POINT`, `ReplySuggestionMessage`, `ReplySuggestionRequest`, `ReplySuggester`, `ResolvedReplySuggester`. A message carries a **`role` of `them`/`us`/`note`, never a direction**: a note is colleagues talking among themselves, which is context worth having and must never be quoted back at a customer, and `in`/`out` cannot say that. **NEW `lib/conversations/reply-suggestion-error.ts`** - `ReplySuggestionError` and `isReplySuggestionError`, in a file of its own **with no imports at all**, because both ends need it: `types.ts` is imported for its types from both halves of the app and must stay free of runtime code, and a module publishing a suggester cannot import the resolver (the registry imports that module). `isReplySuggestionError` matches by name+shape as well as `instanceof`, since one class can be evaluated twice across a bundle boundary and a perfectly good explanation would otherwise arrive as a shrug. **NEW `lib/conversations/reply-suggestions.ts`** - shaped exactly like `providers.ts`/`destinations.ts` (`INSTALLED_MODULE_WHERE`, shape check, shrug at an entry the bundle has not caught up with, **dynamic `await import('@/lib/modules/extension-points')`** to keep out of a registry cycle). Exports `REPLY_SUGGESTION_COUNT` (3), `resolveReplySuggesters`, `firstConfiguredReplySuggester`, `canSuggestReplies()`, `trimTranscript(messages, 24000)` and `suggestReplies()`. `canSuggestReplies` is asked while a reply box is being DRAWN, so it returns before touching the database on a site with nothing published, and `isConfigured()` is cheap by contract. **NEW `components/admin/ReplySuggestions.tsx`** - core chrome, because a reply box is not a rare thing. It **owns no writing box**: `onPreview(text | null)` and `onAccept(text)` are the whole contract, so a contentEditable host, a markdown host and a plain textarea host each keep their own. The host stashes what was there on the **FIRST** preview only - flicking between three drafts and then crossing out must restore what somebody actually wrote, not the draft they looked at before this one. **GOOGLE-AI-STUDIO:** NEW `migrations/003_reply_suggestions.sql` (idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` for `text_model` and `reply_house_style` on the `gas_settings` singleton; two strings read with the six already there did not earn a table). NEW `lib/gemini-text.ts` - `POST .../v1beta/models/{model}:generateContent`, 30s timeout, `responseMimeType: application/json` plus a `responseSchema` of ARRAY-of-STRING with min/max = count. **The schema is load-bearing:** a model asked politely for three numbered replies will one day send two, or four, or a preamble. NEW `lib/reply-prompt.ts` (pure, tested) - house style, then the transcript between fences, then the instruction; **it says the transcript is DATA before the fences as well as after**, and `defuse()` takes any closing fence out of a message, because a stranger typed those words. NEW `lib/reply-suggestions.ts` - the `ReplySuggester` itself, carrying `GoogleAiError`'s own wording and status across into `ReplySuggestionError` so "check the key" and a 429 still read as themselves. Settings, its route and its tab gain both fields; the tab now has **Pictures** and **Replies** headings. `requiresCoreVersion` -> **0.5.1574** on all three modules. **UNIFIED-INBOX:** NEW `lib/reply-transcript.ts` - its own query rather than `listThreadMessages`, because the screen never receives a message's markup (it is fetched into a sandboxed frame one at a time) and anything READING the conversation needs the words inside it; newest 40 out of the database, reversed, `body_text` falling back to `htmlToText(readableHtml(body_html))`. NEW `app/api/threads/[id]/suggest-reply/route.ts` (`unifiedinbox.reply` **and** `canOpenThread` - a draft about a conversation somebody may not read would be a way of reading it). `Composer.tsx` gains `canSuggestReplies`, `offerSuggestions` (reply and reply-all only), `previewSuggestion`/`acceptSuggestion` and an effect that **clears the stash when the panel goes** - a preview left in the box keeps its words, but a stale stash would put a five-minute-old draft back on the next cross. Preview marks nothing dirty; accept does everything a keystroke does. Threaded through `ThreadPane` and `ComposerSlot`; `InboxPanel` resolves it in the **second** `Promise.all` (`canReplyHere.then(...)`) - the first one runs before `canReply` exists and putting it there is a TDZ crash. **CONTACT-FORM:** NEW `lib/thread.ts` - `threadMessagesFor` (the `contact-form.thread-messages` merge lifted out of the detail page, permission-filtered exactly as it was) and `transcriptFor`; the page now calls it, so what it DRAWS and what a suggestion READS cannot disagree. NEW `app/api/admin/contact-form/submissions/[id]/suggest-reply/route.ts` (`contact.reply`). `ThreadMessageContribution` gains an optional **`direction`** - the point carried both ways all along (reply-catcher contributes admin-sent mail as well as submitter replies) and nothing could tell them apart; absent means `in`, which is what the point meant before. `contact-form-reply-catcher` sets it, one line, **optional type seam so either order ships**. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6773 passed, 0 failed, 429 skipped; one earlier run had `lib/puck/module-layout-site-logo.test.tsx` time out under parallel load and it passes on its own at a longer timeout - the AdminNav/sidebar-order changes in this shared tree are another session's), `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a **real PASS 4/4** with `google-ai-studio` among the schemas built - owed because a module migration adds columns. NEW tests: `modules/google-ai-studio/lib/reply-prompt.test.ts` (9) and `lib/conversations/reply-suggestions.test.ts` (4). **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Sidebar links can be dragged into any order, favourites included** - core, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no migration, no new permission key, no env var, no new dependency. **WHAT IT IS:** a per-user arrangement of the admin sidebar, held in `localStorage` under `cactus-sidebar-item-order` as `Record<sectionId, itemId[]>` - deliberately NOT a site setting. Settings > Navigation already owns the shared, permissioned ordering (`AdminMenuConfig.items[].order`, gated behind `config.manage`), and one person tidying their own rail must not move everybody else's. The server still resolves which sections and items exist and who may see them; this only re-orders what it sent. **NEW `lib/nav/sidebar-order.ts`** - the whole ordering algebra, pure and React-free so it can be tested on its own: `parseNavOrder` (localStorage is external input, so a hand-edited or half-written value yields `{}` rather than a crash on first paint), `applyNavOrder`, `orderAfterDrop`, `orderAfterKeyboardMove`, `favouritesAfterDrop`, `favouritesAfterKeyboardMove`. `applyNavOrder` holds the four invariants worth knowing: an id listed under a section it did not come from renders THERE (cross-section drags are the point); the first section to claim an id wins, so a stale arrangement listing a link twice still renders it once; an id the server no longer sends is dropped; and an id the arrangement has never seen - a link a module added since - keeps its server position at the END of its own section. `orderAfterDrop` writes back EVERY section, not only the two involved, because a whole-tree snapshot is the one shape that cannot half-apply when the menu changes underneath it. **NEW `lib/nav/sidebar-order.test.ts`** - 18 tests, all of the above plus both keyboard ends. **NEW `components/admin/useNavReorder.ts`** - the pointer half: hit testing, auto-scroll, click suppression, focus restoration. **POINTER EVENTS, NOT HTML5 DRAG-AND-DROP, AND THAT IS THE REQUIREMENT:** native dragging swaps the cursor for a move/alias glyph and drags a translucent ghost of the link, and this was asked for explicitly not to change the cursor - so there is NO `cursor` rule anywhere in the feature, `draggable={false}` sits on every nav `<Link>` to kill the browser's own link drag, and the only things that move are the carried row (dimmed to 0.45) and a 2px `--color-primary` insertion line. Under 5px of movement is still a click; past that, `consumeClick()` swallows the click the browser synthesises on pointerup so a drag never navigates. **TWO DRAG ZONES, `tree` and `fav`, and a row never crosses between them** - a link joins or leaves Favourites by its star, and nothing else; the zone rides on `data-drag-zone` and a hit test in the wrong zone returns no target. Filter results are a search list and take no drag props at all. **DROPPING INTO A SECTION WITH NO ROW TO AIM AT:** section headings and emptied sections carry `data-drag-section`, so a link can be moved into a section that is collapsed or that you have just emptied (an expanded empty section draws a dashed `.admin-nav-drop-empty` strip while a tree drag is in flight). Dropping onto a collapsed heading fires `onSectionDrop`, which opens that section - otherwise the link looks exactly like it vanished. **TOUCH IS LEFT ALONE** (`e.pointerType === 'touch'` returns early): the same vertical gesture scrolls the rail, and stealing it would leave the menu unscrollable on a phone. **KEYBOARD:** `Alt+ArrowUp` / `Alt+ArrowDown` on a focused link steps it one place and follows it with focus (`aria-keyshortcuts` announces it); stepping off the end of a section moves it into the next, exactly as a drag does. `onNavKeyDown`'s roving Up/Down now bails on `altKey` so the two do not fight. **RESET:** a toolbar button appears only once `hasCustomOrder`, and clears the key. **`components/admin/AdminNav.tsx`:** favourites persistence lifted into `persistFavourites` (now returns its argument, so `persistSections` does too and both read as one expression); renders `reorder.sections` rather than the `sections` prop; `filterMatches` now built from the ordered items so search results follow the arrangement. **`app/globals.css`:** `.admin-nav--dragging` (user-select only), `.admin-nav-row--dragging`, `.admin-nav-row--drop-before/after`, `.admin-nav-section-label--drop`, `.admin-nav-drop-empty`. The insertion line sits at `top:0`/`bottom:0` rather than `-1px` so the favourites' own overflow scroller cannot clip it. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6772 passed, 429 skipped - the single `lib/puck/module-layout-site-logo.test.tsx` timeout under parallel load passes on its own, and the `modules/unified-inbox` type errors seen mid-run are another session's in-flight work in this shared tree). No schema change and no raw SQL, so no backup round-trip or ledger guard was owed. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

It exists because the tab strip has to be drawn BEFORE anybody clicks Settings, so whether the account may read the settings has to be known up front: the endpoint answers to `space-planner.manage` while the panel now opens on `space-planner.access` (widened from `.manage` in the manifest, so the staff who could see the old sidebar link still see these screens). Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A rate limit now waits itself out instead of stopping the job** - `google-ai-studio` v0.1.1, same unreleased tree as the entry below. **THE RULE:** a 429 from Google is not a failure - the key is fine, the request is fine, only the moment is wrong - so the browser waits and asks again, indefinitely, until it has a picture or the run is called off. Everything else still fails at once. **WHY THE BROWSER AND NOT THE SERVER:** a module route has the shared 60s dispatcher ceiling and a quota window has considerably more than that, so a server-side retry would simply time out instead. **gemini.ts:** `GoogleAiError` gains `retryAfterSeconds`; NEW exported `parseRetryAfter(header, now)` reads both legal shapes (delta-seconds, HTTP-date), rejects absent/unparseable/past values and caps at 3600 so a stray header cannot park a browser on a countdown until Christmas; `describeHttpFailure` takes it as a third argument and `generateImage` passes `response.headers.get('retry-after')`. The 429's own sentence lost its "Wait a minute and try again" - nobody is being asked to do that any more. **THE ROUTE:** `jobs/[id]/images` answers a 429 with `NextResponse.json({error, retryable: true, retryAfterSeconds}, {status: 429})` rather than plain `errorResponse`, because the browser needs a fact to act on, not only a sentence. Every other `GoogleAiError` is unchanged. **NEW `lib/backoff.ts`** - `retryDelaySeconds(attempt, retryAfterSeconds, jitter)`: Google's own header wins outright; otherwise 15s, 25s, 40s, 60s and then 60s forever, plus up to 5s of jitter. **The jitter is load-bearing** - four pictures rate-limited together would otherwise retry on the same clock, arrive together and be refused together indefinitely. `NaN`/0/negative attempts fall back to the first step (`STEPS[index] ?? STEPS[0]`, and the fallback is reachable: `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on and `STEPS[NaN]` is undefined). NEW `lib/backoff.test.ts`, 9 tests, covering both it and `parseRetryAfter`. **THE PANEL:** `makeOneImage(id, signal)` returns a **discriminated `ImageResult`** (`{ok:true,candidate}` | `{ok:false,reason:'aborted'}` | `{ok:false,reason:'failed',message}`) rather than throwing - "the owner pressed Stop" is not an error and must not be told apart from one by reading a message, and an aborted `fetch` throws a `TypeError` whose `.message` is "Failed to fetch", which is not English for a site owner. `countDown(seconds, signal, tick)` sleeps in one-second slices purely so the countdown on screen is honest. **ONE `AbortController` per run** in `runRef`, ended by: the **Stop** button, **Discard**, the unmount cleanup (a client-side navigation to another page) and a `pagehide` listener (closing the tab, a hard navigation) - which is exactly the "until we close the tab/move to another page" this was asked for. `stoppedByOwnerRef` decides who gets told: only the Stop button leaves a message, because Discard already said what the owner wanted and a panel that has gone has nobody left to tell. Pictures already made stay on screen to be kept or thrown away - they were paid for either way. **UI:** the primary button reads `Waiting {n}s…`, a **Stop** button appears beside it, and an `alert-info` explains what is happening and that leaving the page open is all that is needed. **TWO UI FIXES IN THE SAME PASS, both reported off the live Deskwell product editor:** (1) the **House style** box was the width of a postcode - a `<textarea>` carries its own intrinsic width from `cols` and only the prompt box escaped it by sitting in a stretching flex column, so `.gas-card textarea` now sets `display:block;width:100%;box-sizing:border-box;resize:vertical` outright rather than relying on where a box happens to sit. (2) The source groups are now `<details>` (`.gas-fold`), **open only for the product's own photographs** - a range with forty variations was forty tiles to scroll past on every visit to reach the prompt. Each folded summary carries its picture count AND how many are ticked inside it, because a fold that hides ticks with no way to see them is worse than the scroll. `display:flex` on a `<summary>` takes the browser's own disclosure triangle with it, so the panel draws its own from borders in `currentColor` (a token) and rotates it on `[open]`, with the transition dropped under `prefers-reduced-motion`. **AND THE TRAP, HIT AGAIN:** a CSS comment written in the usual prose style, naming a property in backticks, TERMINATES the template literal that module CSS lives in - `tsc` reports `TS1005 ',' expected` at a line past the real one. Already in memory for `components/public/*-css.ts`; `components/admin/panel-css.ts` is the same edge and the memory now says so. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6742 passed, 429 skipped). No schema change and no new raw SQL in this half, so nothing further was owed.)

It now renders when `!collapsed || favouriteItems.length > 0`; the toolbar (filter box, **New...**, expand/collapse-all) stays expanded-only, the favourites are drawn as icons in the same order, and the **Favourites** heading is skipped when collapsed - a word with nowhere to sit - leaving the block's own bottom border to mark where the pinned shortcuts end.
