A drifted file's hash can never match again, so the four findings on live Deskwell (shop `043_returnable`, google-sheet-products `002_pull_job`, purchase-orders `004_supplier_catalogues`, unified-inbox `041_spam`) would have sat there for ever - and gone UNREAD again on any change of count, burying a real one among them. All four were verified harmless first: the three real column additions had already arrived via `047_order_item_return_note_catchup`, `003_deletion_plan`, `005_supplier_discount`+`006_catalogue_column_map` (columns confirmed present in `information_schema` on the live database, catch-ups confirmed in the ledger), and the unified-inbox one added COMMENTS ONLY. **FIX 1, automatic:** NEW `scripts/normalise-sql.mjs` strips line comments, block comments and whitespace runs while copying single-quoted and dollar-quoted literals through untouched (a `--` inside one is data). The ledger now records that second hash on every apply and BACK-FILLS it on the next deploy where the raw hash still matches, so a note reworded later is re-recorded silently and never alarms. Its own file, not a function in the runner, because importing the runner opens a database connection - NEW `scripts/normalise-sql.test.ts`, 8 pure cases including the `'-- not a comment'` literal, a doubled quote, a `$body$` block and unterminated comment/literal. **FIX 2, declared:** NEW OPTIONAL manifest key `migrationCatchups` (`lib/modules/manifest.ts`), mapping an edited migration to the file(s) that made it good, with sentinel `"none"` for an edit that changed no SQL - the only way to say so for a row recorded before `sqlChecksum` existed, which is exactly unified-inbox `041_spam`. **Judged PER INSTALL:** the drift only clears once the named catch-up is recorded applied on that site, and the alert otherwise NAMES what the site is waiting for instead of printing two hashes. **TRAP handled:** the catch-up decision is settled AFTER every module has run, not inside the loop - a catch-up applied by this very build reaches the ledger partway through it, and the file it makes good sorts earlier, so deciding in place would raise an alarm about something the same run had just fixed. Classifier run against the four real commit pairs agrees with the hand reading: three SQL-CHANGED, one comment-only. Wiki `Authoring-a-module.md` updated. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test`, and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` for real because the init migration changed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-09 (**The order-size deduction was unreachable on any listing with variations** - `shop` + `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, permission or env change - the column, the rule module, checkout and the storefront were all correct and shipped in shop v0.1.401; only the admin had no way in. **DEFECT, verified against live deskwell:** 1,410 of the 1,411 rows on offer with a `DD-` sale SKU carry an `order_size_deduction`, all stamped by the Deskwell pricing script. The one that does not is a hand-priced VARIATION (`EX000193` / `DD-MVE01`, Mave Medium Mesh Back, `catalogue_hidden`), and there was no box anywhere in the admin to put a figure in. **CAUSE 1:** `modules/shop/components/admin/product-editor/panels/pricing.tsx` returns early at `if (priceManaged)` - a variation-priced listing gets a cut-down Pricing tab, since its own price is never charged - and the Order-size deduction Section sat BELOW that return. On a shop built around variations that is every listing. The parent's own figure is not decorative: `ShopProductDetail.rsc.tsx` builds the pre-selection "Some options drop to ..." line from it. **FIX:** the section is built once into `deductionSection` and rendered by both branches; the managed branch swaps one sentence into the blurb naming the Variations tab. **CAUSE 2:** shop-variations' grid had no column for it at all, so the per-variation amount - the row checkout actually charges against, since `applyOrderSizeDeductions` reads `line.product.orderSizeDeduction` off the CHILD `shp_products` row - could only be set by CSV or by hand in the database. **FIX:** NEW **Deduction** column in `VariationsPanel.tsx`, between Sale SKU and Stock, shown only when `orderSizeDeductionEnabled`; `ProductVariationsSection.tsx` passes the flag from the shop config it already reads; `lib/variants-service.ts` gains the editor row field, the `loadChildRowsAndImages` SELECT, the upsert field, the change-detection line (through `curPrice`, since the two current-row sources differ in shape) and the write; `lib/db/variants.ts` adds it to the CSV importer's `ChildProductFields` map and its SELECT; `app/api/admin/variants/[id]/route.ts` adds the zod field and normalises 0 to null. Blank clears it and is never inherited from the listing. **No manifest bump needed** - shop-variations already requires shop >= 0.1.402 and the column landed in 0.1.401. **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** no `order_size_deduction` column on the shop-variations CSV - it would shift the saved Google-Sheet Pull mapping on a live site, and shop's own product CSV already carries the column. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, 254 tests pass, and both changed raw SELECTs RUN read-only against the live Deskwell schema, because no typecheck, lint, unit test or module build gate executes SQL. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-09 (**An attribute shows the values you have chosen, and the box beside them finds the rest** - `product-attributes-for-shop` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, permission or env change. **DEFECT:** the product editor drew EVERY value the shop has ever recorded for an attribute, ticked or not. On live deskwell that is 160 for Overall Width, 515 for Finish (spec), 548 for Colour, 586 for Product Weight and **5,179 for Catalogue Code** - the few actually ticked lost in the wall, and the Add box shoved off the bottom of the screen. **FIX:** `AddValueBox` became `AttributeValues`, which owns the tick list as well as the box because the two now have to know about each other. The list holds the CHOSEN values only; nothing chosen gives one line naming how many the shop already has. The box is a real combobox - up to `SUGGESTION_LIMIT` (8) existing values not already ticked, labels that START with what was typed first, then `localeCompare(..., {numeric:true})` so 9cm sorts above 90cm; ArrowDown opens it with nothing typed, arrows move, Enter picks, Escape shuts, click works (`onMouseDown` preventDefault, so blur does not shut the list under the pointer); `role=combobox` / `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls` / `aria-activedescendant` wired to a `useId` listbox. Picking NEVER creates. Typing a label the shop already holds and pressing Add ticks that one instead - the endpoint deduped via `reuseExisting` anyway, so this saves the round trip and reads correctly. A value unticked during the sitting is KEPT on screen (`kept` set), or the row would vanish under the cursor and read as having been deleted from the shop. Per-variant helpings (`useForVariations`) keep the plain create-only box with no suggestions - their values live on the variants. NEW `ValueSwatch` renders the picture-or-dot for both the ticks and the suggestions, so a colour attribute's suggestions show their colours. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, 93 module tests pass. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-09 (**A mailshot is one conversation per person, and editing a campaign no longer throws away what you just typed** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, permission or env change. **DEFECT 1, verified on live deskwell:** campaign "One" had `copy_to_sent` on, so its eleven Sent-folder copies were read back by the ordinary collection - and all eleven landed on ONE conversation (`cd99a105`), eleven outbound messages to eleven different companies. **CAUSE, and it is general rather than about campaigns:** `chooseThread`'s subject heuristic wants a shared participant, and `candidateThreads` collected the thread's participants from `from_address` ALONE. Every message this mailbox sends has `emma@deskwelloffice.co.uk` in that column, so any two outbound emails with the same subject inside the 30-day window shared a participant and merged. **FIX, two halves:** `lib/db.ts` `candidateThreads` now unions `from_address`, `unnest(to_addresses)` and `unnest(cc_addresses)` (LIMIT 100, blanks dropped) - the recipient is the whole of what tells one of these apart, and an unanswered outbound thread has nothing else on it; `lib/threading.ts` `chooseThread` takes NEW optional `ownAddresses` and matches on COUNTERPARTIES - participants minus our own addresses - falling back to the full participant list only when NEITHER side has a counterparty, which is exactly colleague mail and keeps 020's two-sided threading working. Omitting `ownAddresses` reproduces the old behaviour exactly, which is why the existing pure tests needed no edit. `lib/sync.ts` passes `ctx.ownAddresses`. 4 new cases in `lib/threading.test.ts`. **NEW npm script `test:inbox-campaign-threads`** -> NEW `modules/unified-inbox/lib/campaign-threads.live.test.ts` (`RUN_INBOX_CAMPAIGN_THREADS=1` + OVH creds; a skip is a FAIL), 3 tests against a real throwaway `cactus_rt_*` Postgres, because that participants query is raw SQL and no typecheck, lint, unit test or module build gate executes one. **LIVE DATA REPAIRED:** the eleven existing messages were split into eleven conversations on the live Deskwell database in one transaction (backup JSON, dry run with ROLLBACK, and an undo script in the session scratchpad) - new threads carry the recipient's own `person_id`/`organisation_id` where an identity exists and NULL where none does, so `runPeoplePass` resolves the rest on the next collection; `uin_processed_messages` rows were re-pointed by `message_id_header`, since one email is met in more than one folder. Nothing else on the site turned out to be falsely merged: the only other threads with 3+ distinct recipients are genuine multi-party PO and account conversations with inbound replies on them. **DEFECT 2:** `CampaignEditor` keyed its inner form on `detail.campaign.updatedAt`, so ANY reload remounted the whole thing and reset every box to the server's copy - topping the list up, pausing, stopping, Send now, Refresh, or a colleague saving from another tab. Half-written follow-ups went that way. **FIX:** the editor now holds `{detail, baseline, draft, moved}` in ONE state atom and the inner form is keyed on `campaign.id`. A reload with nothing unsaved adopts the server's copy as before; a reload with something unsaved KEEPS what was typed, moves the baseline underneath it and shows a line saying the campaign changed. NEW `stillSaveable()` snaps back only the fields the server would now refuse (the address, the labels, `excludeColleagues` and step 0's subject/body once a campaign has left draft), so a campaign that starts while somebody is typing does not leave a form that cannot be saved with the offending box greyed out. NEW `beforeunload` guard while dirty; NEW `onUnsavedChange` prop reported up to `CampaignsPanel`, which now routes every way OUT of an open campaign - another campaign, the do-not-email list, the phone Back button, and creating a new one (asked BEFORE the campaign is made) - through `mayLeave()`. **`CampaignWatch` gained a `stamp` dependency** (`campaign.updatedAt|tally.total|tally.done`) on its `load`: the remount was what refreshed the progress table after Top up, pause or resume, and without it the table would have sat there disagreeing with the counts directly above it. **Other edge cases closed in the same pass.** `WhatSection`: the merge-tag buttons were still live beside a READ-ONLY step 0, so one press added a tag nobody could see or remove and every later save was refused - now hidden when `firstLocked`; clearing "Days to wait" gave `Number('')` = 0, which zod and the column both refuse, so the whole page would not save because of a box somebody was retyping - empty now means the standing 3; **removing a chase no longer renumbers once a campaign has started** - everybody waiting is held against a step NUMBER, so pulling chase 1 out from under a running campaign renumbered chase 3 to 2 and the queue silently received the wrong email (gaps are harmless: `nextStepFor` looks for the next HIGHER index and anybody left on a step that has gone is finished rather than written to); Add a follow-up is now gated on the highest index rather than the count, so a gap does not offer a button that does nothing; `Math.max(...[])` replaced with a reduce. `draft.ts` `faultIn` now names the chase for an out-of-range wait and catches two steps numbered the same. **`app/api/admin/campaigns/[id]/route.ts`: everything refusable is now refused BEFORE anything is written** - the step guard ran after `updateCampaign`, so a save that changed the name AND a running campaign's first message came back "the message cannot be changed" with the new name quietly saved anyway; duplicate `stepIndex` is refused in words rather than hitting `uin_campaign_steps_order_key` as a 500. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `vitest run modules/unified-inbox` **1120 passed / 0 failed / 245 skipped** (the live suites), `npm run test:inbox-campaign-threads` **3 pass** against a real database. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-09 (**Call recordings reach the merged inbox, and words that arrive late reach it too** - `twilio` + `unified-inbox` + one CORE type field, uncommitted working tree. **THE VERIFIED DEFECT, read off live deskwell rather than guessed:** the thread for `+441254965150` holds `call:CA824f0f9d1d6908e36ff51cdde9a3f302`, "Incoming call, 3 min 58 sec", **0 rows in `uin_attachments`**. `listCallsForNumber` has always returned `recordingSids`, and `conversation-provider.ts` threw them away - `toMessages` attached audio for `kind === 'voicemail'` and nothing else. So a site with **Record calls** on had recordings nobody could reach from the conversation. `tw_forwarding_rules` on that number reads `record_calls t, voicemail_enabled t, transcribe_voicemail t, ring_timeout 15`. **PART ONE - the audio.** `Entry` gains `recordingSids: string[]` and `siteNumber`; `audioFor` replaces the voicemail-only branch and files `call-<RE…>.mp3` beside `voicemail-<RE…>.mp3` through the same permission-gated recording proxy. The site number comes from the loop variable, not from the call - it is what says which Twilio **Region** the recording lives in, and for click-to-dial the call's `to` is the other party. **`filterVoicemailSids` is used to REMOVE the voicemail's own recording from the call entry:** a call that rang out into voicemail carries that recording on the call SID too, and offering it under both is one piece of audio pretending to be two things. **PART TWO - the trap underneath, which is why a late transcript never arrived either.** `syncAllProviders` asks each channel for what has happened `since` the newest message it holds, and the twilio provider filtered on exactly that. A voicemail transcription lands MINUTES after the message, making the conversation no newer - so it was filtered out of the listing entirely, and `insertProviderMessage`'s `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` would have refused it anyway. Every voicemail collected inside the transcription window kept "Voicemail, 23 seconds" for good. **NEW OPTIONAL CORE FIELD `ConversationSummary.contentAt`** - when the conversation last CHANGED, as against when it last got a message; never earlier than `lastMessageAt`, absent on channels that never revise. **NEW `uin_threads.provider_content_at`** (`migrations/050_provider_content_at.sql`, init edited in place), read by `providerThreadState` **deliberately not followed through a merge** - it is the channel's own identity and that stays on the losing row - and written by NEW `markProviderContentRead` **only after the messages are actually in hand**, so a pass that ran out of time cannot leave a note claiming it read a revision it never fetched. `settled` gains the second comparison; NULL counts as not caught up, so every existing provider conversation is re-read once and then settles - which is also what back-fills the missing call recordings. `insertProviderMessage` still returns null for a message already held (nothing re-counted, no second webhook) but now refreshes its body through NEW `refreshProviderMessage` and tops up attachments through NEW `attachToMessage`, matched on `external_url` with `WHERE NOT EXISTS` rather than a unique index - adding one to a live table would have to survive whatever rows it already holds. **`REVISION_GRACE_MS` = 30 min replaces the 60s slack** on `since`: re-listing is nearly free because a conversation that comes back unchanged now fails `settled` and is never opened, and for this provider `list` and `thread` share one cached collection anyway. **NEW `tw_voicemails.updated_at`** (`migrations/013`) set by `recordTranscription`, which is what the provider reports as `contentAt`. **PART THREE - typing up a recorded CALL, per Chris's explicit choice of Voice Intelligence, opt-in.** `<Dial>` has no `transcribe` attribute and Twilio will not transcribe a two-party call any other way; Voice Intelligence works from the finished recording and **bills by the minute**, so `tw_forwarding_rules.transcribe_calls` defaults **false** everywhere (`migrations/014`, with `tw_settings.intelligence_service_sid` and NEW table `tw_call_transcripts`, keyed on the RECORDING and worded 'pending'|'completed'|'failed' exactly as `tw_voicemails` is). NEW `lib/intelligence.ts`: it is on **`intelligence.twilio.com`, not the 2010-04-01 host**, so `twilioFetch` cannot be reused - NEW `intelligenceHost()` puts the edge in FRONT (`intelligence.dublin.ie1.twilio.com`), because the Region rule still applies and asking us1 about a Dublin recording returns a flat "not found". The Service is **created by the module on first use** with `WebhookUrl` set at creation and **`AutoTranscribe: 'false'` - which is load-bearing:** true would have Twilio transcribe every recording on the account, including numbers whose owner never asked, and bill for all of them. NEW `transcriptionDialAttrs` adds `recordingStatusCallback` to BOTH dials (first ring and every follow-on), scoped to `recordCalls && transcribeCalls`, with `recordingStatusCallbackEvent="completed"` so Twilio does not also report the recording starting. NEW `app/api/webhooks/twilio/recording/route.ts` (signature-validated over the URL **including the `?number=` it needs** - a forwarded call's recording callback names the two ends of the DIALLED leg and neither need be ours) books the row in BEFORE asking, so a lost reply leaves something recoverable, and swallows its errors rather than 500ing Twilio into a retry loop. NEW `app/api/webhooks/twilio/intelligence/route.ts` **is not signature-validated and cannot be** - Voice Intelligence webhooks are configured on the Service and carry no `X-Twilio-Signature`. Nothing in the body is believed: it names a job, the job must already be one this site booked in, and `fetchTranscriptText` refuses a transcript whose `service_sid` is not ours, so a forged request can make the site re-read a transcript it already owns and nothing else. `lib/retention.ts` now sweeps `tw_call_transcripts` with the recordings - a retention period is somebody saying how long they will keep a conversation, and a written-out copy is the same conversation in a more readable form. **UI:** **Type up recorded calls** appears under **Record calls** only when recording is on, with the per-minute cost stated in bold. **BOTH MODULES' `requiresCoreVersion` RAISED to 0.5.1559** - they set and read `contentAt`, which does not exist on an older core, and the module build gate composes against core's newest RELEASE. **Core must be released before either module.** **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint modules/twilio modules/unified-inbox lib/conversations` **No issues found**, `vitest run modules/twilio modules/unified-inbox` **1285 passed** (new `lib/call-transcription.test.ts` (6), 5 new provider-sync cases on the revision path, 7 new twilio conversation-provider cases on call audio, the voicemail double-offer, `contentAt` and the transcript), full `npm test` **6434 passed / 417 skipped**, and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS against throwaway `cactus_rt_*` databases - required, three module migrations added columns and a table. NOT verified in a browser. **KNOWN LIMIT, said plainly:** the back-fill only reaches conversations inside the 30-minute grace window of the channel's watermark, so the call from today gets its recording on the next tick but a call from last week keeps its bare line.)

Last updated: 2026-09-09 (**A delivery receipt now finds the order confirmation it belongs to** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. **THE PROBLEM IT SOLVES:** a Brevo event was matched to a message on the `X-Mailin-custom` tag and on nothing else. Only mail this module SENT carries that tag; an order confirmation, a purchase order or an order-received notice is sent by the other module and merely COPIED here afterwards (`lib/outbound-record.ts`, the `core.outbound-email-record` point), by which time the email is with the customer and there is no header left to add. So `normaliseBrevoEvent` returned null for every event about one and the copy sat there for ever showing nothing - no delivery, no open, and no sign the customer had followed the link to their order. Verified on live deskwell 2026-09-09: every `uin_messages` row whose `idempotency_key` starts `mod-` had `delivered_at`, `opened_at`, `clicked_at` all NULL and zero rows in `uin_delivery_events`, while every compose-sent row alongside them had both. Brevo's own report showed the clicks. **WHAT CHANGED:** (1) `lib/receipts.ts` - `normaliseBrevoEvent` now returns `NormalisedBrevoEvent` (`{ messageId, providerMessageId, event }`, both ids nullable, at least one present) and reads Brevo's `message-id` / `messageId` / `message_id` through `cleanMessageId`, so it compares equal to the stored `provider_message_id`. Returns null only when neither identifier is present, or the kind is one this module does not file. (2) `lib/db.ts` - new `outboundIdsByProviderMessageId(ids)`, one `= ANY(...)` query for a whole webhook batch, scoped to `direction = 'out'` so our own copy of a reply sitting in Sent under the same Message-ID cannot answer. (3) `app/api/webhooks/brevo/route.ts` - normalises the batch first, looks the untagged ones up in one go (skipped entirely when `trackOpens` is off), then files. Campaign tags still take the tag path. **SCHEMA:** new module migration `modules/unified-inbox/migrations/051_provider_message_lookup.sql` - `uin_messages_outbound_provider_id_idx` on `provider_message_id` `WHERE direction = 'out' AND provider_message_id IS NOT NULL`. The only existing index on that column is UNIQUE `(thread_id, provider_message_id)` partial on inbound provider mail, which leads on the thread and cannot serve this lookup. No new column, so `lib/backup/schema-coverage.test.ts` is unaffected. **TESTS:** `lib/receipts.test.ts` covers both identifiers and the bracket stripping; `lib/click-tracking.live.test.ts` gains a case that runs the new query on a real throwaway Postgres with all 51 migrations applied - `npm run test:inbox-clicks`, 7 passed. **STILL TRUE:** *Followed a link* needs link tracking switched on in the Brevo account, and the nightly housekeeping reconcile is what adds `click` to a webhook registered before M047.)

Last updated: 2026-09-09 (**A released migration can no longer be edited, a payment that produces no order now shouts, and the survey looks at the working tree** - `core` + `shop`, uncommitted working tree. **THE PROBLEM IT SOLVES:** on 2026-09-08 shop's `043_returnable.sql` was released as v0.1.401, applied to the live Deskwell database nine minutes later, and then edited in place to add `shp_order_items.non_returnable_note`; the edit shipped in v0.1.402. `ModuleMigration` is keyed by NAME, so 043 never ran again, the column never arrived, and `insertOrderRows` - which writes that column onto every line - failed inside `materialiseDraftOrder`'s transaction. Checkout was dead from 20:54 that evening. The next order, DW000182 on 2026-09-09, took £193.20 through Square and produced no order row at all; the only detector in the system was the customer telephoning. The catch-up file `047` was written at 01:35 and left untracked for eleven hours. **WHAT CHANGED:** (1) `scripts/check-frozen-migrations.mjs` + `.d.mts` + `.test.ts` - compares every `modules/*/migrations/*.sql` against the same blob in that module's newest `v*` tag and fails on any difference; `001_initial.sql` is exempt because it is edited in place by design; runs in plain `npm test` and standalone; verified against the real blobs (`v0.1.401:043` 446a400e vs `c3b12b0:043` ba017eda). (2) `scripts/run-module-migrations.mjs` now READS the `checksum` column it has always written - an applied file whose bytes have changed is warned about and raises a deduped `schema-drift` notification; never fatal. (3) New `NotificationType` value `alert` (schema.prisma + init migration in place + `prisma/core-reconcile/036_notification_alert_type.sql`), wired into `NotificationBell.tsx` and `app/cactus-admin/notifications/page.tsx`. (4) shop: `migrations/048_stranded_payments.sql` + the same table in `001_initial.sql` + `shp_stranded_payments` in `teardown`; `lib/stranded-payments.ts` records a failure of the draft-to-order transaction with the customer and amount read back off the surviving draft, one row per draft with an `attempts` counter, cleared on any successful materialisation; `lib/checkout-draft.ts` splits the transaction into `createFromDraft`/`runDraftTransaction` so the failure path can record before re-throwing; `GET /api/m/shop/admin/orders?stats=1` returns `stranded`; `OrdersScreen.tsx` + `orders-screen-css.ts` show a warning-token banner naming order number, amount, method and customer. (5) `lib/stranded-payments.live.test.ts` against a real throwaway Postgres, added to `test:ledger-guards` - the raw SQL is otherwise executed by no gate at all. (6) `~/.claude/skills/commit-and-push/scripts/survey.sh` gained a WORKING-TREE HAZARDS section: the frozen-migration check, untracked files per repo, and dirty files touched in the last hour (a peer session released another session's half-written 043 thirty-four minutes before that session finished editing it). **WATCH OUT:** the frozen check needs each module checked out with its tags - a module with none is reported as skipped, never as clean.)

Last updated: 2026-09-09 (**A forwarding number can be rung more than once before the call goes to voicemail** - `twilio` only, uncommitted working tree. **THE PROBLEM IT SOLVES:** the ring timeout exists so a forwarded call is cut off before the receiving handset gives up and answers with its OWN mailbox - a message left there is lost to whoever holds that phone and never reaches the site. That worked, but a timeout short enough to beat a mobile's mailbox (often 15s or less) gives the person one brief chance to pick up. **ONE NEW MODULE MIGRATION.** `migrations/012_forward_attempts.sql`: `tw_forwarding_rules.forward_attempts INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, idempotent; `001_initial.sql` edited in place to match for fresh installs. Default 1 is exactly today's behaviour on every existing number, which is the whole reason it is the default. Bounds `MIN_FORWARD_ATTEMPTS = 1` / `MAX_FORWARD_ATTEMPTS = 5` live in `lib/business-hours.ts` beside the ring-timeout pair, because that is the file the client component may import. **THE COUNT LIVES ON THE ACTION URL, NOT IN THE PAYLOAD.** Every `<Dial>` action request Twilio makes is identical in its parameters - the existing `?leg=2` marker already exists for that reason - so a number rung three times would restart the count on each reply and redial the caller forever. NEW `dialActionUrl(leg, attempt)` in `lib/voicemail.ts` stamps both; **attempt 1 of leg 1 deliberately produces the bare webhook URL with no query at all**, so a call already in flight across a deploy comes back to something the planner still understands. Twilio signs the URL it is handed, so the markers cannot be forged. **THE PLANNER:** `planVoicemailRequest`'s `dial-second` action becomes `{ action: 'dial'; leg; attempt }`, and `ctx` gains `attemptsPerNumber`. Order is now: ring number one up to N times, then number two (if set) up to N times, then voicemail. `clampAttempts` floors and clamps a stored count into the documented range and reads a missing/NaN one as 1 - a row from before the column, or one edited by hand, must not be able to turn a phone call into an endless redial; `parseAttempt` reads an unreadable marker as the first try. **ROUTES:** the voice webhook's `needsDialAction` gains `rule.forwardAttempts > 1` (without it a retry-only rule with voicemail off would have got no action URL and never retried), and its dial action is now `dialActionUrl(1, 1)` - byte-identical to what it emitted before. The voicemail webhook's `secondDialTwiml` becomes `legDialTwiml(rule, called, leg, attempt)`, picking `forwardTo` or `forwardToSecond` off the leg, and hangs up rather than dialling an empty string if the rule lost its target mid-call. **UI, one deliberate move:** the **Ring for (seconds)** field has moved OUT of the Voicemail box and into Forwarding, next to the new **Ring each number this many times**. It had to: it was only rendered when voicemail was switched on, and retries with voicemail off would have left the setting doing the real work unreachable. The hint beside them reads the current values back in a sentence and says plainly what the short ring is for. **LINKED NUMBERS:** `forwardAttempts` is a behaviour field, so it comes from the leader like every other one - asserted in `lib/linked-numbers.test.ts`. **TESTS:** 8 new in `lib/voicemail.test.ts` covering the retry ladder on both legs, the cap, a nonsense stored count, an unreadable marker, stopping the moment somebody answers, and the URL shapes; the `dialActionUrl` block sits at top level with its own `beforeAll` because `getSiteUrl` throws without `SITE_URL`. `lib/blocked-calls.test.ts`'s voice-route mock needed the new export. **GATES:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `npx eslint modules/twilio` **No issues found**, `npx vitest run modules/twilio/lib` **155 passed**, `lib/backup/schema-coverage.test.ts` PASS, and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real **4 passed** against throwaway `cactus_rt_*` databases with the twilio schema among those built - required, a module migration added a column. NOT verified in a browser. Version deliberately not bumped: nothing is committed yet.)

Last updated: 2026-09-09 (**Four cuts at Vercel's Fluid Active CPU bill: cached module APIs, a second cache window for the long tail, a short copy at the host's own edge, and longer windows on offer** - core (schema + migration + reconcile) plus a one-line opt-in in `shop`, `shop-variations`, `reviews-for-shop` and `search`. **WHAT WAS ACTUALLY COSTING THE MONEY**, measured against live deskwell rather than guessed: page HTML *is* cached (`cf-cache-status: HIT`, `age: 3201` on the homepage), so the origin traffic left over was (a) the client islands every product page fires - `curl -sSI .../api/m/reviews-for-shop/public/reviews` returned `cache-control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, `cf-cache-status: MISS`, every single time, and only 2 of ~88 public module routes set any shared-cache header at all; (b) the crawl surface - `sitemap.xml` carries **19,679 URLs over 731 distinct paths**, 18,948 of them option-permutation addresses published on purpose by `shop-variations/lib/sitemap.ts`, each a separate cache entry and a separate full dynamic render when its window closes; (c) `vercelCdnCacheControl()` was a hardcoded `s-maxage=0, must-revalidate`, so Vercel's own edge held **nothing** and every miss at Cloudflare was a function. **NEW `lib/cache/module-api-cache.ts` + a generator change.** A public module route opts in with `export const publicCacheTtl = <seconds>`; `scripts/generate-module-router.mjs` now awaits the handler and passes the response through `applyModuleApiCache`, so the safety rules live in ONE place rather than in each module. Additive like the page cache: GET/HEAD only, 200 only, never over a `Cache-Control` the route set itself, never for a request carrying a session/member/module bypass cookie (`cacheBypassCookieNames()`, the same list the pages use - which is what keeps the shop's staff out-of-stock exemption out of a shared cache), and only when the owner's Speed switch is on. Caps a declaration at 3600s. Sets `CDN-Cache-Control` as well as `Cache-Control` because **Vercel rewrites `Cache-Control` on a module route too** - the live `shop/public/config` response arrives as a bare `cache-control: public` with its `s-maxage=15` stripped. Opted in: shop `products/[slug]/related` + `.../upsells` + `categories/[slug]` + `collections/[slug]` (300), shop-variations `by-slug/[slug]/variations` (**60**, it carries live price and stock), reviews-for-shop `public/reviews` (300 - its GET reads no cookie, the member session is a POST-time concern), search `public/search/sources` (900). **Deliberately NOT opted in:** `search/suggest` (reads both session and member), `product-discovery-tool/public/stats` (a POST), `shop/public/products` (broad query surface, admin-adjacent callers). **TWO NEW `SiteConfig` COLUMNS**, init SQL edited in place + `prisma/core-reconcile/035_page_cache_windows.sql`: `pageCacheLongTtl INTEGER DEFAULT 0` and `vercelEdgeTtl INTEGER DEFAULT 60`. **THE SECOND WINDOW** (`PAGE_CACHE_LONG_TTL_OPTIONS = [0, 3600, 21600, 86400, 604800]`, 0 = "no second window" = exactly the old behaviour) applies to any address carrying a query string and to the machine-read files (`/robots.txt`, `/sitemap*.xml`, `*/feed.xml`) - `usesLongCacheWindow`, `resolveCacheWindow`. Never SHORTER than the ordinary window: the longer of the two wins, so it cannot be used to slow a site down by accident. **`cdnCacheControlForWindow` is new and is not optional:** `cdnCacheControl` runs its argument through `normalisePageCacheTtl`, and 604800 is not a member of the ordinary option list, so reusing it would silently reset a week-long window to five minutes. **`vercelCdnCacheControl(ttl, window)`** now takes the setting and clamps it to the downstream window - a copy upstream that outlives the one downstream would put the stale page BACK into Cloudflare the moment Cloudflare's expired. Cost of the 60s default: a publish's purge takes up to a minute to be visible everywhere; 0 restores instant purges. `normaliseVercelEdgeTtl` deliberately does **not** use the plain `Number()` coercion the other two use - `Number(null)` is 0 and 0 is a meaningful option here, so a missing column would have read as a deliberate "keep nothing". **`PAGE_CACHE_TTL_OPTIONS` gains 21600 and 86400.** `getPageCacheCached` now returns `longTtl` and `vercelEdgeTtl`; `proxy.ts` passes `nextUrl.pathname` and whether there is a query string into the decision. Admin: three selects on Settings > General > Speed (`speed-page-cache-window` extended, new `speed-page-cache-long-window` and `speed-edge-window`), Zod on both new keys in `app/api/admin/config/route.ts`, and either one moving invalidates the config cache and purges the CDN like the window already did. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` 6404 pass / 411 skipped, new `lib/cache/module-api-cache.test.ts` (12) and 27 new cases in `lib/cache/page-cache.test.ts`. NOT verified in a browser. **NOTE FOR THE RELEASE:** the four modules carry an inert one-line export until core ships the dispatcher change, so `requiresCoreVersion` is deliberately NOT raised - an older core ignores the constant rather than breaking on it - but core must be released before the module releases mean anything.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**A second address provider: Google Places alongside Ideal Postcodes** - `address-lookup-for-shop` only (0.1.5 -> **0.1.6**), plus wiki. Core untouched. **DELIBERATELY NOT A SECOND MODULE:** shop resolves `shop.checkout-address-lookup` as `Object.values(entries)[0]` - first registered wins, in manifest order - so two modules both claiming that point would give an install whichever loaded first, silently, with no way to choose. One module, two providers, a radio button. **MIGRATION `migrations/002_google_provider.sql`** (new; mirrored into `001_initial.sql` in place, per the fresh-install rule): `alk_settings.provider TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ideal-postcodes'`, `google_api_key TEXT`, `google_region_codes TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'gb'`. A column per key rather than one shared `api_key`, so trying the other service and switching back does not throw the first key away. **THE SEAM:** new `lib/providers/` - `types.ts` (`AlkProviderClient`: `isValidId`, `autocomplete`, `resolve`), `ideal-postcodes.ts` (moved from `lib/ideal-postcodes.ts`), `google.ts`, `index.ts` (`resolveActiveProvider`). The routes no longer know which service answered. **`AlkSuggestion.id` IS NOW A STRING.** It was a number, and `resolve/route.ts` hard-rejected anything non-numeric because a UDPRN is an integer - a Google place id is opaque text and would have been 400ed at the door. Id validation moved onto the provider: Ideal Postcodes still insists on a round-tripping positive integer, Google on `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,512}$`, which is what keeps a `../` out of the request path. **GOOGLE, THE THREE THINGS THAT ARE NOT OBVIOUS:** (1) `includedPrimaryTypes` is NOT sent - Google accepts only Table A, Table B and its two type collections there, and none of the address types (`street_address`, `premise`, `subpremise`) is among them, so passing one is a 400; country codes do the narrowing instead. (2) The field mask decides the SKU - only `addressComponents` is asked for, which sits in Place Details **Essentials**; adding a Pro field to that mask would quietly move every lookup up a tier. (3) Session tokens: a run of keystrokes plus the one details call after it are billed as one session, so the browser mints a token (`lib/session-token.ts`, `crypto.randomUUID` with a plain-http fallback), sends it on both calls and tears it up after each pick. It is shopper-supplied and ends up in a request to Google, so it is checked against the UUID shape and **dropped rather than rejected** when it does not match - the lookup still works, it is just billed unsessioned. **ADDRESS MAPPING:** Google's components carry no notion of a PAF delivery line, so `mapGoogleAddress` rebuilds the split - unit (`subpremise`/`premise`) on line 1, street on line 2, exactly how PAF divides the same address; `postal_town` then `locality` for the town; `administrative_area_level_2` for the county with **no fallback to level 1**, which in the UK is the nation ('England') and would put a country on a delivery label. **ATTRIBUTION:** Google's terms require the 'Powered by Google' credit wherever its suggestions appear away from a Google map, so the autocomplete response carries `attribution` and the dropdown grows a strip below the listbox (outside the `<ul>`, so the ARIA listbox is not polluted with a non-option). That strip is `#fff` by hand and not a token: Google publishes one logo per background and this is the on-white one. `cspOrigins.img` gains `https://maps.gstatic.com` - core's generated policy allows `www.` and `ssl.gstatic.com` but not `maps.`, so the logo would have been blocked on a real install and the credit silently missing. One `eslint-disable-next-line @next/next/no-img-element` with its reason on the line: `next/image` would need that host in core's `remotePatterns`, which is module-specific config core may not carry. **ENV:** `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` joins `IDEAL_POSTCODES_KEY` as a per-provider fallback. **ADMIN API CHANGED SHAPE** (internal, this module's settings tab is the only caller): PATCH takes `provider` / `idealPostcodesKey` / `googleKey` / `googleRegionCodes`, and `AlkSettingsView` returns a key view per provider plus `ready` for the selected one. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean of this module (12 errors present in `modules/shop/lib/cancel-items.test.ts` are another session's in-flight work, not this change), `eslint modules/address-lookup-for-shop` clean, 34 tests pass (new `lib/providers/google.test.ts`, `lib/region-codes.test.ts`, `lib/session-token.test.ts`), `lib/backup/schema-coverage.test.ts` PASS. `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests) **but it EXCLUDED this module** - the gate skips any module declaring `requiresModules`, and prints so; the new columns are TEXT beside an `api_key TEXT` that has been backed up on the live site since August, so the coverage backstop is what actually stands behind them here. NOT verified in a browser. **SHARED-TREE NOTE:** another session ran `checkout-modules` mid-build and reverted every tracked file in this module repo; everything was re-applied and a snapshot is kept at `<scratchpad>/alk-snapshot/`. Wiki: `Address-Lookup.md` rewritten around the choice of service, `Configuration-reference.md` gains `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY`, `Shop.md` line 1129 no longer names Ideal Postcodes as the only option. **FOLLOW-UP, same working tree, 2026-09-09:** Chris updated the live install and Google autofill answered **502**. The route was swallowing the reason - every provider failure became one status code, and a wrong key, an API never switched on, a browser-restricted key and a project with no billing are four different fixes behind it. New `lib/providers/provider-error.ts` reads the refusing response's body (Google answers `{error:{status,message}}`, Ideal Postcodes `{code,message}`), and both routes now log the provider's own words with `console.error('[address-lookup] ...')`. **The detail stays server-side** - the public 502 is answering a shopper's browser. **THE ACTUAL FAULT WAS NOT THE CODE:** probing `places.googleapis.com` with the key read out of live `alk_settings` returned `403 PERMISSION_DENIED / API_KEY_SERVICE_BLOCKED` - the key carries an API restriction list that does not include Places API (New). Migration 002 had applied, `provider` was `google`, the key was a well-formed 39-character `AIza...`; nothing in the module was wrong. **NEW ADMIN ROUTE `app/api/admin/test/route.ts`** (POST, `shop.manage`) runs one real autocomplete for `'1 High Street'` through the active provider and hands the provider's message straight back, with a **Check it works** card and a **Test the lookup** button on the settings tab. Passing provider error text to the browser is safe HERE and nowhere else: the route is permission-gated and the reader is the person who pasted the key. A rejected key is otherwise indistinguishable from a working one on that screen, and only shows itself at a shopper's checkout. **THE TRAP WORTH REMEMBERING:** this module calls Google from the SERVER, so an HTTP-referrer restriction can never be satisfied - the key needs no application restriction or an IP one, and Places API (New) on its API list. Wiki `Address-Lookup.md` gains both, plus the test step. Version stays **0.1.6** - none of this shipped, so there is nothing to bump past. **CHECKS re-run:** typecheck clean of this module, `eslint modules/address-lookup-for-shop` clean, 34 tests pass. **SECOND FOLLOW-UP, once the key worked: the establishment trap.** Chris typed "22 blackwall basin moorings", picked the right suggestion, and line 1 came back "1 Myers Walk". Not a mapping bug as such - **Google keeps an establishment's own name OUT of `addressComponents` entirely**. Fetched live for that place: `types` are `marina, point_of_interest, establishment`, the components are bare `street_number 1` + `route Myers Walk`, and "Blackwall Basin Moorings" appears ONLY in `displayName` and in the prediction text. So the correct half of the address was being dropped and replaced by a street the shopper had never heard of. **NOT FIXED WITH `displayName`:** that field is **Place Details Pro**, and adding it to the mask would move every resolve call off Essentials - exactly what the mask comment warns against, halving the free allowance (Pro 5,000/month vs Essentials 10,000) and raising the per-1k. **FIXED FREE via `structuredFormat.mainText`** on the autocomplete response, which is the place's name split from the address around it. Verified on Google's pricing table that **Autocomplete (New) is a single Essentials SKU with no field-dependent tiering** (unlike Place Details), so widening that mask costs nothing. The name rides back to the server on resolve as `&name=` (capped 120) because the cheap details call will not return it: `AlkSuggestion.name?`, `AlkProviderClient.resolve(id, sessionToken, placeName)`, Ideal Postcodes ignores it since PAF already carries building names in its own lines. **`mapGoogleAddress` now builds line 1 from a deduped PARTS LIST** - subpremise, premise, then the picked name - joined with ", ", with the street falling to line 2 whenever any part exists. A name equal to the street is dropped, compared through a `normalise()` that flattens case, commas, full stops and doubled spaces, so Google's mainText for an ORDINARY address ("12 High Street") is not pasted in front of itself. **WHAT IT STILL CANNOT DO, said plainly in the wiki:** Google does not know the unit number within a named place, so "22" is the shopper's to type - PAF does know them, which is now the stated reason to prefer Ideal Postcodes for a UK-only shop. **VERIFIED AGAINST THE REAL RESPONSE**, not a fixture invented to pass: the live components for that place, fed through the shipped `mapGoogleAddress`, give `line1 'Blackwall Basin Moorings'`, `line2 '1 Myers Walk'`, `London`, `Greater London`, `E14 5GT`. **CHECKS re-run:** typecheck clean of this module, `eslint modules/address-lookup-for-shop` clean, **40 tests pass** (6 new on the name-versus-street rules).)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**Calling off part of an order: individual lines, individual quantities** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree. **NO schema, migration, route, permission or config change** - `shp_order_request_items` has held a line-and-quantity shape since `015_order_requests.sql`; a cancellation simply never wrote into it. **THE RULE THAT MOVED:** `canRequestCancel` was one all-or-nothing question about a whole order, so one packed parcel or one made-to-order desk refused the lot. `EligibilityInput.cancellable?: CancelLinePosition[]` (`productName`, `cancellableQty`, `outstandingQty`, `returnable`) makes it per line: allowed the moment ANY line can go, and the three refusals - "it is a return now", the named non-cancellable lines, "nothing left" - are only reached when NOTHING on the order can be called off. The field is optional and the old whole-order path is kept behind it, so a caller that has not worked the figures out gets the cautious answer rather than a silent yes; `lib/member-orders.ts` is the only real caller and always looks. **PER CHRIS, EXPLICITLY: a non-returnable line cannot be cancelled either** - goods a shop will not take back are cut, upholstered or ordered in specially the moment the order lands, so `cancellableQty` is 0 on `returnable = false` and `createOrderRequest` refuses it by name. **THE ARITHMETIC, all pure in `lib/order-requests.ts`:** `unrefundedCancelledUnits(cancelled, refunded) = max(cancelled - refunded, 0)` and `outstandingUnits(line) = max(quantity - refunded - dispatched - unrefundedCancelled, 0)`. The subtraction is the whole design: a refund ALREADY takes units off the dispatch list in its own right, so counting an approved-and-refunded cancellation again would shrink the order twice on the ordinary path - approve two of five and refund them, and three are still to be supplied, not one. It is also stable when the money lags the decision by days, which is what a bank-transfer shop does. Plus `cancellableQty(line, alreadyRequested)` and `coversWholeOrder(request, orderItems)` - a cancellation that names every line in full is still told back to the shop as "The whole order", because a list of nine products reads as homework. **THE DISPATCH GUARD, the part that stops this being a trap:** `lib/db/shipments.ts` gains a second aggregated LEFT JOIN over `shp_order_request_items` for `type = 'CANCEL' AND status = 'APPROVED'` (aggregated BEFORE the join, as the dispatch total already is, or each line fans out once per request), `ShpOrderItemDispatch.cancelledQty`, `outstandingQty` through the shared `outstandingUnits`, and `createShipment`'s cap now nets called-off units off `dispatchable` with its own sentence. Without it, a shop that approves on Monday and pays on Friday has nothing take the units off the dispatch screen and all five turn up on Tuesday. `isFullyDispatched` counts the same way. **STORAGE:** `linePositionQuery` splits `requested_qty` into RETURN-only plus `cancel_requested_qty` / `cancel_approved_qty` (a `FILTER (WHERE ...)` aggregate) - cancellations spend UNDISPATCHED units and returns spend DISPATCHED ones, so netting either off the other refuses the return of goods the customer is holding because they called off the part nobody had packed. `lib/member-orders.ts` splits its `spokenFor` tally the same way and only counts PENDING cancellations, since APPROVED ones are already off `outstandingQty`. `MemberOrderLine.cancellableQty`. **THE QUEUE'S "Your call" EXISTS** now tests the ABSENCE of item rows rather than `type = 'CANCEL'`: a part-cancellation names lines, and flagging it off a discretionary line the customer never mentioned tells an owner their hands are tied when they are not. **APPROVAL:** `refundLines` branches on `items.length === 0` rather than on the type, so a part-cancellation refunds exactly what was named; new `cancellationClosesOrder` means a whole-order cancel still closes the order outright while a partial leaves it open - five chairs less two is an order for three - decided off the dispatch summary read back AFTER the decision, so the same figures the dispatch screen uses settle it. **CUSTOMER SURFACE:** `OrderRequestPanel` gives CANCEL the picker RETURN already had, pre-filled to everything cancellable (calling the lot off is the common case, un-ticking is less work than ticking), lines that must stay listed underneath with the reason, and the button reading **Cancel part of this order** where some of it cannot go. The route stops discarding `items` on a CANCEL. **ALSO PER CHRIS:** the two "Nothing from this order has been dispatched yet…" sentences no longer appear on the order page. `RequestEligibility` gains `silent?: boolean` and the page drops those reasons - the endpoint still answers with them, but a customer looking at an order placed this morning can see nothing has shipped, and printing it turns the card into a list of things they cannot do. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint modules/shop` clean, full suite **6367 passed / 0 failed** (24 new in `lib/cancel-items.test.ts` covering the double-count, the lag, the per-line refusals and the whole-order wording), and `modules/shop/lib/db/order-requests.live.test.ts` a real PASS against a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database - **22 tests, 9 of them new**, covering the FILTER aggregate, the dispatch join, the rewritten EXISTS and the refusal to dispatch called-off units, because raw SQL is the one thing typecheck, eslint and the build gate have nothing whatever to say about. `order-access` and `refund-netting` live suites re-run green too. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md` - the cancellation rule reworded per line, a new paragraph on calling off part of an order, and the "approving a cancellation closes the order" line split into the whole-order and partial cases. Other sessions are live in this tree (`CartDeductionNote.tsx`, `detail-parts.tsx`, `delivery-slot.*`, `order-progress.ts`, `order-delivery.test.ts`); stage by file.)

No schema, migration, route or permission change. **THE DEFECT:** the previous entry's fix changed the PARCEL CARD and nothing else, and the sentence Chris was actually looking at is the rail's - a different string in a different file (`lib/order-progress.ts`), built from the booked day and window. Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**Where to leave it: the customer's own delivery instructions, carried through to the supplier's driver** - `shop` + `purchase-orders`, uncommitted working tree. **MIGRATION `modules/shop/migrations/046_delivery_instructions.sql`** (new; mirrored into `001_initial.sql` in place, per the fresh-install rule - it was missing there and was added during the release survey). `shp_orders.delivery_instructions TEXT`, nullable. **DELIBERATELY NOT PART OF `shipping_address`**: an instruction is about this delivery, not about the door, so a shopper who saves the address and orders again next month is not still waiting in on Tuesday - and keeping it off the address keeps it out of every entry in the address book. A column rather than a line in the notes because it has to TRAVEL: on a drop-ship the driver works for the SUPPLIER, and an instruction buried in free text nobody parses never reaches the lorry. **NEW `modules/shop/lib/delivery-instructions.ts`** - the wording and the ceiling, no database of its own: `deliveryInstructionsLabel(config)` (falls back to 'Delivery instructions'), `deliveryInstructionsOffered(config)` (its own function so every surface asks the same question and the answer has somewhere to grow), `DELIVERY_INSTRUCTIONS_MAX_LENGTH = 500` - generous for the genuinely awkward delivery and comfortably short of the 2000 the purchase order's own instructions field allows, so whatever is written always fits through onto the supplier's paperwork. **CONFIG**: `deliveryInstructionsEnabled` (default **false** - a shop whose goods go through a letterbox has no use for the box, and an empty box is one more thing between a shopper and the button), `deliveryInstructionsLabel`, `deliveryInstructionsHint`, with their own **Delivery instructions** section in Shop settings between *Their own order reference* and *Billing address*. **SURFACES**: `app/api/public/config/route.ts` publishes `{ enabled, label, hint, maxLength }` with no `required` flag, because an instruction is something a shopper either has or has not got; `CheckoutShippingClient.tsx` renders the box on the delivery step beside the address it belongs to, holding it in `checkout-state.ts` as `deliveryInstructions: string` so it survives a step back; the payment-intent route validates it with the same `DELIVERY_INSTRUCTIONS_MAX_LENGTH` (a `maxLength` attribute is a courtesy to the shopper, not a rule) and stores **null** when the setting is off, so a hand-rolled POST cannot smuggle one onto a shop that does not ask; `lib/db/orders.ts` writes it on insert and reads it back; `lib/types.ts` and `lib/order-payment-state.ts` carry it. **ADMIN**: the order detail route hands down `deliveryInstructionsLabel` the same way it already hands down `customerReferenceLabel`, and `OrderDetailScreen` prints the instruction under the delivery address in its own `.sox-instructions` box - a box rather than another line so nobody printing a label reads it as part of the address - `white-space: pre-wrap`, tokens only. **PURCHASE ORDERS**: `ShopOrderFacts.deliveryInstructions`, and `shipToFromShopOrder` fills `instructions` with it, trimmed and nothing more (a purchase order that improves on what somebody wrote about their own front door is a purchase order that gets it wrong). **THE CROSS-MODULE TRAP AND ITS FIX:** `readShopOrder` reads the value as ``to_jsonb(o) ->> 'delivery_instructions'`` rather than naming the column, because the two modules are pinned independently and a site can perfectly well run this Purchase Orders against a shop that has not had migration 046 yet. Named as a column the query throws, and the catch at the bottom of that function turns a throw into 'there is no such order' - so the whole panel would have gone down over a field nobody had filled in. `->>` on a missing key is NULL, so an older shop answers 'nothing said' and everything carries on. Only ever filled in as the order is DRAFTED, never written over afterwards: whatever a buyer types into the box wins from then on. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite 6327 passed / 0 failed, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests, `shop` inside the gate's module set) - re-run after the `001_initial.sql` mirror was added, since a gate that ran before the edit proves nothing about it - and `npm run test:ledger-guards` a real PASS (9 files, 91 tests, including shop's new `order-requests.live.test.ts`).  NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md` gains **Asking where to leave it**, `Purchase-Orders.md` a paragraph under **Deliver to**.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**Returns get a third answer, damage gets its own door with photographs, and a return can carry a collection charge** - `shop` + `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree, continues the returns-flag entries below. **MIGRATION `modules/shop/migrations/045_returns_discretion.sql`** (new; `001_initial.sql` edited in place, per the fresh-install rule). All idempotent. `shp_products.returns_discretionary BOOLEAN` **nullable on the same terms as `returnable`** - NULL is "nothing said", which on a variation child means "ask the listing". Deliberately a SECOND column rather than a third value on `returnable`: that flag is a public CSV column, a variations column, a checkout snapshot and a storefront pill, and turning it into text would have every one of those parse a word where they read a boolean, on a live catalogue. `shp_order_items.returns_discretionary BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, snapshotted at checkout beside the flag. `shp_order_requests.return_charge NUMERIC(10,2)` - NULL is "none recorded", not zero. **DAMAGE IS A THIRD REQUEST TYPE**, not a return reason: type check widened to `('CANCEL','RETURN','DAMAGE')`, `shp_order_requests_one_open_idx` re-created as `WHERE status='PENDING' AND type <> 'DAMAGE'` with a matching `..._one_open_damage_idx` for the other half (a damage report refunds nothing by itself, and a second parcel arriving broken must not wait for a return to be decided), and NEW `shp_order_request_photos` (`request_id` FK CASCADE, `media_id` TEXT no FK as `decided_by` is, `url` snapshotted so the queue needs no join into core). Added to `cactus.module.json` teardown. **`lib/returnable.ts`** gains `ReturnsPolicy` = ALLOWED | DISCRETIONARY | NONE, `returnsPolicy(returnable, discretionary)` (a refusal OUTRANKS a discretion, always - the other way round promises a return on goods the shop does not take back), `resolveDiscretionary` (child over parent), `DISCRETIONARY_DEFAULT_NOTE`, `discretionaryNote`, `returnsPolicyNote` (null on ALLOWED), `RETURNS_POLICY_LABEL`, `nonCancellableReason(names)`. One note column serves both answers, with a different stock sentence each. **FAULTY IS GONE FROM `SHP_RETURN_REASONS`** - `RETIRED_REASON_LABELS` keeps `reasonLabel` honest for requests already raised under the old list, and `isValidReason` refuses it, so nothing new can be. `DAMAGED_GOODS_GUIDANCE` sits above the return form with a **Report damage instead** button. NEW `SHP_DAMAGE_REASONS` (FAULTY means something different there), `MAX_DAMAGE_PHOTOS = 6`, `canReportDamage` (no window, no returnable check, needs only that something has been dispatched; blocked by an open DAMAGE only). **NON-RETURNABLE NOW BLOCKS CANCELLING TOO**: `EligibilityInput.nonCancellable?: string[]`, checked in `canRequestCancel` AFTER the dispatch check (once a parcel has gone "it is a return now" is the truer answer), naming the lines rather than refusing in the abstract. Goods a shop will not take back are cut, upholstered or ordered in specially the moment the order lands. **RETURN CHARGE**: NEW `lib/return-charge.ts` `netOffReturnCharge(lines, charge)` - spread PRO RATA by value, never parked on one line, because `processRefund` caps each line against the units it covers and writes a `shp_refund_items` row per line; rounding remainder onto the largest line so the lines always sum to the promised figure; a charge that swallows the refund is REFUSED rather than rounded to nothing. `decideRequest` writes it inside a `CASE WHEN "type" = 'RETURN' ... ::numeric` so no route can hang one on a cancellation or on damage. `approveOrderRequest` reads it back off the row, nets the refund, names it in the refund reason and in the email. **THE BUG THE LIVE SUITE FOUND, pre-existing:** `createOrderRequest`'s catch matched the unique-index NAME, and Prisma hands a raw-query violation over as "Raw query failed. Code: 23505. Message: Key (order_id)=(...) already exists." with the constraint name stripped - so a customer double-tapping the button got a 500, not the friendly 409. **FIX:** an explicit open-request-of-this-KIND check inside the advisory lock (authoritative there, unlike the index), with the catch kept as a backstop now matching `23505` + `(order_id)`. **SURFACES.** Product editor Stock tab: the `nonReturnable` switch becomes a three-way `returnsPolicy` select with the note revealed on either non-ALLOWED answer; ALLOWED stores NULL in BOTH columns (null is what a child reads as "ask the listing"). Variations grid: the Returns select gains **Only if we agree**, writing both columns every time so a row cannot hold a stale discretion under a fresh "no returns". Storefront staff pill (`detail-parts.tsx`, `VariantParts.AdminReturnsNote`) reads three ways. Admin queue renamed **Cancellations, returns & damage**: a **Your call** badge from an EXISTS across `shp_order_items` (all lines on a cancel, named lines otherwise), the photographs full-size behind a click, per-type decision labels (**Putting it right** / **Turn it down**), and a **Return charge** box on a return approval. Customer panel: three buttons, per-line discretion notes said BEFORE they ask, and a photo uploader that shrinks to 1600px/JPEG 0.82 in the browser (the limit is 4MB and a phone camera is not). **NEW ROUTE** `app/api/member/orders/[id]/photos` (POST, multipart, one file at a time) - `requireOrderAccess` so a guest with a postcode can use it, rasters ONLY (validateUpload accepts SVG; a photograph never is), `uploadMedia` into `shop/damage-reports`, `saveMediaRecord` decorative, rate limited 20/min. The report POST takes `photoMediaIds` and resolves the urls from the library itself - ids on trust, urls never. **CONFIG** `damageReportsEnabled` (default true) + its switch in Shop settings. **EMAILS**: four new templates (`shop.damage-received` / `-resolved` / `-declined`, `shop.admin-new-damage`), all transactional, plus `returnCharge`/`hasReturnCharge` on `shop.request-approved`; three new SMS bodies (all three stages, unlike the request pair, because a customer on texts-only has told us something arrived broken and silence is not an answer). **SPREADSHEETS**: `returns_discretionary` on the Products CSV (boolean, optional, new "Returns" group), and the Variations **Returns** column now round-trips `Yes` / `Discretion` / `No` / blank, writing both columns on import. **CHECKS:** `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean on everything this change touches, full suite 6313 passed / 0 failed (47 new: `return-charge.test.ts`, plus policy, reasons, cancel and damage rules in `returnable.test.ts`), `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests, 182s, `shop` inside the module set), and NEW `modules/shop/lib/db/order-requests.live.test.ts` - 13 tests against a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database, added to `test:ledger-guards`, covering the ::numeric CASE, the discretionary EXISTS, both partial indexes and the photo rows, because raw SQL is the one thing `tsc`, `eslint` and the build gate have nothing to say about. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md` gains the three answers, **When something arrives broken**, the cancel consequence and the return-charge paragraph.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**The returns answer on the product page, for staff only - plus the snapshot bug that would have lost every owner's wording** - `shop` + `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree, continues the returns-flag entry below. **THE BUG, found on review before it shipped:** the first cut read the reason back off the line's own product at order-render time, on the reasoning that the flag is a right (snapshot it) while the wording is only wording (show the latest). That is right for a plain product and WRONG for a listing with variations: an order line's `product_id` is the hidden CHILD row, and a child never carries `non_returnable_note` - the owner writes one on the LISTING, by design, because a reason per colour is nobody's idea of a good time. So every variation line would have been handed the stock sentence and the owner's own words quietly lost. **FIX:** `shp_order_items.non_returnable_note TEXT` added to `043_returnable.sql` (unreleased, edited in place) and to `001_initial.sql`; resolved at checkout and snapshotted beside the flag, `lib/member-orders.ts` reads the snapshot rather than the product. **THE SEAM RESHAPED:** `CartLineResolution.returnable?: boolean | null` becomes `returns?: { returnable: boolean; note: string | null } | null` - one concept, both halves, because the half that is easy to forget is the one that broke it. `shop-variations`' `getVariantReturnable` becomes `getVariantReturns`, and `variant-title-cache.ts` batches `parentReturnNoteByProduct` alongside the flag. `lib/checkout.ts` settles the note the line's-own-row-then-listing way, matching the flag's child-over-parent rule; `ResolvedCartLine.nonReturnableNote`, threaded through both order-creating routes and `lib/order-payment-state.ts`. **THE STAFF DISPLAY (what was asked for).** NEW `modules/shop/lib/admin-returns.ts` - `canSeeReturnsPolicy()`, third of its kind beside `admin-stock.ts` and `admin-codes.ts`, same `shop.products` + `allowAccess` bar, same React-cached session that costs an anonymous visitor nothing. `DetailPartContext.showAdminReturns`, resolved in `ShopProductDetail.rsc.tsx`'s existing `Promise.all`. **Plain products:** a `.spd-sku-staff` pill in shop's own SKU part - NOT a new Puck block, because a new block would have to be dragged into every existing Product layout before it appeared - reading `returnable`/`nonReturnableNote` straight off the context's product. Withheld when `_ctx.slot` is set, exactly as the Badges part withholds the parent's stock figure, so a claimed product does not print two answers. The part's early return now tolerates a product with no codes at all. `skuCss` gains `flex-wrap`/`max-width`: the returns pill carries a sentence where the code pills carry a word. **Products with options:** `VariantSelectorPayload` gains `showReturns` + `baseReturns { returnable, note }`, and each `VariantSelectorVariant` an already-resolved `returnable?: boolean` - all three WITHHELD from a shopper's payload rather than merely unrendered, the same treatment `stockCount`/`sku`/`saleSku` get, so there is nothing in the network tab. NEW `AdminReturnsNote` in `VariantParts.tsx`, rendered after `AdminStockNote`/`AdminSkuNote` at both sites (`VariantParts.tsx` and `DetailSlotPartsClient.tsx`). It speaks BEFORE a combination is settled, unlike the codes note, because the listing's answer is the answer for very nearly every row; the combination's own takes over once settled, and where the two differ it says "this option only" rather than appearing to change its mind. `undefined` on a variant is "not known" (an older cached payload) and falls through to the listing, never to a yes. Wording is said both ways round - "Returns: accepted" as well as "not accepted" - for the reason "Stock: not tracked" beats a blank. Tokens only, no hex. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite 6271 passed / 0 failed (3 new tests on the note's precedence), and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests, 186s). **Worth noting: `shop` is now INSIDE that gate's module set** - the `$$` skip in `readModuleSchemas` is gone, the harness builds `shop` and reports what it left out and why - so the entry below saying otherwise is stale and both new `shp_order_items` columns were genuinely dumped and restored byte-identical. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md` and `Shop-variations.md` gain the staff box, and the "what counts is how it was marked when the order was placed" line now covers the wording too. **PIN, caught in the release survey:** `shop-variations` now imports `canSeeReturnsPolicy` from the NEW `modules/shop/lib/admin-returns.ts`, plus `returnsPolicy`, `resolveDiscretionary`, `nonReturnableNote` and `RETURNS_POLICY_LABEL` newly exported from `shop/lib/returnable.ts`, so its `requiresModules` shop `minVersion` goes **0.1.401 -> 0.1.402**. Nothing local catches this - the working tree has both modules side by side and every check passes - and an install that took the new shop-variations against the old shop would fail its build on a file that is not there. Release order: shop, then shop-variations and reviews-for-shop, then core pinning all three.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**The proof of delivery an owner loses by completing the order himself, and a parcel that still says "arranged" after it has been** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree. **WHAT WENT WRONG ON THE FIRST REAL ONE:** DW000172 was delivered at 14:23, marked COMPLETED by hand, and its signature was never taken - because `listShipmentsForTrackingPoll` excludes parcels on COMPLETED/CANCELLED/REFUNDED orders, so the moment the owner closed the order the parcel left the queue for ever. That is the normal thing to do when the van has just been, which makes it the normal way to lose the proof. **FIX:** the poll query gains a second branch - a COMPLETED order's parcel stays in the queue while `signature_url IS NULL` and `shipped_at` is within `SIGNATURE_CATCHUP_DAYS` (14). It settles itself: the run that captures the signature makes the row fail both branches. Cancelled and refunded orders are in neither. Re-applying COMPLETED to an already-complete order is already a no-op (`applyOrderStatusChange` hangs every side effect off `changed`), so the widened set cannot re-send a completion email. **MIGRATION `042_signature_catchup_index.sql`** (mirrored into `001_initial.sql`): partial index on `tracking_checked_at WHERE tracking_url IS NOT NULL AND signature_url IS NULL`. **THE SQL WAS RUN, not just compiled** - `PREPARE`d and `EXECUTE`d against the live database read-only, and the catch-up branch proven to match the intended row with its signature test inverted. `int * INTERVAL '1 day'` is valid; nothing but SELECTs were issued. **ORDER PAGE:** the parcels card now reads "Delivered on 8th of September 2026" in place of "Arranged for today between 10am and 1pm" once the parcel has arrived. Printed ONLY off a real courier timestamp (`signedAt ?? deliveredAt`) - `arrived` alone can mean nothing more than the booked window having elapsed, and a clock passing 1pm is not grounds for telling somebody their furniture came. New `formatDeliveredDay` in `lib/order-display.ts` ("8th of September 2026" - keeps `formatDeliveryDay`'s spoken voice, drops the weekday, adds the year, since afterwards it is a record); new `lib/order-display.test.ts`, 5 tests including the ordinals and the timezone edge (23:30 UTC on the 8th is the 9th in London). **LIVE DATA (asked for this turn, backed up first):** DW000172's parcel `28cae3a5` had its signature pulled in by hand through the module's own code - `signed_by` Megan, `signed_at` 2026-09-08 13:23Z, `signature_url` `https://media.deskwell.co.uk/media/vKXOqTh3ZqmxyEnHtlMjO-delivery-signature-dw000172.jpeg` (65,740 bytes, serves 200 image/jpeg) - and its `tracking_stage` rewritten from the old page-dump to `Complete` with `delivered_at` 13:23Z. Row backed up to the scratchpad first. Note for anyone doing this again: the app reads `B2_APPLICATION_KEY_ID`/`B2_APPLICATION_KEY`, the Deskwell `.env` stores the same pair as `B2_KEY_ID`/`B2_KEY`, and `CLOUDFLARE_WORKER_URL` is not in that file at all (it is `https://media.deskwell.co.uk`, read off existing `Media` rows). Their S3 serves the signature with the FILENAME in the content-type header, which is exactly why the type is sniffed from the bytes. **CHECKS:** typecheck clean, eslint clean, full suite 6266 passed, backup round-trip a real PASS (4 tests, 186s) - and this time `shop` was genuinely in the gate's module set, core's newer gate having stopped skipping it. NOT verified in a browser.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**A customer can now review everything on a finished order from the order page itself** - `shop` + `reviews-for-shop`, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, permission or core change; one new core-adjacent seam living in shop, one new module route. **NEW EXTENSION POINT `shop.member-order-panels`, `modules/shop/lib/member-order-panels.ts`** - the public cousin of the admin screen's `shop.order-detail-panels`, and deliberately NOT the same point: one hands a member of staff an order to act on, the other hands the customer their own, and sharing it would have meant every panel checking which side of the shop it had landed on. Shape copied from `shop.product-detail-tabs`: `title`/`titleFor`/`order` on the PROVIDER object (the manifest's `label` is stripped at install time and only restored on the next deploy), `load(context)` returning null for "nothing to say", every provider resolved rather than only the first, a throwing `load` dropped and logged, host supplies the card. `ShopMemberOrderContext` carries orderId/orderNumber/status/paymentStatus/customerEmail/customerName plus `viewerEmail`/`viewerName`/`signedIn` - **shop settles who is looking before any provider runs**, because a contributor working it out for itself would be re-implementing `lib/order-viewer.ts`, the single rule deciding whether anybody may see the order at all. `productIds` are the order's own ids, i.e. the catalogue-hidden child rows on a variations shop; a provider that cares resolves them through `shop.product-page-resolver`. **HOST:** `app/public/shop/account/orders/[id]/page.tsx` resolves once and renders each panel inside shop's own `OrderCard`, between the receipt and the paired reference cards. **`reviews-for-shop`** registers `lib/order-panel-provider.ts` on it. **Only on `status === 'COMPLETED'`** - not "paid", which says nothing about whether the thing arrived, and not "shipped", which says it has left rather than landed; the nightly invitation run's own SHIPPED-or-COMPLETED rule is untouched and unshared. Returns null on a members-only shop being read by a guest, and on an order whose every product this address has already reviewed. **NEW `lib/order-reviewables.ts`** is the shared half the card and the route must never disagree on: order lines -> `resolveReviewableProduct` (parents, not the hidden variant children) -> deduplicated by page -> `findAlreadyReviewed`. **NEW ROUTE `POST /api/m/reviews-for-shop/public/order-reviews`**, and a route of its own rather than a flag on the product-page one because the identity works the opposite way round: it accepts **no name and no email at all**, loads the order, calls shop's `resolveOrderViewer` again (the page having rendered the form is not evidence of anything - a browser can post here directly), and takes the author off the member's account or, for a guest, off the order. `verifiedPurchase: true` by construction. Rate limited 30/hour per IP against the product-page form's 5, since one order is many submissions. **THE FORM ASKS FOR AS LITTLE AS IT CAN:** `components/public/OrderReviewForm.tsx` shows five stars and nothing else until one is picked - the headline and comment box appear only once there is a rating to explain, so a five-item order opens as five rows of stars rather than a page of homework. `useId()` per form, because an order page carries one per product and a shared id would point every label at the first product's boxes and leave the radio groups fighting over one `name`. **THE TRAP, caught by `lib/modules/import-cycles.test.ts` in plain `npm test`:** the generated public registry statically imports every provider, so `order-panel-provider` -> `order-reviewables` -> `reviewable-product` -> shop's `product-page-resolver` -> the registry is a cycle. `tsc` and `eslint` are both perfectly happy with it and Turbopack fails the production build with "Cannot access 'x' before initialization". `orderReviewables` is therefore reached through `await import(...)` inside `load`, the same fix `lib/detail-tabs.ts` carries. **`reviews-for-shop` `requiresModules` shop minVersion 0.1.245 -> 0.1.402**, because it now imports a file that arrives with that shop release - release shop first, then reviews, then core pinning both. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite 6261 passed / 0 failed. No schema change, so the backup round-trip gate is not in scope. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Reviews.md` gains **Reviewing from the order itself**, `Shop.md` a paragraph under what a signed-in customer sees, `Authoring-a-module.md` **A whole card on a customer's own order page**.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**shop-variations asked for an older shop than it actually needs, and its build gate caught it** - `shop-variations` v0.1.175, released. No schema, migration, route, permission or core change; one line of `cactus.module.json`. **WHAT HAPPENED:** v0.1.174 added the Returns column, which imports `@/modules/shop/lib/returnable` - a file that arrives with `shop` v0.1.401 - while `requiresModules` still asked only for `shop` >= 0.1.396. The type seam is optional in both directions, which is what made the entry below say the two could ship in either order; the IMPORT is not optional, and that is the half that decides whether a build resolves. **THE GATE FOUND IT THE HONEST WAY:** composed against core's newest RELEASE (v0.5.1553), whose pins carry `shop` v0.1.400, the tag build failed with `Module not found: Can't resolve '@/modules/shop/lib/returnable'`. Nothing local could have: this tree has shop v0.1.401 in it. **THE FIX:** `requiresModules` minVersion 0.1.396 -> 0.1.401, which `lib/modules/dependencies.ts` enforces (`compareVersions(version, dep.minVersion) < 0` is refused) and `lib/modules/pin-check.ts` reports, so an install taking shop-variations ahead of shop is told to update shop first rather than failing its build. **THE ORDERING TRAP THAT FOLLOWS:** v0.1.175's own gate cannot go green until core is released pinning shop v0.1.401, because the gate composes against core's newest release. Sequence was: release shop, release shop-variations, release core carrying both pins, re-dispatch the gate.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**Order-size deduction: an amount inside the shelf price that stops being charged once the basket is big enough** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree. Recorded here at release time because the chat that built it did not write an entry. **MIGRATION `modules/shop/migrations/042_order_size_deduction.sql`** (new; `001_initial.sql` edited in place for the same four columns, per the fresh-install rule).
