All idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`: `shp_products.order_size_deduction NUMERIC(10,2)` (per UNIT, in stored price terms; NULL is "this line carries none", which is not a recorded 0), `shp_suppliers.order_size_deduction_threshold NUMERIC(10,2)` (how much of THAT supplier's goods the basket must hold; NULL means the supplier has no rule and nothing of theirs ever deducts), `shp_suppliers.order_size_deduction_note TEXT` (the owner's own "why", per shop, which is why it is a column and not copy in the module), `shp_order_items.order_size_deduction NUMERIC(10,2)` snapshotted at order creation so an order can still say what came off it after the catalogue moved on. Suppliers join to products BY NAME (007), so no id is needed. **IT IS NOT DELIVERY AND MUST NEVER BE CALLED CARRIAGE** - delivery is a service the shop sells and prices; this is money already inside the goods price that stops being charged. The two sit on the same product page and were confused once during specification, which is why the column is named after what it is rather than what caused it. **NEW `lib/order-size-deduction.ts`** (pure, dependency-free bar `formatMoney`, so the storefront islands can import it without dragging the server data layer into the browser bundle - the same arrangement as `lib/min-order.ts`): `OrderSizeDeductionRule`, `OrderSizeDeductionLine`, `OrderSizeDeductionState`. Qualification is judged PRE-deduction; a line's named charges are SUBTRACTED from the qualifying subtotal, because a delivery service priced inside a line is not goods and a basket that only clears the threshold on it never reached it; a line currently on offer never deducts, so a stale amount left on a product whose sale has ended cannot take money off a full price; a line with no supplier never qualifies and never deducts. **NEW `lib/order-size-deduction-view.ts`** composes the finished sentence, used by both the server render and the public estimate route so the wording cannot drift. **NEW `components/public/OrderSizeDeductionClient.tsx`** asks for the settled combination's line once the shopper picks one, because the page was rendered against the LISTING and on a shop selling variations the listing's own row is very nearly never the thing being bought. **NEW ROUTE `GET/POST /api/m/shop/public/order-size-deduction`** returns the finished sentence and not the figures behind it - no arithmetic about the shop's prices is handed to a browser - with tax display applied server-side. **NEW ROUTE `GET /api/m/shop/admin/reports/order-size-deduction`** (`shop.products`) drives two catalogue checks in Shop > Reports: amounts that can never apply, and products missing one. Neither is a defect a build could see - a catalogue is filled by imports and scripts. **CONFIG:** `orderSizeDeductionEnabled` (default FALSE, so an install taking this update gets four unused columns and no behaviour change) and `orderSizeDeductionShowInBasket` (default true). **SURFACES:** product editor Pricing panel, Suppliers screen and its schema (`orderSizeDeductionThreshold`, `orderSizeDeductionNote`), Reports screen, the cart in all four of its shapes (`CartChrome`, `CartDrawerClient`, `CartFullClient`, `CartPageClient`), the product detail parts, and the checkout snapshot. Tests: `lib/order-size-deduction.test.ts`, `lib/order-size-deduction-checkout.test.ts`. NOT verified in a browser.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**A CDN purge that Cloudflare refused was reported as a success, and a site behind Cloudflare could keep an unpurgeable copy of every page** - core only, uncommitted working tree. Recorded here at release time because the chat that built it did not write an entry. No schema, migration, route or permission change. **DEFECT ONE, `lib/cache/cdn-purge.ts`:** Cloudflare answers a purge it will not perform with **HTTP 200** and a body saying `{"success": false, "errors": [...]}` - a token missing Zone.Cache Purge and a zone id the token cannot see both arrive that way. Reading only `res.ok` therefore counted a purge that never happened as a success, which is the worst of the three outcomes: the owner presses the button, is told it worked, and hunts for their change everywhere except the cache still serving the old one. New `readPurgeOutcome` parses the body with Zod (`CloudflareReplySchema`) and quotes Cloudflare's own errors - they carry the useful part, the permission it wants or the zone it cannot find - trimmed to 300 characters because this reaches a warning line and, on the manual button, an owner's screen. Both callers use it: the silent `purgeCdnPaths` warns properly, `requestPurgeEverything` throws with the real reason. `lib/cache/cdn-purge.test.ts` is new. **DEFECT TWO, `proxy.ts` + `lib/config/site.ts`:** the `Vercel-CDN-Cache-Control` header that stops Vercel keeping its own copy was gated on `isCdnPurgeConfigured()`, which reads the purge TOKEN - a secret. A runtime that cannot read it reported "no CDN configured" and quietly let Vercel keep a copy nothing can purge; the admin route that fires the purge CAN read the token, so such a site purged Cloudflare successfully, refilled it from Vercel's stale copy, and reported a purge that changed nothing. The gate is now the owner's own `behindCloudflare` database flag, which every runtime can see, OR the credentials, for a site that set them without ticking the box. `getPageCacheCached` returns `behindCloudflare` alongside `enabled`/`ttl` because the proxy needs it in the same breath on the same request; the cache reset in `invalidateSiteConfigCache` follows.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**The backup round-trip gate had been quietly skipping shop - the module holding most of a site's rows** - core only, uncommitted working tree. Recorded here at release time because the chat that built it did not write an entry. No schema, migration, route or permission change. **WHAT WAS WRONG:** `lib/backup/roundtrip.test.ts` built each module's schema by reading its migrations through `splitSqlStatements`, the BACKUP FORMAT's own splitter. That splitter is correctly not dollar-quote aware, because the format it parses never emits `$$`. Our migrations do - `shop` alone has six `$$`-quoted DO blocks - so feeding them to it chopped the blocks in half, and the rule "skip any module whose SQL contains `$$`" was written to keep the run from breaking. The result was a gate that reported a clean pass while never having been shown the tables most of a site's data lives in. **THE FIX, NEW `lib/backup/migration-sql.ts`:** a SECOND splitter, `splitMigrationStatements`, used only for reading migrations off disk in tests. Two parsers rather than one widened one is the point - teaching the restore splitter migration syntax would be a change to the code path a real restore executes, for a test's benefit. It handles `--` line comments, `/* */` blocks, single-quoted strings and `$tag$ ... $tag$` bodies of any tag; a bare `;` outside all of those ends a statement. Not a SQL parser and not trying to be, but exercised against every migration in the repository by the two suites below, so a mis-split fails loudly against a real Postgres rather than half-building a schema. `roundtrip.test.ts` keeps `splitSqlStatements` for the CORE init migration, which IS written in the constrained SQL the format emits - so that path still exercises the real restore code. One exclusion remains, `requiresModules` - which still keeps `shop-variations` and every other shop add-on out, for the different and honest reason that their migrations expect another module's tables - and EVERY exclusion is now printed whether it was decided while reading or while applying: a gate that narrows itself in silence reports a pass it has not earned, which is exactly what this one did for as long as the dollar-quote rule existed. `lib/backup/shop-sql.test.ts` follows suit.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**Delivery options for a chair's colour were painted over by the answer about the whole chair** - `advanced-shipping-for-shop` only, uncommitted working tree. Recorded here at release time because the chat that built it did not write an entry. No schema, migration, route, permission or core change. **THE DEFECT, one file, `components/public/DeliveryServicePicker.tsx`:** a page whose options are settled from the URL asks the estimate route twice in quick succession - about the LISTING on mount, because the variation islands have not published their selection yet, and about the settled VARIATION the moment they do. The listing's answer resolves every variation of it and is by far the slower of the two, so it landed SECOND and painted all eight of a chair's delivery services over the three the chosen colour actually carries. Not cosmetic: it offers a shopper services their combination cannot have. **THE FIX:** a `requestSeq` ref - every ask takes a number, and an answer whose number is no longer the newest is dropped.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**Postcodes a shipping zone does not cover** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree. Zones could say what they DID cover and nothing else, as a list of prefixes. A shop delivering to mainland Britain has one catch-all zone and needs the Highlands, the islands, Northern Ireland and the Crown dependencies carved back out of it, and there is no inclusion list that says that - only a list of what to take out again. **MIGRATION `modules/shop/migrations/044_zone_excluded_postcodes.sql`** (new; `001_initial.sql` edited in place for the same column, per the fresh-install rule). One idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`: `shp_shipping_zones.excluded_postcodes JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'`, JSONB to match `postcodes` on the same table so both lists serialise and restore by the identical path. **RANGES, NOT JUST PREFIXES - NEW `modules/shop/lib/postcode-patterns.ts`** (pure, dependency-free, imported by both the server and the admin client). The real exclusion list is written as district ranges - `AB30-AB32`, `BT1-BT94`, `PH10-PH26`, `PO30-PO41` - and **no prefix spelling of any of them is correct**: `AB3` reaches AB33-AB39; `PH1` swallows Perth, which is delivered to; `PO3` swallows Portsmouth while PO30-PO41 is the Isle of Wight. A range compares the district as a NUMBER, which is the only reading that gets those right. Exports `normalisePostcode`, `outwardCodeOf`, `parsePostcodePattern`, `isUnderstoodPostcodePattern`, `matchPostcodePattern`, `bestPostcodeMatch`, `matchesAnyPostcodePattern`; both lists on a zone take both shapes, and anything that is not a well-formed range parses as a prefix, so every list already saved keeps meaning what it meant. **THE TRAP THAT DECIDES THE WHOLE FILE:** the district cannot be read off the raw normalised string. `GY1 0AA` normalises to `GY10AA` and a greedy digit read gives district 10; `PH1 0AA` gives 10 and lands inside PH10-PH26. So `outwardCodeOf` tests for a complete postcode (`^[A-Z]{1,2}[0-9][A-Z0-9]?[0-9][A-Z]{2}$`) and strips the three-character inward code BEFORE the district is parsed; a partial postcode - an outward code alone, which is all the cart has before the checkout asks for the rest - is already the outward code and is left alone. `lib/postcode-patterns.test.ts`, 26 tests, built round the real 21-line Highlands list, with `PO3 5JT` in and `PO31 8QU` out pinned by name. **ZONE RESOLUTION - `lib/db/tax-shipping.ts`.** `findShippingZoneForPostcode` is now a thin wrapper over **`resolveShippingZoneForPostcode`** returning `ShippingZoneResolution = { zone, excluded }`, over the pure **`decideShippingZone(zones, postcode)`** (exported for test, takes anything shaped `ZoneLists`). A zone's excluded list is checked FIRST and takes that zone out of the running outright, **catch-all included** - an exclusion that lost to the catch-all would carve nothing, which is the entire point. Otherwise most-specific inclusion wins, then catch-all. `excluded` is true **only when `zone === null` AND some zone excluded them**, which is what separates "carved out" from "shop has no zones set up yet"; a new install must never show a shopper a refusal. `lib/db/shipping-zone-choice.test.ts`, 11 tests, including the arrangement where a dearer "Highlands and islands" zone picks up what the mainland zone excluded - that is NOT a refusal. **THE CHECKOUT GATE.** `app/api/public/checkout/payment-intent/route.ts` (where the rule is kept) and `app/api/public/checkout/session/route.ts` (which warns the delivery step early, before anyone reaches for a card) both return 400 with the shop's wording when `excluded`. Without it an excluded order went through with no delivery option and **nothing charged to carry the goods**, which is worse than refusing it. `app/api/admin/orders/route.ts` is deliberately NOT gated - an order typed in by hand is the exception the owner has already agreed to. **CONFIG + WORDING.** `lib/config.ts` gains `excludedPostcodeMessage` (default `''`; JSON blob on `shp_settings`, no migration). **NEW `lib/excluded-postcode.ts`**: `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_POSTCODE_MESSAGE` and `excludedPostcodeMessage(configured)`, so a cleared box gives a sentence rather than an empty refusal. Edited on Settings -> Shop -> Checkout under the min/max order values. **ADMIN.** `components/admin/TaxShippingScreen.tsx`: the zone drawer is three columns - name, **Postcodes covered**, **Postcodes excluded** - and a warning lists any line in either box that parses as neither a postcode nor a range (a cross-area "range" like `AB30-BT32`), because a rule that quietly matches nobody is only found when an order turns up from somewhere the shop does not go. `zoneSummary` counts the exclusions. `POST`/`PUT /api/m/shop/admin/shipping-zones` take `excludedPostcodes`; `createShippingZone` gains a defaulted third argument, `updateShippingZone` a third optional field, `ShpShippingZone` the `excludedPostcodes` property. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint modules/shop` clean, shop + backup suites 1178 passed / 0 failed. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki `Shop.md` gains **Postcodes you don't deliver to** and rewrites the zone-matching paragraph for ranges.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**Things you cannot send back: a returns flag per product and per variation** - `shop` + `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree. Some things simply cannot come back - a desk cut to a customer's measurements, a chair in a fabric picked off a card - and the returns machinery from `015_order_requests.sql` had no way to say so, offering a "Return something" button on every order. **MIGRATION `modules/shop/migrations/043_returnable.sql`** (new; `001_initial.sql` edited in place for the same three columns, per the fresh-install rule). All idempotent: `shp_products.returnable BOOLEAN` **nullable on purpose** - NULL is "nothing said", NOT "no", which is what lets a `shop-variations` child fall back to its listing exactly as `min_order_quantity` does (021); a `NOT NULL DEFAULT true` would have every child row shouting over a bespoke parent. `shp_products.non_returnable_note TEXT` (the owner's own wording, parent-only - a reason per colour is nobody's idea of a good time). `shp_order_items.returnable BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true`, **snapshotted at order creation** like `order_size_deduction` (042): the product can be deleted (`product_id` is SET NULL), and an owner marking a product non-returnable must not reach back and strip the right from orders already placed under the old terms. **NEW `modules/shop/lib/returnable.ts`** (dependency-free, like `lib/min-order.ts`): `isReturnable` (null -> true), `resolveReturnable(child, parent)` (child wins in BOTH directions), `NON_RETURNABLE_DEFAULT_NOTE`, `nonReturnableNote`, `NOTHING_RETURNABLE_REASON`. `lib/returnable.test.ts`, 10 tests. **THE VARIATION SEAM.** A variation child's own `returnable` is very nearly always NULL and only `shop-variations` can see the listing, so `CartLineResolution` gains an optional `returnable?: boolean | null` beside `minOrder` - the same shape of problem, the same fix. `shop-variations/lib/variant-title-cache.ts` gains `parentReturnableByProduct` in its request batch plus `getVariantReturnable`, and `lib/line-resolver.ts` returns it on both paths. Optional throughout, so core and the module can ship in either order. `lib/checkout.ts` settles it once per line - `isReturnable(product.returnable) && metaResolution.returnable !== false`, the STRICT way round, either source saying no is a no - onto `ResolvedCartLine.returnable`, snapshotted by both order-creating routes (`public/checkout/payment-intent`, `admin/orders`) and by `lib/order-payment-state.ts`'s prospective items. **ENFORCEMENT, three layers.** `lib/order-requests.ts`: `EligibilityInput.anyReturnable?: boolean`, checked in `canRequestReturn` BEFORE the window so an all-bespoke order is told the honest reason rather than a deadline that was never going to help it; optional, so a caller that did not look gets today's answer and not a silent refusal. `lib/member-orders.ts`: `MemberOrderLine` gains `returnable` + `notReturnableNote`, `returnableQty` is forced to 0 on a line that cannot go back, and `loadOrderDetail` computes `anyReturnable` off the item snapshots. The note is read off the product as it stands TODAY while the flag is the order's own snapshot - the right must not move, the wording may. `lib/db/order-requests.ts`: `linePositionQuery` selects `oi."returnable"` and `createOrderRequest` refuses by name before the arithmetic, so a hand-rolled POST gets the reason rather than "at most 0 of". **ADMIN.** Product editor Stock tab gains a **Returns** section (`nonReturnable` switch + revealed 300-char note; stored the other way up - unticked writes `null`, not `true`, because null is what a child reads as "ask the listing"). Zod on both product routes (`returnable`, `nonReturnableNote`), `db/products.ts` mapper/create/COLUMN_MAP/duplicate. Variations grid gains a **Returns** column - a three-state SELECT, not a checkbox, because "As product" has to stay reachable - with `returnable` on the variant PATCH and through `upsertVariantForCombination` + `ChildProductFields` change detection. **SPREADSHEETS.** Products CSV gains `returnable` + `non_returnable_reason` (optional, appended, `returnable` in `BOOLEAN_CSV_COLUMNS`, new "Returns" picker group); Variations CSV gains **Returns** after Min Qty in header/kinds/row/`VARIATIONS_FIXED_COLUMNS`/picker. Both are tri-state on import: a blank cell is "nothing said" and changes nothing, which is what makes sweeping one bespoke range worth doing there. **NOT DONE, deliberately:** no storefront notice before purchase - that needs a new Puck part in the Product Detail layout, which owners must place by hand, and is a separate job. Cancel requests are untouched: a cancel happens before anything has been made or moved. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite 6250 passed / 0 failed, and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests, 183s) against a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database - though `shop` is still outside that gate's module set (`readModuleSchemas` skips any module whose SQL contains `$$`), so the cover for these columns is `schema-coverage.test.ts` plus the fact that `bool` is a long-supported udt. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki: `Shop.md` gains **Things you can't take back**, `Shop-variations.md` the Returns column and its CSV line, `Google-Sheet-Products.md` the two new sheet columns.)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**Four scheduled jobs had been failing every night, and the site could only say "did not finish"** - core plus `search`, `live-chat`, `purchase-orders`, `reviews-for-shop`, `shop` and `ultimate-seo`, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, route, permission or dependency change. **WHAT WAS WRONG, ALL FOUR ON THE LIVE SITE'S `CronRun` TABLE:** (1) **Prisma binds a JavaScript integer as `bigint`, and every named `make_interval()` argument except `secs` is `int4`** - so `make_interval(days => ${n})` is `42883: function make_interval(days => bigint) does not exist`, every time. Four modules carried that line. `modules/search/lib/indexer.ts` `purgeOldQueries` and `modules/live-chat/lib/db.ts` `deleteConversationsOlderThan` turned their nightly cron into an HTTP 500; `modules/purchase-orders/lib/paid-sweep.ts` `paidWithNothingRaised` has the statement inside a catch that degrades to "nothing to sweep", so it reported success while drafting nothing - the live database has an order it should have caught; `modules/reviews-for-shop/lib/db/invites.ts` `listInviteCandidates` is guarded by an `invitesEnabled` check and was one toggle away from the same. All four now cast `::int4`. (2) `modules/shop/lib/db/refunds.ts` `reconcileStaleRefunds` selected `o."payment_provider"` - **`shp_orders` has never had a column by that name**, the column is `payment_method` - so the hourly refund reconcile 500'd on every single run since it shipped. Renamed in the SELECT and in the row type. (3) The weekly SEO audit was cut off mid-crawl every week: core's cron dispatcher capped each job at 25s while `modules/ultimate-seo/lib/audit.ts` budgets 45s, and its clock started *after* `collectUrls` had already fetched the sitemap. `TIME_BUDGET_MS` is now 38s and `started` is set on entry, so the budget covers the whole run. **CORE, `app/api/cron/dispatch/route.ts`:** `MAX_PER_JOB_MS` (25s) is **gone** - it was tighter than the 60s the shared module dispatcher grants every module route, so a job written to use its ceiling could never pass. A job now gets whatever is left of the tick; `MIN_PER_JOB_MS` (5s) defers one there is no longer time for, untouched, and the stamp-before-call ordering still stops a slow job hogging ticks. `lastError` gains the route's own `error`/`message` field where it sent one (`describeFailure`, 300 chars), and a successful job's unread body is cancelled rather than left holding its connection. **The framework masks an uncaught throw as a bare "Internal Server Error"**, so all four failing cron routes now catch and report: `search/cron/reindex`, `live-chat/cron/retention`, `shop/cron/reconcile-refunds`, `ultimate-seo/cron/audit`. **NEW GATE, `lib/db/raw-interval-casts.test.ts`:** plain `npm test`, no credentials, walks `app/`, `components/`, `lib/` and `modules/` and fails on any `make_interval()` int argument interpolated without a cast. Nothing else could see this class - raw SQL is a string to `tsc` and `eslint`, the module build gate never executes a query, and `purchase-orders`' own live SQL test missed it because node-postgres sends an untyped `$1` that Postgres infers as `int4`, which only Prisma's binding does not. Verified by running all five corrected statements against the live Deskwell database as reads. Wiki: `Architecture-overview.md` (per-job budget, what `lastError` holds), `Authoring-a-module.md` (catch your own errors, budget against the tick, the `make_interval` trap).)

Last updated: 2026-09-08 (**A map with the van on it while the parcel is out, proof of delivery kept as our own copy, and the parser defect that left a delivered order open** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree. **THE DEFECT FIRST, because it was live:** `parseMultidropStages` sliced each timeline step from its own opening tag to the NEXT step's, and the last step had no next - so step 7 ran to the end of the document and a delivered parcel's stage was recorded as `Complete` followed by the contact buttons, the signature card, the feedback panel and the page's own `trackingConfig` script. That 900-character blob matched no `deliveredStages` setting, so `delivered_at` stayed null and DW000172 (delivered 14:23, polled 14:25) sat in SHIPPED. Step extents now come from counting `<div>` depth (`elementEnd`), plus a 120-character cap on a label - the settings box's own limit - so a future redesign yields no stage rather than a page dump in a field the shop makes decisions on. The old fixture stopped at `<!-- end .timeline -->`, which is precisely why it never caught this; a second fixture, `__fixtures__/multidrop-delivered.html`, is a REAL delivered page including everything after the timeline, redacted of the trade reference, phone number, signee, coordinates and their API keys. **MIGRATION `041_delivery_live_tracking.sql`** (mirrored into `001_initial.sql`): `shp_shipments` gains `tracking_client_id`, `tracking_route_id`, `crew_line`, `drops_away`, `vehicle_lat`, `vehicle_lng`, `vehicle_heading`, `vehicle_fixed_at`, `vehicle_polled_at`, `destination_lat`, `destination_lng`, `signed_by`, `signed_at`, `signature_url`, `signature_key`. Coordinates are TEXT in the courier's own digits - they are copied and handed to a map, never summed. **NEW `lib/tracking/multidrop-page.ts`:** `parseTrackingConfig` (their `var trackingConfig` block - client id, route id, status, their pin for the drop), `parseCrewLine` (`#eta-status`), `dropsAwayFromCrewLine`, `parseSignature`, `parseCourierTimestamp`. Drops-away is read out of their SENTENCE and never from `dropNumber`/`currentDrop`: on the first real delivery their sentence said "1 more drop" while both numbers said 17. Their timestamps are day-first and parsed by hand against the site timezone - `new Date('08/09/2026')` is the 9th of August. An unrecognised wording gives null, which every caller reads as "not known" and never as "you are next". **NEW `lib/tracking/multidrop-position.ts`:** `positionUrl` + `fetchVehiclePosition` against `/?action=get_latest_location&cl=&route=`, ~100 bytes of JSON, Zod-parsed because it is a third party's reply on a customer-facing path; the URL is built off the tracking link's own origin and re-checked with `isMultidropUrl`, and both ids must be digits. **NEW `lib/tracking/position-cache.ts`:** per-instance, keyed on the ROUND, 30s floor, with concurrent callers sharing one in-flight request - four tabs on one office delivery is one question to the courier, not four. **NEW `lib/tracking/live-delivery.ts`** (pure): `positionFreshness` (seconds/minutes/hours, stale at five minutes - the courier's own threshold) and `livePollIntervalMs` (5 min, 1 min once drops-away <= 1). **NEW `lib/tracking/signature-capture.ts`:** fetches the courier's image once, caps it at 5 MB, SNIFFS the magic bytes for the type - the first real one was served as `.png` and was a JPEG - and stores it through core's `uploadMedia`, so it lands in the site's own bucket with a random key and no media-library row. Failure is always null: the parcel is still delivered. **NEW ROUTE `GET /api/m/shop/public/orders/[id]/live-delivery?shipment=`:** gated by `resolveOrderViewer` (the same rule as the order page, called not copied), rate-limited per IP, and it hands the browser coordinates and a sentence - never the tracking URL or the trade reference. Returns `pollAfterMs` so the tick is decided where the drop count is known. **CRON** stays hourly and unchanged in cadence - it now also records the page details and captures the signature on the run that first sees a delivered stage, guarded both in the route and in `recordSignature`'s WHERE clause. **UI:** `components/public/DeliveryLiveMap.tsx` (client) with `maplibre-loader.ts` - MapLibre 3.6.2 pinned from unpkg at runtime, because a module cannot add an npm dependency and half a megabyte of map does not belong in every install's bundle; tiles from OpenFreeMap, never the courier's own tile server. Polls only while visible, stops after an hour, and a blocked CDN leaves the words in place. Manifest gains `cspOrigins` (script/style unpkg, connect/img openfreemap). Signature and signed-by render on the customer's order page and in `OrderDetailScreen`'s Parcels list. `lib/order-display.ts` gains `formatOrderDateTime`. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite 6178 passed, and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests, 154s) - though `shop` remains outside that gate's module set (`readModuleSchemas` skips any module whose SQL contains `$$`), so the actual cover for these columns is `schema-coverage.test.ts` and the fact that they are TEXT, INTEGER and TIMESTAMP. NOT verified in a browser. Wiki `Shop.md` gains **Watching the van on the day** and **Proof of delivery**.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**The inbox on a phone and a tablet, rebuilt** - `unified-inbox` plus one core token, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, route, permission or dependency change. **WHAT WAS WRONG:** below 900px the frame was a stack of blocks on a page that scrolled as one - rail, then the list or the conversation - so the rail, the list's head and the way back scrolled off the top the moment anybody read a message, and the note bar stuck to the bottom of the page rather than the pane. Below 1200px the rail "lay down" into one strip that scrolled sideways: a dozen names with the headings removed, the one that was on somewhere in the middle, and nothing to say there were more off the edge. Dialogs were cards floating in a rem of dimmed page; menus were 280px panels pinned to a button's corner; icon buttons were 28px, tabs 26px, the context cross 18px. **THE FRAME (`styles.tsx`):** `.uin-app` is now `position: sticky` and screen-tall at EVERY width, one grid template per state rather than per breakpoint - the phone template is `"rail" "find" "list"` and `[data-open="1"]` swaps in `"rail" "find" "read" "ctx"`, because a hidden grid item still leaves its track and an empty 1fr track is half a screen of nothing; the `find` row is an `auto` row that is 0 when the search head is not in the markup, which retired every `[data-find="1"]` template variant. At `max-width: 768px` (core's own burger breakpoint) the frame goes edge to edge: `margin: calc(-1 * var(--space-2))` cancels core's padding, corners and side borders go, `top`/`height` are measured off `--admin-mobile-topbar-height` (fallback 52px). **CORE:** `app/globals.css` gains `--admin-mobile-topbar-height: 52px` in `:root` and `.admin-mobile-topbar` reads it - the module reads it through a fallback so it needs no core version bump. **THE RAIL (`NavRail.tsx` + `styles.tsx`):** `.uin-rail-me` moves OUT of `.uin-rail-scroll` to be the head of the rail at every width (no sticky needed). Below 1200px it is the bar: a new `.uin-rail-places` button (hamburger, the current place's dot/icon, its name, its count, a chevron; `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`) and `.uin-rail-scroll` becomes a fixed left drawer (`min(20rem, 86vw)`, z 10000, `translateX(-100%)` + `visibility` until `.uin-rail[data-drawer="open"]`), with a new `.uin-rail-drawer-head` (avatar, name, close) and a `.uin-rail-backdrop` button drawn only while open. Escape, the backdrop, the cross, or clicking any link inside shuts it; focus goes to the close on open and back to the places button on close; never open on first render so server and client agree. `here` is worked out from the same RailItem lists the rail draws (a folder under a colleague is "Sam · Sent"). The old lying-down rules (row flex, hidden headings, hairlines between groups, static foot) are gone. `MenuIcon` added to `icons.tsx`. **THE CONVERSATION:** `.uin-thread-head` and `.uin-camp-head` are sticky at every width (the pane always scrolls its own contents now). The way back on a phone is a chevron at the START of the subject line (`order: -1; flex: none`) with the word Back beside it from 600px; the five panes (`ThreadPane`, `PersonView`, `ContactCard`, `OrganisationCard`, `ContactImport`) wrap the words in `.uin-back-words` so they can go. Under 640px `.uin-thread-actions` is a nowrap row that scrolls sideways (scrollbar hidden) instead of wrapping into rows. `CampaignEditor` gains `onBack` (threaded through the inner `Campaign`) and draws a `.uin-camp-back` button, phone only, wired to `go({ campaign: null, view: null })` in `CampaignsPanel` - the list is hidden there and the rail's Campaigns link is now behind a drawer. Tighter padding on the head, body, list head and message under 600px. **DIALOGS (≤599px):** `.uin-modal` loses its padding and aligns to the bottom; `.uin-modal-card` is a full-width sheet with top corners only; compose, attach, picker and search cards are `100dvh`; feet pad for `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`. **MENUS (≤599px):** `.uin-menu`, `.uin-compose-menu`, `.uin-ctxbar-menu` become bottom sheets - `top:auto/left:0/width:auto` with `!important`, the ONLY `!important`s in the file, because Dropdown/ComposeMenu/ContextRecords write top/left/width as inline styles from the button's rect and an inline style cannot otherwise be overruled from a stylesheet. **TOUCH:** `@media (pointer: coarse)` lifts rail items to 2.6rem, icon/compose/search/close buttons to 2.5rem, tabs 2.25rem, chips 2rem, menu items 2.75rem, calendar days 2.5rem, the context cross to 1.75rem (was 1.125rem), the swatches, the rich-text buttons, the refresh and bell - keyed on the pointer, not the width. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `vitest run modules/unified-inbox/components` 34 passed. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's. Wiki `Unified-Inbox.md` gains **On a phone or a tablet** under The screen, replacing the sentence about the rail lying down; module README gains a bullet.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**The new import-cycle guard could refuse an install's build over a list it did not write** - core only, uncommitted working tree. No schema, route, permission or dependency change. **THE DEFECT, SHIPPED IN v0.5.1552 AND CAUGHT BY THE GATE IT WAS MEANT TO PROVE:** `scripts/check-import-cycles.mjs` exits 1 on a `KNOWN` entry that no longer cycles, on the reasonable grounds that an allowance nobody prunes stops meaning anything. But WHICH cycles exist depends on which modules are installed - the edge that closes a loop here may live in a module another site never installed - so staleness is a fact about one tree, not about the code. filters-for-shop v0.1.51's gate, re-dispatched against core v0.5.1552 with the six fixed modules pinned, aborted at `[prebuild] import cycle check failed` with `modules/shop/lib/payments/registry.ts` reported as no longer cycling: true in that composition, false here. The build that was supposed to demonstrate the fix was refused by the fix. Left alone this would have failed a real install's update for the same reason. **THE FIX:** `stale` is now a `console.warn` at prebuild and never an exit code; `unexpected` - a cycle NOT on the list, the thing the guard exists for - still fails, so the count can still only go down. The anti-rot half keeps its teeth where it can afford them: `lib/modules/import-cycles.test.ts` still fails on a stale entry, because the platform tree is the tree the list was written against and the tree it is pruned in. Both halves carry the reasoning in a comment so the next person does not re-tighten it. **CHECKS:** `node scripts/check-import-cycles.mjs` clean, `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**The phone bar's chat button did nothing for staff, and "hide the bubble on phones" left their console sitting on the page** - `live-chat` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, route, permission or core change; the setting it obeys, `hideBubbleOnMobile`, already existed. **THE DEFECT:** `components/AgentConsole.tsx` is what a signed-in member of staff gets on the public site instead of the customer widget, and it listened for nothing. Core's Mobile Bar chat cell opens chat by firing `LIVE_CHAT_OPEN_EVENT`, which only the visitor-facing loader heard - so for anybody with `livechat.view` the bar's chat button was inert. The same component ignored `hideBubbleOnMobile` as well, so a site that turned the bubble off for phones precisely because its bottom bar already carried a chat cell still had a second, larger door to the same room sitting over the page for its own staff. **THE FIX:** the console now listens for `LIVE_CHAT_OPEN_EVENT` and opens on it, `LiveChatBlock.rsc.tsx` passes `hideOnMobile={config.hideBubbleOnMobile}` through, and the launcher is HIDDEN rather than unmounted under 640px (`.lc-agent-host`, `display:none !important` because the button's placement is an inline style and would otherwise outrank a stylesheet) so it keeps polling and the badge is right the moment the bar opens it. Both the launcher and the open panel now clear core's bar by `var(--cactus-bottom-bar-offset, 0px)`, which is 0 on a site without one, so desktop and bar-less phones are untouched. **COPY:** the setting's hint and `wiki/Live-Chat.md` now say the setting covers staff too.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**The junk button now asks before it moves anything, every time** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, route, permission, dependency or core change. **THE DEFECT:** both spam buttons gated the confirmation on whether there was also a sender to block. `SpamButton.tsx`'s `mark()` opened the dialog only when `worthAsking` (`canBlock && senderAddress && !senderBlocked`), and `ThreadListView.tsx`'s `markPickedSpam()` only when `canBlock && blockable.length > 0`; otherwise the press moved the lot on the spot. So the one press with nothing to offer - a sender already turned away, a conversation between colleagues, a caller with no number, or a reader without the permission to block - was the one press with no confirmation and no way back, which is exactly the press a finger lands on by accident in a toolbar. **THE FIX:** the press now only ever opens the question; `worthAsking` decides WHICH question, not whether one is asked. With nobody to block: title `Move it to the spam folder?` (or `Move N conversations to spam?` in the bulk bar), body pointing at the same button in the Spam folder as the way back, one confirm labelled `Move it to spam` wired to `moveOnly()`/`movePickedOnly()`, no middle answer, and `destructive` OFF so the keyboard starts on a move that undoes itself rather than on a red button. **ONE STATE SHAPE CHANGE:** `ThreadListView`'s `blocking` is now `string[] | null` - null means the dialog is shut, `[]` means it is open with nothing to block. An empty array could not carry both readings, which is what tied "is it open" to "is there anybody to block" in the first place; `askedAbout = blocking ?? []` is what the dialog reads. **COPY:** `wiki/Unified-Inbox.md` updated in three places. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**"Purge everything now" purged the CDN and left the site's own copy of every page exactly where it was** - core only, uncommitted working tree. No schema, permission or dependency change. **THE DEFECT:** `app/(public)/[slug]/page.tsx` renders with `revalidate = false`, so Next holds a top-level page's HTML indefinitely and only `revalidatePath()` drops it. `app/api/admin/cache/purge/route.ts` called Cloudflare's `purge_everything` and nothing else, so the whole sequence was: Cloudflare discards its copy, asks the origin, is handed back the identical stale HTML, caches it again for the full window. Net effect of pressing the button on an affected page: none, for up to an hour on a site with the window set to 3600. Reported as "some pages still appear stuck for about an hour", and "some" was the tell - `/` and `/[slug]/[...path]` are `force-dynamic` and were always fine, so the shop looked right while `/about` and friends did not. **THE FIX:** `revalidatePath('/', 'layout')` before the CDN call at both `purge everything` sites - the manual button, and a page-cache setting change in `app/api/admin/config/route.ts:271` (switching the cache off or moving its window had the same hole). Order is load-bearing and commented as such: purge before revalidate leaves a gap in which the CDN refills from an origin that is still stale. The per-path sites (`app/api/admin/pages/**`) already revalidated first and were correct; the two full-purge sites were the exception. **NOT the cause, ruled out by reading the code:** Vercel's edge holds nothing here, because `proxy.ts:251` sends `Vercel-CDN-Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=0, must-revalidate` whenever `isCdnPurgeConfigured()` - Vercel has no purge API, so the copy is deliberately pushed one hop down to the cache that can be emptied. **COPY:** the button's hint and `wiki/Configuration-reference.md` said "every stored copy on Cloudflare"; both now say "every stored copy of your pages", which is what it does. `wiki/Architecture-overview.md` gains the rule as a paragraph of its own. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean. Not committed, not released.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**A generated registry and the module code it imports were in an import cycle, and it failed a production build** - core plus six modules, uncommitted working tree. No schema, route, permission or dependency change. **THE DEFECT:** `lib/modules/extension-points.public.ts` is generated and statically imports every component every installed module contributes (114 files here); a module file that consumed that map with a plain `import` closed a loop. Turbopack merges the modules in a cycle into ONE scope, where a `const` read while that scope is still evaluating throws `Cannot access 'q' before initialization` - and whether it throws depends on the order the graph is entered in, which depends on which modules are installed. So the same code builds on one site and dies on another. `tsc`, `eslint` and 6144 tests were all green throughout. It surfaced as a RED BUILD GATE on filters-for-shop v0.1.51, a module with one line of CSS changed, after core v0.5.1550 added `/api/admin/message-destinations` - the first route to evaluate the registry at build time. **MEASURED, NOT GUESSED:** a graph walk counting static edges only (a dynamic `import()` is a bundler edge but DEFERS evaluation, so it cannot cause a temporal dead zone; `import type` is erased) found **27 cycles**, closed by 7 different edges, one of them core's own `lib/media/usage-providers.ts`. Cutting an edge reveals the next-shortest closer, so this was solved to a fixed point rather than one at a time. **THE FIX, 27 -> 2:** each consuming function reaches the registry through `await import(...)` instead of a top-of-file import. Core: `lib/media/usage-providers.ts` (now async, its one caller in `lib/media/references.ts` awaits it), `lib/conversations/providers.ts`, `lib/dialler/registry.ts`, and `lib/documents/footer.tsx` - that last one was a PDF footer statically pulling in the whole `module-rsc-components` barrel, and cutting it killed four cycles at once; the file already lazy-loaded `config.rsc` for the same reason. Modules: shop (`product-saved`, `card-price`, `card-media`, `stock-visibility`, `collection-index-sources`, `detail-slot`, `detail-spec`, `detail-tabs`, `gallery-media`, `line-meta`), search (`query`, `shop-product-text`, `SiteSearchResultsBlock.rsc`), unified-inbox (`provider-registry`), purchase-orders (`capabilities`), contact-form (`InboxPanel`), shop-variations (`ProductVariationsSection`). **TWO LEFT, RECORDED NOT IGNORED:** `shop/lib/payments/registry.ts` (`moduleProviders()` is sync and feeds ten sync exports - async is a change to shop's payment API) and `shop/lib/checkout-address-lookup.ts` (hands a component to a client island from four call sites, one a non-async Puck RSC block). Both want a real render to verify, which needs a build. **NEW GUARD `scripts/check-import-cycles.mjs`** + `lib/modules/import-cycles.test.ts` + a hand-written `check-import-cycles.d.mts`, all mirroring the `check-client-graph` trio: walks the same graph, fails on any cycle NOT on an explicit `KNOWN` list, AND fails on a `KNOWN` entry that no longer cycles so the allowance cannot rot. Wired into `scripts/prebuild.mjs` as step 4, beside the client-graph check, because prebuild is the only moment THIS install's pinned module versions are assembled together. It checks all three generated registries (`extension-points.public`, `extension-points`, `module-rsc-components`) - widening past the first one is what found the footer cycle. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, full suite green including the live database suites and the backup round-trip. **NOT RELEASED** - release order matters here and is Chris's call: the gate composes a candidate against core's newest RELEASE, so proving this through filters-for-shop's gate needs core released with the fixed modules pinned first.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**The shop now reads the courier's own tracking page on a schedule, and finishes an order off when every parcel has arrived** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree. **MIGRATION `040_tracking_stages.sql`** (mirrored into `001_initial.sql`): `shp_shipments` gains `tracking_stage`, `tracking_stage_at`, `tracking_checked_at`, `delivered_at`, plus a partial index on `tracking_checked_at` where a tracking url exists and delivery has not happened - the poller's own query. **NEW `lib/tracking/multidrop.ts`:** `parseMultidropStages`, `furthestStage`, `isMultidropUrl`. Multidrop's page is plain server-rendered HTML with seven fixed steps (`<div id="tl-stepN" class="timeline-step done">`), so no browser and no DOM library: it SPLITS on the step ids rather than regex-matching a balanced element, because a step CONTAINS its own `<div class="tl-time">` and the obvious regex swallows the timestamp into the stage name (it did, first go). `furthestStage` takes the LAST done step, not the first not-done one, so a courier ticking a later step reads as progress rather than the parcel going backwards. Tested against a REAL saved page in `lib/tracking/__fixtures__/multidrop-timeline.html`, not a hand-written one. **NEW `lib/tracking/stage-meaning.ts`:** what a stage MEANS is per-courier settings (`trackingSource`, `outForDeliveryStages`, `deliveredStages`) rather than code - whether "Assigned to Crew" means a van is out is a question for the carrier, and the answer must not need a release. Case-insensitive, trimmed, delivered wins a stage listed in both. **CRON:** `/api/m/shop/cron/delivery-tracking`, hourly in the manifest, so it appears under Settings > Schedules. Early-exits when no courier is polled; otherwise 25 parcels a run, oldest-checked first, 3 at a time, 8s timeout. A failed fetch or an unrecognisable page stamps `tracking_checked_at` ONLY - silence is never read as "not delivered" - and an unpollable parcel is stamped too, or it sits at the head of the queue for ever being skipped. Auto-complete goes through `applyOrderStatusChange` (never a direct status write) and needs BOTH every parcel delivered and `summary.fullyDispatched`. **RAIL:** `parcelDelivery` now returns `outForDelivery` / `arrived` from the courier's stage, falling back to the clock, and the rail's label follows: Delivery scheduled / Out for delivery / Delivery. **CHECKS:** typecheck, eslint, 6137 tests, and the backup round-trip PASS - though `shop` remains outside that gate's module set, so the real cover for these columns is `schema-coverage.test.ts` and the fact that they are TEXT and TIMESTAMP.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**Couriers move to their own tab, the rail stops claiming a van is out a day early, and the delivery line reads like a person wrote it** - `shop` only, on top of v0.1.398, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no migration, no new dependency. **COURIERS TAB:** the editor moves off `ShopSettingsTab` (where it sat in the `checkout` block between Order history and Cancellations, and where nobody found it) onto `TaxShippingScreen` as that screen's own second tab, `?tab=couriers`. New `components/admin/CourierSettingsPanel.tsx` owns the load and save, and it re-reads `/api/m/shop/admin/settings` immediately BEFORE the PUT and changes only `deliveryCouriers` on that fresh copy - `ShopSettingsTab` saves a whole snapshot, and two screens editing one settings blob is how a save silently undoes somebody else's. `TabStrip` now always renders (previously only when a module had contributed a tab). **LABEL:** `OrderDelivery` gains `underway`, and the delivery step reads **Delivery scheduled** / **Out for delivery** / **Delivery** by phase rather than calling a parcel booked for tomorrow "out for delivery" today. **WORDING:** `lib/delivery-slot.ts` gains `formatClockTime` (10am, 1pm, 9.30am, and midday/midnight by name - "12pm" is ambiguous to plenty of people), `formatDeliveryWindowSpoken` and `formatDeliveryDayRelative` (today / tomorrow / bare weekday to six days out / full date beyond, lower case so it sits inside "Arranged for tomorrow"). `parcelDelivery` uses the relative pair; **the EMAIL deliberately keeps `formatDeliveryDay`**, since "booked in for tomorrow" is true when it is sent and wrong by breakfast. The rail's note capitalises the first letter. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck`, `eslint` and 6120 tests all clean. Backup gate not re-run: nothing about the schema changed this time.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**The Mobile Bar drew itself as a bulleted list on desktop - fixed, and the live header rolled back** - core only, uncommitted working tree. No schema, route, permission or dependency change. **THE DEFECT, found on the live Deskwell site, not by a test:** `mobileBarCss` emitted its ENTIRE stylesheet inside the breakpoint query the owner chose, on the reasoning that a phones-only bar should cost a desktop page nothing. It cost it rather more than nothing. **The markup is rendered at every width** - a breakpoint decides how the bar is PAINTED, not whether the block ran - so on desktop the query matched nothing, no rule applied, and the block dumped a bare `<ul>` of Home/Account/Basket/Menu/Message into the header as bulleted text, with the menu panel and its scrim unrolled underneath it. `position:fixed` was behind the same query, so the list sat in flow and shoved the real desktop header out of view. **FIX:** new `hiddenBase(sel)` emitted UNCONDITIONALLY and FIRST - `{display:none}` on the bar plus a second rule naming `.cmb-sheet` and `.cmb-scrim` by hand (both are fixed-position boxes; hiding only the bar leaves them covering the page). The wrapped rules then turn all three back on with `display:block`, equal specificity and later in the sheet, so the query wins wherever it applies and a `barOn:'all'` bar still shows with no query at all. Cost to a desktop page is now three declarations, which is the price of the markup existing. `body{padding-bottom}` and `:root{--cactus-bottom-bar-offset}` stay inside the query, unchanged. **THE TEST ENSHRINED THE BUG:** `mobileBar.test.ts` asserted `css.startsWith('@media(max-width:')` - it was checking for exactly the thing that broke the site. Replaced with three cases: the hide comes first and before any `@media`; the panel and scrim are hidden BY NAME; and all three come back inside the query, asserted per selector rather than by counting `display:block` (a count also catches `.cmb-icon>svg` and says nothing about which box went missing). 7 cases now. **Gates:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npx vitest run` **6108 passed / 0 failed / 385 skipped**. **LIVE DATABASE:** the Deskwell header layout (`Layout` id `starter-header-tall-live`) had been written with the new phone header and bar; it is now **rolled back** - `builderData` AND `publishedData` both restored from the pre-change dumps and verified byte-identical, `publishedAt` unmoved. Both columns were restored in one statement without first recording what `publishedData` held, so **whether Publish had been pressed is unknown and now unknowable** - record the current value before overwriting, next time. Backups, a corrected `rollback.sh` and `build-rollback-sql.py` are in the session scratchpad under `deskwell-header-backup/`; the first `rollback.sh` FAILED when Chris ran it (`SyntaxError: unexpected character after line continuation character`) because it built its SQL in a `python3 -c` one-liner whose nested shell quoting did not survive - the generator is a real file now, and the script verifies with `diff` afterwards rather than trusting the UPDATE. **NOT re-applied to the live site** - the fix has to be released and the install updated first.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**A delivery day, a four-hour window, a van that moves across the rail, and couriers as a list** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree. **THE CASE:** a two-stage courier books the DAY when it collects and confirms the WINDOW the evening before, by which time there is nothing left to dispatch - so the second half had nowhere to go, and some couriers' "tracking" page is really the shop's trade portal (account number, pro-forma status, the supplier's own telephone number) and cannot be put in front of a customer at all. **MIGRATION `039_delivery_slots.sql`** (mirrored into `001_initial.sql`): `shp_shipments` gains `courier_id`, `delivery_date`, `delivery_slot_start`, `delivery_slot_end`, `slot_notified_at`, with CHECK constraints on the two shapes. All TEXT, deliberately: a DATE read back through Prisma is a UTC-midnight instant that prints as the day BEFORE anywhere west of UTC, which is the one mistake a feature about naming the right day cannot make. **NEW `lib/delivery-slot.ts`** (pure, takes the clock as an argument): `isDeliveryDate` (rejects 2026-02-31), `isSlotTime`, `formatDeliveryDay` ("Tuesday 8th of September", no Intl - the input has no timezone to hand a formatter), `formatDeliveryWindow`, `nowInTimezone` (en-CA + h23), and `deliveryProgress` -> `{progress, phase: upcoming|today|during|passed}`. **NEW `lib/courier-faqs.ts`**: `courierForShipment` (by id, else by NAME so a courier added today applies to parcels already in flight), `customerMaySeeTracking`, `faqsForShipment`, `courierFaqUrl` (parsed, never string-appended - a guest order link already carries `?t=`). **NEW `lib/order-delivery.ts`**: `parcelDelivery` per parcel and `railDelivery`, which picks the SOONEST delivery still to come rather than the one that finishes the order. **CONFIG:** `deliveryCouriers` - `{id, name, showTrackingLink, faqs[{id, question, answer}]}`, empty by default, edited in Shop settings > Checkout > Couriers (`components/admin/CourierSettings.tsx`). **ROUTE:** `PATCH /api/m/shop/admin/orders/[id]/dispatch` fills in a parcel after the event (details only - quantities still mean undo and re-dispatch, under the lock); the window is validated as it will be AFTER the save, the courier NAME is read from settings rather than from the browser, and `claimSlotNotification` takes the right to email in the same statement that records it. **EMAIL:** new `shop.delivery-slot-confirmed` (`lib/delivery-slot-email.ts`), sent once per parcel when a day and both times exist. **RAIL:** `orderProgressSteps` gains an optional fifth `delivery` step between Dispatched and Complete, and arrived is NOT complete - a delivery can fail, and ticking Complete off a clock tells somebody standing in an empty hall their furniture is there. `DeliveryVanDot.tsx` is a client component ticking every 60s off the same pure function the server called. **ORDER PAGE:** "Arranged for Tuesday 8th of September, between 10:00 and 13:00", the tracking link suppressed per courier, and `CourierFaqModal.tsx` - plain text answers printed as text, opened by `?faq=1` from the email, with a `<noscript>` `<details>` copy so the email link never lands on an empty page. **CHECKS:** `npm run typecheck`, `eslint`, 6106 tests, and the backup round-trip gate PASS against real throwaway databases. NOTE: `shop` has always been outside that gate's module set - `readModuleSchemas` skips any module whose migrations use `$$`, and `001_initial.sql` has since long before this.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**The writing box now shows what will be quoted under the reply** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema, migration, route, permission or dependency change. **THE GAP:** the quotation is built at SEND time in `lib/compose.ts` (`quoteForReply` / `quoteForForward`) - it has to be, since the markup that leaves is sanitised on its way out and a stranger's email never touches the admin page - so the composer said nothing whatever about it and the only way to see what a customer would receive was to send it and read your own copy. **NEW `lib/quoted-preview.ts`, WITH NO IMPORTS AT ALL:** `QuotedPreview` (`id`, `sentAtMs` - a Date in props reaches a client component as an empty object - `attribution`, `forwardHeader`, `hasHtml`, `hasRemoteImages`, `ownSender`, `bodyText`) and the pure `pickQuotedPreview(previews, replyToId)`, which repeats the send route's rule: the message that was pressed, else the newest, else nothing. Import-free deliberately - `Composer.tsx` is a client component and `compose.ts` reaches the sanitiser and the site clock, which is exactly the leak `scripts/check-client-graph.mjs` exists to catch (run clean). **`lib/compose.ts`:** the four From/Date/Subject/To rows lift out of `quoteForForward` into a new exported `forwardHeaderRows`, so the panel shows the same rows the forwarded message carries rather than a second copy that could drift. **`ThreadPane.tsx`** builds `quotedPreviews` off the messages it has already read (notes excluded - never quotable), `bodyText` carried ONLY for a message with no markup, and passes it to both `ComposerSlot`s. **`Composer.tsx`** derives `quoting` with `useMemo`, and under the writing box draws a `<details>` - **Show the earlier messages**, or **Show what you are forwarding** - with the attribution line (or the forward header block) and then the message itself: `MessageBody` for markup, `MessageText` with `foldQuoted={false}` for words. Gated on `!noting && style.addressed`: a note goes to nobody and a channel reply is handed to the owning module as one string with nothing beneath it. **Body mounts only when opened** (`showingQuoted` + `onToggle`) - a shut `<details>` still MOUNTS its children, so every reply anybody started would otherwise have fetched a whole message into a frame and run its measuring script. **`MessageBody.tsx`** gains `showQuoted?: boolean`, which appends `quoted=1` to the body route's query - the route already honoured it (`collapseQuoted`), so the panel shows the whole chain expanded rather than a fold inside a fold. **`styles.tsx`:** `.uin-quoted-preview`, `.uin-quoted-preview-body` (rule down the left, `--color-border`), `.uin-quoted-preview-line` - all tokens, no hex. **NEW TESTS:** `lib/quoted-preview.test.ts` (5). **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint modules/unified-inbox` clean, `vitest run modules/unified-inbox` **1109 passed / 0 failed / 241 skipped**, `node scripts/check-client-graph.mjs` clean. No SQL touched, so the backup round-trip and live suites stand from the entry below. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's. Wiki `Unified-Inbox.md` gains a paragraph under **Which message you are answering**; module README says it in a sentence.)

Last updated: 2026-09-07 (**A supplier can now invoice you through their own link, and an order waits at Pending close until somebody has read it** - `purchase-orders` v0.1.30, uncommitted working tree. **ONE NEW MODULE MIGRATION `migrations/011_supplier_invoices.sql`** (001 carries all of it for fresh installs): `po_orders_status_check` DROPped and re-ADDed with **`PENDING_CLOSE`** (Postgres has no ALTER CONSTRAINT for a CHECK); `po_bills.stated_total NUMERIC(12,2)` and `po_bills.source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ADMIN'` + `po_bills_source_check ('ADMIN','PORTAL')`; `po_portal_events_kind_check` widened with `INVOICED`. **NEW FILES:** `lib/order-settle.ts`, `lib/portal-invoice.ts`, `lib/portal-invoice.test.ts`, `app/api/admin/bills/scan/route.ts`, `app/api/public/portal/invoice/route.ts`. **THE STATUS:** `PENDING_CLOSE` is a real `PoStatus`, not a derived label - unlike the proforma stage of 2026-09-04, because it gates a TRANSITION and a derived label cannot. `lifecycle.ts`: `close` accepts it, `reopen` now runs from `['CLOSED','PENDING_CLOSE']`, and new `closeBlockedReason(status, unsettledBills)` refuses Close **only from PENDING_CLOSE** - every other route to CLOSED is somebody here deciding. `from-order.ts` `LIVE_STATUSES` gained it (miss that and a paid shop order raises a duplicate PO); `statusAfterReceipts` already returns null for anything off its list, so a receipt cannot trample it; `STATUS_TONE` in `components/admin/ui.tsx` is exhaustive and `tsc` caught it. **`billing.ts`:** `shouldAutoClose` is REPLACED by `closeOutcome(status, lines, openReturns, unsettledBills) -> 'CLOSED' | 'PENDING_CLOSE' | null`; new `totalMismatch(stated, computed)` with a flat **5p** tolerance (a percentage of a five-thousand-pound order waves a hundred pounds through) and a fifth variance kind `TOTAL`, emitted by `matchBill` only when there IS an order, so `NOT_MATCHED` still carries no flags. **`lib/order-settle.ts`** is the one place an order finishes: called from the bill transition route (on APPROVE **and now VOID** - voiding the last unread invoice settles it too), from the portal invoice route with a **null user**, and from `app/api/admin/orders/[id]/receipts/route.ts` (the delivery that completes an order the invoices got to first). `setOrderStatus` therefore takes `userId: string | null` and **omits the `updated_by_user_id` SET when null** rather than writing null - a supplier is not a user of this site and neither answer would be true. **READING THE DOCUMENT:** `document-reference.ts` gains kind `'invoice'`, `dateInText`/`dateFromText` (day-first for ambiguous `08/09`, ISO, written months both ways round; refuses due/delivery/despatch dates and anything outside now-10y..now+1y) and `totalFromText` (labels most-specific-first, LAST money figure on the label's line, two decimal places compulsory). **Trap:** `\btotal\b` matches inside **Sub-total** - a hyphen is a word boundary - so the character before the match is checked with `/[A-Za-z-]$/`. New `guessInvoiceDetails()` never throws; every field independently null. `app/api/admin/bills/scan/route.ts` reads a picked file and **stores nothing** - the bytes go up against the bill after Save, so a scan on a bill nobody entered leaves no orphan in Media. **PORTAL:** own route, not a third kind on `/portal/upload` (it writes a bill, not a document). `config.portalInvoicesEnabled` defaults **false** - the other two portal switches default true, and the difference is that this one writes what you owe somebody. Lines are priced off the ORDER (`portalInvoiceLines`), never off anything sent; over-invoicing is **REFUSED here** (the module's flag-never-refuse rule is about copying a claim off paper at this end) via `qtyProblem(..., 'invoicing')`; the bill is created with `userId=null`, `source='PORTAL'`, status DRAFT, and **never reaches the books** - only an approve does. The **file is stored before the bill is created**, deliberately: a duplicate invoice number is the failure that actually happens and it leaves a spare copy of a document we already have, where the other order loses the file. **ADMIN UI:** `LineDraft` gains `included` and every order line is drawn ticked or not, with **Tick everything on the order** / **Untick them all**, a header checkbox with `indeterminate`, and `linesFromBill` now appends the order's uncovered lines UNTICKED so a part-invoice can be corrected. New **Total, as their invoice states it** field, compared live against `totals.total`. **`setJob('slips')`** after a portal despatch, not `open('slips')`, which would wipe the thank-you it opens over. **LIVE SQL GATE:** 3 new cases in `sql-guards.live.test.ts` - `npm run test:po-sql` **8/8 real PASS** against a throwaway OVH database. It caught a genuine one: **a `numeric` column comes back as `Prisma.Decimal` and stringifies without trailing zeros** (`'590'`, not `'590.00'`), so the mismatch sentence now goes through `<Money>`. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` **6056 passed / 385 skipped / 0 failed**, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` **4/4 real PASS** (columns were added), `npm run test:po-sql` **8/8 real PASS**. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's. Wiki `Purchase-Orders.md`: the status table, Entering one, the match, Orders that close themselves, the portal list, a new **Letting suppliers invoice you through the link**, and Settings.)

Press Reply on the fourth message of nine and the ninth message's body was quoted under the answer, and `In-Reply-To`/`References` threaded against it too, so the reply landed at the end of the customer's thread rather than against what it answered. **NEW MODULE MIGRATION `migrations/049_draft_in_reply_to.sql`:** `uin_drafts.in_reply_to_message_id TEXT` + FK to `uin_messages` `ON DELETE SET NULL` (a draft must outlive the message it answers - losing somebody's writing is the worse bug). Idempotent, TEXT only, so the backup schema-coverage backstop needs no new branch. **`cactus.module.json` teardown reordered:** `uin_drafts` moves ahead of `uin_messages`, which the existing `teardown.test.ts` "lists children before their parents" guard caught the moment the FK landed. **`lib/db.ts`:** `getQuotableMessage(id, threadId?)` is now SCOPED - `AND "direction" <> 'note'` always, plus `AND "thread_id" = $2` when a conversation is given. The id arrives from a browser and the body it names is about to be quoted into outgoing post, so an id from another inbox must find nothing, and an internal note must never be quotable at all. `mapDraft`, `DraftInput` and all three arms of `saveDraft` (UPDATE / INSERT / ON CONFLICT) carry the new column. **`lib/send.ts`:** `prepareSend` prefers the named parent, scoped to the thread, and falls back to the newest when it finds nothing (deleted message, merged thread) rather than refusing a send and losing the writing. **CLIENT:** `composer-open.tsx` `Opened` gains `messageId` and `toggle(mode, messageId?)` - same mode on a DIFFERENT message re-aims rather than closing; `MessageMenu` takes `messageId` and passes it on Reply / Reply all / Forward; `ThreadPane` passes `message.direction === 'note' ? null : message.id`; `ComposerOpen` hands it to `Composer` as `requestedReplyToId`; `Composer` holds `replyToId` (initialised from the prop or the draft), re-aims on each later press, sends it, saves it on the draft and clears it after a send. **`lib/validation.ts`** `DraftBody`, the module's `app/api/drafts/route.ts` (only on a conversation) and `lib/scheduled-send.ts` (so a reply set for Monday quotes what it was written against) carry it end to end. **NEW TESTS:** 5 in `lib/send.test.ts` (quotes the answered message, threads against it, looks it up scoped, falls back when it has gone, unchanged when nobody said which) and NEW `lib/quoted-message.live.test.ts` (6, gated on `RUN_INBOX_QUOTE=1` + `OVH_*`, `npm run test:inbox-quote`; a skip is a FAIL) because the migration and the scoped SELECT are raw SQL and no typecheck, lint or build ever executes one. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint modules/unified-inbox` clean, `vitest run modules/unified-inbox` **1104 passed / 0 failed / 241 skipped**, backup schema-coverage 1 passed, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` **4 passed against a real throwaway database** (a column was added), `RUN_INBOX_SCHEDULE_GUARDS=1` scheduled-send live **20 passed** (it executes `saveDraft`'s three arms), and the new live suite **6 passed** against `cactus_rt_uinquote_*` on the OVH VPS. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's. Wiki `Unified-Inbox.md` gains **Which message you are answering**; module README's reading-and-replying bullet says the same in a sentence.)

Brevo files an `opened` event against that send, the webhook at `app/api/webhooks/brevo` comes back through `normaliseBrevoEvent`, and `opened_at` / `open_count` on our own row record the SENDER reading their own outbox. **NEW `modules/unified-inbox/lib/tracking-beacons.ts`:** `isOpenBeacon(url)` and `isClickWrapper(url)`, pure string functions with **no imports at all** - it is reached from a client component (`LinkPeek.tsx`), so it must not drag `remote-images.ts` and its `node:dns` into the browser graph.
