New `lib/proforma-stage.ts` derives it instead - `proformaStage`/`orderStatusLabel`/`proformaWaitsOnUs`, applying to `SENT` ONLY (once acknowledged, that is the more useful fact) - and `PoOrderSummary` gained three plain booleans `proformaRequired`/`proformaReceived`/`proformaPaid`, derived once by a new `proformaFacts()` in `lib/db.ts` so the list and the order can never disagree; `proformaReceived` is a filed document OR a recorded date, and remembering only the first is how a posted-in proforma reads as still waiting. `StatusBadge` takes optional `label`/`tone` overrides, new `OrderStatusBadge` fills them in, and the RECEIVED stage - the one that is ours to move - wears the warning tone in a list of forty orders. Used on the order screen, the Orders list and shop's own order panel (`listPosForShopOrder` gained the same three columns); NOT on Receiving/Reports (by then it is settled), the status FILTER (which must offer real statuses), the printed document, or the supplier's portal (which has its own proforma line). **Accounts department:** migration `010_accounts_email.sql` adds `po_suppliers.accounts_email` + `proforma_paid_to_accounts BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (001 carries both), through `SupplierBody`/`toSupplierInput`/`SupplierInput`/both raw SQL statements/`SuppliersScreen`. New `proformaPaidRecipients()` in `lib/email.ts` is the ONLY email in the module that asks: it swaps the `to` for the accounts address, KEEPS the copy-to list (that is the site's own list, not the supplier's ordering address), and falls back to the ordering address wherever the switch is on but the address is missing. The switch is forced false without an address in both the form and `toSupplierInput`, so it can never send the note nowhere. 5 new tests in `proforma-stage.test.ts`. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean for this module, module suite green, backup round-trip 4/4 real PASS. **Two unrelated things seen in the shared tree and left alone:** `modules/filters-for-shop/lib/variation-links.ts` is deleted in another session's working tree, which is 20 `tsc` errors in `filters-for-shop`/`product-discovery-tool` and stops three `purchase-orders` test files COLLECTING through the generated extension-point graph (proved green with that one specifier stubbed: 92/92). And **two live-SQL gates fight over the same server**: `test:backup-roundtrip` and `test:filters-sql` both call `dropStaleTestObjects`, which sweeps every `cactus_rt_%` database - so a concurrent run in another session kills this one mid-schema with `57P01 terminating connection due to administrator command`, four times in a row, until a clear window. The sweep is prefix-scoped but not RUN-scoped.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**A folder can now own its post, whoever the email was addressed to** - `unified-inbox` v0.1.45, uncommitted working tree. Routing was Delivered-To -> To -> Cc -> catch-all, so an email dragged into the purchasing@ folder by hand and addressed to an old address the site has never served landed under Not filed. New per-inbox setting `folder_owns_mail` (migration `024_folder_owns_mail.sql`, plus the column in 001 for fresh installs; BOOLEAN, so the backup schema-coverage backstop needs no new branch): `routeToInbox`/`routeSentToInbox`/`placeMessage` take an optional `folderInboxId` and a new `matchedOn: 'folder'` pass that sits BELOW the three address passes and ABOVE the catch-all. Deliberately NOT in `NAMED_RECIPIENT`, so a message from one of our own addresses stays outbound - owning a folder settles whose conversation a message joins, never which way it faces. `folderOwnersFor` lives in `sync-plan.ts` (keyed lower-case; two inboxes claiming one folder means NOBODY owns it, because filing a customer's mail on a coin toss is worse than not filing it) and `syncConnection` hands the answer to each `syncFolder`. **`adoptUnroutedFolderMail` in `lib/db.ts`** sweeps what is already there when the setting is switched on - a message is parsed once and its location remembered, so nothing else would ever go back for the email somebody turned the setting on for; only threads with no inbox and no `provider_module` and a message from that connection in that folder move, and their `routed_on` goes 'none' -> 'folder' so they stop counting as post nobody reads. PATCH `/api/m/unified-inbox/admin/inboxes/[id]` returns `adopted` and the panel says how many moved. New live gate `modules/unified-inbox/lib/folder-adoption.live.test.ts` (`RUN_INBOX_ADOPTION_GUARDS=1`, folded into `npm run test:inbox-guards`) - **7/7 real PASS** against a throwaway `cactus_rt_uinada_*` database; backup round-trip **4/4 real PASS** with 024 applied. 12 new unit tests across `addresses.test.ts` and `sync-plan.test.ts`; `npm run typecheck` clean for these files, `eslint` clean, 5181 tests green. **Note for anyone running the VPS gates:** `dropStaleTestObjects` sweeps the whole `cactus_rt_` prefix, so two agents running any two live suites at once destroy each other's throwaway databases mid-run - the failures look like `terminating connection due to administrator command` and `role ... does not exist`. Wait for the server to go quiet rather than believing the first red.)

The card link was a variation's own child-product address, which `shop-variations`' product-page resolver opens as the parent page on that variation's WHOLE combination - so ticking Finish landed the shopper on a product page that had also answered colour, arms and base for them, from a variation they never chose. The link is now the card's own href plus one option parameter per ticked filter, read back by `shop-variations/lib/url-selection.ts` (`?finish=oak&colour=blue`); every unticked option stays unchosen. New `modules/filters-for-shop/lib/option-param.ts` (`optionParamKey`, `optionParamFragment`, `withOptionParams` - the slugify mirrors shop-variations' own, spelled out rather than imported because the two modules share tables, not code) plus `lib/option-param.test.ts`. `FltSwap` loses `href` and gains `param: string | null`; the OPTION swap query returns `o."name"`/`ov."slug"` instead of the child slug; `FltCombo` is `{ filterIds }` only and the fold query drops its `shp_products` join; `getProductFilterMatches(productIds, groups)` no longer takes a `ProductUrlStyle` (4 call sites). `swap-pack` gains an interned param table `q` and `p` is `Record<productId, FltPackedSwap[]>` (was `[hrefPrefix, swaps]`); `commonPrefix` and `lib/variation-links.ts` are gone with the href folding, as is `pickVariationIndex` - both `dressCard`s take `params` instead of a `deepHref`. `internVariations` no longer emits `links`, so `FltVariationIndex` is `{ filterIds, combos, byProduct }`. pdt's `requiresModules` floor for `filters-for-shop` goes to 0.1.50 (it imports `option-param`, absent in 0.1.49). `npm run typecheck`, `eslint`, 177 module tests green; `npm run test:filters-sql` / `test:discovery-sql` real PASS: `npm run test:filters-sql` 4/4 and `npm run test:discovery-sql` 11/11, both against throwaway `cactus_rt_*` databases. **Also in pdt v0.1.8:** the desktop "Narrow down" pill now leaves when the products do. `DiscoveryShell` gains a second `IntersectionObserver`, on `gridRef`, setting `productsScrolledPast` by the same arithmetic the questions panel uses the other way up (not intersecting AND `boundingClientRect.bottom <= 0`); `showJump` is `jumpLayout && questionsScrolledOff && !productsScrolledPast`. Re-observed on `windowIds`/`extraCards` so a fetched page moving the grid's bottom edge cannot leave a stale observation behind. The grid, not `.pdt-results`: the finish CTA under it is not a product to narrow. Phone/tablet `.pdt-bar` is untouched.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Supplier paperwork now files from BOTH ends, and reads its own reference number off the file** - `purchase-orders` v0.1.30, uncommitted working tree. New admin route `app/api/admin/orders/[id]/documents/route.ts`: POST multipart files a `proforma`, an `acknowledgement` or a `payment-proof` against an order, PATCH takes `proformaRef`/`ackRef`/`proformaAmount` typed in or corrected (an empty string CLEARS - hence new flat writers `setProformaReference`/`setAcknowledgementReference`/`setProformaAmount` beside the upload's COALESCE write, which must never clear what somebody typed). Permissions split on purpose: filing their paperwork is `canCreate`, the proof of payment is `canApprove || canBills`, because it is part of paying. Migration `009_payment_proof.sql` adds `po_orders.proforma_payment_proof_media_id` + `proforma_proof_sent_at` (001 carries both for fresh installs); `clearProformaPayment` now nulls the sent stamp too, and `lib/media-usage-provider.ts` gained a fourth UNION arm so the library never offers a proof of payment up as clutter. `POST .../proforma` takes `sendProof`, fetches the filed proof through core's `downloadMedia` (new `mediaAttachment` in `lib/proforma.ts`) and hangs it on the existing *Proforma paid* email - `sendProformaPaid` gained a `proof` parameter and adds its own "attached" sentence only when one actually travels; `proforma_proof_sent_at` is stamped only on a successful send. With `sendProof` the route no longer short-circuits on `alreadyPaid`, which is what makes **Send the proof again** work on an order paid last week. **New `lib/pdf-text.ts`: a PDF text reader written by hand** - a module cannot add an npm dependency, core's package.json is what an install installs - covering brute-force object scan (no xref parsing, so appended and compressed-xref files still read), FlateDecode or no filter, the standard security handler for the EMPTY user password (RC4 V1/V2 by hand, since OpenSSL 3 drops RC4 without the legacy provider, plus AESV2; R5/R6 refused rather than guessed), and text operators into lines. `lib/document-reference.ts` turns that into a guess: labelled search first (*Invoice No.*, *Sales No.*, value beside the label or on the next line), filename second. It refuses dates, money, camera/scanner names (`IMG_0042`) and - the trap that prompted the rule - **our own PO number, which is on their acknowledgement under *Cust Order No.***. Applied at both doors: the admin upload and `app/api/public/portal/upload/route.ts`, and in both only where the box was left empty AND the order carries no reference already. The two real supplier PDFs that prompted this were verified locally and are NOT in the repo (a real customer's address is on them); the committed tests build a PDF of the same shape instead - `pdf-text.test.ts` (6) and `document-reference.test.ts` (13). `ProformaCard` in `OrderScreen.tsx` rebuilt around four rows (proforma, proof of payment, paid, acknowledgement) with `FiledDocument`/`FilePicker`/`ReferenceField` helpers; `ReferenceField` is KEYED on the server value at both call sites rather than syncing with an effect, which `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rightly refuses. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` clean, 5164 tests green, and the backup round-trip gate run for real against a throwaway OVH database: 4/4 PASS, no skip.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**A module's automatic email is filed as a conversation in the inbox it went out from** - core + `unified-inbox`, uncommitted working tree. New core extension point `core.outbound-email-record` (`lib/email/record.ts`, `OutboundEmailRecorder`/`RecordedOutboundEmail`), offered from `sendEmail` after a successful dispatch and only when the payload carries `moduleName` - so core's own auth and system mail is never offered. Every recorder installed is offered it, not first-wins; failures are caught and logged; `attachments` is the post-`usableAttachments` list, so a filed copy cannot claim a document that was dropped for size. Unified Inbox registers `unifiedInboxOutboundRecord` (`lib/outbound-record.ts`, `serverOnly`): looks up `getModuleSenderInboxId(moduleName)`, does nothing without one, otherwise `createOutboundThread` + `insertOutboundMessage` (`authorUserId: null` - `OutboundMessageInput.authorUserId` widened to `string | null`) + `settleDelivery` with the cleaned provider Message-ID, which is what makes the supplier's reply thread onto it via `threadsForMessageIds`' `provider_message_id` arm. Attachments are stored under the module's own prefix through the existing `cacheAttachment`, and skipped whole - `hasAttachments: false` - on a site with no media provider, rather than leaving a paperclip that opens onto an error. **No migration**: every column already existed. New tests `lib/email/record.test.ts` (6) and `modules/unified-inbox/lib/outbound-record.test.ts` (6); `lib/email/send.test.ts` now stubs `@/lib/email/identity` and `@/lib/email/record` - resolving them for real drags the whole generated extension-point graph in and blew the 5s timeout under a parallel run. `tsc --noEmit` clean for these files (two pre-existing `FltCombo` errors in the checked-out `filters-for-shop`/`product-discovery-tool` trees are unrelated), `eslint .` clean, 5138 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**A filtered or discovered card now opens the product on the variation that answers EVERY choice, not the first one** - `filters-for-shop` v0.1.49 + `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.7, uncommitted working tree. Both shells already swapped the card link to a variation's own deep link (`preselectOnClick`, on by default and on in the live database) - but the link was `swapList[0]`, the representative variation of the FIRST ticked filter, chosen with no reference to the other ticks. Answer three questions and the product page opened on one of your answers and two variations' worth of somebody else's. `getProductFilterMatches` now keeps a representative variation per COMBINATION as well as per filter: `FltProductMatches.combos` is `Map<string, FltCombo[]>` (`{ filterIds, href }`) instead of `Map<string, string[][]>`, the fold query gaining a `DISTINCT ON (product_id, combo)` over a three-level derived table so the earliest variation in the owner's order wins and the outer `ORDER BY variant_position` puts a product's combinations back into that order. `internVariations` emits `links` alongside `byProduct` - `[shared prefix, one tail per entry]`, folded with `swap-pack`'s now-exported `commonPrefix`, entry-for-entry aligned with the ids (the duplicate-combination case keeps the FIRST variation's link, which is what makes the alignment true). New pure helpers in `filter-logic.ts`: `pickCombinationFilters` (one tick per group - two ticks in one group are alternatives no variation is both of), `pickVariationIndex` (first combination carrying the lot; a wanted filter no variation carries at all - a price band, a parent spec - is dropped rather than ruling everything out), and `comboFilterIds` for the two RSCs that only want the maths' view. New `lib/variation-links.ts` decodes the fold. Fallback chain in both `dressCard`s is deepHref -> first swap -> the card's own href, so nothing regresses where no single variation fits. **Second defect, discovery only:** the browse steps' own filters (`resolved.lockedFilterIds`) were absent from the card dressing entirely, so choosing "Rivet Forge" as your sort of chair swapped no photo and preselected nothing - only the step-three ticks counted. New `chosen` memo unions them into a selection used by the dressing pass ONLY; `selected` stays the shopper's own ticks, because the questions, the counts and the address must never offer a locked filter as something to untick. **Cross-module ordering:** pdt's `requiresModules` floor for `filters-for-shop` goes to 0.1.49 - it imports `variation-links`, which does not exist in 0.1.48. New gate `npm run test:filters-sql` (`RUN_FILTERS_SQL=1`, `modules/filters-for-shop/lib/matching-sql.live.test.ts`) provisions a throwaway `cactus_rt_flt_*` database on the OVH VPS, applies core + shop + shop-variations + filters and runs the matcher for real: 4/4 real PASS, no skip. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5138 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**The floating narrow-down button was not exempt from core's button fill, so one colour setting gave two colours** - `product-discovery-tool`, uncommitted working tree. `.pdt-jump` is a lone `<button>` inside `.pdt-features`, which carries no `PDT_UNSTYLED` of its own (the marker sits on the module's chrome CONTAINERS, and this button has no container), so core's `main button:not([data-cactus-unstyled] …):hover{background:var(--btn-hover-bg) !important}` beat `--pdt-bar-btn-hover-bg` on the desktop twin while the phone's button - inside the marked `.pdt-bar` - honoured it. Same setting, two colours, which is exactly how it was reported. Attribute now on the button itself; the selector exempts the element as well as the subtree. New `components/public/chrome-exempt.test.ts`, 3 cases, reading the shell's SOURCE: no stylesheet, type or render test can see an attribute that is absent, and the failure mode is a colour rather than an error. **Verified the guard fails without the fix before keeping it** - removed the attribute, watched the case go red, restored. It also pins the other half of the rule: `.pdt-wrap` must NOT be marked, or the exemption (which covers a whole subtree with no way back in) would take the site's hover off every product card's buttons in the results. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5115 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**Custom Puck fields have to draw their own labels** - `product-discovery-tool`, uncommitted working tree. Puck renders no label for a `type: 'custom'` field - the render owns the whole row - so `flowField` and `layoutField` arrived in the sidebar as bare controls, the card-layout one reading only "Use shop default" at the bottom of the panel with nothing saying what it was. Both now wrap in a local `labelled()`, the same shape `purchase-orders`' `po-shared.tsx` uses. The four colour fields added in 0.1.3 were never affected: `SiteColourField` takes a `label` prop and draws it itself, which is why they were written that way. **Reported as "the colour fields are missing", and they are not:** a cache-busted fetch of the live homepage returns 0.1.3's stylesheet (`.pdt-bar{...background:none;border:0}`, questions `position:sticky`), and the deployment's build log shows `checkout-modules` cloning this module at v0.1.3 and applying `002_step_headings`. My first fetch had `age: 1831` and returned the pre-update sheet, which I read as "the site is running 0.1.1" - wrong, and the correction is the point: **on that host a plain GET can be half an hour stale, so cache-bust before concluding anything about what is deployed.** The remaining explanation for a sidebar with no colour fields is an editor tab held open across the deploy, serving the previous build's JS.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**The question bar stops sticking; a floating button fetches it back, and the step heading stops drawing a box** - `product-discovery-tool`, uncommitted working tree. The across-the-top questions were `position:sticky` with an `.is-stuck` ground; both are gone, and with them the sentinel, its absolute-positioning rule, the `rootMargin` offset arithmetic and `StepFeatures`' `collapseSignal` (shutting the open question on scroll only existed because a pinned full-width one covered the products - nothing is pinned now, so a shopper who scrolls back finds it as they left it). In their place: an IntersectionObserver on the PANEL itself - `!isIntersecting && boundingClientRect.bottom <= 0` is exactly "the questions are off the top", with the bottom check ruling out the below-the-fold reading - and a `.pdt-jump` button, `position:fixed;top:var(--pdt-sticky-top,7rem);left:50%`, carrying `.pdt-bar-btn` as well so ONE set of block colour settings dresses the phone's bar button and this one. It calls `scrollIntoView`, and `scroll-margin-top:var(--pdt-sticky-top,7rem)` on the panel is what stops that landing under the site's fixed header. `.pdt-features.pdt-pos-top` stays `display:block` - it no longer has to be, but a two-row grid is not better and the reason is one edit away from mattering again. **Second fix, unrelated: `.pdt-step-title` draws no focus ring.** The heading takes focus on every step change so the step is announced; Safari hands a programmatically focused element `:focus-visible` after an ordinary mouse CLICK, which put a box round "Which sort of desks?" and left it there through every later step. The heading is not tabbable, so there is no keyboard journey to indicate and the announcement - the actual point of the focus move - is untouched. Also removed a reflex `@media (prefers-reduced-motion:reduce){.pdt-jump{transition:none}}`: the button has no transition, and a SECOND reduced-motion block broke the existing test, which finds the first one - a good failure, since the next rule added to the wrong block would have been silently unprotected. `discovery-css.test.ts` at 15 cases. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5112 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**The narrow-down button floats, and a block can colour it** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.3, core pin 0.5.1494. `.pdt-bar` loses `background:var(--color-bg)` and its top rule: painted, it showed as a strip of page colour above and below the button wherever the section behind it was another shade, which on the homepage's subtle-background section it always is. The button carries `box-shadow:0 6px 18px rgba(0,0,0,.18)` instead, which is what lifts it off the products it floats over. Four new block fields - `barButtonBg`, `barButtonText`, `barButtonHoverBg`, `barButtonHoverText` - each a `SiteColourField` from `@/lib/puck/fields/registry` (the proxy, as `purchase-orders` imports it), so they offer the site's own palette as `var(--color-N)` AND carry a dark-mode arm encoded as `light-dark(l, d)`: the site sets `color-scheme`, the browser resolves the arm, and nothing on the render side has to know which mode is on - one value covers both, which is why this is four fields and not eight. Delivered as CUSTOM PROPERTIES on `.pdt-bar` rather than inline declarations on the button (a hover cannot be written inline, and the sheet already owns every state), with the module's own colour as each rule's fallback so a block setting none is byte-identical to before. The border follows the fill wherever one is set - a solid button ringed in the page's border colour reads as a mistake, and a fifth question nobody wants to answer is not the fix. Hovered text falls back to the RESTING text before the theme's, so colouring the fill alone still leaves a readable label. Two cases added to `discovery-css.test.ts`, 16 in all. `tsc`, `eslint .` green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**Choice and option pictures come from the media library, not a url box** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.2, core pin v0.5.1493. New `components/admin/PicturePicker.tsx` - thumbnail, Choose/Replace/Remove, over `shop`'s own `MediaPickerModal` (reuse, not a second picker; the module already hard-depends on shop and this adds nothing to it, so the module-to-module isolation rule is untouched) - replaces the plain "Picture URL" inputs in `FlowScreen`'s node editor and `GuidanceScreen`'s note editor. What is STORED is still the url, because that is what the storefront renders and what both media mechanisms key on; the picker only stops it being typed, which is what puts the picture in the library's folders and therefore inside `discoveryMediaUsageProvider` and `discoveryMediaReferenceRewriter`. `HINT` moved into admin/shared for the field note. **The reported 404 was not a rewriter failure:** traced on the live database - the Pedestals node had been rewritten `.jpeg` -> `.webp` by this module's own rewriter when the file was optimised, the new url returns 200, and the dead one Chris saw was the old address in a cached page. The real hazard the trace turned up is the FILE: `data/product_discovery_2026_09_03/deskwell-furniture-finder.json` still held the pre-optimise url for that node AND the original pictures for four more that had since been changed by hand in the admin, so a re-import would have resurrected one dead url and reverted four deliberate edits. All five copied back out of the live flow, and both READMEs now say that a saved flow file's pictures go stale the moment one is edited on the site - download fresh from the Guidance tab before re-applying. `tsc`, `eslint .` green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**A flow writes its own step headings** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.2, core pin v0.5.1493. "What are you looking for?", "Which sort of desks?" and "What matters to you?" were literals in the shell; they are the flow's words, not the module's, so `pdt_flows` gains `first_step_heading`, `later_step_heading`, `features_heading` - all TEXT, all nullable, **no SQL DEFAULT on purpose**: empty means "the wording the module ships", so improving that copy later reaches every site that has not overridden it, which a baked-in default never would. NEW numbered migration `002_step_headings.sql` (idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) with the same three columns added to `001_initial.sql` for fresh installs - the overlap is harmless and the ledger runs 002 once. Resolution is a pure `stepHeading()` in `lib/flow.ts` beside `PDT_DEFAULT_HEADINGS` and `PDT_CHOICE_TOKEN`, 5 new cases in `flow.test.ts`: the later-step template substitutes `{choice}` with the label AS WRITTEN (the derived fallback lowercases it mid-sentence, but an owner's own sentence may start with it), a template naming no choice is used verbatim at every depth, and a whitespace-only override counts as none - an owner clearing the box leaves `''`, not null. Carried through `PdtFlow`, `FLOW_COLUMNS`, `rowToFlow`, `PdtFlowUpdate`/`updateFlow` (tri-state CASE WHEN, as its neighbours), the PUT route's zod, the Flow tab's settings form (three inputs, the defaults as placeholders so an owner sees what they are replacing, plus a new `HINT` style in admin/shared), and the import/export file so a flow still moves between sites whole. **Both live gates run, both real passes:** `npm run test:discovery-sql` 11/11 (raw SQL is executed by no standard gate, and this change edits `FLOW_COLUMNS`, `updateFlow` and the import upsert) and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` 4/4 (module migration touching columns). Note the round-trip's own module set does not include this module, so the new columns' coverage comes from `lib/backup/schema-coverage.test.ts`, which parses every `modules/*/migrations/*.sql` and passed in the ordinary suite. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5111 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**The questions across the top become a sticky filter bar, and nothing hides behind "More options" any more** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.2, core pin v0.5.1493. `StepFeatures` drops the secondary fold entirely: every question renders, importance now decides ORDER alone (primary first), and the `showSecondary` state and `.pdt-secondary-toggle` rules go with it. In `startCollapsed` mode - which is the across-the-top layout on a wide screen, and only that - opening a question closes the one before it, because an open question now takes `grid-column:1/-1` and lays its options out in `repeat(auto-fit,minmax(min(100%,215px),1fr))`, so two open would be the wall of ticks the layout exists to avoid. The bar is `position:sticky;top:var(--pdt-sticky-top,7rem);z-index:5`, the variable named after filters' own `--flt-sticky-top` and defaulting to the same 7rem. **`.pdt-features.pdt-pos-top` had to stop being a one-column grid and become `display:block`** (gap -> margin): a grid item's containing block is its grid area and a sticky box cannot leave its containing block, so the bar sat in row one of a two-row grid pinned to its own height and stuck to nothing at all. Filters' sidebar works precisely because its panel's grid area is the full height of the results beside it. **Stuck is detected with a sentinel, not a scroll handler**: `position:sticky` fires no event, so a zero-height absolutely-positioned `.pdt-sticky-sentinel` sits at the top of `.pdt-features` (absolute because in the grid flow it would be a row of its own and the 20px gap would push the bar down for a marker with no height) and an IntersectionObserver watches it with `rootMargin: -<offset>px 0 0 0`, where the offset is read off the bar's own computed `top` - so "the sentinel has left" means "the bar has stuck" to the pixel, at whatever offset the site sets, instead of the header's height later. On sticking, `collapseSignal` increments and `StepFeatures` shuts everything (render-phase adjustment, not an effect: `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` forbids the effect form and an effect would paint the open question once more first) and `.is-stuck` gives the bar a ground, a shadow and a 78vh cap with its own scroll. Padding is deliberately identical stuck and not - a sticky box keeps its space in the flow, so resizing it on stick shunts the page under it. `questionsStuck` is DERIVED from `stuckLayout && sentinelPassed` rather than reset in the effect, for the same lint rule. Verified against the live homepage's section wrapper: core clips a Section's overflow only for radius, background image, overlay, shadow or grid-scan (`config.core.tsx` ~1212), and `section-finder` has none of them, so sticky is not trapped. `discovery-css.test.ts` at 13 cases. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5105 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**Browse steps: square pictures, a hover lift, a foot that fills the gap, and a starting sort** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.1, core pin v0.5.1489. `StepBrowse` moves "Compare these" / "Not sure yet" INSIDE the `.pdt-browse` grid as a final item, so an unfilled cell at the end of a short last row holds them (`is-beside`, stacked and centred) and a full last row pushes them to a spanning centred row (`is-below`, `grid-column:1/-1`). Which one cannot be decided in CSS - `auto-fit` picks the column count from the container's width - so `useGridColumns` reads the used `grid-template-columns` back off the element under a `ResizeObserver` and counts the tracks; `live.length % columns !== 0` is the gap test, and before the first measurement it renders `is-below`, the arrangement that is right at any count. Layout effect, not passive: after paint the move is a visible jump. Two new block settings: `firstStepFoot` ('show'/'hide', defaulting to HIDE so a block saved before the field existed gets the plainer step) gates that foot on the FIRST browse step only - deeper steps always have it - because skipping step one of a catalogue-wide flow asks for the whole shop, while step one of a category-scoped flow is a real question; and `defaultSort`, the same `FLT_SORT_OPTIONS` list the dropdown offers with 'recommended' standing in for the empty value, defaulting to `best-selling`. Sort is applied SERVER-side exactly as `ShopFilterGrid.rsc` does it (`sortValueFromParam` then `sortProductIds` over `productIds`, and the resulting `orderedIds` feeds the cards drawn, the `orderIndex` used to intern shelf membership AND the shell's `serverOrder`, so there is one answer to what order these are in) - sorting only in the browser would rearrange the grid under the shopper on hydration. `DiscoveryShell` starts `sort` at `defaultSort` and writes the sort param only when it differs, spelling "Recommended" as `FLT_SORT_RECOMMENDED_PARAM`, again mirroring filters. `.pdt-choice` gains shop's own card hover value for value (`translateY(-4px)`, `0 8px 30px rgba(0,0,0,.10)`, `.25s ease`) plus a `prefers-reduced-motion` block that drops the transform; `.pdt-choice-pic` / `.pdt-choice-icon` go 4:3 -> 1:1. `discovery-css.test.ts` at 10 cases. Deskwell data: the five root nodes' `explainer` cleared so step one carries no "What is this?" - the link is driven by that column alone - re-imported live, 46/62/107 unchanged otherwise, previous file kept as `deskwell-furniture-finder.v1-with-step1-explainers.json`. The runner in that folder had `pathToFileURL(new URL(...).pathname)` for its CSS hook, which double-encodes a path containing a space and only failed once the file moved under "Git Local"; now `register('./css-hooks.mjs', import.meta.url)`. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5096 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**The guided flow stops borrowing the site's button chrome, and the block gains four settings** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.1, core pin v0.5.1489. **The amber hover was never this module's**: core's `lib/design/tokens.ts` paints `main button:not([data-cactus-unstyled] …):hover{background:var(--btn-hover-bg) !important}` and recolours the text only on elements carrying `data-cactus-hover-fg`, so every button the wizard draws - a browse card, an answer chip, a "More about X" link - flipped to the theme gold with its own text colour intact and unreadable. `!important` cannot be outranked, so the fix is not to match: new `PDT_UNSTYLED` in `lib/types.ts`, spread onto the ten chrome containers (`pdt-progress`, `pdt-chips`, `pdt-browse`, `pdt-browse-foot`, `pdt-empty`, `pdt-questions`, `pdt-toolbar`, `pdt-picks`, `pdt-recovery`, `pdt-cards-failed`, `pdt-pager`, `pdt-bar`, `pdt-dialog`). Deliberately NOT on `.pdt-wrap`: shop's product cards render inside it and their buttons ARE the site's chrome - the exemption covers a whole subtree with no way back in, so a root marker would have stripped the hover off every Add to Basket in the results. Having opted out there is no fallback, so the module now owns every hover: `--color-primary-subtle` tint plus a primary border on cards, chips and the bar button; `.pdt-link:hover` and `.pdt-clear:hover` change `color` to `--color-text` and paint NO background (black on a light theme, white on a dark one, no pill behind three words); `.pdt-question-head:hover` underlines. Note core already ships the other half of this - `data-cactus-hover-fg` opts an element into `--btn-hover-text` - which is the answer for chrome that WANTS the site fill. Four block settings: `flowSlug` becomes a custom field, `components/puck/FlowPickerField.tsx`, a select over `GET {PDT_API}/flows` that falls back to a text input when the fetch fails or the stored slug names nothing, so the block runs a chosen flow on a homepage or category page instead of only on the flow's own route; `autoOpenQuestions` opens the drawer on arrival at step three, guarded three ways (only on the way IN via `onFeaturesRef`, only when `movedRef.current` so a deep link opens on the products, only when `isSheet`); `drawerOptions` puts a question's options in `repeat(auto-fit,minmax(min(100%,215px),1fr))` inside the drawer, which is three or four across a tablet and one on a phone; `questionsPosition` as before. The sticky bar loses its second button - "See N products" closed a drawer that the X and the scrim already close, and with the drawer shut it pointed at products that were already on screen and already live - so `.pdt-bar-primary` is gone with it rather than left as a dead rule. `discovery-css.test.ts` grows to 7 cases, pinning the link hover as colour-only and `pdt-opts-grid` as drawer-only. `tsc`, `eslint .` and 5093 tests green.)

A scheduled message can carry a follow-up - once it has gone the conversation is snoozed until the chase is due and assigned to whoever WROTE the draft, not whoever sent it - and inbound mail from the person a scheduled message is addressed to stands that message down rather than letting it ask a question they have just answered. Migration 022, `lib/follow-up.ts`, `holdScheduledDraftsFor`. Live SQL suite 18/18 and the backup round-trip a real PASS.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**Write it now, send it later** - `unified-inbox` v0.1.40, core pin v0.5.1478. A draft can carry a departure time and goes out on its own; the scheduled-send cron claims due rows with SKIP LOCKED and the send route's idempotency key is the draft id, so one message goes once. Previously: **A layout type added to an EXISTING module now seeds its layout - core **v0.5.1474**, `filters-for-shop` **0.1.47**.** Root cause of the supplier-pages complaint two entries down, and it is structural, not a supplier bug. `Module.layoutsSeededAt` is ONE stamp for a whole module, and `planModuleSeeds` only gives a stamped module another go when `types.every((t) => !typesWithRows.has(t))`. Deskwell's shop has 48 `shopCategory` rows, so that is false forever: **any layout type added to shop in a later update can never seed**. `shopSupplier` reached a live site with starters and no layout, the page fell back to the plain grid, and the filter panel - the whole point - was absent until the owner hand-built a layout.

Counting Layout rows cannot fix it. "No rows for this type" reads identically whether the type is new or whether the owner **deleted** its layout on purpose to fall back to the built-in page, and re-minting the latter is core redesigning a live site unasked - which is exactly what the existing narrow test was protecting. So what has actually been seeded is now recorded.

**New `Module.seededLayoutTypes TEXT[] NOT NULL DEFAULT ARRAY[]::TEXT[]`** - schema.prisma, the init migration edited in place, and `prisma/core-reconcile/033_module_seeded_layout_types.sql` for existing installs. **Back-filled EMPTY on purpose.** New pure `planTypeSeeds(moduleName, ledger, declaredTypes)` in `lib/setup/starterLayouts.ts` returns `{seed, ledger}` with three rules: an EMPTY ledger on a stamped module means it predates the column, so it **adopts everything it declares today and seeds nothing** (no live site gets a layout minted retrospectively - only types appearing after this lands are ever seeded); a real ledger seeds whatever is declared but missing from it; and **declaring nothing means the module's code is absent from THIS build** (see isModuleInBuild), so the ledger is left exactly as it is rather than adopted empty, which would wipe it and let the next build re-seed every type.

`seedPendingModuleLayouts` gains a top-up pass before the existing full-seed loop, skipping any module that loop will handle. **It deliberately does NOT call `autoPlaceModuleBlocks`** - placement is first-install-only and re-running it would re-add marker blocks the owner has since deleted. A new layout type is not a new install. `seedModuleDefaultLayouts` takes an optional `onlyTypes`, narrowed BEFORE `planModuleSeedTemplates` so its one-per-type choice is made over the new types alone. The full-seed path now writes the ledger alongside the stamp.

**filters-for-shop 0.1.47** marks `starter-shop-supplier-filtered` `publishByDefault: true`, so the seeded supplier page is the one WITH the panel; flagged beats unflagged in `planModuleSeedTemplates`, and a shop without this module still seeds shop's plain starter. Note `moduleStarterTemplates(name)` filters by the type's OWNING module, so a contributed starter for `shopSupplier` is included when shop seeds - which is what makes this work at all.

Verified: `tsc` clean, `eslint` clean, `npm test` 4961 passed (6 new `planTypeSeeds` cases, including "keeps a type on the ledger after the owner deletes its layout"), **`npm run test:backup-roundtrip` real PASS** - mandatory here, the core init migration gained a column, and unlike the shop tables the round-trip DOES cover core's `Module`, so the new `text[]` is genuinely exercised against the `udt_name` rules. filters gate green on the tag before release.

**Standing rule now in memory**: a new Puck layout type ships with a default layout auto-created. A hardcoded fallback page is not an acceptable resting state when it cannot do what the feature was asked for.)

Previously: **The timezone sweep, pinned and finished - core **v0.5.1471** pinning `shop` **v0.1.376**, `unified-inbox` **v0.1.34**, `contact-form` **v0.1.40**, `contact-form-reply-catcher` **v0.1.13** and `advanced-shipping-for-shop` **v0.1.50**.** All five gates green on their tags, all five released. Nothing pins them and the updater only ever offers releases, so they are inert. Do not create releases for them.

## unified-inbox - attachments become Media Library items (working tree, unreleased)

Attachments were written under one flat private prefix (`media/unified-inbox/<messageId>/<attachmentId>-<name>`) with no `Media` row at all, on purpose, so that a customer's invoice could not turn up in the media picker. The site owner asked for the opposite. They are ordinary library items now, filed by correspondent.

**Where they go.** `Inbox / <their address> / Received` and `Inbox / <their address> / Sent`, built with core's own `getOrCreateFolderByPath` (the same call the shop uses for product images). Folder display names keep the real address; the storage path is whatever `resolveFolderPath` sanitises it to, and the key MUST be that one - media library folders are physical, and `lib/media/reconcile.ts` matches each path segment against the sanitised folder name. Key shape: `<mediaKeyPrefix>/<folder path>/<attachmentId>-<safeFilename>`. The attachment id stays in the name because the media Worker serves anything under `media/` to whoever asks by key, so privacy still rests on the key being unguessable.

**Sent is filed by the RECIPIENT.** `from_address` on an outbound message is our own mailbox; filing by it would put every reply the site has written into a handful of folders named after the site. `to_addresses[1]` instead.

**What stays out of the library**, because both would drown it: inline parts (`content_id IS NOT NULL`) and anything with no correspondent (an internal note). They keep the private prefix, no row, and `lib/media-private-storage.ts` still declares the folder.

- **`lib/attachment-filing.ts`** (new) - `filingFor()` (the rule, pure), `folderForFiling()`, `filedAttachmentKey()`, `releaseStoredObject()`.
- **`lib/attachments.ts`** - `cacheAttachment` is the ONE place bytes are stored (inbound first-open, a human Send, and another module's outbound record all reach it), so the folder rule is applied once rather than three times. It now mints the `Media` row, returns the key it stored under, and `libraryRowFor` reuses an existing row rather than tripping `Media.key`'s unique index when the same attachment is stored twice.
- **`lib/send.ts`** - `GatheredAttachment.staged`. A file DROPPED on a message went into storage before there was a recipient to file it under, so after the send it is filed properly; a file picked from the library and a forwarded original are left exactly where they are (moving a product photograph into a Sent folder would 404 the shop page pointing at it). The staging copy is left to the existing 7-day abandoned-upload sweep rather than deleted here - once the attachment row is re-keyed nothing points at it, which is precisely what that sweep looks for, and deleting it here would race a draft holding the same reference.
- **`lib/media-reference-rewriter.ts`** (new, `core.media-reference-rewriters`) - optimise, resize, crop, replace-file and folder rename all move a blob to a fresh key and url. Core cannot reach `uin_attachments`; without this, renaming the `Inbox` folder turns every paperclip into a 404. Matches all three columns exactly and nothing is parsed, because the de-duplicate path calls the rewriter a second time with media IDs in the key slots.
- **`lib/media-usage-provider.ts`** - now contributes `media_id` as well as `media_key`/`media_url`. See "never not in use" below.
- **`lib/attachment-backfill.ts`** (new) - `sweepAttachmentFiling({ deadline })`, 10 per pass. Read, write to the new key, and only THEN delete the old copy: interrupted between the last two, what is left is a copy nothing points at (recoverable, and the storage check offers it up); the other order loses the file. Two ways a file counts as a failure rather than a success - a null back from `cacheAttachment` (the write failed and the row still points at the old copy, which must therefore NOT be deleted) and the same key back (the filing rules declined it, so it has not moved and is still in the count). Counting either as filed on a driver that loops until the count reaches zero is a live-lock.
- **`app/api/cron/housekeeping/route.ts`** - runs it last, on `FILING_BUDGET_MS` (12s), after the sweeps somebody is actually waiting on. It is catching up on history rather than keeping up with the present, so a morning where it gets no time at all costs nothing. Reports `attachmentsFiled` / `attachmentsToFile` / `attachmentFilingFailures`.
- **No setting and no button, deliberately.** A settings section and a `POST /admin/attachments/backfill` route existed for one revision and were removed: "your files are in the wrong folder, shall I move them?" is a question a site owner has no way of answering, and being asked it is worse than not being asked it.

**The backfill reaches a live site the way every other change does**: module release, then the owner presses update, then `055` adds the columns and the nightly tidy starts moving files. Filing them by hand beforehand would mean hand-applying `055` to a live database, which is never done here, and would leave the site running code that knows nothing about `owns_object` against attachments whose keys had just moved.

**`055_attachment_library_filing.sql` (NEW migration).** `media_id TEXT` and `owns_object BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` on `uin_attachments`, a partial index on `media_id`, and a one-off `UPDATE ... WHERE media_key LIKE '%unified-inbox/%'` to draw the ownership line for historic rows - the folder name IS the test for anything written before the column existed, and this is the only moment it can be applied.

**The bug `owns_object` fixes, which predates all of this.** `media_key` held bytes this module wrote AND library items somebody picked, and all four delete paths deleted the blob regardless: emptying the bin removed a product photograph from storage while the shop's library row sat there pointing at nothing. `releaseStoredObject` is now the only way `app/api/messages/[id]/remove`, `app/api/bin/empty`, `lib/retention.ts` and `lib/person-data.ts` remove anything - it refuses outright on a file the module does not own, and takes the `Media` row along with the bytes on one it does. Bytes first, row second, for the module's usual reason.

**One object, two rows - forwards.** `gatherAttachments` re-uses `attachment.mediaKey` for `includeOriginalAttachments`, so a forward's row points at the original's bytes. Three places had to know:
- The migration hands ownership to the **earliest row per `media_key`** (`DISTINCT ON (media_key) ... ORDER BY media_key, created_at, id`), not to every row under the private folder. The message the file arrived on owns it.
- `releaseStoredObject` asks `attachmentKeySharedElsewhere` and does nothing while another row still holds the key. When BOTH rows go in one sweep the rows are still present during the object loop, so neither deletes and the object is left as a leftover the storage check offers up - the safe direction, because bytes that vanish while a paperclip still shows are a support ticket nobody can answer.
- The backfill calls `repointAttachmentsSharingKey` before deleting the old copy, so the forward follows the bytes. It is given the new key and url and deliberately NOT the `media_id` or the ownership: one object, one library item, one owner.
A forwarded attachment therefore appears in the library once, under the folder of whoever originally sent it, rather than twice. Copying it into a Sent folder as well was the alternative and was rejected: two library items for one file, and each deletable independently of the other.

**Never "not in use".** Core decides a library item is referenced by looking for its url, key OR id in what the usage providers hand over. Url and key both MOVE (optimise, rename); the id does not. So the provider hands over all three, and an attachment cannot read as unreferenced in the window between a move and the rewriter catching up - which matters because "not in use" is the verdict that arms a bulk-delete button and the thing it would delete is a customer's paperwork. `lib/media-usage-provider.test.ts` asserts each form separately against core's real `isMediaInUse`, plus a strange-filename fallback case and a negative control so the vouching cannot quietly become "everything is in use".

**Known limit:** an inline part that arrived before migration 048 has no `content_id` (048 records it at sync time only), so an old signature logo is indistinguishable from a real attachment and will be filed. Same limit the inline-image rendering has.

**Tests:** `lib/attachment-filing.test.ts` (13 - the filing rule including Sent-by-recipient, and five `releaseStoredObject` cases covering ownership and sharing) and `lib/media-usage-provider.test.ts` (5). `lib/send.test.ts`'s db mock gains `stagedUploadKeys`.

**`lib/attachment-filing.live.test.ts` + `npm run test:inbox-attachment-filing` (`RUN_INBOX_ATTACHMENT_FILING=1`).** Ten tests against a real throwaway Postgres on the OVH VPS, because every statement here is raw SQL and NO standard gate executes one - `tsc` and `eslint` see a template string, a build never runs a query, and the module build gate compiles rather than connects. It seeds the rows BEFORE applying `055` and reads `owns_object` after, which is the only way to see what the `DISTINCT ON` actually did; it is also the only thing that executes the `to_addresses[1]` subscript in `UNFILED_ATTACHMENTS` and the `= ANY(::text[])` in `stagedUploadKeys`. A skip is a fail. The backup round-trip gate (`npm run test:backup-roundtrip`) was also run for the schema change: 4 passed, 0 skipped.

## core - the homepage stops being two pages (working tree, unreleased)

The page assigned in **Settings → General → Homepage** was served at the root by `app/(public)/page.tsx` **and** at its own slug by `app/(public)/[slug]/page.tsx`, each carrying a canonical tag pointing at itself, and `app/sitemap.ts` listed both. On Deskwell that is `deskwell.co.uk` and `deskwell.co.uk/home` - identical content, both indexed, both ranking, the site's own two results competing for the same searches.

- **`app/(public)/[slug]/page.tsx`** - new cached `getHomepageId()` (one `SiteConfig` read per request, `.catch(() => null)`). `InfoPageRoute` calls `permanentRedirect('/')` (308) when the row it found is the assigned homepage. Placed **before** the draft check, so an admin following an old link lands on the root where the draft actually renders, and outside every `try`/`catch`, because `permanentRedirect` works by throwing. `generateStaticParams` filters the homepage's slug out - prerendering a page that only ever answers with a redirect.
- **`app/sitemap.ts`** - the `InfoPage` query now selects `id` too and skips the homepage row. The bare domain is already entry one at priority 1.
- **`app/api/admin/config/route.ts`** - `revalidatePath('/', 'layout')` when `homepageId` is present in the payload. `[slug]` is held indefinitely under `revalidate = false`; without this, moving the homepage would leave the old slug redirecting for ever and the new one serving a cached copy of the homepage.

No setting, no schema change, no migration, no new permission key, no env var. The redirect follows `SiteConfig.homepageId` whatever the page's slug happens to be, so it is right on every install and hardcodes nothing about Deskwell. Wiki: `Managing-pages.md` (Setting the homepage) and `Architecture-overview.md`.

## shop - report an issue, again, from a link in an email (working tree, unreleased)

Three asks in one: a link an owner can put in an email that opens the issue-report form on the customer's own order page; a button that stays after a report rather than disappearing; and `Report damage` renamed `Report an issue`.

**More than one report open at once - `modules/shop/migrations/053_multiple_issue_reports.sql`** (new; drops `shp_order_requests_one_open_damage_idx`, adds a plain `shp_order_requests_order_status_idx` in its place so "what is open on this order" still has an index). The one-open guard exists so two approvals cannot refund the same lines twice; a `DAMAGE` request refunds nothing and names lines without spending them, so nothing double-counts when two are open. Refusing the second one only meant the customer who opened the next carton emailed instead. `canReportDamage` in `modules/shop/lib/order-requests.ts` lost its `openDamageRequest` refusal and `EligibilityInput` lost the field; `createOrderRequest` in `modules/shop/lib/db/order-requests.ts` now runs the one-open SELECT **only for `type !== 'DAMAGE'`** (the `Prisma.sql` kind switch is gone) and its 23505 catch has one message rather than two. `MemberOrderDetail.openDamageRequest` → **`openDamageRequests: ShpOrderRequestWithItems[]`**, oldest first; the order page maps a note per report, each with its own `WithdrawRequestButton`.

**The deep link - `modules/shop/lib/order-link-intent.ts`** (new). `REPORT_ISSUE_QUERY_KEY = 'report'` and `reportIssueUrl(orderUrl)` live in `lib/order-requests.ts`, built by parsing rather than appending (a guest's order link already carries `?t=`). **The bug this uncovered:** every order link in every email points at `/shop/track-order/NUMBER`, and that page threw the query string away on BOTH of its ways out - the `redirect()` for a proved viewer and the `body.path` push in `OrderAccessForm`. The delivery email's `?faq=1` link had therefore never once opened the delivery questions for anybody. `orderLinkIntentQuery()` carries an **allowlist** (`faq`, `report`, value must be the literal `1`) across the gate; `OrderAccessForm` gained an `intent` prop on its `confirm` branch. Forwarding the whole query string would have let a crafted link put arbitrary query on the authenticated page.

**The merge tags - `{{reportIssueUrl}}` / `{{#if hasReportIssueUrl}}`**. `orderUrlVars(url, config)` in `modules/shop/lib/email.ts` builds all four values from one url; `orderTrackingVars` delegates to it and `notifyOrderCustomer` uses it, so every order email carries them. **Empty where the shop has `damageReportsEnabled` off**, which takes the whole `{{#if}}` line with it - a link that lands on a page with no button is worse than no link. Listed in `mergeTags` only on the emails sent AFTER something has left the building (`status-shipped`, `partial-shipped`, `status-completed`, `delivery-slot-confirmed`, `replacement-dispatched`, `replacement-delivered`) - there is nothing to report on an undispatched order. A default sentence was added to the first three; core's `sampleValueFor` already previews anything ending `url` as a sample link, so no core change.

**Copy and chrome - `modules/shop/components/public/OrderRequestPanel.tsx`.** `Report damage` → **`Report an issue`**, and → **`Report another issue`** with its own blurb when `openReports > 0`. Form heading and `Report damage instead` follow; the order page's pending note now reads `Issue reported.`. `REQUEST_TYPE_LABEL` is untouched - the admin queue shares it. The form's action row is `justifyContent: 'flex-end'` with **Never mind to the LEFT of Send report**. New props `openReports` (count) and `openReportInitially` (the email link), the second honoured only when `damage.allowed` and something has actually been dispatched. The panel now renders unconditionally on the page and **returns `null` itself** when nothing is offered and every refusal is `silent` - a card headed "Something not right?" with nothing under it reads as a thing that failed to load.

**Checks:** `npm run typecheck` and `eslint` clean; `npx vitest run modules/shop` 1351 pass. `modules/shop/lib/db/order-requests.live.test.ts` re-run against a real throwaway OVH database - **22/22**, including the flipped expectation (`takes a second issue report while the first is still open`) and a `filter(...)` assertion that two are open; the two downstream tests that did `find(r => r.type === 'DAMAGE')` now pin by `reason`, since there are two by then. New unit file `modules/shop/lib/order-link-intent.test.ts` (8 tests). Backup round-trip **not** run: migration 053 adds no table, column, sequence or type, so serialisation is untouched.

## shop - replacement parts, and tracking one (working tree, unreleased)

A customer reports something broken (a `DAMAGE` request, which has existed since migration 045 with its own reason list, six photographs, guest access by postcode and four emails). Until now the shop's answer ended at an email. The part itself - picked, boxed, handed to a courier - was recorded nowhere, and the customer could not see where it was.

**A replacement is an ORDER OF ITS OWN, hung off the original.** Not a fudge, and not negotiable: every piece of delivery tracking in this module hangs off `shp_shipments.order_id`, which is NOT NULL and points at `shp_orders` - parcels, tracking stages, the carrier scan feed, DPD, GFS, live vehicle position, signatures, the customer's own tracking page, the dispatch emails. A bespoke `shp_replacements` table would have meant writing dispatch and tracking a second time, worse, to reach the same place. Making it an order cost **zero** new tracking code.

**Schema - `modules/shop/migrations/052_replacement_orders.sql`** (new; `001_initial.sql` edited in place for all five columns, per the fresh-install rule). All idempotent, constraints dropped-then-added so a re-run cannot stack two copies:
- `shp_orders.kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'SALE'` CHECK IN (`'SALE'`,`'REPLACEMENT'`), indexed.
- `shp_orders.parent_order_id TEXT` FK → `shp_orders(id)` ON DELETE **SET NULL**, partial index `WHERE parent_order_id IS NOT NULL`.
- `shp_order_items.replaces_order_item_id TEXT` FK → `shp_order_items(id)` SET NULL, partial index. **The whole point of the feature:** a replacement is almost never the thing that was bought - nobody sends a second chair, they send the gas lift out of it - so the part names the LINE it puts right. `parent_order_id` alone cannot say which of five lines.
- `shp_order_requests.replacement_order_id TEXT` FK → `shp_orders(id)` SET NULL. Joins the asking to the doing.
- `shp_products.parts_only BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, indexed. A column of its own rather than a second meaning for `catalogue_hidden`, which shop-variations owns and which **the admin product list also hides** - a part whose stock and cost nobody can open is not worth cataloguing.

**The standing rule, written into the migration and every query that reads it: MONEY FOLLOWS `payment_status`, COUNTS FOLLOW `kind`.** A free replacement is worth nothing and disturbs no revenue or tax figure on its own; what it would disturb is a COUNT of orders. Never written as `total > 0`, because a chargeable replacement IS real money. Filtered: `getOrdersOverview.paid_orders_30d`, `getCustomerSummary.order_count`/`paid_order_count`/`first_order_at`, `app/api/admin/customers`, `app/api/admin/dashboard-widget`, `app/api/admin/reports/revenue`. Deliberately NOT filtered: every `SUM("total")`, and the tax report (a charged part carries real VAT). `getOrdersOverview.to_dispatch` is NOT filtered either - a replacement waiting to go out is a real job.

**`lib/replacements.ts`** (new). `createReplacementOrder` resolves lines (a catalogued part by `productId`, or free text - `shp_order_items.product_id` has always been nullable), checks every named `replacesOrderItemId` belongs to the parent, refuses a replacement of a replacement (one level only), computes tax per line off the parent postcode's zone, and inserts. Born **PROCESSING + PAID** with `paid_at` now: PENDING would tell the customer on their own order page that they owe money for the shop's mistake, and the owner's dashboard that there is a payment to chase. `NO_CHARGE_METHOD = 'NONE'` on a £0 one - not a registered provider, labelled "No charge" in `order-labels.ts` and `CheckoutPaymentClient.tsx`. **No delivery is calculated**; the zone is looked up for its tax rates only, because a gas lift in a jiffy bag must not attract the two-man furniture rate (`lib/checkout.ts:600` would have). A free line is `returnable: false` with a stock sentence; a charged line keeps the product's own answer.

**Numbering.** `nextReplacementNumber` in `lib/db/orders.ts`: `DW000182-R1`, `-R2`. Never touches `shp_order_number_seq`, so no gaps and no double `nextval`. Read off the highest suffix already in use, not off a count, so a deleted one cannot hand its number on. Racy by nature - the insert retries three times and lets `shp_orders_order_number_key` settle it.

**Two SQL traps, both found by running it:**
1. `substring("order_number" FROM ${n})` - **Prisma sends a JS number as int8 and Postgres has no `substring(text, int8)` overload**. `ERROR: function pg_catalog.substring(text, bigint) does not exist`. Passed `tsc`, `eslint`, `npm test` and the build gate; failed on the first real call. Fixed with `::int`.
2. The order number prefix is an owner-typed setting, so it can hold a `%`. The `LIKE` pattern is escaped and carries `ESCAPE '\'`, or `100%-R%` reads as "100, anything, -R, anything" and hands out a number already in use.

**`modules/shop/lib/db/replacements.live.test.ts`** (new, 9 tests) covers both, plus the `kind` CHECK, the self-FK, and the counts-vs-money split. Added to `npm run test:ledger-guards` (now 11 files, 115 tests). Gated on `RUN_LEDGER_GUARDS=1`/`RUN_BACKUP_ROUNDTRIP=1` + the OVH credentials; a skip is a FAIL.

**Emails and SMS - three new triggers, `REPLACEMENT_SENT`, `REPLACEMENT_DISPATCHED` and `REPLACEMENT_DELIVERED`** (`shop.replacement-sent`, `shop.replacement-dispatched`, both transactional, both with matching SMS). Their own wording rather than the order templates: every sentence in those starts from the premise that the customer bought something, and "thanks for your order, total £0.00" reads as a second mistake on top of the first. Both lead with the PARENT order number - the replacement's own is one the customer has never seen. `lib/replacement-emails.ts` (new) holds `parentOrderVars` and `sendReplacementRaisedEmail`. `lib/shipment-email.ts` diverts a replacement's parcel before the part-dispatch branches; `lib/order-status.ts` gains `REPLACEMENT_STATUS_EMAIL_TRIGGER`, which maps SHIPPED and COMPLETED **only** - "we are getting your order ready" is about something the customer bought, and "thanks for shopping with us" is the wrong sentence to end a complaint on.

**Completion is already generic, and now proven so.** `app/api/cron/delivery-tracking` finishes an order off through `applyOrderStatusChange` once `allShipmentsDelivered` and `summary.fullyDispatched` are both true, and **none** of the three queries behind that (`listShipmentsForTrackingPoll`, `listOrdersAwaitingCompletion`, `allShipmentsDelivered`) filters on `kind` - so a replacement is polled, swept and completed exactly as a sale is, with no change needed. Asserted in the live test rather than assumed: a replacement that never reached the sweep would sit in Processing for ever with the part on the customer's doormat and nothing anywhere looking wrong. `REPLACEMENT_DELIVERED` is what goes out when it closes, with `replacementDeliveryVars` reading the day and the signature off the LATEST delivered parcel (not off the order - "delivered" is something a courier said about a box; COMPLETED is only this module's conclusion from it).

**Tracking that lands after dispatch - `054_tracking_notified.sql`** (new; mirrored into `001_initial.sql`). `shp_shipments.tracking_notified_at TIMESTAMP(3)`, the twin of `slot_notified_at` from 039 and claimed the same way (`claimTrackingNotification` - the stamp set by the statement that reads it, so two admins saving a parcel together cannot both believe they are the one sending). A courier that collects in the morning and issues the number that afternoon has already had its dispatch note sent, saying the goods left and giving no way of following them; typing the number in afterwards told the customer nothing at all.

Two new triggers, **`TRACKING_ADDED`** and **`REPLACEMENT_TRACKING_ADDED`** (+ SMS), sent from `lib/tracking-added-email.ts`. The rule is that the parcel **GAINED** something followable: `hasFollowableTracking` tests number/url/short code and NOT the carrier - a courier name is who has the box, not tracking - and the PATCH route compares the row before the save with the row after (`existing` was already read for the window check). A parcel dispatched with its number on it carried that number in its dispatch note and says nothing twice. `emailTracking` is a separate flag from `emailCustomer`: two different messages on two different days, and an owner filling in a window on a parcel whose number already went out must not be made to choose.

**A guest who proved the parent could not open its replacement.** Guest access is a signed cookie of ORDER IDS (`lib/guest-order-access.ts`), so the customer who reported the broken chair was bounced to a postcode form by the link we emailed them AND by the link on their own order page. `orderViewerFor` now admits a replacement whose `parentOrderId` is in that list - the same proof, since a replacement goes to the address copied off its parent. One level only, so it cannot walk a chain. `OrderForViewer` makes `parentOrderId` optional, so a payload predating the column reads as "not a replacement" and refuses rather than admits. Six tests in `lib/order-viewer.test.ts` (new), both directions.

**The tracking box could not find a replacement number.** `orderNumberCandidates` strips everything that is not a letter or a digit, so `DW000182-R1` became `DW000182R1`, which is nobody's order. Now a trailing `R<1-4 digits>` is pulled off, the base goes through the existing padding rules, and the suffix is put back - `DW000182-R1`, `dw000182r1`, `182-R1` and `182r1` all land on it. The stripped form is dropped from the candidate list rather than left widening the `IN`. A prefix containing an R (`OSR`) can be read as one of these; that candidate matches nothing, except on the one shop whose prefix IS `OS-R`, where it is the right answer.

**Smaller ones, same pass.** The parent's ITEM LINES now say what was sent for each (`replacementLines` on the order route, mapped by `replaces_order_item_id`) - the column existed for this and only the card was using it. The admin orders list badges a replacement, and the orders export gains `kind` and `replacement_for` columns at the FRONT, beside the order number, because a £0 row in an accounts export is a question. The admin list search is `ILIKE %term%` on `order_number`, so searching `DW000182` already finds its replacements - checked, unchanged.

**Notification preferences are carried, not defaulted.** `CreateOrderInput` gains `notifyEmail`/`notifySms`/`notifyPhone` and `createReplacementOrder` copies the parent's. A MEMBER's choice is read off their account either way, but a guest's lives on the order row alone - so without this, a guest who ticked "text me" about the chair would have heard nothing by text about the part replacing it.

**A free replacement raises no invoice.** `applyOrderStatusChange` gains `worthInvoicing = order.kind !== 'REPLACEMENT' || Number(order.total) > 0`, so a shop set to invoice on DISPATCHED or COMPLETED does not send a customer a £0 invoice for a part it sent to put its own mistake right. Tests the MONEY, not the kind: a charged replacement is an ordinary sale of a spare and is invoiced like one.

**Routes.** `app/api/admin/orders/[id]/replacement/route.ts` (new): GET lists, POST raises, both hung off the ORIGINAL order's id because that is the number everybody quotes. POST is gated `shop.orders` WITHOUT `allowAccess`. `app/api/admin/orders/[id]` GET now also returns `replacements` and `parentOrder`. `app/api/admin/products` accepts `?partsOnly=true|false` (three-valued: absent means both, which is what the admin list wants) and `partsOnly` on create/update.

**Admin UI.** `components/admin/ReplacementModal.tsx` (new) - lines start EMPTY, unlike the refund modal, because a replacement is nearly never an order line; the order's lines appear only as the "putting right" answer beside each. Part field searches `?partsOnly=true` (debounced 250ms) and also takes free text. "Send a replacement" on `OrderDetailScreen` (not on a replacement itself) and on any DAMAGE row in `RequestsScreen`, which fetches the order's lines on click and pre-selects the reported ones. A **Replacements sent** card on the parent, a `Replacement` badge and a parent link on the child. The **spare part** tick lives in the product editor's organisation panel.

**Customer-facing.** `loadOrderDetail` gains `replacements` (each with its item names and dispatch state) and `parentOrder`; `listOrderSummariesForMember` gains `parentOrderNumber` and `replacementCount` in ONE query for the page. The order page carries a green note high up - above the delivery address, because somebody who reported a broken leg opens the page to find out where the new one is - listing each replacement as a link. The replacement's own page links back. `OrderSummaryCard` badges both directions and shows "No charge" instead of £0.00.

**Storefront exclusion for parts** is one condition inside `listProducts`' `storefront` branch, which covers every grid and public route at once, plus `lib/sitemap.ts` (2 places) and `lib/search-cards.tsx`.

**Not built, deliberately:** the generic manual-order screen. The POST at `app/api/admin/orders/route.ts:58` has existed for ages with nothing calling it; building the screen means customer search, address entry, a product picker, price overrides, coupons and payment capture - a separate feature with its own design questions, not a side-effect of replacements.

---

## modular-configurator-for-shop - a new module: modular layouts in 3D (v0.1.0, working tree, unreleased)

New module folder `modules/modular-configurator-for-shop/` (its own local git repo, no GitHub remote yet) and a `modules.json` entry pinned at `v0.1.0`, which does not exist as a tag until the repo is created and released.

**Tables.** `mcf_product_configs` (`product_id` PK + FK `shp_products` ON DELETE CASCADE, `enabled` boolean, `config` jsonb, `created_at`, `updated_at`). `teardown` lists it. Config refers to the unit option by NAME and values by SLUG (same rule as product add-ons). Zod shape in `lib/config-schema.ts`; stored rows read defensively (`parseStoredConfig`).

**Requires** shop 0.1.429, shop-variations 0.1.187, product-3d-views-for-shop 0.1.105; `requiresCoreVersion` 0.5.1601. product-attributes-for-shop is optional (footprint suggestions read `pat_*` behind `to_regclass`).

**Routes.** `app/api/admin/products/[productId]/route.ts` -> `GET` (payload: saved config, product options with spec-suggested footprints) and `PUT` (Zod `SaveConfiguratorBodySchema` + `validateConfigAgainstOptions`), both `requireShopUser('shop.products')`.

**Extension points.** `shop.product-editor-sections` id `modular-configurator-inline` ("Layout builder", order 62) -> `components/admin/ModularConfiguratorSection`; `shop.cart-line-resolver` + `-prefetch` id `modular-configurator` -> `lib/line-resolver` (`resolveLayoutLineMeta`, `prefetchLayoutLines`).

**Puck block.** `ShopModularConfigurator` (`components/puck/ShopModularConfigurator.tsx` editor half with a static sample; `.rsc.tsx` resolves `currentProductSlug()` -> `loadConfiguratorBlockData`, null unless the product has an enabled set-up). Fields `heading`, `intro`. Not auto-placed: an owner adds it to the product layout.

**Placement maths** (`lib/chain-geometry.ts`, pure): a layout is a chain; each unit has an entry and exit face; `placeChain` glues entry to previous exit (back ends touching, fronts the same way). Straight units enter left / exit right; a corner with its second back on the LEFT enters through its front and leaves through its right, on the RIGHT enters left and leaves through its front - both turn towards the seats' front, so one corner product builds either L. `anchorLayout` keeps surviving units still after an edit. `lib/chain-editing.ts` refuses arms mid-chain, overlaps (AABB, 1 mm tolerance) and the size limit. 15 tests pin the corner adjacency both ways round, L, U and a four-corner ring.

**Storefront.** `components/public/ConfiguratorCard.tsx` joins shop-variations' selection store (`useVariationSelection(slug, packedBootstrap)`), so layout-level options ARE the page's options. `LayoutWorkspace` is a native `<dialog>` (full-screen under 820px); the 3D stage is `LayoutStageLazy` (`next/dynamic`, `ssr:false`), so three.js loads only when the builder opens. `lib/three/layout-scene.ts` is an imperative scene (real-size units, eased moves, ghosts, selection, dimension lines, angled/above camera) using p3d's `addLights`, `loadModel`, `applyFabricPaint`, `fetchBundle` (NO add-on context), `disposeRenderer`. Models are fitted to the declared width. Fallback: signed representative-variation model file, else a block built from the footprint. Plan view `LayoutPlan.tsx` (SVG, mm viewBox, keyboard operable). URL param `modular-layout` (`lib/layout-code.ts`, slugs, `~option-key:value-slug` per-unit choices). All chrome under `data-cactus-unstyled`, colours from tokens (`configurator-css.ts`); WebGL colours read from tokens at runtime (`theme-colour.ts`).

**Basket.** `lib/basket-lines.ts`: one line per distinct child, quantity = count x layouts, `lineId` `mcl:<layoutId>:<childId>` (<= 64), meta `modularLayout` (`lib/line-meta.ts`, capped for the 4000-byte meta limit). `add-layout-to-basket.ts` calls `collectPurchaseCompanions` per unit and merges `mainMeta` (so product add-ons' stamp lands); companion LINES only from the first unit. `lib/layout-groups.ts` (prefetch planner): head = the layout line product add-ons already stamped `productAddons.role === 'main'` (group key adopted) else first line; others attachments ordered ahead of accessories (`order` -1000+n); head carries Layout / Arrangement fields and a note when a line has been removed.

**Tests.** 39 unit tests (`npx vitest run modules/modular-configurator-for-shop`). Live SQL suite `lib/db/configurator-sql.live.test.ts` behind `RUN_MODULAR_CONFIGURATOR_SQL=1` + OVH creds (no core package.json script, deliberately): migration twice, config upsert/read jsonb, spec footprints, admin payload, cascade - 5/5 pass against a throwaway `cactus_rt_mcf_*` database.

**Owed before release:** GitHub repo `cactus-foundation-modules/modular-configurator-for-shop`, card art (prompt #37 in `plans/module-card-art-prompts.md`; `module-art.webp` is referenced by the README but not yet present), build-gate workflow via `node scripts/install-module-ci.mjs modular-configurator-for-shop`, wiki push (`wiki/Modular-Configurator.md` + Home row).

---

## google-ai-studio - a new module, and AI product photographs (v0.1.0, core pin v0.5.1571)

New module, released 2026-09-10 and pinned in core:

```json
{ "name": "google-ai-studio", "repoUrl": "https://github.com/cactus-foundation-modules/google-ai-studio", "version": "v0.1.0" }
```

**Files.** `cactus.module.json`, `package.json`, `README.md`, `.github/workflows/build-gate.yml` (written by `scripts/install-module-ci.mjs`), `migrations/001_initial.sql` and `migrations/002_job_source_images.sql`, `lib/{settings,gemini,capabilities,sources,references,prompt,jobs,media-import}.ts` (+ `lib/prompt.test.ts`), `components/admin/{SettingsTab,ProductPhotosSection,ProductPhotosPanel,panel-css}.tsx|ts`, and seven routes under `app/api/admin/`. Card art in all three places: `public/module-art/google-ai-studio.webp` (core, the one the Modules page reads), `modules/google-ai-studio/module-art.webp`, and the top of the module README; prompt appended to `plans/module-card-art-prompts.md` as entry 36.

**Tables.** `gas_settings` (singleton), `gas_jobs`, `gas_job_images`, `gas_job_source_images` (v0.1.1 - reference pictures with no url of their own, e.g. a captured 3D view). `teardown` lists them child-first. No foreign key to `shp_products` or to `User` - deliberately: this module installs where neither exists, and a job is scratch that must not vanish mid-review because an admin account was deleted.

**Dependencies: none declared.** It contributes to a shop extension point without depending on the shop, which works only because nothing in its graph statically imports `@/modules/shop/...`. Three capability probes (`lib/capabilities.ts`, 30s cache, by table name): `shp_product_media` gives the panel and the product's own pictures, `svr_variants` adds each variation's lead photograph, `pat_attribute_values` adds picture attributes set on the product or its variations. Each source group is capped at 60.

**It publishes `core.reply-suggestions`** from the unreleased tree: `lib/reply-suggestions.ts` (`serverOnly`), with `lib/gemini-text.ts` for `generateContent` and `lib/reply-prompt.ts` for the prompt. Two more columns on `gas_settings` (`text_model`, `reply_house_style`, `migrations/003_reply_suggestions.sql`). It names no consumer - `unified-inbox` and `contact-form` each read the seam from a route of their own, and a site with neither has nowhere for the button to appear. See the entry at the top of this file.

**It hosts a point of its own** from v0.1.1: `google-ai-studio.reference-image-sources`, for modules that can make an extra reference picture. `product-3d-views-for-shop` v0.1.100 contributes the first one. See the entry at the top of this file for the contract.

**The one diff outside it** is shop's `gallery-add-bus.ts` plus the listener in `panels/media.tsx` - a generic "put these pictures in the gallery being edited" seam, named after the capability rather than after this module, and usable by any contributor without importing shop. Documented in `wiki/Authoring-a-module.md`. Shop must be released (0.1.412) before that half works anywhere; without it the panel still saves accepted pictures to the media library and says so.

**Two prompts, on purpose.** The house style (`gas_settings.product_photo_prompt`, editable in Settings) is prepended to the job's own words (`lib/prompt.ts`), so the specific instruction is what the model reads last. The house style can be edited for a single job from the panel without changing the setting.

**What actually shipped, and what has not.** The module went out first (`v0.1.0`), then the core pin (`v0.5.1571`). **Shop 0.1.412 is still owed** - `gallery-add-bus.ts` and the listener in `panels/media.tsx` are uncommitted, because that repo's tree also holds another session's in-flight guest-order-access work and a repo gets ONE release per shipping session. Until it goes, an owner who accepts a picture is told it landed in the media library and adds it with the Add images button; the panel checks (the event is cancelable and the tab answers by preventing default) rather than assuming. Whoever ships shop next should take the seam with them - a patch of both files is in this session's scratchpad, and see [[reference_shared_tree_module_revert_defence]].
