## core - every admin screen names itself (working tree, unreleased)

Symptom: a browser's history and tab strip showed the site name, once per admin screen, thirty times over. Two separate causes.

**Module screens - the metadata was being thrown away.** Every module admin screen renders through `app/cactus-admin/m/[module]/[[...path]]/page.tsx`, and Next only ever reads `metadata` / `generateMetadata` from the route file. A module's own `export const metadata` was therefore dead on arrival - roughly 80 of them across the module set, all correct, none reaching a browser. That route now exports `generateMetadata`, mirroring what `app/(public)/[slug]/[...path]/page.tsx` already did on the public side. Chain: the module page's own `generateMetadata`, then its static `metadata`, then a fallback. Every step is wrapped - a title is never worth a 500 on the screen behind it.

- `scripts/generate-module-router.mjs`: `resolveModulePage` now returns `metadata` and `generateMetadata` off the loaded page module (it already returned `generateMetadata` on the public resolver). `PageModule` gains `metadata?: unknown`. `lib/modules/router.ts` is generated - regenerate, never hand-edit.
- NEW `resolveModulePageTitle(moduleSlug, path, manifests)` in `lib/nav/admin-menu.ts` - the fallback. Longest `navEntries` path that prefixes this screen wins, so `/m/shop/orders/<id>` is titled "Trading" after the sidebar link it sits under. Then the last path segment that is not an id, then the module's slug with any `-for-<host>` suffix dropped. Prefix matching is on whole segments: `/m/shop/order` does not match the `/m/shop/orders` link. Manifests come from `getInstalledModules()`, which is React-`cache()`d and already read by the admin layout on the same request, so the fallback costs no extra query.
- NEW `adminScreenTitle(section, tab?)` in the same file - the one core format, `"Section — Admin"` or `"Section: Tab — Admin"`. A `?tab=` id is **sentence-cased, not Title-Cased** (`Users: Pending approval`): the id is a slug standing in for a label somebody wrote, and Title Case renames it. The em dash matches what module screens already ship; the root layout's `%s | <site name>` template still appends the site name.
- NEW `lib/nav/admin-page-title.test.ts` - 9 tests, both functions, no database.

**Core screens - most had no title at all.** Eight of twenty-two did. A client component cannot export metadata, so the client screens are titled from a layout in their own segment.

- `export const metadata` added: `app/cactus-admin/account/page.tsx` ("My account"), `app/cactus-admin/login/page.tsx` ("Sign in"), and on existing layouts `appearance/layout.tsx` ("Appearance"), `layouts/layout.tsx`, `menus/layout.tsx`, `modules/layout.tsx`, `config/privacy-generator/layout.tsx`.
- `generateMetadata` added: `config/page.tsx`, `users/page.tsx` and `inbox/page.tsx` read `?tab=` and name the tab (`Settings: Integrations — Admin`). Only a page can do this - a layout never sees `searchParams`. The Inbox goes further and takes the tab's real label straight off the manifests (`core.inbox-tabs` entry with that id) rather than tidying the slug up, so it reads `Inbox: Contact form — Admin`, which is what the tab strip says. Read through the React-cached `getInstalledModules()`, not `resolveInboxTabs()` - the latter answers the same question but costs its own query plus a permission check per tab, and this is naming a browser tab. `ALL_TAB_ID` (`core-all`) is not a label, so the merged view stays plain `Inbox — Admin`.
- NEW layout files, metadata only, each a pass-through returning `children`: `layouts/[id]/layout.tsx`, `layouts/new/layout.tsx`, `menus/[id]/layout.tsx`, `pages/[id]/layout.tsx`, `pages/new/layout.tsx`. The three `[id]` ones look the record's name up (`Layout.name`, `Menu.name`, `InfoPage.title`) so two open editors are told apart: `Pages: Delivery and returns — Admin`.
- `members/[id]/page.tsx` swaps its static "Member detail" for the member's own name (`displayName` / `fullName` / `username`).
- `appearance/page.tsx` stays untitled on purpose - it is a bare `redirect` to `/appearance/styles` and renders nothing.

No schema change, no new dependency, no route added or removed.

## shop - order-size deduction (working tree, unreleased)

An amount already inside a shelf price that comes back off once the basket holds enough of that supplier's goods. Platform-generic, **off by default**, inert until both halves are configured. NOT delivery and never to be called carriage - `advanced-shipping` charges real delivery on the same page and the two were confused once while this was being specified.

**Schema - `modules/shop/migrations/042_order_size_deduction.sql`** (new; `001_initial.sql` edited in place for the two `shp_products`/`shp_order_items` columns, per the standing fresh-install rule). Four nullable columns, all idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`: `shp_products.order_size_deduction NUMERIC(10,2)` (per UNIT), `shp_suppliers.order_size_deduction_threshold NUMERIC(10,2)`, `shp_suppliers.order_size_deduction_note TEXT`, `shp_order_items.order_size_deduction NUMERIC(10,2)` (snapshot, already inside `unit_price`). The note column is dropped again by `051_drop_order_size_deduction_note.sql`; 042 is frozen and still adds it. NULL means none, which is not a recorded 0. No supplier id anywhere - products join suppliers BY NAME, as they have since 007.

**Config** (`lib/config.ts`): `orderSizeDeductionEnabled` (default `false`) and `orderSizeDeductionShowInBasket` (default `true`). Exposed to the admin product editor as `orderSizeDeduction: { enabled }` from `app/api/public/config/route.ts`, the same shape `supplierField` has.

**The rule - `lib/order-size-deduction.ts`** (new, pure, dependency-free bar `formatMoney`, so client islands can import it). `deductionAmount` (null/0/negative/NaN all read as none), `orderSizeDeductionStates`, `applyOrderSizeDeduction`, the four approved sentences and `orderSizeDeductionNotes`. Load-bearing clauses, each with a test: qualification is judged **pre-deduction** so one pass can never oscillate; per unit not per line; full-price lines count towards the threshold and lose nothing; only a line **currently on offer** ever loses money; suppliers scored separately; a line with no supplier never qualifies; **the qualifying subtotal subtracts every resolver charge**, because `advanced-shipping`'s delivery money is already inside `unitPrice` on live baskets and a raw `lineSubtotal` clears thresholds the goods never reached.

**`lib/order-size-deduction-view.ts`** (new, pure) composes the product-page sentence. One place, because the RSC and the public route both word it and a second copy is how a listing promises one figure before a colour is picked and another after.

**Checkout** (`lib/checkout.ts`): `applyOrderSizeDeductions` is a **second whole-basket pass immediately after `applyMinimumOrderQuantities`**, the same shape that pass has. It reduces `unitPrice` and deliberately does NOT go through `resolveDiscounts` - `resolveOrderTotals` spreads `discountRatio` proportionally across every line's taxable base, so the VAT relief would land partly on full-price lines that never carried an amount. `ResolvedCartLine` gains `orderSizeDeduction?: number | null` (per unit, display only, already subtracted). **`resolveCartLinesWithDeduction` is new and returns `{ lines, orderSizeDeduction }`; `resolveCartLines` delegates to it.** The split exists because the pass is NOT idempotent - running it over already-reduced lines would deduct twice - so anything wanting the states must take them from the pass that made the lines. Two shortcuts mean an ordinary shop fires no query: the config switch, then "does any line actually carry an amount".

**Order record**: `insertOrderRows` writes the new column; `CreateOrderInput.items` and `ShpOrderItem` carry it; `payment-intent`, `admin/orders` and `order-payment-state` fill it in.

**Basket**: `app/api/public/cart/validate/route.ts` returns `orderSizeDeduction` and `unitPriceBeforeDeduction` per line (both `displayAmount`-converted) and folds shop's own note into the existing `notes` array ahead of the providers' - four surfaces for one line of code. `CartLinePrice` + `lineSubtotalBeforeDeduction` are new in `CartChrome.tsx` and used by `CartFullClient`, `CartPageClient` and `CartDrawerClient`; `.scl-price-was` is new in `cart-line-css.ts`.

**Product page.** `DetailPartContext` gains `supplierBadge` and `orderSizeDeduction`; `ShopProductDetail.rsc.tsx` does **one** `getSupplierByName` serving both, and only when something on the page could use it. `ShopDetailBadges` gains `showSupplier` (default `'no'`, so no existing layout sprouts a badge) plus `.spd-badge-supplier`. **`ShopDetailOrderSizeDeduction` is a new block, not an addition inside the Price part** - `shop-variations`' `detail-parts-provider.ts` puts `Price` in `coveredSlots` whenever the layout carries `ShopVariantPrice`, so `ShopDetailPriceRsc` returns null there and anything nested inside it renders nothing on precisely the sites that want this. Added to `cactus.module.json` `puckBlocks`, to `DETAIL_PART_TYPES` in `inject-part-context.ts`, and to all three `shopProductDetailStarters()`. `components/public/OrderSizeDeductionClient.tsx` (new) follows the shopper's combination over the existing `cactus-shop-variant-selection` window event - **no import in either direction, and `shop-variations` needs no change at all** - and asks the new `app/api/public/order-size-deduction/route.ts` for the finished sentence, per-id cached, with a ticket guard so a fast clicker lands on the option they chose rather than the reply that arrived last.

**Admin.** Product amount follows `minOrderQuantity`'s trace exactly (`types.ts`, `db/products.ts` mapper/create/update map/duplicate, both Zod bodies, `product-editor/model.ts` + Pricing panel, `csv.ts`, `csv-rows.ts`, `import-engine.ts`). Supplier threshold on `SuppliersScreen.tsx` and `supplier-schema.ts`. Settings switches on `ShopSettingsTab.tsx`. **New third Reports tab**, from `listOrderSizeDeductionChecks` (`lib/db/suppliers.ts`) via `app/api/admin/reports/order-size-deduction/route.ts`: rows stamped with more than they sell for, and rows on offer whose supplier has a threshold with nothing stamped. Both generic - core learns nothing about catalogues or sale-code prefixes.

**Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `vitest run modules/shop` 1117+ passed / 0 failed (27 new: 18 pure rule + 9 checkout pass), `csv-roundtrip.test.ts` extended. **`npm run test:backup-roundtrip` real PASS** (4/4, byte-identical restore) - not a skip. **`npm run test:shop-sql` real PASS** (11/11) including a new `order-size deduction SQL against a real database` block that runs `getDeductionRules` and `listOrderSizeDeductionChecks` themselves against a throwaway Postgres; both gained an optional `{ client }` for it, the same arrangement `lib/db/addresses.ts` has. Raw SQL is a string to typecheck, eslint and the module build gate, so Postgres is the only proof.

**Not built here:** the Deskwell-side script work that fills the column (`plan-office-essentials.mjs` must emit its `carriage[catalogue]` figure, `apply-office-essentials.mjs` must carry it through **inside the FOR UPDATE staleness comparison**, not alongside it). Until that lands nothing carries an amount and this side is inert.
