## unified-inbox - the whole screen, rebuilt as a mail program (working tree, unreleased)

A visual rebuild of `/hq/inbox`, no schema and no API change. Every route, permission, query and access rule is exactly as it was.

**The shape.** It was a page: two strips of tabs, a row of filter chips, then a list beside whatever was open, the lot of it scrolling as one document. It is now **one framed box the height of the window, divided by hairlines into four panes** - rail, list, reading pane, context - each scrolling its own contents. `.uin-app` in `components/admin/inbox/styles.tsx` is a named-area grid with four cuts: one column under 900px (the rail lies down as a scrolling strip and the list and the reading pane take turns, driven by `data-open`), list + reading pane at 900px, the rail stands up as a column at 1200px, and the context panel takes a fourth column at 1500px. `data-context` guards that last one, or a conversation with nothing attached left a twenty-rem strip of empty ground on the end.

**`components/admin/inbox/NavRail.tsx`** (new) replaces `InboxTabs.tsx` (deleted). Same props, same links, same drag-to-reorder and the same Alt+arrow keyboard equivalent - up and down now rather than left and right. Two behaviour changes worth knowing: the drag handlers moved off each row and onto the `<ul>`, reading the address back off `data-uin-id` under the pointer (fourteen addresses was fourteen identical sets of five handlers, and passing them through a child component's props tripped `react-hooks/refs`, because the compiler follows `move -> save -> lastServerKey.current` and calls the whole object ref-tainted); and "Write a message" is now offered on every list rather than only where the status row was not drawn.

**`StatusTabs.tsx`** is a segmented control (`.uin-seg`) rather than core's `TabStrip`, and no longer carries the search or the compose button. **`Filters.tsx`** gained the search box and lost the total. **`ContactsToolbar.tsx`** uses the same segmented control for People/Organisations. Nothing in the module imports `TabStrip` any more except the settings tab, and the `.uin-tab*` classes are gone with it.

**`InboxPanel.tsx`**: the head of the list column (`.uin-col-head`) now holds the view's name, its total, the segments and the filters, so all of it stays put while the rows scroll under it. The reading pane (`.uin-read`) is **always drawn**, with a "Nothing open" placeholder when it is empty. `PersonView`, `ContactCard`, `OrganisationCard` and `ContactImport` all render `.uin-thread` already, so they need no wrapper; bare notices get their air from `.uin-read > .uin-empty`.

**`.uin-thread-top`** is new markup in `ThreadPane` and those four: subject on the left, the way out hard against the right - a cross on a wide window, "Back to the list" on its own line on a phone. Rows lost the wide across-in-one-line variant and its container query (the list column is never the full screen any more), the date moved to the top right of a row via `column-reverse` on `.uin-row-meta`, and messages lost their internal rules so a conversation reads as a run of messages rather than a stack of forms.

**Grounds.** The rail and the context panel are `--color-bg`, the list and the reading pane `--color-surface`. That is the one pairing in core's palette that differs in BOTH themes; `--color-bg-subtle` is the same colour as `--color-surface` in dark mode, which is the trap documented at the top of `styles.tsx` and the reason `.uin-bulk` moved off it too. No hex anywhere in the sheet.

**Campaigns** keeps the rail and nothing else: `.uin-app-wide` is `overflow-x: clip` with `overflow-y: visible`, because the campaign form's save bar is `position: sticky; bottom: 0` and any scroll container round it makes the bar sit at the bottom of the form instead of the bottom of the screen.

**Gates:** `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean, `vitest run modules/unified-inbox` 698 passed / 0 failed.

**Second pass - the rail, the list head, the rows, and people's own pictures.**

**Rail.** Four groups now: **Yours** (the pinned address, All, *Assigned to me*, Drafts, Sent), **Team inboxes**, **Channels**, **Everything else**. Whoever is reading sits at the head of it with their picture and a square pen button. Each address carries a **coloured dot**, derived from its id by `toneFor()` into five semantic tokens (`--color-primary`, `--color-info`, `--color-warning`, `--color-destructive`, `--color-text-secondary`) - five and not more because a sixth would have to be a hex value, and the standing rule is that a hex is a colour that is wrong in one of the two themes. **Assigned to me** is new and is a place rather than a filter chip; the "Mine" chip is gone, so the two things that meant the same thing and disagreed about being on are now one. It costs one extra `countThreads`. Keyboard reorder is simpler: the team list holds nothing but movable addresses, so a link's index in it IS the address's position and the old pinned/All offset arithmetic is gone.

**List head.** Search first, in a bordered box with the magnifier inside it (`.uin-search`), with the one control that changes order rather than contents beside it. Then plain text tabs with an underline on the chosen one (`.uin-tabs` / `.uin-tab`) - the pill segmented control from the first pass is gone, and so is `.uin-seg*`. The total rides at the end of the tab row. The view-name row now only draws on Drafts and Sent, which have no tab row to carry it.

**Sort.** `?sort=oldest`. Two `Prisma.sql` constants (`THREAD_LIST_ORDER`, `THREAD_LIST_ORDER_OLDEST`) picked by a boolean - **nothing a reader types reaches an ORDER BY**. `NULLS` flips with the direction. `threadListQuery` takes `oldestFirst`; `threadsForPerson` still passes the default.

**Rows.** `Sender › whoever has it` (the assignee, or the address it came in on), the turned reply arrow on the preview when `lastDirection === 'out'`, and a message-count circle. `messageCount` and `lastDirection` were already on `ThreadListRow`, so none of that needed a query change; `personId` did, and is one extra column in the SELECT.

**Avatars - Gravatar and Libravatar.** New `lib/avatars.ts` + `lib/avatars.test.ts` (15 tests) and `app/api/avatar/[kind]/[id]/route.ts`.
- **SHA-256 only.** Both services file an account under the MD5 *and* the SHA-256 of the same address, so trying the legacy hash too is a fourth round trip that can never find anything the third did not.
- **Three sources in order:** the sender's own domain if it publishes `_avatars-sec._tcp` (Libravatar federation, cached an hour, positives and negatives), then `seccdn.libravatar.org`, then `www.gravatar.com`. `d=404` on all three, which is what makes the chain work at all - without it each service answers with a generated pattern and the first one always "succeeds".
- **SSRF guard, and it is the reason federation is safe to have.** A federated host comes out of DNS belonging to *whoever sent us mail*. `isPublicAddress()` refuses loopback, RFC1918, CGNAT, link-local (**including 169.254.169.254**, the cloud metadata endpoint), multicast, reserved, ULA, and IPv4-in-IPv6. Every address the name resolves to must pass. https only - the plain `_avatars._tcp` record on port 80 is never used. 4s timeout, 512KB cap, `image/*` content-type required.
- **Addressed by WHO, never by hash:** `/api/m/unified-inbox/avatar/person/<id>` and `/user/<id>`. No email and no hash appears in any page's markup, no third party learns a reader's IP, and no CSP `img-src` origin has to be opened up. Same shape as core's `app/api/members/avatar-proxy`. `private, max-age=86400` on a hit, `private, max-age=3600` on a miss (the common case), `nosniff` plus a `default-src 'none'; sandbox` CSP on the response.
- **Off by default**, migration `028_avatars.sql` (`show_avatars BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`, idempotent, no dollar-quoting). The reason is written in the migration and matches the line this module already takes about `trackOpens`: asking a third party whether it holds a picture for an address tells that third party we hold the address, and these are customers' addresses. One tick on **Settings → Unified Inbox → People**.
- `<Avatar>` lays the picture *over* the initials rather than instead of them, so nothing shifts when one arrives and a 404 shows what was always there. Drawn on list rows, contact rows, message heads and the rail. **Not** on Sent, Drafts or organisation rows - no person id to key on, and a hash in the URL is exactly what this design refuses.

**Gates, second pass:** `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean, `check-client-graph.mjs` clean, `vitest run modules/unified-inbox` 713 passed / 0 failed. Both ORDER BY variants **executed** against the live database read-only (no gate runs raw SQL - see `reference_raw_sql_untested_by_npm_test`). **`npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS, 4/4, no skips**, with `unified-inbox` in the built module list - required because 028 adds a column.



**Third pass - two real bugs, and the sections the first two passes did not touch.**

**BUG: a finished campaign could not be saved at all.** `CampaignEditor` gated the Save button on `editable = !settled`, so on a `done` or `stopped` campaign the button was not rendered - while `WhenSection`'s boxes stayed editable, which is the worst shape a form can have: it takes your typing and has nowhere to put it. Save now draws on every status. Who it goes to and the first message stay locked by the sections themselves, which is where the server's rule actually lives.

**BUG, same area: the route refused a save on PRESENCE of `categoryIds`, not on a change.** `if (!isDraft && data.categoryIds !== undefined)`. The whole form saves in one press, so a started campaign posts its own unchanged labels every time - meaning no started campaign could have its name corrected or its clock changed either. Now compared with `sameCategoryIds()` (moved to `lib/campaigns/guards.ts`, 5 tests). Related: `WhatSection` locked the first message on `running`, but the server's rule is `!isDraft`, so a **paused** campaign offered a box whose save was refused. Now `firstLocked = status !== 'draft'`, and it says why in the form.

**BUG: contact import died with "That file could not be read."** Two separate causes collapsed into one useless sentence.
- `await request.json().catch(() => null)` fed `null` to Zod, so a body the host refused for **size** produced the same message as a malformed file. A few thousand contacts is megabytes of JSON. The client now posts in **chunks of 250** (`IMPORT_CHUNK`) with a `rowOffset`, adding the summaries up and stopping at the first refusal while keeping what already went in - and the route tells a body that never arrived (413, plain English) apart from one that did not fit the shape.
- The schema answered a dozen bounds with one sentence. `describeImportFault()` (moved to `lib/contacts.ts`, 5 tests) names the row and column: *"row 414, column H"*. Row numbers carry the chunk offset, so the line named is the line in the spreadsheet. Cell cap raised 5,000 → 20,000 chars (a notes column pasted out of a CRM runs to a page); columns 200 → 500; `importContacts` takes `rowOffset` so a problem is numbered against the file.

**Campaign editor, de-cluttered.** `WhenSection` opened with fifteen controls - two clocks, a weekday tick, a holiday list, three number boxes, a warm-up tick and another box under it. Now: when to begin, then **one sentence saying what the pace comes to** ("About 320 a day, at any hour, any day of the week, 90 seconds apart"), with all seven knobs behind a disclosure. The sentence is the answer, not a summary of the settings. The name moved out of its own bordered section into the header as a borderless inline field. **Start sending now saves first if anything is unsaved** - it used to be absent until Save had been pressed, so the button vanished exactly when somebody had just finished typing.

**Restyled to match the shell:** composer (toolbar strips top and bottom, one outline, quiet ground), new-message modal, `.uin-fields` (hairlines, no outer box), recipient suggestions, confirm dialog, link peek, drop overlay, pagination, send-later, contact card, category chips, person timeline, and the whole of campaigns - sections are now ruled off rather than four outlined cards inside an outlined pane holding outlined blocks holding outlined fields. `.uin-camp-why` disclosures got a rotating triangle.

**Correction from the second pass:** I replaced the segmented pill on the status tabs with underlined text tabs, reasoning from memory that mail programs underline. The reference screenshot plainly shows a filled pill; the pill is back. Its ground is `--color-surface-raised`, not `--color-bg-subtle` - on a `--color-surface` pane those are the same colour in dark mode, which is the trap at the top of the stylesheet.

**Gates, third pass:** `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean, `check-client-graph.mjs` clean, `vitest run modules/unified-inbox` **723 passed / 0 failed** (+10). No migration and no raw SQL touched this round, so neither the backup round-trip nor a live SQL run is re-triggered.


## core + gazette + boards + space-planner-for-shop - the build cache stops being thrown away (working tree, unreleased)

**The finding, from Vercel's own logs.** Vercel caches `node_modules` and `.next/cache` between deploys and refuses to keep the result over 1.5 GB: `Build cache size 2.04 GB exceeds limit of 1.50 GB. Invalidating cache.` A COLD build writes ~1.33 GB and fits; the WARM build after it merges the restored Turbopack cache with its own new entries, lands at 1.66-2.08 GB and is thrown away whole - so the deploy after that pays a cold `npm install` (~30s), a cold compile (180s against 35s warm) and a full output re-upload with nothing to dedupe against (101s against 78s). Self-sustaining, and it alternated: 5 of the last 13 deploys on deskwell.co.uk started from a clean state, at 367s against 170s.

**`scripts/prune-build-cache.mjs`** (new), spawned by `scripts/next-build.mjs` after a build that SUCCEEDED. Three stages, ordered by what each is worth to the next deploy - a warm `node_modules` saves ~30s of install, a warm Turbopack cache saves up to 145s of compile, so node_modules is spent first and the compile cache last. (1) build-only packages always: the Prisma CLI and `@prisma/engines`, `typescript`, `eslint`, `vitest` and the `@babel`/`@rollup`/`@esbuild`/`@types`/`@typescript-eslint`/`@vitest`/`@vitejs`/`@eslint*` scopes, measured at **196 MB**. (2) if still over budget, every other package no trace mentions, another **334 MB** (1.0 GB -> 511 MB all told). (3) if still over, `.next/cache/turbopack`. Nothing is removed on a guess: `next build` has already written `.next/**/*.nft.json` saying exactly which files Vercel will copy into the functions, and a package is only removed when it appears in NONE of them. `next` and `@next/*` are never removed at any stage - Vercel's Next builder assembles `.vercel/output` from node_modules AFTER `npm run build` returns, and resolves the installed Next to do it. Which is also why this cannot run any earlier: the traces do not exist until the build ends, and nothing traced may be removed. Armed on Vercel only (`CACTUS_PRUNE_BUILD_CACHE=1` arms it anywhere, `=0` disarms it, `CACTUS_BUILD_CACHE_BUDGET_MB` moves the 1400 MB budget), and it can never fail a build that has already succeeded. npm restores what it took in 33s and needs no lockfile surgery, proved by running it and reinstalling.

**`@sparticuz/chromium` -> `@sparticuz/chromium-min`, pinned exactly.** The full package ships the browser as brotli packs, 66 MB in node_modules and another 66 MB forced into every `/api/m/**` function by `outputFileTracingIncludes` (the packs are read with `fs` against the package's own directory, so the tracer cannot see them - the same class of problem as draco3d's wasm). The "-min" package is 72 KB and fetches the pack to `/tmp` on the first print in a cold container. **`lib/documents/chromium.ts`** (new) owns finding a browser for everybody: `resolvePrintBrowser()` returns `{ executablePath, args }` or null, local Chrome by `CHROME_PATH` or the usual install paths off a deployment, the packaged one on it. `lib/documents/pdf.ts` and `modules/space-planner-for-shop/lib/pdf.ts` both call it and each keeps its own refusal wording; the space planner's own copy of the Chrome path list is gone. `CHROMIUM_PACK_URL` (new, optional) serves the pack from your own storage instead of the GitHub release. **The pack must match the package**, which is why the dependency is pinned with no caret and `CHROMIUM_VERSION` in that file names the same version - move them together.

**Shiki out of the Gazette post editor's browser bundle.** `modules/gazette/components/puck/body/GazetteCode.tsx` imported `codeToHtml` and `createCssVariablesTheme` from `shiki` at module scope, and called the second one at module scope so nothing could be shaken out. `bodyEditorConfig` imports that file and `PostEditor.tsx` is `'use client'`, so the whole default bundle - the highlighter, the oniguruma engine and all ~346 grammars, each its own chunk for the bundler to compile - was compiled into the editor's client bundle, to render a `<pre>` of plain text. Split: `GazetteCode.tsx` keeps `LANGUAGE_OPTIONS`, `escapeCode`, the editor render and the field def and may never import shiki again; new **`GazetteCodeRsc.tsx`** holds the highlighting and only `bodyRscConfig` reaches it. It builds `createHighlighterCore` over exactly the 18 grammars behind the 19 offered languages (plaintext needs none), written out one `import()` per line because a computed `import('shiki/langs/' + name)` is the barrel that would pull all 346 back. 46 grammars once the embedded ones are counted - `ruby` alone carries 32, `php` 10 - against 346. Memoised in a module-level promise: the default bundle's `codeToHtml` keeps its own singleton and a hand-built core highlighter does not. Same `cssVariablesTheme` name and `--gz-shiki-` prefix, so the emitted `<pre class="shiki cactus">` and every CSS variable are unchanged and no stylesheet moves. `modules/boards/components/puck/body/BoardsCode.tsx` had the identical shape and got the identical split (`BoardsCodeRsc.tsx`, `--brd-shiki-` prefix, `brd-code` classes, same 18 grammars). Worse there, in fact: boards' `bodyEditorConfig` is reached from `components/public/ThreadComposer.tsx`, which is a PUBLIC client component, so the full bundle was shipping to anybody writing a post rather than to an admin. Measured on a build with both modules installed: distinct `@shikijs/langs` grammar files traced into the server functions went from **254 to 29**, and no client chunk carries the shiki library at all any more - the three that still match on the string are CSS variable names (`--gz-shiki-bg`) and a `shiki.style` selector.

**Release order is load-bearing, and proved.** Core dropping `@sparticuz/chromium` from its dependencies breaks the OLD pinned space-planner, which still does `import('@sparticuz/chromium')`: reverting that one file and building gives `Module not found: Can't resolve '@sparticuz/chromium'` and the build fails, so a CORE-ONLY update would not land. The two must ride the same commit, which is what `app/api/admin/updates/route.ts` does when the core update is taken with its modules bundled (`resolveUpdateBatch` judges each module against the core version the deploy is MOVING TO, so a module requiring the incoming core is satisfied by the build carrying it). The new module therefore declares `requiresCoreVersion: 0.5.1526`, up from 0.5.1288, so it can never be installed against a core without `lib/documents/chromium.ts` - a minimum, not an exact match (`reasonForUnmetRequirements` in `lib/modules/compat.ts`), so any core at or above that satisfies it. Cut the core release at 0.5.1526 or higher.

**Gates:** `tsc --noEmit` clean, `scripts/check-client-graph.mjs` clean, and a real `next build` compiled successfully in 50s with no database (it stops at page collection, which is after the part that proves the new module resolutions bundle). From that build's own traces: `@sparticuz/chromium` no longer traced, `@sparticuz/chromium-min` traced in its place, and the only client chunk still carrying shiki is `BoardsCode`'s. Prune tested against the real tree and in a sandbox for all three stages, including that `next`, `@next/*` and traced packages survive stage 2.

## core + unified-inbox - no Reply-To when it only repeats the From address (working tree, unreleased)

Every message this module sent carried `Reply-To: <the inbox>` while going out `From: <the same inbox>` - `sendingIdentity(inbox)` returns `inbox.address` and all three construction sites hardcoded `replyTo: inbox.address`, so the header was redundant on every single send. Reply-To exists to say "answer somewhere other than the sender"; pointing it at the sender says nothing, and every spam scorer an owner is likely to check their mail against marks a message down for carrying one.

**Core, `lib/email/index.ts`:** new `replyToWorthSending(payload, fromAddress)`, applied in BOTH `sendViaBrevo` and `sendViaSmtp` - after `resolveSender`, because the From may come from the site config rather than the payload and the comparison is meaningless before that is worked out. Trimmed and case-folded. This is the guard that covers every module at once, including anything that sets `replyTo` on `OutboundEmailIdentity`. 5 tests in `lib/email/send.test.ts`.

**Module:** `SendableMessage.replyTo` is now `string | null` and means "what actually goes out" rather than "what we would like". Decided once at each construction site (`send.ts` x2, `campaigns/message.ts`) via an exported `replyToWorthSending` in `lib/transport.ts`, so `deliver()` and the Sent-folder MIME copy (`buildRawMessage`) agree without either repeating the rule - a Sent copy carrying a header the real message did not is a small lie somebody eventually debugs. `outbound-identity.ts` no longer sets `replyTo` at all: its From is the same address, so it could never have been anything but redundant.

The old test asserted the redundant header on purpose ("so an answer comes back here even if a server rewrites the sender"). That rewrite case is a mailing list rewriting `From:` downstream, which is not what any of these sends are; the test now asserts the header is absent and says why.

## unified-inbox - links in a message are looked at before they are followed (v0.1.46 working tree, unreleased)

Links in an email were dead on the press. The frame is sandboxed `allow-scripts allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox` with no `allow-top-navigation`, and `openLinksInNewTab` stamps `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"` on everything - which should pop a tab and in practice did not. Rather than chase the popup blocker, the click is intercepted, which is the better behaviour anyway: **the words on a link in a stranger's email are written by the stranger**, and "View your invoice" over a host in another country is not an edge case, it is the whole of phishing.

**New `components/admin/inbox/LinkPeek.tsx`** - modal showing the host on its own line, the link's own text when it differs from the address, and the whole address in a selectable monospace block that wraps at any character (a tracking link is 400 characters with no spaces). Buttons: open in a new tab (`window.open(href, '_blank', 'noopener,noreferrer')`, then close), copy the address, close. Escape and a backdrop press close it; the backdrop press checks `event.target === event.currentTarget` so a drag that began on the address does not. **Only `http:`, `https:` and `mailto:` are ever openable** - anything else is shown, refused, and called suspicious out loud. Second lock on the door the sanitiser already shut on `javascript:`/`data:`.

**HTML messages:** the frame's nonce'd script (`resizeScript` renamed `frameScript`) gains a capture-phase click handler that `preventDefault`s and posts `{uinLink: {href, text}}` to the parent. It reads the DOM `href` PROPERTY, not the attribute, so what is shown is the absolute address the browser would actually have visited. Left button only - a cmd/ctrl/shift/middle click is somebody deliberately asking for a tab, and `target="_blank"` still handles it. That attribute stays in the markup as the no-script fallback: if the script is blocked, a working link beats a dead one. `MessageBody` checks `event.source === frame.contentWindow` exactly as it already did for the height, and treats the payload as two untrusted strings with hard length caps.

**The whole email, not the first two screens** - `unified-inbox`, uncommitted working tree. `MessageBody.tsx` capped the frame at `MAX_HEIGHT = 4000`, about two screens; anything longer (a newsletter is routinely twenty thousand pixels) could only be reached by scrolling inside the message, which is the one thing the frame exists not to do. Cap raised to `200000` and demoted in the comments to a runaway guard rather than a size anybody chose. `frameScript` in `lib/message-document.ts` now keeps reporting rather than reporting once: a `ResizeObserver` on `documentElement` and `body`, plus capture-phase `load`/`error` (pictures arrive after the markup and each one makes the message taller), on top of the existing `load`/`resize`/`toggle`/60ms/400ms. Sends are deduplicated on the value and hard-stopped after 60, because a frame told its own height can change height because of it and two disagreeing layouts would otherwise talk to one another for ever. **A sideways scrollbar stands in the frame's height, not the message's**, so `measure()` adds `window.innerHeight - doc.clientHeight` when `scrollWidth > clientWidth` - zero where the browser draws its scrollbars over the top. `MessageBody` posts `{uinAppliedHeight}` back down after applying, and the frame sets `documentElement.style.overflowY = 'hidden'` only when what it got covers what it asked for: hiding it unconditionally would crop a message the page would not make room for, with no way to reach the rest. `message-document.test.ts` 7/7; eslint clean on both files.

**Plain-text messages** had no links at all. **New pure `lib/linkify.ts`** (`splitLinks`/`trimTrailing`/`hrefFor`, 10 tests in `linkify.test.ts`) returns text/link pieces - never markup, so a message full of angle brackets stays one. Narrow on purpose: `http`, `https`, bare `www.`, nothing else, because a false positive makes prose look pressable. **Brackets are COUNTED, not looked for** - `(see https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chair_(furniture))` ends in two closers, one the address's and one the sentence's, and `!out.includes("(")` keeps both and links to a 404. That was a red test before it was a comment. `MessageText` moved out of `ThreadPane` into its own `use client` file (ThreadPane is an RSC); `scripts/check-client-graph.mjs` clean.

## unified-inbox - the campaign editor, rebuilt (v0.1.46 working tree, unreleased)

The four-step editor is gone. It had a save button on three of its four tabs, each saving a third of the campaign, plus a fourth button ("Use this list") that turned the audience from a number on screen into rows in a table - and a campaign that had not had that fourth press sat there reading **"1 would get this"** directly above **"Nobody is on the list yet"**. Both sentences were true. Neither was any use.

**One form, one Save.** `CampaignEditor.tsx` is now a shell (loads the detail, owns the draft, owns the save) around three section components in `campaigns/sections/` - `WhoSection`, `WhatSection`, `WhenSection` - plus a readiness panel and **one sticky action bar** (`.uin-camp-bar-actions`, `position: sticky; bottom: 0`) that is in the same place on every status. The whole page is one `CampaignDraft` (`campaigns/draft.ts`: `draftFrom` / `toPatch` / `faultIn` / `sameDraft` / `audienceMoved`). **Every number on the draft is a string** - a number input that is really a number cannot be empty, and every box on When is allowed to be.

**Saving is what writes the list down.** `save()` PATCHes the lot, then - only while the campaign is still a draft, nothing has been sent, and either the audience selection moved or `tally.total === 0` - POSTs `audience` with `mode: 'rebuild'`. There is no second button. Rebuild is skipped when the audience did not move, because it is a query over every contact.

**Start sending is not rendered until `readiness.problems.length === 0` and nothing is unsaved.** A button that refuses when you press it is worse than a button that is not there yet.

**Empty means no restriction, everywhere on When.** New `OPEN_WINDOW` in `window.ts` (0-1440, weekdays off, 90s pace) and `createCampaign` now writes the window columns out explicitly instead of leaning on the table's defaults of 480/1020/weekdays - so a new campaign opens with every box blank. `CampaignWindowBody` takes `null` on `startTime`/`endTime`/`intervalSeconds`/`jitterSeconds`/`rampStart`; the PATCH route widens null to 0 / 1440 / the standing pace rather than quietly restoring office hours. `isAllDay()` feeds two new readiness WARNINGS (no hours set; weekends left in) - warnings, not problems, so it is a decision somebody reads and presses past. `DEFAULT_WINDOW` is untouched and stays the office-hours fixture the clock tests are written against.

**A past `start_at` no longer makes an old campaign unsaveable.** The whole form saves at once now, so an untouched "do not begin before" box arrives on every save with last week's value in it. `decideSendAt` refuses that. The route now only refuses when the value actually differs from what is stored (`toWallClock` comparison).

**`describeCampaignFault`** (`lib/campaigns/faults.ts` + `faults.test.ts`, 7 tests) maps Zod issue paths to sentences with the real limit in them - "the gap between messages has to be between 20 seconds and an hour" instead of "That change could not be saved." Array indices are stripped from the path so `steps.2.body` and `steps.0.body` are one entry. Unmapped paths fall back to the old sentence rather than to Zod's own wording. Used by `POST /admin/campaigns` and `PATCH /admin/campaigns/[id]`. The client's `faultIn` catches the same cases before the request is made.

**Send now**, on any waiting row of the progress table. New route `POST /admin/campaigns/[id]/recipients/send-now` → `sendOneRecipientNow` in `runner.ts`, which goes through the SAME `sendToRecipient` as every other send - the file's own rule about not having a second sending path. What it skips is only the WAIT: the window, the daily allowance and the lane's clock. What it does not skip is one-at-a-time (`claimLaneNow` still respects a live claim) or the duplicate guard (`claimRecipient` insists on `state = 'queued'`). Refused outright on a draft, because everything standing between a draft and a real customer is checked when somebody presses start. It puts the gap back on the lane afterwards, so pressing it does not land a second message a moment later.

**A finished campaign that is topped up starts again.** `runOneCampaign` marks a campaign `done` the moment its queue empties, and `listRunnableCampaigns` only ever looks at `running` - so topping up a finished campaign added people who then said "Waiting" for ever with nothing on earth due to look at them. The audience POST now re-reads the tally after a top-up and sets `running` when anybody is queued. Deliberately keyed on the QUEUE rather than on what this top-up added, so a campaign already stuck in that state is put right by pressing the button. Paused and stopped are left alone: both were a person's decision.

**`campaign-sql.live.test.ts`** (`npm run test:inbox-campaign-sql`, `RUN_INBOX_CAMPAIGN_SQL=1`, real throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database on the OVH VPS) executes the three raw queries nothing else runs: the new `createCampaign` INSERT against the window CHECK, `claimRecipient` (second press returns null; a recipient on another campaign returns null; the neighbour is untouched), and `claimLaneNow` (takes a lane an hour early, refuses one already held). 5 tests, all pass. `releaseLane` UPDATEs and does not create, so a lane has to be claimed into existence first - that cost one red run.

**Send it all again**, on a campaign that is `done` or `stopped`. New route `POST /admin/campaigns/[id]/restart` (body `{confirm: true}`, refuses any other status, takes `canReplyToInbox` on the sending address) → `restartAudienceFor` + `resetCampaignForNewRun`. It comes back a DRAFT, so the second run goes through the same start, the same readiness check and the same warnings as the first.

**The order inside `restartAudienceFor` is the whole function**, and both halves fail silently if it is wrong. `clearRecipients` runs BEFORE `buildAudienceFor` rather than inside it, because (a) the cooldown is `recentlyMailedAmong`, asked of `uin_campaign_sends` by address across every campaign, and `buildAudienceFor` asks it before it clears - so this campaign's own sends from ten minutes ago would exclude every single person it just wrote to and the rebuild would come back empty; and (b) `uin_campaign_sends` has UNIQUE `(recipient_id, step_index)`, so keeping the old recipient rows would mean every message in the second run hit that index and was skipped as already sent. New recipient rows are what make a second copy possible at all. The cost, stated on screen in as many words: sends cascade off recipients (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), so the previous run's per-message record is destroyed. What survives is `uin_suppressions` - its `campaign_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` and the campaign is not deleted, so every unsubscribe and every hard bounce is still excluded by the rebuild.

`resetCampaignForNewRun` is its own statement because `setCampaignStatus` holds `started_at` with a COALESCE on purpose (so pause/resume never rewrites when a campaign first went out) - and a restart is the one case where clearing it IS what is meant. `tested_at` is deliberately left: somebody who has tested this exact wording has done the thing the check exists for, and making them do it twice teaches them to click past it. Two more cases in `campaign-sql.live.test.ts` (7 total, all green) cover the reset and the cascade-plus-cooldown mechanism.

**No horizontal scroll on any inbox page.** `InboxPanel` now returns a single root `<div className="uin-page">` on all four of its return paths, styled `max-width: 100%; overflow-x: clip`. Core's `.admin-content` is a stretched flex item with no clipping of its own, so one element a few hundred pixels too wide inside it gave the WHOLE admin page a horizontal scrollbar and a screenful of background to the right of the content. **`clip`, not `hidden`** - `overflow-x: hidden` computes `overflow-y` to `auto`, makes the element a scroll container, and the sticky reading frame (`.uin[data-thread="open"]`) and the campaign save bar would then stick to it instead of to the page. Every region inside already handles its own overflow, so this clips bleed rather than content. Alongside it, `.uin-msg-text` gains `overflow-wrap: anywhere`: `pre-wrap` wraps at spaces, and a three-hundred-character tracking link has none. The offending element was not identified from source - the live admin needs a login - so this is containment at the module boundary; if any scroll survives, `[...document.querySelectorAll("*")].filter(e => e.getBoundingClientRect().right > document.documentElement.clientWidth + 1)` in the console names it.

**Old links still work.** `view` was `who|what|when|watch`; it is now `edit|progress`, and `CampaignsPanel` maps anything that is not `progress`/`watch` to `edit`. `.uin-camp-step` renamed `.uin-camp-section` throughout.

## unified-inbox - campaigns (v0.1.46, working tree, unreleased)

**Migration:** `modules/unified-inbox/migrations/027_campaigns.sql`. Seven tables, no dollar-quoting anywhere, every column TEXT/TEXT[]/INTEGER/BOOLEAN/TIMESTAMP(3) so the schema-coverage backstop needs no new branch. `uin_campaigns` (name, `inbox_id` FK **ON DELETE SET NULL** so removing an address never takes the record of what was sent from it, status `draft|running|paused|done|stopped`, `pause_kind` `manual|bounces|provider|address-gone` + `pause_reason`, `include_signature`, `include_unsubscribe`, `copy_to_sent`, `start_at`, `window_start_minute`/`window_end_minute`/`weekdays_only`/`skip_dates TEXT[]`/`interval_seconds`/`jitter_seconds`/`daily_cap`/`ramp_enabled`/`ramp_start`, `exclude_colleagues`, `tested_at`, CHECKs on every bound). `uin_campaign_categories` join. `uin_campaign_steps` (`step_index` 0-3, `wait_days` 1-90 null on step 0, subject nullable = "Re: the first one", body). `uin_campaign_recipients` (name/company/address SNAPSHOT, `person_id` FK **ON DELETE CASCADE** - these rows hold personal data and an erasure has to take them; state, `step_index`, `due_at`, `claimed_at`, `first_message_id`/`last_message_id`/`first_subject`, UNIQUE on `(campaign_id, lower(address))` which is the dedupe rule enforced where it cannot be got wrong). `uin_campaign_sends` (UNIQUE `(recipient_id, step_index)` - the anti-duplicate guarantee, row written BEFORE the mail service is called; `address` denormalised for the cooldown query; delivery/open/bounce columns). `uin_campaign_lanes` (one row per inbox: `next_send_at`, `claimed_at`). `uin_suppressions` (global, UNIQUE on `lower(btrim(address))`, `campaign_id` SET NULL so an unsubscribe outlives the campaign). Settings columns: `campaign_cooldown_days` (7), `campaign_log_months` (24), `campaign_footer_address`, `campaign_tick_token`. All seven in `teardown`, children first.

**The pace belongs to the ADDRESS, not the campaign.** Two campaigns out of `hi@` at 90s each would be one email every 45s from `hi@`, which is not what anybody set - hence `uin_campaign_lanes`, claimed and released per run. **Nothing sleeps in a function**: the gap lives in `next_send_at`, a tick arriving early sends nothing, so the pace is the pace of the tick. Catch-up is bounded at `MAX_SENDS_PER_RUN = 5` however far behind the lane is, because emptying an hour's backlog into one second is precisely the burst the 90s exists to avoid.

**Three ways in, one engine.** `runDueCampaigns()` (`lib/campaigns/runner.ts`, 18s budget) is called by `GET|POST /api/m/unified-inbox/cron/campaigns` (Bearer `CRON_SECRET`, **or** `?key=` matching `campaign_tick_token` - the free minute-pingers cannot set headers, same bargain as the Brevo webhook address) and by `POST /api/m/unified-inbox/admin/campaigns/tick` (session + permission, called every 30s by `CampaignTicker` while the tab is visible). No separate manual path: a second sending path is a second set of bugs and the second one sends the duplicate.

**lib/campaigns/** - `window.ts` (pure clock: `nextSlot`, `isInsideWindow`, `slotsPerDay`, `rampCapForDay`, `forecastFinish`, `gapAfterSend`; wall-clock through the site zone throughout, so 08:00 is still 08:00 the Monday after the clocks change - proved), `personalise.ts` (five tags, `{{first_name|there}}` inline fallback, unknown tag renders its fallback rather than braces), `unsubscribe.ts` (HMAC of the address under a key derived from `ENCRYPTION_KEY`, so the link survives the campaign's deletion and nobody can opt anybody else out; `List-Unsubscribe` + `List-Unsubscribe-Post` one-click), `audience.ts` (pure include/exclude with the reason in English), `guards.ts` (bounce guard 5% after 50; `isCampaignWideFailure` tells a bad address from a broken campaign), `replies.ts` (reuses the collector's own `auto_kind`, plus a subject test for the out-of-office that carries no headers), `store.ts` (all the raw SQL; `claimNextRecipient` is `UPDATE ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), `message.ts` (borrows `assembleBody`/`outgoingHeaders`/`renderInboxSignature`; a chase is `In-Reply-To` the first message; tracking tag `uinc-<sendId>`), `build.ts`, `readiness.ts`, `preflight.ts` (SPF/DMARC lookup, warning not problem), `events.ts`, `types.ts`.

**Routes:** `GET|POST /admin/campaigns`, `GET|PATCH|DELETE /admin/campaigns/[id]`, `GET|POST /admin/campaigns/[id]/audience`, `POST /admin/campaigns/[id]/state` (start/pause/resume/stop; problems refuse, warnings come back once with `needsAcceptance` and take a second press), `POST /admin/campaigns/[id]/test` (personalised from a REAL recipient, subject prefixed `[TEST]`, stamps `tested_at` which is what unlocks start), `GET /admin/campaigns/[id]/recipients`, `POST /admin/campaigns/tick`, `GET|POST|DELETE /admin/suppressions`, `GET|POST /api/m/unified-inbox/unsubscribe` (PUBLIC, no session, answers HTML with its own inline styles - it renders years later for somebody not logged in). New permission **`unifiedinbox.campaigns`**; sending still needs `canReplyToInbox` on the chosen address.

**Edit rule:** a draft may be changed in every way; a running campaign may have its later steps and its clock changed but not its first message, its list or its sending address - four hundred people have had the old wording.

**Wired into existing code:** `uin_settings` gains three columns + mapper/updater in `lib/db.ts`; `ensureCampaignTickToken`/`getCampaignTickToken`/`regenerateCampaignTickToken` beside the Brevo webhook secret; `personErasePreview` gains `campaignRows`; the Brevo webhook routes a `uinc-` tag to `applyCampaignEvent` and takes campaign BOUNCES even when `trackOpens` is off (a dead address is the mail system reporting a failure, not tracking a person); the housekeeping cron prunes `uin_campaign_recipients` for campaigns finished beyond `campaign_log_months`, 500 a night; `lib/list.ts` gains `campaignsOnly`/`campaignId`; `InboxTabs` gains the tab; `InboxPanel` returns a full-width pane for it. Drive-by: the Contacts tab never highlighted (`currentTab` had no branch for `contactsOnly`) - one line, fixed in passing.

**UI:** `components/admin/inbox/campaigns/` - `CampaignsPanel` (list + routing, client-fetched because the counts move while somebody watches), `CampaignEditor` (Who/What/When/Watch; the editing steps are keyed on `campaign.updatedAt` so a save replaces the boxes rather than an effect copying them), `CampaignWatch`, `SuppressionsPanel`, `CampaignTicker`, `api.ts`. ~200 lines of `uin-camp-*` CSS in `styles.tsx`, tokens only.

**Gates:** `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean; `npm test` 5495 pass / 0 fail, of which 85 are the new `window.test.ts`, `personalise.test.ts`, `unsubscribe.test.ts`, `audience.test.ts` and `guards.test.ts`. **The backup round-trip passes with `unified-inbox` in the built list** - all four cases green against real throwaway databases, so these seven tables are dumped, restored and compared byte-for-byte rather than skipped.

**One trap worth writing down, because it cost an afternoon.** An earlier run reported `module schemas left out: unified-inbox ()` and still went green. Two things made that hard to read. The empty parentheses are the harness's own doing - it prints `message.split('\n')[0]` and a Prisma error's first line is blank, so EVERY refusal prints as `()`. And the refusal itself was the module's 27 migration files exceeding the 55-second per-module transaction timeout, which only happened over an SSH tunnel; run against the server directly it applies in time and is covered. So: a `left out` line naming a module you have just changed means the gate proved nothing about it, whatever the pass says at the bottom - re-run it on a decent connection before believing the green. (The tunnel is the documented fallback for a network that swallows 5432: `ssh -N -L 55432:127.0.0.1:5432`, then `OVH_DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 OVH_DB_PORT=55432`. It works, and it is slow enough to cause this.)

## unified-inbox - contact categories (working tree, unreleased)

**Migration:** `modules/unified-inbox/migrations/026_contact_categories.sql`. Two tables. `uin_contact_categories` (`id`, `name`, `sort_order`, timestamps) with a UNIQUE index on the EXPRESSION `lower(btrim("name"))` - which is why `findOrCreateCategory` writes `ON CONFLICT (lower(btrim("name")))` and not `ON CONFLICT ("name")`: the latter is a syntax error against an expression index and nothing but a real database would say so. `uin_person_categories` is the join, PK on the pair (so a re-run import is a no-op rather than a second row), both FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE`, plus an index on `category_id` for the filter. Both added to `teardown` in `cactus.module.json`, children first.

**db.ts:** `mapCategory`, `listCategories` (with `peopleCount`, merged-away excluded), `getCategory`, `findCategoryByName`, `findOrCreateCategory`, `createCategory` (appends at `MAX(sort_order)+1`), `renameCategory`, `deleteCategory`, `reorderCategories`, `categoriesForPerson`, `categoriesForPeople` (one query for a whole page), `setPersonCategories` (replace - deletes by `NOT IN` the survivors so an existing membership keeps its date), `addPersonCategories` (add only). `listPeople` gains a `categoryId` filter as an `EXISTS` subquery. `countPeople`/`countOrganisations` from the previous entry are gone - the pre-existing `peopleCount()` already answers both in one query and `InboxPanel` uses it.

**contacts.ts:** `'categories'` added to `CONTACT_FIELDS` (in the "Who they are" group), label "Categories", header aliases covering category/categories/group(s)/type/contact type/customer type/tag(s)/label(s)/segment/list/classification. `splitCategories` splits on `,` `;` `|` and newline - never on a space, or "Trade customer" becomes two - de-duplicates ignoring case keeping the first spelling, and stops at `MAX_CATEGORIES_PER_CONTACT` (20) so a mis-mapped notes column cannot invent hundreds. `joinCategories` is the inverse. `rowToContact`'s two-columns-onto-one-field join is comma-separated for categories and space-separated for everything else. `ImportSummary` gains `categoriesCreated`.

**contact-store.ts:** `CategoryMode` (`'replace'` for a form, `'add'` for a file) and `extraCategoryIds` on `saveContact`; `applyCategories` treats an ABSENT `categories` field as "leave them alone" and an empty one as "none of them". `ImportOptions.categoryName` is the blanket category, resolved once per run; per-row names are resolved through a `Map` cache and the draft's own `categories` is blanked before `saveContact` so the names are not looked up twice.

**Routes:** `GET|POST /api/m/unified-inbox/categories` (POST `reply` - a category is typed onto a card, and a list only an administrator may extend stays as whoever set the site up imagined it), `PATCH|DELETE /categories/[id]` (`manage` - renaming changes what everybody wearing it is called), `POST /categories/reorder` (`manage`). `GET /people` gains `?category=` and returns each row's labels; the import route passes `categoryName` through. New schemas `CategoryBody`, `CategoryOrderBody`; `ContactBody.categories`; `ContactImportBody.categoryName`.

**UI:** new `CategoryPicker` (ticks, not a text box - two people spelling "Supplier" three ways is the failure mode - with a **New category** box for one that does not exist, added to the ticked set and not saved until the card is), a filter row of chips under the contacts toolbar (`cat=<id>` in the URL, cleared by the tab links and by switching People/Organisations), labels as tags on the list rows and under the name on a person's page, a **Category** box in the importer, and `CategoriesSection` in **Settings -> Unified Inbox -> People** for rename/remove/reorder. The settings GET now serves `categories`. New CSS: `.uin-categories`, `.uin-category-chips`, `.uin-category-chip` (pressed said by fill AND border, plus `aria-pressed`), `.uin-category-new`.

**Gates:** `contacts.test.ts` 33 pure cases (8 new, covering the splitter and the header aliases). `contacts.live.test.ts` grew 13 cases for the expression index, the upsert, replace-vs-add, the filter, the page-wide fetch, the cascade on both sides and the importer's two category paths - see the note on that suite below.

## unified-inbox - Contacts, and an address book import (working tree, unreleased)

**Migration:** `modules/unified-inbox/migrations/025_contacts.sql`. No new tables. `uin_people` gains `first_name`, `last_name`, `job_title`, `website`, `address_line1/2`, `address_city`, `address_county`, `address_postcode`, `address_country` (all TEXT, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) and `origin TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'mail'` with a CHECK on `('mail','hand','import')`; `uin_organisations` gains `email`, `phone`, `website`, `notes`, the same six address columns and the same `origin`. Two indexes: `uin_people_name_idx (last_name, first_name)` and `uin_organisations_name_idx (name)`. A one-off UPDATE splits an existing two-word `display_name` with no `@` in it into first/last, so the list has something to sort by on day one; anything longer or shorter is left alone. TEXT only, so `isSupportedUdtName` needs no new branch.

**Phone numbers are NOT a column.** They stay in `uin_person_identities` alongside the addresses, which is what makes a number typed onto a card recognise the caller who rings it. A `phone` column beside that table would be a second place to look and the two would drift.

**New pure lib:** `lib/contacts.ts` - `CONTACT_FIELDS`/`_LABELS`/`_GROUPS`, `contactDisplayName`, `splitName`, `addressLines`, a hand-rolled `parseCsv` (BOM-stripping; no papaparse dependency, Directory/shop precedent), `buildImportTemplateCsv`, `guessColumnMap` (header aliases for Outlook, Google Contacts, Apple, Mailchimp; two passes so a file with both `Name` and `First name` uses the specific columns), `rowToContact`, `isWorthImporting`. Imported by both the browser and the routes.

**New server lib:** `lib/contact-store.ts` - `saveContact(personId | null, draft, {origin, organisationId?})` and `importContacts(rows, map, {updateExisting})`. One code path for the card and the importer, on purpose. An identity somebody else holds is reported, never moved. Organisations are matched on `lower(btrim(name))` before one is created, and the import caches that lookup per run.

**db.ts:** `PERSON_SELECT`/`mapPerson` widened; `createPerson`/`updatePerson` take a `PersonDetails` bag driven by one `PERSON_DETAIL_COLUMNS` table so insert and update cannot drift; `listPeople` gains `sort: 'name' | 'recent'`, an `organisationId` filter, a wider search (first/last/job title/postcode/town) and a `phone` subquery; new `countPeople`, `countOrganisations`, `countThreadsForPerson`, `mapOrganisation`, `listOrganisations`, `findOrganisationByName`, `createOrganisation`, `updateOrganisation`, `deleteOrganisation`, `deleteIdentityForPerson`. `addIdentity` now returns `boolean` (whether the row went in).

**Routes:** `GET|POST /api/m/unified-inbox/people` (POST new, `reply`), `PATCH /people/[id]` (now the whole card, `reply`), `POST /people/[id]/identities` + `DELETE /people/[id]/identities/[identityId]` (`reply`; the person is in the WHERE, not checked-then-trusted), `GET|POST /api/m/unified-inbox/organisations`, `PATCH|DELETE /organisations/[id]` (DELETE takes `manage`), `POST /api/m/unified-inbox/contacts/import` (`manage`, `maxDuration = 300`). New Zod schemas in `lib/validation.ts`: `ContactBody`, `IdentityBody`, `OrganisationBody`, `OrganisationPatchBody`, `ContactImportBody`.

**URL params** (`lib/list.ts`): `inbox=contacts` -> `contactsOnly`; `view=organisations` -> `contactsView`; `org=<id>` -> `organisationId` (opens the card in the organisations view, filters the list in the people view); `import=1`; `edit=1`; `person=new` / `org=new` via the exported `NEW_CONTACT`. All four added to `carried` in `InboxPanel`, and cleared by `InboxTabs`' tab links.

**Components:** `ContactsToolbar`, `ContactsListView`, `OrganisationsListView`, `ContactCard`, `OrganisationCard`, `ContactImport`, `ContactIdentities`, `ContactFieldRows` (shared `CardField`/`CardNotes`/`CardSection`). The import parses the file in the browser and posts rows + column targets - nothing is uploaded, so there is no copy in storage to sweep up. New CSS: `.uin-card`, `.uin-card-section`, `.uin-card-notes`, `.uin-card-check`, `.uin-postal`; the field rows reuse the composer's `.uin-fields`.

**PersonActions lost its edit panel.** It held a name box and a note box, which was everything a person had. The card now has fourteen fields and lives on its own screen, so `PersonActionsProvider` takes `editHref` instead of `displayName`/`notes` and the bar offers a link. Merge, split, export and erase are untouched.

**A contact with no conversations is no longer hidden.** `InboxPanel` used to treat "no threads this reader may see" as "not yours to know about", which was right while writing in was the only way to become a person. It now asks `countThreadsForPerson` as well, so a contact added by hand is visible to anybody with `view` and a supplier who only ever wrote to `accounts@` is still hidden (E17 intact).

**Gates:** new `modules/unified-inbox/lib/contacts.test.ts` (25 pure cases) and `modules/unified-inbox/lib/contacts.live.test.ts` (18 cases against a real throwaway `cactus_rt_*` Postgres with all 25 migrations applied - the `origin` CHECK, the surname ordering, the identity uniqueness, the whole importer). `RUN_INBOX_CONTACTS=1`, wired as `npm run test:inbox-contacts`. 18/18 real PASS. Backup round-trip 4/4 real PASS with 025 applied. `npm test` 5402 passed, `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean.

## unified-inbox - a folder can own its post (v0.1.45, working tree, unreleased)

**Migration:** `modules/unified-inbox/migrations/024_folder_owns_mail.sql` - one column, `uin_inboxes.folder_owns_mail BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`. 001 carries it too for fresh installs. BOOLEAN only, so `isSupportedUdtName` needs no new branch.

**Routing:** `lib/addresses.ts`. `RoutingDecision.matchedOn` gains `'folder'`. `routeToInbox(headers, inboxes, folderInboxId?)` tries Delivered-To, To, Cc, then the folder owner, then the catch-all; `routeSentToInbox` and `placeMessage` pass it through. `'folder'` is NOT in `NAMED_RECIPIENT`: a message from one of our own addresses found in a folder somebody else owns stays outbound and is simply filed there.

**Which inbox owns which folder:** `folderOwnersFor(inboxes)` in `lib/sync-plan.ts` (exported, unit tested). Lower-cased folder names, and a folder claimed by two inboxes maps to `null` - nobody owns it and routing falls back to the addresses.

**The mail already in the folder:** `adoptUnroutedFolderMail(inboxId)` in `lib/db.ts`, called from PATCH `app/api/admin/inboxes/[id]/route.ts` only when the flag goes false -> true. `UPDATE uin_threads ... FROM uin_inboxes` with a correlated `EXISTS` on `uin_messages` (same connection, `lower(imap_folder)` match), restricted to `inbox_id IS NULL AND provider_module IS NULL`, then `routed_on = 'folder'` for the messages it moved. The route answers `adopted`, and `InboxesPanel` says how many conversations moved.

**UI:** one `CheckField` above the catch-all one ("Everything in that folder belongs to this address"), plus a **Whole folder** chip on the inbox list row.

**Gates:** new `modules/unified-inbox/lib/folder-adoption.live.test.ts`, `RUN_INBOX_ADOPTION_GUARDS=1`, added to `npm run test:inbox-guards`. 7 cases: the dragged email, folder-name casing, an already-filed conversation left alone, another folder, the same folder name on another account, live chat/contact form threads, and a send-only address. 7/7 real PASS. Backup round-trip 4/4 real PASS with 024 applied.

## core + unified-inbox - a module's sent email is filed as a conversation (working tree, unreleased)

**No migration.** Every column used already exists; the only type change is `OutboundMessageInput.authorUserId`, widened to `string | null` in `modules/unified-inbox/lib/db.ts`.

**New core extension point:** `core.outbound-email-record`, in `lib/email/record.ts`. `recordOutboundModuleEmail(email)` walks installed modules' `manifest.extensionPoints`, exactly as `lib/email/identity.ts` does, and offers the sent message to **every** recorder rather than stopping at the first - two modules wanting a copy is not a contradiction. Returns before touching the database when the generated registry has no entry for the point.

**Core call site:** `keepCopyForModule()` in `lib/email/index.ts`, awaited inside `sendEmail`'s success branch after `recordEmailSend`. Guarded on `payload.moduleName`, so core's own auth/system/member mail is never offered. Wrapped in try/catch: the message has already been accepted by the service, and filing is not a thing to fail a checkout over. `attachments` is `usableAttachments(message.attachments)` - what actually travelled, not what was asked for.

**Unified Inbox recorder:** `modules/unified-inbox/lib/outbound-record.ts`, registered `serverOnly`. The switch was the one the site already threw: `getModuleSenderInboxId(moduleName)`, i.e. the inbox chosen on that module's own settings tab. **Superseded 2026-09-07 by migration 046** - filing now reads `getModuleCopyInboxId(moduleName)`, its own setting, because a shop wants the copies without changing the address a confirmation arrives from. No inbox, nothing filed. Otherwise `createOutboundThread` (unread false, `last_direction` out) + `insertOutboundMessage` with `authorUserId: null` + `settleDelivery` carrying `cleanMessageId(providerMessageId)`.

**Why the provider id is the point:** most services stamp their own Message-ID over anything we set, so the supplier's `In-Reply-To` quotes Brevo's. `threadsForMessageIds` already matches `provider_message_id` as well as `message_id_header`, so storing it is what makes the reply land under the order instead of starting a third conversation. Record links come free on the next sync tick - `threadsNeedingLinks` picks up any thread with `linked_at` behind `last_message_at`, and the `po` pattern finds the order number in the subject.

**Attachments:** stored through the existing `cacheAttachment`, under this module's own prefix with no media library row, and vouched for by `lib/media-usage-provider.ts` like every other attachment. Storage is checked BEFORE the message row is written, so a site with no media provider gets `hasAttachments: false` and no attachment rows rather than a paperclip that opens onto an error.

**Tests:** `lib/email/record.test.ts` (6 - module mail offered, core mail not, failed send not, oversized attachment excluded, a throwing recorder cannot take the send down) and `modules/unified-inbox/lib/outbound-record.test.ts` (6 - no inbox files nothing, deleted inbox files nothing, thread/message shape, provider id kept, document kept, no storage means no claimed attachment). `lib/email/send.test.ts` gained stubs for `@/lib/email/identity` and `@/lib/email/record`: resolving either for real pulls in the whole generated extension-point graph, which exceeds the 5s test timeout when the suite runs in parallel.

---

## unified-inbox - chase it up, and stand it down (v0.1.41, core pin v0.5.1479)

**Migration:** `modules/unified-inbox/migrations/022_follow_up_and_hold.sql`. Three columns on `uin_drafts` - `follow_up_minutes INTEGER` (CHECKed 1440..527040), `held_by_thread_id TEXT` (FK `uin_threads` ON DELETE SET NULL) and `held_at TIMESTAMP(3)` - plus a partial index `uin_drafts_held_idx`. INTEGER/TEXT/TIMESTAMP only, so the backup schema-coverage backstop needs no new branch. 021 untouched.

**Follow-up.** A scheduled draft may carry a length of time. Once the message has actually gone the conversation is SNOOZED for that long and ASSIGNED to `draft.author_user_id` - not to whoever pressed Send, because a shared address means a colleague can finish somebody else's message and the person waiting on an answer is the one who asked the question. Expressed as a snooze deliberately: `reopenOnReply` already wakes a snoozed conversation, so a chase nobody needs cancels itself. New `lib/follow-up.ts` (`applyFollowUpAfterSend`) is called from BOTH send paths - the cron in `lib/scheduled-send.ts` and `app/api/send/route.ts` for a hand-sent draft - and never throws: the message has gone.

**`deleteDraft` now goes through `deleteDraftReturning` (DELETE ... RETURNING \*)** so `discardDraftAfterSend` hands back the row it removed; the chase outlives the draft that carried it without a second query racing the delete.

**Standing a message down.** Inbound, non-automated mail whose sender matches a scheduled draft's To line (case-insensitively) clears `send_state` and sets the two held columns - `holdScheduledDraftsFor`, called from `writeSide` in `lib/sync.ts` beside `reopenOnReply`. The TIME IS KEPT so the screen can say what it was going to do; a time with no state is an ordinary draft to every other query. Only `send_state = 'scheduled'` rows: one already claimed as `'sending'` may be at the mail server. Two new thread events, `held` and `awaiting`, both unattended.

**UI:** `SendLater` gains the follow-up menu and the held notice; `ThreadPane` shows the held-draft banner (fed by `draftsHeldByThread`, read through the same visibility clause as every other draft read) and names the chase's owner in the log; `DraftListView` shows `Held - they wrote first` via `scheduleLabel`. `DraftBody` gains `followUpMinutes`, bounded by `decideFollowUp`.

**Verified:** `tsc` clean, `eslint` clean, `npm test` for the module 544 passed, and `RUN_INBOX_SCHEDULE_GUARDS=1` `scheduled-send.live.test.ts` a real PASS against a throwaway VPS database - the migration and all five new statements executed, including the hold's `unnest`/`lower` match and the author-not-sender assignment.

---

## unified-inbox - write it now, send it later (v0.1.40, core pin v0.5.1478)

**Migration:** `modules/unified-inbox/migrations/021_scheduled_send.sql`. Four columns on `uin_drafts` - `send_at TIMESTAMP(3)`, `send_state TEXT` (`scheduled` | `sending` | `failed`, CHECKed), `send_error TEXT`, `claimed_at TIMESTAMP(3)` - plus two partial indexes (`uin_drafts_due_idx`, `uin_drafts_claimed_idx`) and a CHECK that a state cannot exist without a time. All TEXT/TIMESTAMP, so the backup schema-coverage backstop needs no new branch. 013 is untouched: an applied migration is never edited, and 021 runs after it on fresh installs anyway.

**A scheduled message is a DRAFT WITH A DEPARTURE TIME, not a third kind of thing.** No new table, no new tab, no new access rule: `draftScope`/`editScope`, the Drafts list, the Drafts count, the cascade when an inbox or a user is deleted and the tidy-up after a send all already govern it. It is deliberately not a status on `uin_messages` - an unsent message must never be walked by retention, webhooks, threading or the Sent list.

**New:** `lib/scheduled.ts` (pure - `decideSendAt` turns a typed wall clock into an instant **in the site's zone** via core's `instantAtWallClock`, `toWallClock` back again, `describeSendAt`/`scheduleLabel`, `plainTextToHtml`, `MIN_LEAD_MS` 60s, `MAX_AHEAD_DAYS` 366, `STALE_CLAIM_MS` 10min) with `lib/scheduled.test.ts`; `lib/scheduled-send.ts` (`runDueScheduledSends`, `SCHEDULED_BUDGET_MS` 20s, `SCHEDULED_BATCH` 10); `components/admin/inbox/SendLater.tsx`; `app/api/cron/scheduled-send/route.ts`.

**New cron job:** `/api/m/unified-inbox/cron/scheduled-send`, `*/5 * * * *` in `cactus.module.json`. The site's dispatcher is hourly (daily on Hobby), so that means "every tick", and the copy says "at that time or shortly after, never before it" rather than promising a minute. `POST /api/m/unified-inbox/admin/check-now` also flushes what is due first, with an 8s slice, and reports `sentOnSchedule` - somebody watching the screen is a better clock than the tick.

**Sending twice is guarded twice.** `claimDueScheduledDrafts` moves the row out of `scheduled` in the same UPDATE that finds it (`... WHERE id IN (SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED)`), so a second run gets nothing; and the idempotency key handed to `sendMessage` is `scheduled-<draftId>`, so even two claims land on one `uin_messages` row.

**Rights are checked when it LEAVES, not when it was set.** New in `lib/access.ts`: `canUserReplyToInbox(userId, inboxId)` and `userCanReply(userId)`, the send-side twins of `canUserViewInbox`. Somebody taken off `accounts@` on Friday does not have a message leave as `accounts@` on Monday - it fails with a sentence and the writing is kept.

**`saveDraft` now has three answers about time, not two:** a Date schedules, `null` cancels, and **left out means leave it alone** - the SET clause is assembled from two `Prisma.sql` fragments for exactly that. Pressing "Save as a draft" on a message set for the morning must not quietly cancel the morning. `DraftBody` gains `sendAt` as a wall-clock string (`"2026-09-04T09:00"`, no zone); the drafts route refuses a past time, a time over a year out, a missing address, an empty body and (for a new conversation) missing recipients or subject **at the point somebody presses the button**, not at 3am.

**Live SQL guard:** `lib/scheduled-send.live.test.ts`, opted into with `RUN_INBOX_SCHEDULE_GUARDS=1` against a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database on the OVH VPS. Nothing else executes these statements - the claim's SKIP LOCKED semantics and the two-fragment SET are claims about Postgres, not about TypeScript.

**UI:** `SendLater` under the box in both composers (reply and new message), the picker's floor (`minSendAt`) computed on the server in the site's zone and carried through `ThreadPane` → `ComposerSlot` → `Composer`. Drafts rows carry a `uin-tag-snoozed` "Goes out ..." tag, or `uin-tag-failed` "Did not go out" with the reason in the title. `DraftReadOnlyView` says the same for a colleague's. New styles: `.uin-sendlater`, `.uin-sendlater-picker`.

**Also in this release, from separate work in the same tree:** `chooseSignatureSource` takes a third argument, `sendingInboxIsSomebodysOwn`, and returns the sending inbox outright when it is true - an address that is somebody's own signs as them whoever pressed Send, so a colleague posting Marcus's draft does not sign it in their own name. `lib/send.ts` asks `inboxIsSomebodysOwn(inbox.id)` first and only reads the sender's own default inbox when the answer is no.

**Wiki:** `Unified-Inbox.md`, new "Sending it later" section.

---

## unified-inbox - the writing box opens on request, and the header is pinned (v0.1.39, core pin v0.5.1477)

No migration, no API change. The composer was mounted under every conversation whether anybody was writing or not.

**New:** `components/admin/inbox/ComposerOpen.tsx` - `ComposerOpenProvider` (client context holding `{ mode, at } | null`), `ReplyActions` (the Reply / Forward / Internal note buttons, for the head) and `ComposerSlot` (the box itself, where the composer always sat). Two places, one answer, which is why it is a context rather than state in either. Keyed on `thread.id` in `ThreadPane`, so opening the next conversation starts shut.

**Reply to all is deliberately not a fourth button** - it is a variation on Reply and only exists when somebody else is on the message, so it stays a chip inside the box. The Reply button reads as pressed for both.

**Changed:** `Composer` exports `ComposerMode` and takes `requestedMode` / `requestedAt`. `requestedAt` counts presses so a second press of the same button still reads as an instruction; it is applied **during render** (React's adjust-state-on-prop-change idiom), not in an effect - `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` refuses the effect version, and rightly.

**Not rendered while shut, rather than hidden:** the composer holds a draft, an idempotency token and a beforeunload guard, none of which should be alive on a conversation nobody is writing on. A draft opens the box on the way in - `openAs = draft && draft.mode !== 'new' ? draft.mode : null` - because a draft nobody can see is a draft nobody finishes.

**`cannotReplyReason` moved with it.** It used to be an alert inside the always-open box; with the box shut, a missing Reply button and no explanation reads as a fault, so `ReplyActions` shows the reason beside the buttons when `!canReply`. The composer still shows it too.

**Sticky again, and only above 900px.** `.uin-thread-head` gets `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 3` inside the media query where `.uin` is the sticky frame and each pane scrolls its own contents. Below that the page scrolls and the band would pin to the phone's viewport, which is what the comment removed here warned about - the original unpinning was because the composer sat open directly beneath it, and that is no longer true.

**Wiki:** `Unified-Inbox.md`, the reply paragraph and the newest-first paragraph.

---

## unified-inbox - pressing refresh refreshes, it does not refuse (v0.1.38, core pin v0.5.1476)

No migration. `POST /api/m/unified-inbox/admin/check-now` no longer answers 429 "A check has just run - give it N seconds and try again."

**Three things were wrong with the old rule.** It refused the WHOLE request when ANY one account had been visited inside the minute, so a second mailbox that was hours stale went uncollected. It refused rather than refreshing, so the press did nothing at all - the client only calls `router.refresh()` on an ok. And it used one cooldown for a round the page ran on its own and one a person pressed for, which are not the same question.

**New:** `lib/check-cooldown.ts` - `COOLDOWN_MS` (60s, automatic), `PRESSED_COOLDOWN_MS` (10s, pressed), `cooldownFor(automatic)`, `dueForCheck(accounts, cooldownMs, now)` returning `{ due, restedSeconds }`. Pure, with `lib/check-cooldown.test.ts` (9 cases) - the whole point being that this rule is only ever wrong in ways `tsc` has no opinion about.

**Changed:** the route filters to the due accounts and syncs those one at a time against one shared deadline (as `syncAllConnections` does; it no longer calls it). All resting is `200 { ok: true, checked: false, message: 'Your mail was checked N seconds ago, so this is up to date.' }` - the screen reloads on the back of it. Successful checks now carry `checked: true`.

**Changed:** `components/admin/inbox/CheckNowButton.tsx` posts `{ auto: quiet }` - the interval's rounds are `auto: true` and keep the minute; a press gets the ten seconds.

**Wiki:** `Unified-Inbox.md`, the refresh-button paragraph.

---

## unified-inbox - our own reply, recognised when a relay rewrote its Message-ID (v0.1.37, core pin v0.5.1475)

No migration. Fixes replies appearing twice in a conversation: once as the row the send path wrote, once as the delivered copy collected from IMAP.

**The mechanism, read off the live database, not inferred.** Brevo's relay replaces the `Message-ID` we set with one of its own (`<uuid>@smtp-relay.sendinblue.com`) - the API answer echoes ours, so `EmailLog.providerId` holds our id and looks fine, while the mail on the wire carries the relay's. When the delivered copy is collected (which happens on every message between two of the site's own addresses, and on any account that files a copy of its own outgoing mail into a folder we read), `findOutboundByMessageId` cannot recognise it and it is filed as a second message. The Sent-folder copy is unaffected - we APPEND our own bytes, so it still carries our id. `append_to_sent` is **not** the trigger, despite looking like one.

**New:** `lib/relay-copy.ts` - `chooseRelayCopy(candidates, incoming)`, `RELAY_COPY_WINDOW_MS` (120s), `OutboundCandidate`. Pure, no database. Matches on sender, exact `to` and `cc` sets, normalised subject and time, and picks the nearest by time so two replies a minute apart pair off one-to-one.

**New:** `lib/db.ts` `unlocatedOutboundNear({ fromAddress, sentAt, windowMs })` - outbound email rows with `connection_id IS NULL`, which is the "never been found in a mailbox" marker (the send path leaves it empty; `attachLocation` fills it, so a row is claimable once). `recordRelayIdentity(id, relayMessageId)` writes the relay's id to `provider_message_id`.

**Changed:** `lib/sync.ts` - `matchRelayCopy()` runs when `findOutboundByMessageId` returns nothing and the sender is one of this account's own addresses; its result feeds the existing `ours` branch unchanged, so the internal path still writes the colleague's side.

**Second thing it fixes:** `threadsForMessageIds` already matched `provider_message_id`, but nothing ever populated it - `deliver()` returns null because core does not hand Brevo's id back. A customer's reply quotes the relay's id in `In-Reply-To`, so header threading was falling through to the subject heuristic on every reply to a message this hub sent. Claiming a copy fills that column in.

**Gate:** `lib/relay-copy.test.ts` (9 cases) plus a real-database case in `lib/internal-threads.live.test.ts` - claims the row, proves it cannot be claimed twice, and proves the relay id then leads to the conversation. `npm run test:inbox-guards`, 16 tests, real PASS.

**Existing duplicate rows are not cleaned up by this.** The fix stops new ones. Deskwell's 19 were removed by hand on 2026-09-03 - each duplicate merged into the row it was a copy of (relay id onto `provider_message_id`, location by COALESCE) and then deleted, `message_count` recounted on the six conversations involved, with a JSON backup and a verified rollback script. A pairing that was not one to one aborted the transaction.

---

## unified-inbox - a reply puts a conversation back in Open (in the working tree, unreleased)

Module **v0.1.36**, core pin **v0.5.1473**. Supersedes v0.1.35, which did this for `snoozed` only. No migration: `uin_events.kind` is free text and `uin_events.user_id` is already nullable.

**API change (v0.1.35 -> v0.1.36):** `wakeSnoozedThread(id): Promise<boolean>` is gone, replaced by `reopenOnReply(id): Promise<ReopenedFrom>` where `ReopenedFrom = 'snoozed' | 'done' | null`. Widened from `"status" = 'snoozed'` to `"status" <> 'open'`, so both ways out of Open are reversed by one rule.

**The statement is the unusual bit.** The old status has to come back to the caller, and plain `RETURNING` hands back the value just written - so it is a CTE that selects `id, status` under `FOR UPDATE`, then `UPDATE ... FROM "before" ... RETURNING "before"."status"`. `FOR UPDATE` is also what settles two ticks racing: the second blocks, re-reads under EvalPlanQual, finds the row open and matches nothing. Executed for real, not assumed.

**Why `done` mattered more than `snoozed`:** `unreadCounts` (db.ts) counts `unread = true AND "status" <> 'done'`. A reply to a done conversation set `unread`, bumped `last_message_at` and produced no badge anywhere - unread and invisible, indefinitely. A snoozed one at least resurfaced via `wakeDueThreads`. `unreadCounts` is deliberately left as it is; reopening is the fix.

**New event kind:** `'woken'` added to `ThreadEventKind`, written with `user_id` NULL. Detail carries `{ was: 'snoozed' | 'done' }` plus `{ direction }` from mail or `{ providerModule }` from another module's channel.

**Changed:** `lib/sync.ts` `writeSide()` calls `reopenOnReply` after `touchThread` when `!automated`, both directions - an inbound message here is the customer, and an OUTBOUND one is a colleague answering outside this hub, because a reply typed here is turned away earlier by `findOutboundByMessageId`. `lib/provider-sync.ts` does the same when `stored > 0`, after `recountProviderThread`.

**Deliberately does not reopen:** automated mail (out-of-office, bounce - same `automated` flag E7 uses for the unread flag, and the thing that stops a mailing list dragging a finished conversation back weekly), internal notes (`insertNote` still touches nothing), and this hub's own send (never reaches `touchThread`).

**Changed:** `components/admin/inbox/ThreadPane.tsx` - `unattendedEvent(event)` replaces the `UNATTENDED_EVENTS` map, since the wording now depends on `detail.was`. An actorless event renders its own whole sentence instead of "Somebody ...".

**Retention is unaffected:** `threadsDueForRetention` filters on `last_message_at`, never on status.

**Gate:** `lib/reopen-on-reply.live.test.ts` (renamed from `snooze-wake.live.test.ts`), `RUN_INBOX_REOPEN_GUARDS=1` (renamed from `RUN_INBOX_SNOOZE_GUARDS`), needs `OVH_SERVER`/`OVH_USER`/`OVH_PASSWORD`. Eight tests against a real throwaway Postgres: opens from `snoozed` and from `done` and reports which, clears the stamp with it, idempotent from both, leaves `open` alone, touches no bystander, accepts a null `user_id` on the event, moves the row between the status tabs' lists, and restores the unread badge that `done` suppresses. A skip is a fail.

**Script:** `npm run test:inbox-guards` - runs `reopen-on-reply.live.test.ts` and `internal-threads.live.test.ts` with both flags set, `--no-file-parallelism`.

---

## unified-inbox - the discussion form (in the working tree, unreleased)

**New component:** `components/admin/inbox/ColleagueField.tsx` - the To line on Start A Discussion. A token field: chosen colleagues sit as `.uin-chip .uin-chip-clear .uin-person-chip` tags with a cross apiece, and the box beside them filters the `staff` list already handed down (no request - the list is small, known and on the page). Arrows move the highlight, Return/Tab take it, Backspace on an empty box lifts the last tag, Escape shuts the menu and `stopPropagation`s so the dialog stays open. Its value is the `mentions` array the discussions route already took.

**Changed:** `components/admin/inbox/DiscussionView.tsx` - modal title `A discussion` -> `Start A Discussion`; To is now the colleague field; the address tickboxes were labelled **In** and have since been removed altogether (see the entry at the top of this file - the address is settled on the server); the "Whoever can read that address can read the discussion." hint is gone and the multi-address hint only shows when more than one is ticked; the old "Let somebody know" chip picker and its `MENTION_CHIPS` search box are gone (the To line replaced them); the button strip is right-aligned (`.uin-composer-row--end`) with Cancel then **Start the discussion**.

**Styles** (`components/admin/inbox/styles.tsx`): new `.uin-people`, `.uin-people > input`, `.uin-person-chip .uin-chip-clear-x`, `.uin-composer-row--end`.

---

## unified-inbox - colleague mail on both conversations (in the working tree, unreleased)

**Migration** `migrations/020_internal_threads.sql`. Swaps the `uin_messages` unique index from `(connection_id, message_id_header)` to `(connection_id, thread_id, message_id_header)`; adds column `internal_key` with a unique index on `(thread_id, internal_key)`; winds `uin_sync_state.last_seen_uid` back, per folder, to just before the oldest internal message in it, so the sweep re-reads and fills in missing sides. Idempotent.

**New:** `lib/addresses.ts` `internalSides()`; `lib/threading.ts` `internalPairKey()`, `ThreadRef`, `chooseThread({ restrictToInbox })`; `lib/db.ts` `threadsHoldingIdentity()`, `recentRecipients()`, `threadsForMessageIds()` now returns `Map<string, ThreadRef[]>`; `lib/sync.ts` `writeSide()` covers both the mirrored and the ordinary path.

**New route:** `app/api/admin/recipients/route.ts` → `GET /api/m/unified-inbox/admin/recipients?inbox&q&limit`. Bounded by `visibleInboxIds`. Zod on the query string.

**New component:** `components/admin/inbox/RecipientField.tsx` - combobox behind the To and Cc lines on Write a message. Styles under `.uin-recipient-field` / `.uin-suggestions` in `components/admin/inbox/styles.tsx`.

**Changed:** `components/admin/inbox/CheckNowButton.tsx` - an automatic check no longer posts a notice, even when it collected something. Only a pressed button reports.

**Gate:** `lib/internal-threads.live.test.ts`, `RUN_INBOX_INTERNAL_GUARDS=1`, needs `OVH_SERVER`/`OVH_USER`/`OVH_PASSWORD`. Executes migration 020 and both unique indexes against a real throwaway Postgres. A skip is a fail.
